# MONTH OF OCTOBER

*(Woodcut headpiece depicting the Protection of the Most Holy Theotokos)*[^5]

*Having 31 days.*

*October 1. The Protection of the Most Holy Theotokos:*

*On the 1st day: the Holy Apostle Ananias, one of the Seventy; and our venerable Father Romanus the Melodist.*
*On the same day we celebrate the Protection of the Most Holy Theotokos.*
*The Feast of the Most Holy Theotokos:*[^6] *The service is written following this service.*
*At Vespers, the customary kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Apostle:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Being called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*
Having received the divine radiance, when blinded by the better bidding,[^7] Saul, who had previously been held fast by darkness, came unto thee asking for divine purification. Then, O all-blessed one, like a wise hierarch, thou didst adopt him as a son through baptism, who thereafter was to adopt the whole inhabited world as sons. Therefore with him we bless thee, Christ's apostle, O God-wise Ananias; pray that our souls may be saved.

Having been illumined divinely and most gloriously, louder than thunder, O blessed one, thou didst trumpet the saving word of God, and didst awaken those sleeping in the graves to light; casting away the deadening that descends upon the dead and returning those held fast in Hades, which thou didst accomplish through Him Who put death to death, making them honorable vessels once again of Jesus, the Savior of our souls.

As a bearer of light, as a divine preacher, as a God-chosen witness of the passions of Christ and a fellow heir and partaker of the future ineffable glory, abiding with the Master and ever enjoying the out-flowing radiance from the unwaning Light, O God-proclaimer: [preserve] those who now celebrate thy bright feast now; by thy prayers, O Ananias, deliver from gloomy calamities.

*Other Stichera of Saint Romanus, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Нб҃ныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
The fair-sounding psaltery of the Divine Spirit, the glorious pipe of the Church singing divine songs of wonders: he sets before us all his sweet-singing banquets, and thereby cheers the God-wise.

A most bright and insightful luminary, a sweet-sounding harp, a string of the Spirit's well-signifying words, he sings clearly, teaching the ends of the earth to glorify with unceasing hymns the one radiance of the Godhead.

As thou standest before the Master of all and hast acquired great boldness before Him, pray that we who celebrate thy memory, thy bright solemnity, may be delivered from tribulations and afflictions, O God-blessed Romanus.

*Glory to the Apostle, in the same Tone [Tone 1], By Byzantios:*[^8]
O chosen vessel, great receptacle of the Spirit, thou didst enlighten Paul the Apostle, O venerable one, having been taught by God through revelation, as an eyewitness of the Word and initiate of His wonders, and previously an apostle of the disciples, and a faithful steward of the New Covenant. Therefore, imitating Christ the Teacher, thou didst shed thy blood, having become a confessor through martyric suffering, O Ananias, Christ's sacred leader; and having finished the course and preserved the unstolen faith, dwelling with the celestial ministers in the highest, pray that our souls may be saved.

*Both now, Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Прехва́льніи мч҃нцы):*
Standing by the Cross of thy Son and God, and beholding His long-suffering, the pure Mother said weeping: Woe is me, sweetest Child! Why dost Thou suffer these things unjustly, O Word of God, that Thou mayest save mankind?

*Aposticha of the Octoechos; and Sticheron of the Apostle, Tone 4, with the verses:*
*Verse:* Their proclamation has gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the universe. [LXX Ps. 18:5]
Thou hast appeared as a receptacle of passion-bearers, O Ananias the Apostle: pelted with stones as with flowers, thou didst shed thy blood for Him Who willingly suffered crucifixion in the flesh for our sake. Therefore Damascus also boasts in thy sacred solemnity today; and not only it, but the whole world under heaven, openly proclaiming the signs of thy wonders, cries aloud: Pray unto God to grant us remission of sins.

*Glory to the Venerable One, Tone 6:*
Thou hast appeared as the prime cause of good things and salvation, O Romanus our father; for having composed angelic psalmody, thou didst show forth thy life in God-befitting manner. Pray unto Christ God to deliver from afflictions and calamities those who sing unto thee.

*Both now, Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Beholding Thee crucified, O Christ, she who bare Thee cried aloud: What is this strange mystery that I behold, O my Son? And how dost Thou die hanging in the flesh upon the Tree, O Giver of Life?

*Troparion of the Apostle, Tone 3:*
O holy Apostle Ananias, pray unto the merciful God to grant our souls remission of transgressions.

*Glory to the Venerable One, Tone 8:*
In thee, O father, was preserved with certainty that which is according to the image; for taking up the cross, thou didst follow Christ, and by thy deeds didst teach us to disregard the flesh, for it passes away, and to attend unto the soul, an immortal thing. Therefore thy spirit rejoices with the angels, O venerable Romanus.

*Both now, Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion:*
*At Matins: one Canon from the Octoechos, and that of the Apostle, to 4; whose acrostic is: "Let the glory of Ananias be glorified." The composition of the Monk John, Tone 8:*[^9]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Having traversed the water as dry land, and escaped the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Redeemer and God!

*Troparia:*
Let us open our mouths in praises; let us articulate our speech in memory of the chief pastor Ananias, all ye faithful, and with him let us sing unto our God.

The most glorious Ananias leads in song the undefiled bride, the Church that is here, unto the most pure Christ; with her, let us sing unto our God.

Thou wast the first disciple, O Ananias, giving sight to the new disciple who of old was a persecutor, and thou singest: Let us sing unto our God!

*Theotokion:*
The angelic ranks and the generations of mortals unceasingly praise thee, unwedded Mother; for thou didst bear their Creator as an Infant in thine arms.

*Another Canon of the Venerable One, Tone 6. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Traversing the deep on foot as on dry land, Israel, seeing the pursuer Pharaoh drowned, cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!

*Troparia:*
Send down upon us light-beaming rays from heaven through thy prayers, O wise and godly Romanus, that we may praise thy sacred memory in hymns.

From thy youth offering thyself wholly unto Christ, thou didst follow Him, illuminating thy mind with radiant beams from on high, O Romanus.

Adorned with the beauty of the All-Working and Holy Spirit, like a light-beaming star, Thou hast appeared to the ends of the world through the brightness of thy virtues, O wise Romanus.

*Theotokion:*
In the last times, the co-unoriginate Word came forth ineffably from thee; and He restored Adam, the first man who had fallen into corruption, O Theotokos.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou art the confirmation of those who run unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit sings of Thee.

*Troparia:*
Following the command of thy Teacher, thou didst become a hierarch and a passion-bearing offering, O apostle.

By thy God-pleasing prayers, make the Redeemer merciful unto those who sing of thee, O Apostle Ananias.

Lo, the Lord appeared unto Thee, checking the onslaught of the wild beast, having darkened his face with ineffable light;

*Theotokion:*
We Christians have acquired thee as our refuge and our wall, and we glorify thee unceasingly, O unwedded one.

*Another [Canon], Heirmos:*
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Troparia:*
Thy mind, O wise Romanus, became an abode of the Holy Trinity, being filled piously with true knowledge, pouring forth divine proclamations.

Thy honorable tongue gushed forth streams of eloquence, imparting divine things and wisely elucidating the ineffable Nativity of Christ unto us from the Virgin.

Thou dost richly inebriate our thoughts with wise teachings and beautiful psalmody, and dost fill them with the most divine sweetness, O God-proclaiming Romanus.

*Theotokion:*
Thou art higher than all the hosts, O Virgin, for thou didst conceive in thy womb their Creator, the Son of God, and having given birth, thou remainest ever a Virgin.

*Kontakion of the Venerable One, Tone 8:*
Adorned from thy youth with the divine virtues of the Spirit, O all-wise Romanus, thou wast the most honorable adornment of the Church of Christ thou wast; for having adorned her with exquisite singing, O blessed one, therefore we pray thee: grant unto those who desire thy divine gift, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, O all-blessed father, beauty of the Church!

*Sessional Hymn of the Apostle, Tone 8, Podoben: "Guiding unto wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Enlightened by divine knowledge, thou hast appeared as a priest and a martyr, and thou wast a divine apostle, O Ananias. And having immersed in water the former persecutor called Saul, who was blinded by the bidding from on high, O glorious one, by God-given naming thou didst show him forth as better, an enlightener of our souls. Therefore we cry unto thee: Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love.

*Glory to the Venerable One, Tone 6, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезна́чалное сло́во):*
Planted like a tree, O blessed father, by the outflows of the waters of fasting, by divine cooperation thou didst render unto the Planter and Creator the fruits of thy labors in due season. Therefore we sing of thy holy memory, honoring thee, O venerable Romanus.

*Both now, Theotokion:*
O fervent and invincible intercessor, certain and unashamed hope, wall, protection, and haven of those who run unto thee, O pure Ever-Virgin: with the angels, pray unto thy Son and God to give peace unto the world, and salvation, and great mercy.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee hanging upon the Cross willingly between robbers, O Christ, Thy Mother, rent in her maternal bowels, said: O sinless Son, how art Thou unjustly nailed to the Cross like an evildoer, desiring to quicken mankind, as Thou art compassionate?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have considered Thy works, and glorified Thy Divinity.

*Troparia:*
Instructed by Thee, O Christ, the ever-memorable Ananias martyrially vanquishes death, having received the honors from Thee.

Thou wast a disciple of the persecuted Word, O ever-memorable Ananias, and a teacher of piety unto the persecutor.

The Lord showed thee unto Saul adorning thyself in God-honoring manner, and manifested thee, O Ananias, as the mediator of his healing.

*Theotokion:*

Pray unto God Whom thou didst bear, O all-pure Virgin, to deliver from every circumstance all who sing unto thee.

*Another [Canon], Heirmos:*
Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church sings in God-befitting manner, crying out from a pure understanding, feasting in the Lord.

*Troparia:*
Thou wast a receptacle of the Holy Spirit and a fire-breathing mouth, proclaiming divine psalmody and delighting the souls of those who run unto thee, O Romanus.

Thou wast a distinct psaltery and a harp, drawing every mind unto thyself by thy narrations and thy beautiful psalmody.

The Good Spirit, of the all-luminous and Most Holy Spirit, O all-praised one, took up abode in thy light-bearing soul, and made thee a receptacle and wise God-proclaimer.

*Theotokion:*
The serpent poured poison into the hearing of our foremother, but thou, O pure one, didst shake it off, having conceived at the Archangel's voice and given birth to Christ.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy face, O unwaning Light, and an alien darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But convert me, and direct my paths toward the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee.

*Troparia:*
A gushing fountain has shown thee to be a divine haven for those who were in Hebrew bonds, and the unapproachable Light made thee a ray for those in darkness, O blessed Ananias, dwelling mercifully in thee as in a praiseworthy disciple.

He Who knows secret things and knows all things before they come to be, revealed unto thee, while hiding, the persecutor who was formerly hidden, the sun of truth being shattered by him after no long time, O apostle.[^10]

The end of Thy dispensation has been attained, untraceable in its design; therefore, O Savior, death was made fearful and abolished. For this reason Ananias, as a victor and hierarch, is slaughtered rejoicing for Thy sake.

*Theotokion:*
Having maternal boldness before thy Son, O all-pure one, despise not kindred care for us, we pray thee; for we Christians present thee alone as a merciful propitiation unto the Master.

*Another [Canon], Heirmos:*
By Thy divine light, O Good One, illuminate the souls of those who rise early unto Thee with love, I pray Thee, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, as the true God, calling them out from the gloom of sin.

*Troparia:*
Having found Thee as a most God-befitting sanctuary, a most pure vessel, the grace of the Comforter, O God-wise Romanus, made Thee a temple adorned with light, and took up abode in Thee.

Having truly given birth to the incarnate Son of God, the Ever-Virgin, appearing unto thee by night, enlightened thy soul and filled thy mind with divine knowledge.[^11]

By the writings of thy sacred words, O ever-memorable one, thou hast truly appeared sweetest unto all, cheering all, enlightening the minds of all, and teaching all saving knowledge.

*Theotokion:*
O pure one who didst bear the Light, the co-eternal Word of the Father: by thy radiant prayers enlighten my mind, and drive away the darkness of my soul, O all-pure Sovereign Lady.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are my transgressions, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and give ear unto me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparia:*
Having learned to serve the living God, O most sacred one, Thou didst dismantle the manifold wickedness of the foolish, teaching them to worship the one three-hypostatic Godhead, O Ananias.

Having received the rudders of the Church into thy hands, thou didst come unto Christ the Pilot, O apostle; and by the Spirit, O Ananias, thou didst steer her like a divine ship.

Having created the straight path, O Good One, Thou didst command Thy glorious disciple to be sent unto him who walks uprightly, giving wings in prayer unto the vessel of Thy most upright election.

*Theotokion:*
That we may be delivered from grievous transgressions by thy supplications, O pure Parent of God, and that we may attain, O all-pure one, the divine radiance of the Son of God Who was ineffably incarnate of thee.

*Another [Canon], Heirmos:*
Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Greatly-Merciful One!

*Troparia:*
Being filled with illumination, and divinely led up to the height of virtues, thou didst disregard all earthly things, and wast an unshaken pillar and a luminary of piety.

Thou didst wither the desires of the flesh, and didst adorn thy soul with the brightest purity with the radiance of purity; and with the comeliness of virtues thou didst surround thyself with desirable beauty.

Having hated transient and worldly, corruptible and passing pleasure, clinging unto the temple of the Mother of God: thou didst purify thy mind, both soul and body, O worthy of glory.

*Theotokion:*
Clearly foreseeing, the radiant prophets, knowing the things that should come to pass in the end from the Virgin, O my Christ, Thy blameless birth upon earth, the God-seers manifestly proclaimed.

*Kontakion of the Apostle, Tone 2, Podoben: "In prayers" (Въ моли́твахъ):*
In prayers most fervent intercessor, and unto those who ask a most speedy hearer: receive our supplication, O Ananias, and pray Christ to have mercy on us, Who alone rests among the saints.

*Ikos:*
By the illumination of Thy radiance, O Christ, having enlightened the darkness of my heart and my blindness, grant me word, that with pure mind I may praise the God-radiant virtues of the apostle and the sufferings of the blessed one; for Thou art the fountain of wisdom and the giver of good things, O abundantly-merciful One. Therefore, having clarified my tongue, receive this sung praise, O Thou Who alone restest among the saints.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The youths having come from Judea in Babylon of old, by faith in the Trinity trampled down the flame of the furnace, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
Let His chosen flock sound forth wisely with clear-sounding trumpet the sufferings of the pastor Ananias, crying aloud unto Christ its Chief Pastor: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Let the venerable Church of Christ glorify thee, O Ananias, as a disciple of God, a faithful hierarch, and a witness of divine sufferings, proclaiming: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Neither hiding by the craft of death, but through love of the flock, seeing the wolf become meek like a lamb, Ananias cried aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^12]

*Theotokion:*
Incarnate of a virginal womb, Thou didst appear, O Christ, for our salvation. Therefore, knowing Thy Mother to be the Theotokos, we cry in orthodox manner: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Another [Canon], Heirmos:*
The youths in Babylon feared not the flame of the furnace, but cast into the midst of the flame, being dewed, sang: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

Thou didst have the Mother of God, Mary, as a God-wise teacher, instructing thee and teaching thee, and commanding thee to sing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

Thou didst shine forth in the world like a brightest sun, O God-wise Romanus, brightly illuminating those who cry in faith: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

The proclamation of thy words filled the universe, and taught mankind to sing beautifully unto Christ: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Now all pious generations bless thee, O pure Theotokos, as thou didst foretell, and glorify in faith: Blessed, O all-pure one, is the fruit of thy womb!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The Chaldean tyrant furiously heated the furnace sevenfold for the pious; but having seen them saved by a greater power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
The glorious Ananias deemed it strange to hear, from the rapacious destroyer of Thy flock, O Master, a transformation beyond word unto the pastoral rank. Therefore leaping for joy he cried: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

Let thy most glorious and blessed city rejoice today and leap for joy in spirit, having obtained thy protection, O glorious one; and giving thanks unto the Savior, let it cry aloud: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^13]

By Thy life-creating command, O most venerable Ananias, thou didst proclaim unto Saul the power of Him Who was seen in light-creating manner; and thou didst avert his blindness, having laid on thy healing hands; and thou didst pluck out the serpentine scales like a husk, crying: Supremely exalt Christ unto the ages![^14]

*Triadikon:*
The thrice-radiant Essence, the single shining ray, of one three-hypostatic Nature: the Unoriginate Father, the consubstantial Word of the Father, and the co-reigning, consubstantial Spirit: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
O all-holy Theotokos, saving in necessities and warmly interceding for all who are in sorrow: have compassion, save me, and raise me up who have grievously fallen; [extend] a hand of help before the end, giving [it], lest the deathly night seize me sleeping, that I may duly glorify thee unto the ages.

*Another [Canon], Heirmos:*
In Babylon the blessed youths, braving dangers for the laws of the fathers, spat upon the senseless decree of the ruler; and united with the fire by which they were not burned, they sang a worthy song to the Almighty: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
Having shaken off all the beauty, sweetness, and glory of the world, thou didst give wings unto thy soul toward the celestial choirs, toward the glory and radiance yonder, O wise one, toward ineffable beauties, toward the abodes yonder, toward the Light of the Godhead, crying: Supremely we exalt Thee, O Christ, unto the ages!

Kindled with divine desire, thou didst set ascents in thy heart, and didst acquire a bodiless life in the flesh, being likened unto the angelic ranks, O wise Romanus; and in faith crying aloud: Supremely we exalt Thee, O Christ, unto the ages![^15]

By the sublime utterance of thy divine and sweetest words, O most blessed Romanus, thou delightest every thought of men, and makest them sweet as a certain enriching nourisher, and illuminatest brightly, and teachest to cry: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Having deceived me with the promise of equality with God, the most crafty, man-killing serpent snatched me away like a captive from the sweetness of paradise; but the Master by nature, the Son of God, having become incarnate, came forth from the Virgin Mary, and deifying me, took me up to the height, Whom we supremely exalt unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Thee, the unwedded Mother of God the Most High; thee who beyond understanding didst bear by the Word the truly God, who hast become [higher than] the most pure Powers: with unceasing hymns of glory we magnify!

*Troparia:*
Thou didst dispel the darkness of ignorance, O all-praised one, and didst cause the true three-hypostatic light to shine upon Damascus; but as a loving father and beloved chief pastor, preserve it in the orthodox faith![^16]

Celebrating this annual festival, come, shining radiantly with the dignity of soul, let us hymn the thrice-radiant virtue of the servant of the Trinity and preacher, Ananias!

...let us hymn!

Higher than human dignity was thine, O blessed Ananias; for thou wast a beloved disciple, wast known as a shepherd, and as a sufferer for Christ wast glorified, adorned with crowns of martyrdom.

*Theotokion:*
O all-pure and most blameless one, vessel of sweet fragrance: entreat Christ Whom thou didst bear, to grant unto our King victory over enemies,[^17] and to grant relief from the yoke of servitude unto thy people; for thee do we unceasingly magnify.

*Another [Canon], Heirmos:*
Ineffable is the birth of the seedless conception, the incorrupt fruit of the unwedded Mother; for the birth renews natures. Therefore all generations in orthodox manner magnify thee as the Mother unwedded of God.

*Troparia:*
Thou wast joined unto the celestial bodiless choirs, O glorious one, where ineffable joy abides, and everlasting delight, where that Light and rejoicing of the Godhead is, where ineffable glory is, O most blessed one.

Dwelling within divine glory with the righteous, thou beholdest the beauty of things ineffable, thou beholdest unending sweetness, thou beholdest celestial beauty, truly desirable, O wise one; thou seest the choirs of patriarchs, O glorious Romanus.

Deliver from afflictions by thy prayers, O God-wise one, those who faithfully celebrate thy light-bearing memory; and from every harm and alien sorrow, O Romanus; and make them worthy to obtain heavenly glory and the Kingdom on high, O most blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
By the ancient fall, the human race of the first-created was taken captive, O Virgin Theotokos, and enslaved by sinful passions; but the all-good God, born of thee, in His compassion recalled him.

*Exapostilarion of the Octoechos:*
*Glory to the Apostle, Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ оу҆слы́шите):*
Damascus now boasts of thee, O apostle, unto which thou didst extend strength by thy martyrdom, O Ananias, having become a teacher unto him who persecuted Christ, as an eminent disciple of the persecuted Christ, unto Whom pray thou for us.

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
O thy mysteries! O thy marvels! How after childbirth didst thou remain a virgin, as before childbirth? How didst thou contain God, Who is nowhere containable? But truly ineffable are all thy wonders, O blessed Virgin Mother Mary.

*At the Aposticha:*

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos, Glory to the Apostle, Tone 8 (By Anatolius):*
O disciple of the Savior, glorious hierarch, glory of martyrs, Ananias, brightest luminary and ruler of our city: fervently pray that thy flock be delivered from perils, and that our souls be saved.

*Now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
Beholding Thee nailed to the Cross, O Jesus, and willingly accepting sufferings, the Virgin and Thy Mother, O Master, cried out: Woe is me, sweet Child! How dost Thou unjustly endure wounds, O Physician Who didst heal human infirmity and from corruption delivered all by Thy tender mercy?

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Tone on 4; and of the Apostle, Ode 3 on 4.*
*Prokeimenon of the Apostle, Tone 8:*
Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.
*Verse:* The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands.
*Epistle: Acts of the Apostles, Section 23, from the half [Acts 9:10–19].*
*Alleluia, Tone 4:*
The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord.
*Verse:* God is glorified in the council of the saints.
*Gospel according to Luke, Section 51 [Luke 10:16–21]:* "He who hears you..."
*Communion Verse:* Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.

*(Engraving — Woodcut illustration: The Protection of the Most Holy Theotokos, depicting the Mother of God holding her veil over the congregation)*

*On the same day, we celebrate the Protection of our Most Holy Sovereign Lady Theotokos and Ever-Virgin Mary. And if it be her temple, or if the superior wish to celebrate an All-Night Vigil, her service is sung thus:*[^18]

*At Small Vespers, Idiomelon Stichera, Tone 1, on 4:*
The only God Who rests upon the highest thrones gave unto His servant to see, far above earthly things, the most divine hands stretched forth in supplication, praying for the city and for the people unto Thee, O Sovereign Lady: Protect us now also, O Lady, with the omophorion of thy mercy! *(Twice)*

The city that honors thee and duly glorifies thee, O all-pure one, thou dost protect with thy precious omophorion from the assault from the assault of adversaries; and from famine, earthquake, and civil strife, O Virgin unwedded Bride of God, protection and intercession of mankind.

O ensouled palace of Christ, rampart, protection, and strength of the faithful: by thee thy city is saved, O Mother of God; by thy help orthodox kings are crowned; boasting ever in thee, they carry victories over the pagans; for thou dost protect them with the omophorion of thy mercy.[^19]

*Glory, and now and ever, Tone 6:*
Come, ye assemblies of feast-lovers, let us hymn the most holy Virgin! For today the angels celebrate with the saints the venerable protection of the Mother of God, and call us earthly ones unto praise, to cry out with unceasing voice: Rejoice, marvelous adornment of the Church, and most marvelous protection of the whole world! Rejoice, aid of faithful kings in battles and preservation of soldiers! Rejoice, our protection and intercession, for the Christian race unceasingly blesses thee!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O house of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
Glory in the heavens and grace upon earth, as the Queen, in the company of the saints, prays in church for all of us.
*Verse:* Give ear, O daughter, and see, and incline thine ear.[^20]
Come, ye faithful, into the holy church; for there the Mother of God protects the people with her omophorion from all assaults of the enemy.
*Verse:* The rich among the people shall entreat thy countenance.[^21]
O all-pure Theotokos, forget not us thy servants; but pray for us who praise thee in faith.

*Glory, and now and ever, Tone 2:*
Come, all ye feast-lovers, let us bless the venerable protection of the Mother of God; for she stretched forth her hands maternally unto the Son, and by her holy protection the world was defended. Therefore with mouth and heart, and with hymns and spiritual songs, with all who run unto her let us celebrate radiantly!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Today, we faithful people radiant celebrate, being overshadowed by thy coming, O Mother of God; and gazing upon thy most pure icon, with compunction we say: Protect us with thy precious veil, and deliver us from every evil, entreating thy Son, Christ our God, that He may save our souls!

*At Great Vespers: Blessed is the man, the entire Kathisma.*

*Tone 4, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ ди́вное чꙋ́до):*
O what a great protection thou art for the sorrowful, O pure Theotokos, a speedy helper, salvation and establishment of the world, abyss of mercy, fountain of the wisdom of God, veil unto the world! Let us, the faithful, hymn, praising her radiant omophorion ineffably: Rejoice, O full of grace, the Lord is with thee, grant through thyself great mercy unto the world! *(Twice)*

Marvelously did Isaiah prophesy; for he said: In the last days the mountain of the Lord and the house of the Lord shall be manifest on the top of the mountain.[^22] And we have known, O Sovereign Lady, the true fulfillment in thee; for mountains and hills are adorned with the many churches of thy feasts. Therefore joyfully we cry unto thee: Rejoice, O full of grace, the Lord is with thee, grant through thyself great mercy unto the world!

A marvelous adornment thou art for all the faithful, the true fulfillment of the prophets, the glory of the apostles and embellishment of martyrs, the praise of virginity, and a marvelous protection for the whole world! Protect with the omophorion of thy mercy, O Sovereign Lady, the King and all the people, who cry aloud: Rejoice, O full of grace, the Lord is with thee, grant through thyself great mercy unto the world!

*Other Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Я҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
As a God-planted paradise and place of the Tree of Life, watered by the Holy Spirit, we know thee, O Theotokos, who didst bear the Creator of all, Who nourishes the faithful with the Bread of Life. Pray Him for us with the Forerunner, protecting the King and the people with thy precious omophorion from every assault. *(Twice)*

Heaven and earth are sanctified, the church is illumined, and all the people rejoice; for behold, the Mother of God, entering invisibly with angelic hosts, with the Forerunner and the Theologian, with the prophets and apostles, prays Christ for Christians, to have mercy upon the city and the people who glorify the feast of her protection.

The heavenly angels and earthly men bless thee who didst bear the God of all, as the beauty of Jacob and the heavenly ladder whereby the Lord descended to earth; for their images then revealed thy honor and glory, O Theotokos; for thou prayest for the whole world, protecting with thy mercy those who celebrate thy venerable feast.

*Glory, and now and ever, Tone 8:*
All the spiritual hierarchies of the bodiless hosts rejoice with us, spiritually assembling a visible choir, seeing the Queen and Lady of all, gloriously praised by the faithful under many names; and the spirits of the righteous rejoice, being beholders of the vision, as in the air she suppliantly stretches forth her most venerable hands, asking peace for the world, and for kings might and confirmation, and the salvation of our souls.

*The Entrance, the Prokeimenon of the day, and the Readings:*

*Reading from Genesis [28:10–17]:*
Jacob went forth from the Well of the Oath, and went to Haran. And he found a place, and slept there; for the sun was set; and he took of the stones of that place, and put it under his head, and slept in that place. And he dreamed a dream; and behold, a ladder set up on the earth, whose top reached to heaven; and the angels of God were ascending and descending on it; and the Lord leaned upon it, and said: I am the God of Abraham thy father, and the God of Isaac; fear not: the land whereon thou sleepest, to thee will I give it, and to thy seed. And thy seed shall be as the sand of the earth, and shall spread abroad to the sea, and to the south, and to the north, and to the east; and in thee shall all the tribes of the earth be blessed, and in thy seed. And behold, I am with thee, preserving thee in all the way whithersoever thou shalt go; and I will bring thee back into this land; for I will not leave thee, until I have done all that I have spoken to thee. And Jacob awaked out of his sleep, and said: Surely the Lord is in this place; and I knew it not. And he was afraid, and said: How fearful is this place! This is none other than the house of God, and this is the gate of heaven.

*Reading from the Prophecy of Ezekiel [43:27–44:4]:*
Thus says the Lord: It shall come to pass from the eighth day and forward, that the priests shall make your burnt-offerings upon the altar, and your peace-offerings; and I will accept you, says Adonai the Lord. And He brought me back by the way of the outer gate of the sanctuary, which looks toward the east; and it was shut. And the Lord said unto me: This gate shall be shut, it shall not be opened, and no man shall pass through it; for the Lord God of Israel shall enter by it, and it shall be shut. For the Prince, He shall sit in it to eat bread; by the way of the porch of the gate shall He enter, and by the way of the same shall He go out. And He brought me by the way of the north gate, opposite the house; and I looked, and behold, the house of the Lord was full of glory.

*Reading from Proverbs [9:1–11]:*
Wisdom has built herself a house, and supported it with seven pillars. She has killed her sacrifices; she has mingled her wine in her bowl, and prepared her table. She has sent forth her servants, calling with a high proclamation unto the cup, saying: Whoso is simple, let him turn aside unto me; and to those who lack understanding she said: Come, eat of my bread, and drink of the wine which I have mingled for you. Forsake senselessness, and ye shall live; and seek wisdom, that ye may live, and improve understanding with knowledge. He who reproves the evil shall get to himself dishonor; and he who reproves an ungodly man shall defame himself; for reproofs to the ungodly are wounds to him. Reprove not the evil, lest they hate thee; reprove a wise man, and he will love thee. Give instruction to a wise man, and he will be yet wiser; teach a just man, and he will increase in receiving it. The fear of the Lord is the beginning of wisdom, and the counsel of the saints is understanding; for to know the law is the character of a good mind. For in this manner thou shalt live long, and years of thy life shall be added unto thee.

*At the Litiya, Stichera, Tone 3:*
Today the most radiant feast of thy venerable veil has arrived, O all-pure Virgin! Brighter than the sun thou dost enlighten the people who from a pure heart confess thee to be the true Mother of God, and cry unto thy Son: O Christ God, by the prayers of the Ever-Virgin who purely and without corruption gave Thee birth, deliver not Thy heritage unto warring enemies; but as Thou art merciful, save our souls in peace!

*Tone 4:*
Desiring to sing a worthy praise unto thee, O all-pure Sovereign Lady, the people of thy Son cannot do so; for thou ever intercedest for all the faithful, and protectest them with thy precious veil, and prayest that all may be saved who honor thee in faith and venerate thee as the true Theotokos.

*Tone 2:*
Assembling today, let us faithful bless the most blameless Queen of all, the all-pure Virgin Mother of Christ God; for she ever stretches forth her hands maternally unto the Son; whom also the marvelous Andrew saw in the air, protecting the people with her precious veil; and unto her let us cry with compunction: Rejoice, protection, intercession, and salvation of our souls![^23]

*Glory, and now and ever, the same Tone [Tone 2]:*
Having purified our understanding and mind, with the angels let us also celebrate, radiantly beginning the Davidic the Davidic song unto the Maiden Bride of Christ, King of all and our God, saying: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou and the ark of Thy holiness![^24] For having adorned her as a fair palace, Thou didst assign her unto Thy city, O Master, to order and protect it from contrary pagans by Thy mighty power, through her prayers.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2:*
Being higher than all things heavenly and earthly, more glorious than the Cherubim and more honorable than all creation: Christ granted thee as a protectress and mighty veil unto the Christian race, to protect and save people who have sinned and flee unto thee. Therefore, as a refuge unto all, we hymn thee, O Sovereign Lady, and radiantly celebrate the venerable feast of thy veil, praying Christ to grant us great mercy.

*Tone 5. Verse:* Give ear, O daughter, and see, and incline thine ear.[^25]
Let us, ye people, radiantly sing the Davidic song unto the Maiden Bride, the Mother of God, the King of all, Christ: The Queen stood at Thy right hand, O Master, arrayed in a vesture woven with gold, and adorned with divine beauty.[^26] For having embellished this chosen one among women more than the whole world, through His great mercy He was well pleased to be born of her, and gave her as a helper unto His people, to order and protect His servants from every affliction, her, the only blessed one.

*Tone 7. Verse:* The rich among the people shall entreat thy countenance.[^27]
Thou art a great and most glorious mountain, higher than Mount Sinai, O Theotokos; for that mountain, unable to endure the descent of God's glory in figures and shadows, was kindled with fire, and thunder and lightning were there; but thou, having the whole of the divine Fire within thee, didst unburnt bear in thy womb the Word of God Who bears all things by His nod; and having maternal boldness toward Him, help, O Sovereign Lady, those who faithfully celebrate thy venerable feast, and forget not to visit maternally; for thou hast received from God the gift to order and protect thy flock named after Christ, thy servants.

*Glory, and now and ever, Tone 2:*
As with a most radiant crown, O all-pure Theotokos, with thy precious veil the Church of God has clothed herself; and she is illumined, rejoicing today, and mystically keeps festival, O Sovereign Lady, crying unto thee: Rejoice, raiment precious raiment, and crown of God's glory! Rejoice, only perfection of glory and everlasting joy! Rejoice, harbor and deliverance, and our salvation, unto those who flee unto thee!

*Then the Troparion, Tone 4:*
Today, ye faithful people...

*At Matins, after the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*
Surrounded by the divine splendors of the angelic hosts, and receiving venerable service with the ranks of the prophets and apostles, as the Mother of God, visit us thy servants, praying Christ our God to grant us great mercy.

*Glory, and now and ever, the same.*

*After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 6:*
Strength unto the helpless art thou truly, O all-pure Mother of God; wherefrom we, the humble, are exalted through thee, and are held fast in thee on high; thou art our protection and mediatress toward God.

*Glory, and now and ever, the same.*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
Truly more honorable than the ancient ark, thy all-pure omophorion, shining brighter than electrum[^28]—not materially, but spiritually—stretched forth by thine incorrupt, all-pure hands, mercifully preserves those who honor the well-adorned feast of thy protection, O Sovereign Lady; wherein having assembled, we fervently celebrate.

*Glory, and now and ever, the same.*

*The Gradual, the First Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
I will remember Thy name in every generation and generation.
*Verse:* The rich among the people shall entreat thy countenance.
*Let every breath:*
*Gospel according to Luke, composite reading 4 [1:39–49, 56]:*
*Mary arose:*
*Psalm 50.*
*Glory:* *By the prayers of the Theotokos:* *And now and ever, the same.*
*Have mercy upon me, O God:*

*Then the Sticheron, Tone 6:*
The spiritual ranks of the angelic powers rejoice with us, spiritually assembling a sensory choir, seeing the Queen and Sovereign Lady of all, praised by the faithful under many names; and the spirits of the righteous rejoice, being beholders of the vision, as in the air she suppliantly stretches forth her most venerable hands, asking appeasement for the world, and for kings might and confirmation[^29], and the salvation of our souls.

*Canon of the Theotokos, to 12; the Heirmos twice. Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
I shall open my mouth, and it will be filled with the Spirit, and I shall pour forth a word unto the Queen and Mother; and I shall appear radiantly keeping festival, and I shall sing, rejoicing in her wonders.

*Troparion:*
With the ranks of the holy angels, and with the assemblies of the prophets and apostles, in great glory, as the Mother of God having entered into the church today, thou prayest for Christians, delivering all from assault and sorrow, protecting them with thy mercy.

*Troparion:*
Moses called thee the tabernacle, and the rod of Aaron; for thou didst blossom forth Christ, the Tree of Life; toward Whom having boldness, pray for us who honor thee, O Queen, to deliver us from every evil, that we may glorify the feast of thy protection.

*Troparion:*
Not as of old David, having gathered choirs before the ark, dances; but rather now running before thee into the church with the ranks of the saints, bowing before thee they say: Pray for the people who honor thee, that glorifying thy protection, we may celebrate venerably.

*Troparion:*
The angelic ranks sing of thee, O Theotokos, and the patriarchs with hierarchs glorify thee, running before thy face into the church; with whom the holy Andrew then saw thee praying unto God for us sinners, to have mercy upon the people who glorify the feast of thy protection.

*Katavasia: the same Heirmos.*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Establish thy singers, O Theotokos, thou living and plentiful fountain, who have gathered themselves as a spiritual choir, and in thy divine glory vouchsafe them crowns of glory.

*Troparion:*
Having budded forth the divine Ear of corn, manifestly as an untilled field: rejoice, living table who didst contain the Bread of Life! Rejoice, inexhaustible fountain of the Water of Life, O Sovereign Lady!

*Troparion:*
Thy servants, O Sovereign Lady, standing with faith in thy church, await thy mercy: visit our humility, and with thy holy protection defend the King[^30] and the people from every evil.

*Troparion:*
O Virgin of many names, venerably prefigured by the prophets, who now serve thee with the angels; with them pray God for us, that rejoicing, we all may radiantly celebrate the holy day of thy protection.

*Troparion:*
Gideon prefigured thee as the fleece, for upon thee as dew descended Christ God; Whom pray, O Theotokos, to grant victory unto our King against enemies, that having overthrown them as the Midianites[^31], he may glorify thy holy feast.

*Troparion:*
More than the rays of the sun, with thy gleaming omophorion thou dost enlighten and the people, and thou drivest away the darkness of our sins through thy visitation, O Theotokos, praying unto thy Son and God for us.

*After Ode 3, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5:*
Fervent protectress and invincible, certain hope and unashamed, wall and protection and haven of those who flee unto thee, O pure Ever-Virgin: pray thy Son and God with the angels to grant peace unto the world, and salvation, and great mercy.

*Glory, and now and ever, the same.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Seated in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, in a light cloud Jesus the most divine came by an incorrupt hand, and saved those who cry: Glory, O Christ, unto Thy power!

*Troparion:*
In voices of songs we cry unto thee, O Virgin all-hymned: Rejoice, fertile mountain, curdled by the Spirit! Rejoice, lampstand, and vessel bearing the manna, sweetening the senses of all the pious!

*Troparion:*
More than the ark of Aaron, God has consecrated thee entirely by the Holy Spirit, O Theotokos, and commanded the angels to serve thee; with whom pray for the city and the people who honor thy venerable feast.

*Troparion:*
Come now in glory into thy church, O Theotokos, with the assemblies of all the saints, even as then the holy Andrew saw thee in the light air, praying for Christians; and grant us thy mercy.

*Troparion:*
Strengthen, O Sovereign Lady, our King who glorifies thee against contrary enemies, as David against Goliath[^32], that rejoicing in thee we may cry: Rejoice, holy protection and protectress of our city!

*Troparion:*
Unto thee we fall down in faith, O Lady, and bowing down, we thankfully cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Virgin full of God's grace, our protection and rampart, and helper of those who are in afflictions; save us who flee unto thee, for in thee do we hope!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
All things were amazed at thy divine glory; for thou, O Virgin who hast not known wedlock, didst hold in thy womb the God Who is over all, and didst bear the timeless Son, granting peace unto all who sing of thee.

*Troparion:*
Solomon of old depicted thee as a bed, and the bridal chamber of the Heavenly King, surrounded by Seraphim[^33], which was spoken concerning thee, O Theotokos; therefore we now pray thee, all-hymned Mother of God: Cover us from all afflictions!

*Troparion:*
Unto thee the chiefs of the angels and the venerable prophets with apostles, as unto the Mother of God, venerably minister, seeing thee making prayer for the world; and having heard thy requests, the Lord will save the city and the people who hope in thee.

*Troparion:*
Isaiah, the great among the prophets, foretold thee, saying: A Virgin without knowing wedlock shall bear God; for thou, pure Mother, wast holier than all, for in thy womb and upon thy arms thou didst bear God; Whom pray for us, overshadowing with thy protection those who glorify thee with faith.

*Troparion:*
Soaring with spiritual wings, the assemblies of the saints mystically came to serve thee, O Theotokos, seeing thee in a light cloud of glory praying unto Christ the Savior to grant victory unto our King, to destroy those who war against us.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Celebrating this divine and all-venerable feast of the Mother of God, come, ye godly-minded, let us clap our hands, and glorify God Who was born of her.

*Troparion:*
The godly-minded priests, standing in thy church with the pious people, await thy mercy, O Theotokos: change our sorrow into joy; for thou didst bear the Joy, Who has forgiven the sins of all men.

*Troparion:*
Unto thee all the earth brings gifts, as unto the Queen and Mother of God; kings and princes bow down, and all people rejoice, protected from every evil by thy prayers, O Theotokos.

*Troparion:*
Daniel depicted thee beforehand as a great mountain[^34]; for from thee without seed was Christ born, and He crushed all demonic deceit, and filled the whole earth with His faith; Whom pray for us, O Theotokos, who glorify the feast of thy protection.

*Troparion:*
We cry unto thee with the angelic voice, O all-pure one: Rejoice, throne of God, whereon Ezekiel saw the Lord in the likeness of a man, borne by the Cherubim[^35]; with them pray for us, O Theotokos, that He may save our souls.

*Kontakion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва дне́сь):*
Today the Virgin stands forth in the church, and with the choirs of the saints invisibly prays to God for us; angels with hierarchs bow down, and apostles with prophets dance; for on our behalf the Theotokos prays the pre-eternal God.

*Ikos:*
Come, ye people, let us delight in her most glorious wonders; for through her Adam was delivered from corruption; for she was also made an ark, not by Noah, but by God. Of old indeed Moses was unable to see God in the burning bush; but now the whole earth has known Him Who was born of her Him Who was born of her, the Son of God, unto Whom she prays for us; therefore we glorify her as the Mother of God; for on our behalf the Theotokos prays the pre-eternal God.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The godly-minded worshipped not creation rather than the Creator, but manfully trampling upon the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, O praised Lord and God of the fathers!

*Troparion:*
Unwritten by many prophets, thou wast unknown, O Virgin, even unto the angels ministering to God; but now we all know thee to be the Theotokos, and we require thy help and protection, O blessed one!

*Troparion:*
The mountain shadowed by the Spirit, which Habakkuk saw[^36], pouring forth healing sweetness unto the faithful: O Virgin Theotokos, heal us who cry unto thy Son: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
He Who bowed the heavens dwelt within thee, O Virgin, and now looks upon thy prayer, O pure Theotokos, fulfilling thy request, O Queen; Whom fervently pray for us, for in thee do we hope, O blessed one!

*Troparion:*
O Creator and our Savior, Christ God, receive the prayer of Thy Mother, which she brings unto Thee for us sinners, that rejoicing in Thee we may sing: Blessed art Thou, O praised God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The birthgiving of the Theotokos saved the pious youths in the furnace; then typified, but now realized, it raises the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Praise the Lord, ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
With the ranks of the angels, O Sovereign Lady, with the venerable and glorious prophets, with the supreme apostles, and with the hieromartyrs and hierarchs, pray God for us sinners, who have glorified the feast of thy protection in the Ruthenian land[^37].

*Troparion:*
Cast down pride and uprisings, and destroy the counsels of unjust princes; destroy those who start wars, O all-pure Queen, Mother of God; and exalt the horn of our pious King[^38], that we may glorify thy feast, O all-pure Virgin Theotokos, crying: Sing of the Lord, ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Offering song unto thee with our lips, we faithfully bow down with our soul, for within us our hearts burn: O all-pure Mother of God, have mercy upon us who pray unto thee, and who sing of the Lord and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Weighted down with many sins, I am at a loss worthily to write praises of thy protection, O Theotokos; but as the Mother of God, adorn thy feast with wonders, that rejoicing we all may sing of the Lord and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy, illumined by the Spirit, and let the nature of the bodiless minds keep festival, honoring the sacred festival of the Mother of God, and let them cry: Rejoice, all-blessed Theotokos, pure Ever-Virgin!

*Troparion:*
O King on high, sitting with the Father and praised by the Seraphim, look upon the maternal prayer which she brings unto Thee for us sinners, and cleanse our sins; save the city, and multiply the people; grant the King health of body and victories over pagans, through the prayers of her who gave Thee birth.

*Troparion:*
We cry unto thee with the angelic voice, O Virgin chosen of God: Rejoice, thou who didst lead Adam back into paradise! Rejoice, thou who drivest away demons by thy name! Rejoice, hope of Christians! Rejoice, sanctification of souls! Rejoice, guardian of our city!

*Troparion:*
Remember us in thy prayers, O Lady Virgin Theotokos, that we perish not because of the multitude of our sins; protect us from every evil and bitter afflictions; for in thee do we hope, and honoring the feast of thy protection, we magnify thee.

*Troparion:*
As the Mother of God thou hast received the gift from God to heal the infirmities of all Christians, and to deliver from afflictions, and to remit sins, and to save from captivity and every necessity; but despise not us also, O Lady, for thou knowest what we need: health for our bodies and salvation for our souls.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ оу҆слы́шите):*
O most holy Lady, Virgin Theotokos, protect us with thy marvelous omophorion, preserving the King and the people from every evil; even as the marvelous Andrew saw thee in the Church of Blachernae praying[^39]; and now, O Lady, send down upon us thy great mercy. *(Three times)*

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
Unto thee we fall down, O Lady, humbly bowing down, and we pray thee: Rejoice, O Lady blessed of God, Virgin of many names, prefigured by the prophets venerably prefigured; for upon thee as dew descended Christ God, and thou didst give birth, O pure one who knewest not wedlock, and upon thy hands didst bear the Creator and Lord of all; Whom now pray for us, who faithfully glorify the feast of thy protection. *(Twice)*

*Sticheron:*
The angelic ranks sing of thee, O Virgin Theotokos, and the patriarchs with hierarchs glorify thee, and the holy apostles, as unto the Mother of God, venerably minister unto thee, seeing thee making prayer for the world unto Christ God, the Savior of all; Whom pray to deliver the city and the people who faithfully glorify thee and honor the feast of thy protection.

*Sticheron:*
More than the ark of Aaron, God has consecrated thee entirely by the Holy Spirit, O Theotokos; and more than the rays of the sun, with thy shining omophorion thou dost enlighten the church and the people, and drivest away the darkness of our sins; and thou deliverest from afflictions and sorrows those who with faith honor the feast of thy venerable protection.

*Glory, and now and ever, Tone 6:*
As the marvelous Andrew saw thee in the air, with the multitude of archangels, with the apostles and prophets, and a multitude of martyrs, praying unto thy Son and our God for the city and the people, O Theotokos, and covering them with thy precious veil: fail not now also, O all-pure one, to save the chosen heritage of thy Son, who celebrate thy all-pure feast, O all-hymned one!

*The Great Doxology, and the Dismissal.*
*At the Divine Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon, Ode 3 and Ode 6.*
*Prokeimenon, the Song of the Theotokos, Tone 3:*
My soul magnifies the Lord, and my spirit has rejoiced in God my Savior.
*Verse:* For He has looked upon the lowliness of His handmaiden.
*Epistle to the Hebrews, composite reading 320 [9:1–7]:*[^40]
The first tabernacle had...
*Alleluia, Tone 8.*
*Verse:* Give ear, O daughter, and see... *Verse:* The rich among the people shall entreat thy countenance...
*Gospel according to Luke, composite reading 54 [10:38–42, 11:27–28]:*[^41]
Jesus entered into a certain village...
*Communion Hymn:* I will take the cup of salvation...

*Be it also known that if the feast of the Protection of the Most Holy Theotokos happens on a weekday, apart from a Sunday, and one wishes to sing together with the Protection the service of the Apostle Ananias and the Venerable Romanus, let him sing as follows:*[^42] *At Small Vespers, Stichera of the Protection on 4, Tone 1, Automela. Glory, and now and ever, Tone 6: "Come, ye assemblies of the lovers of the feast." At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O house of Ephratha." Glory, and now and ever, Tone 3: "Come, all ye lovers of the feast." Troparion of the Protection, Tone 4: "Today, ye faithful people." Then.. (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ).*

*...the Little Litany, and the Dismissal. At Great Vespers, we sing "Blessed is the man," the 1st Antiphon. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Protection on 4; of the Apostle on 3; and of Saint Romanus on 3. Glory to the Apostle; and now and ever, of the Protection, Tone 8: "The spiritual ranks rejoice with us." The Entrance. The Prokeimenon of the day. The Readings, 3, of the Protection: 1st, from Genesis: "Jacob went forth." 2nd, from the Prophecy of Ezekiel: "It shall come to pass from the eighth day." 3rd, from Proverbs: "Wisdom has built herself a house." The customary litanies. At the Litiya, Stichera of the Protection of the Theotokos, Tone 3: "Today the most radiant feast has arrived." Glory to the Apostle; and now and ever, of the Protection of the Theotokos, Tone 2: "Having purified our understanding and mind." At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Theotokos, Tone 2, Automela: "Being higher than all." Glory to the Venerable One; and now and ever, of the Protection, Tone 2: "As with a most radiant crown." At the Blessing of the Loaves, Troparion of the Protection, Tone 4: "Today, ye faithful people," three times; and "Blessed be the name of the Lord," three times, with chanting; and Psalm 33: "I will bless the Lord;" and the reading.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Protection of the Theotokos; then of the Apostle; Glory to Saint Romanus; and now and ever, of the Protection. And the customary kathismata; after the kathisma, Sessional Hymn of the Protection, and the reading. After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn of the Protection, and the reading. The Gradual, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4: "I will remember Thy name." Verse: "The rich among the people shall entreat thy countenance." "Let every breath." Gospel according to Luke, composite reading 4: "Mary arose." Psalm 50. Glory: "By the prayers of the Theotokos;" and now and ever, the same. "Have mercy upon me, O God." Then the Sticheron, Tone 8: "The spiritual ranks rejoice with us."*

*Canon of the Protection of the Theotokos with the Heirmos to 6; and of the Apostle to 4; and of the Venerable One to 4. Katavasia: "I shall open my mouth." After Ode 3, Kontakion of the Apostle, and of the Venerable One; and Sessional Hymn of the Apostle; Glory to the Venerable One; and now and ever, of the Protection; and the reading. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Protection, Tone 3: "Today the Virgin stands forth in the church;" and the Ikos; and the Prologue. After Ode 9, Exapostilarion of the Protection; Glory to the Apostle; and now and ever, of the Protection. At the Praises, Stichera of the Protection on 4; Glory to the Apostle; and now and ever, of the Protection. The Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Apostle; Glory to the Venerable One; and now and ever, of the Protection. Litanies; and the Dismissal; and the First Hour. At the First Hour, Troparion of the Protection; Glory to the Apostle; and now and ever, the Theotokion of the Hours. After the Trisagion, Kontakion of the Protection; and the complete Dismissal. Likewise also at the other Hours, at the beginning we say the Troparion of the Protection, and we alternate the troparia of the Saints; and at all the Hours we say the Kontakion of the Protection.*

*At the Divine Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon of the Protection, Ode 3 on 4; and of the Apostle Ode 6 on 4. After the Entrance, Troparion of the Protection, and of the Apostle, and of the Venerable One. Kontakion of the Apostle; Glory to the Venerable One; and now and ever, of the Protection. Prokeimenon, the Song of the Theotokos, Tone 3: "My soul magnifies the Lord." Verse: "For He has looked upon the lowliness of His handmaiden." And of the Apostle: "Their sound has gone forth into all the earth." Epistle of the Protection, to the Hebrews, composite reading 320: "The first tabernacle had." Another, of the Acts, composite reading 21, from the middle: "There was a certain disciple at Damascus." Alleluia, Tone 8. Verse: "Give ear, O daughter, and see." Verse: "The rich among the people shall entreat thy countenance." And of the Apostle: "The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord." Gospel according to Luke, composite reading 54: "Jesus entered into a certain village." And of the Apostle, Luke, composite reading 51: "He who hears you." Communion Hymn: "I will take the cup of salvation." Another: "Their sound has gone forth into all the earth." The daily Epistle and Gospel we read beforehand.*

*It is fitting to know: If the feast of the Most Holy Theotokos, of her venerable Protection, and of the Holy Apostle Ananias happens on a Sunday:*[^43]

*Behold: On Saturday evening, at Small Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Resurrectional Stichera of the Tone on 4; Glory, and now and ever, of the Protection. At the Aposticha, the Resurrectional Sticheron, 1; and the Aposticha of the Protection of Great Vespers with their refrains; Glory, and now and ever, of the Protection, of Small Vespers. After the Trisagion, the Resurrectional Troparion; Glory, and now and ever, of the Protection. The Little Litany, and the Dismissal. At Great Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Resurrectional Stichera, 3; and the Anatolian Sticheron, 1; and of the Protection, 3; and of the Apostle Ananias, 3. Glory of the Protection; and now and ever, the Dogmatic Theotokion of the Tone. The Readings of the Protection, 3. At the Litiya: Stichera of the Protection; Glory to the Apostle; and now and ever, of the Protection. At the Aposticha, Resurrectional Stichera; Glory to the Apostle; and now and ever, of the Protection. At the Blessing of the Loaves, Troparion of the Protection, three times.*

*Behold: But if there is no vigil, we say the Resurrectional Troparion; Glory to the Apostle; and now and ever, of the Protection. At Matins: at "God is the Lord," the Resurrectional Troparion, twice; Glory to the Apostle; and now and ever, of the Protection. After the Kathismata, Resurrectional Sessional Hymns, and their Theotokia. Then the Polyeleos, and the Troparia: "The angelic assembly was amazed;" the Hypakoe of the Tone; and the Sessional Hymns of the Protection. The Gradual and the Prokeimenon of the Tone. The Resurrectional Gospel, and the rest. The Resurrectional Canon to 4; and of the Protection to 6; and of the Apostle to 4. Katavasia: "I shall open my mouth." After Ode 3, Kontakion of the Protection, and of the Apostle, and the Sessional Hymn of the Apostle; Glory, and now and ever, of the Protection. After Ode 6, the Resurrectional Kontakion. At Ode 9 we sing "More honorable than the Cherubim." The Resurrectional Exapostilarion; Glory to the Apostle; and now and ever, of the Protection. At the Praises, Resurrectional Stichera, 4; and of the Protection, 3, Prosomoia; and the Automelon which is appointed for "Glory," with the refrains of Vespers. Glory, the Gospel Sticheron; and now and ever: "Thou art most blessed." The Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, the Resurrectional Troparion. Litanies; and the Dismissal; and the First Hour. At the First Hour, the Resurrectional Troparion; Glory of the Protection; and now and ever, the Theotokion of the Hours. After the Our Father, the Kontakion of the Protection. At the Third Hour, the Resurrectional Troparion; Glory to the Apostle; and now and ever, the Theotokion of the Hours. After the Our Father, the Resurrectional Kontakion. Likewise also at the other Hours we say the Kontakion, alternating between the Resurrection and the Protection. At the Divine Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Tone on 4; and of the Theotokos, Ode 3 on 4; and of the Apostle, Ode 6 on 4. After the Entrance, the Resurrectional Troparion, and of the Protection, and of the Apostle. The Resurrectional Kontakion; Glory, Kontakion of the Apostle; and now and ever, of the Protection. The Prokeimenon, the Epistle, Alleluia, the Gospel, and the Communion Hymn, first of the Resurrection, and thereafter of the Theotokos. But the service of the Venerable Romanus is omitted, and is sung at Compline.*

*(Woodcut headpiece)*[^44]

*In the same month, on the 2nd day: the Holy Hieromartyr Cyprian, and the Holy Martyr Justina; and the Holy and Blessed Andrew, Fool-for-Christ.*[^45]
*His service is written after this service.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 6, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Я҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
With priestly anointing and martyric blood thou didst draw near to God most perfectly, O all-glorious Cyprian, flower of nature, beauty of discourses, summit of wisdom, rule of uprightness of dogmas, all-harmonious rectitude of the canons, and comeliness of the churches.

A fair renown unto those who suffer, a crown unto the martyrs, thou didst whiten the crowns of struggles, O godly-minded one, to endure most valiantly through diverse tortures, bonds, and prisons, and the strippings of bodies, and scourging, and the bitterest cold, and finally unto wounds and death, O all-venerable Cyprian.

Resisting demonic sorceries with the songs of the Spirit and the sign of the Cross, thou didst preserve thy virginity, and as an unconquerable martyr thou didst bring thyself unto Christ as a most sacred slaughter; therefore thou hast received the crowns of victory, O Justina, adorned splendor unto deeds and martyrs.

*Glory, Tone 2:*
The deceit of teachings and the knowledge of idols thou didst truly reject, and thou didst clothe thyself with the weapon of the Cross for salvation, O all-blessed Cyprian. Burning thy books of magic, thou didst put off the old man, and the knowledge of God, of the consubstantial Trinity, dwelt within thee: entreat Her, O hierarch, together with the angels, that our souls may be saved.

*Now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
Beholding Thee nailed to the tree of the Cross, O Jesus, she who knew not wedlock weeping said: O sweetest Child, why hast Thou left me alone who gave Thee birth, O unapproachable Light of the unoriginate Father? Haste and be glorified, that they who glorify Thy divine passions may receive divine glory.

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos.*
*Glory, Tone 2:*
Let us praise Cyprian, the radiant light of God, conversation-partner of Paul and partaker in his deeds; for he rejoices with the angels, having received the crown of incorruption from the only God, and prays that our souls may be saved.

*Now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
When the undefiled ewe-lamb beheld her Lamb led willingly as a man to the slaughter, weeping she said: Dost Thou hasten now to make me childless who gave Thee birth, O Christ? What is this that Thou hast done, O Redeemer of all? Yet do I hymn and glorify Thine exceeding goodness that surpasses mind and word, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
And having been a sharer of the way and a successor on the thrones of the Apostles, thou didst find the practice, O God-inspired one, to be an ascent unto contemplation; therefore, rightly dividing the word of truth, thou didst suffer even unto blood for the sake of the faith, O hieromartyr Cyprian: pray Christ God that our souls may be saved.[^46]

*At Matins, both canons of the Octoechos, 2 of the Martyrs; and of the Saints to 6. Canon, whose acrostic is: "I sing, O blessed one, thy radiant grace." Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Traversing the Red Sea's deep with unwetted feet, ancient Israel ancient Israel of old conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness by the cross-stretched hands of Moses.

*Troparion:*
Now dwelling with the supernal ranks, O sacred and divine head, look down from heaven upon those who piously hymn thee, and preserve them by thy prayers.

*Troparion:*
Held fast of old in the darkness of ignorance, and inflamed by the soul-destroying passions of the flesh through intemperance, thou didst suddenly find a most glorious change, O all-blessed one.[^47]

*Troparion:*
Though seated aforetime upon the seat of murderers, O father, yet as a successor of the elders thou didst again glorify Christ, having manifestly been transformed by the Lord's divine grace.

*Theotokion:*
A virgin thou didst remain after childbirth, for having given birth to the Creator of all and the Lord Himself, He appeared unto us in the flesh in an unusual and strange manner, O Mother of God, Mother Ever-Virgin.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and my refuge and affirmation!

*Troparion:*
Having transferred the love of thy soul unto Christ like Paul, O all-wise Cyprian, thou didst become His disciple.

*Troparion:*
Christ betroths unto Thee, in place of garments of passions, the gift of the vesture of glory, and the robe of regeneration.

*Troparion:*
Having formerly been an enduring chronicler of fasting, afterward, O all-praised Cyprian, thou wast a witness of the truth.

*Theotokion:*
Naturally and truly do we faithful honor thee as the Theotokos; for thou didst give birth to God Who became flesh, O all-immaculate one.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of the Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Trained diligently in deception, like Paul thou wast called from heaven, guided by the Cross unto the light of knowledge; for inflamed with love for the venerable virgin, for her sake thou didst join thyself unto the Creator of men. Therefore, having rebuked the weakness of the enemy, with her thou wast accounted worthy of the choir of martyrs, O Cyprian, adornment of hierarchs: pray Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love. *(Twice)*

*Glory, and now and ever, Theotokion:*
Fallen into manifold tribulations from enemies visible and invisible, held fast by the tempest of my countless transgressions: as unto my fervent intercession and protection, O pure one, unto the haven of thy goodness do I flee. Therefore, O all-pure one, fervently pray Him Who was incarnate of thee without seed on behalf of all thy servants, who unceasingly pray unto thee, O all-pure Theotokos: ever entreating Him to grant remission of sins unto those who worthily hymn thy glory.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb, the Shepherd, and the Redeemer upon the Cross, the Ewe-lamb exclaimed, weeping, and bitterly lamenting cried aloud: The world indeed rejoices, receiving through Thee redemption, but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of Thy loving-kindness, O long-suffering Lord! O abyss of mercy and inexhaustible fountain, take pity and grant remission of sins unto those who in faith sing of Thy divine passions!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Seeing Thee, the Sun of Righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her station, worthily crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Having awakened, O divine voice, from thy former madness, thou didst refute the demonic deceit and all the soul-corrupting delusion, and rejoicing didst cry: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Adorned with virtue, Justina, being strengthened and earnestly praying unto the pure and highly favored Virgin, escaped the snares and wiles of the enemy.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by the faith of thy Bridegroom, and clad in the power of the Cross, thou didst remain untroubled, contending against the demons: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Theotokion:*
He Who is free by nature, in the form of a servant became impoverished through the wealth of goodness, O Mother Ever-Virgin, receiving all humanity from thee according to His hypostasis.[^48]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou, my Lord, hast come as a light into the world, a holy light turning back from gloomy ignorance those who hymn Thee in faith.

*Troparion:*
Having the pure Virgin Mother of God as thy defender, having become rich, O Justina, thou didst preserve thy venerable virginity unharmed.

*Troparion:*
Let us honor Justina as the ensouled and most fair icon of Christ, the hidden beauty, and the treasure that cannot be stolen.

*Troparion:*
The everlasting and undefiled bride of Christ, having righteously received both suffering and fasting, wears a double crown.

*Theotokion:*
Neither the angelic nor the human mind can tell of the ineffable the ineffable and most glorious wonder of thy childbirth, O all-pure one.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with a voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the demonic blood by the blood which flowed from Thy side for mercy's sake.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst reach the bottom of the nethermost malice, but again didst ascend unto the highest virtue, O father, to the heights, having been most gloriously transformed by divine baptism.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast formerly a minister of demons, but thou didst show thyself to be Christ's God-revealed disciple, having loved the final desire with constant love.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast brought many martyrs unto the Theotokos, displaying a most great commerce: to purchase the heavenly kingdom with a little blood, O God-wise one.

*Theotokion:*
Burst asunder the bonds of my transgressions, O Bride of God, of the law of sin, granting freedom through the law of life, having given birth to the Master of the law.

*Kontakion, Tone 1, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ли́къ а҆́гглскїй):*
Turned from the magical art unto divine knowledge, O God-wise one, thou didst show thyself to the world as a most wise physician, granting healings unto those who honor thee, Cyprian with Justina: with her entreat the Lover of mankind, the Master, that our souls may be saved.

*Ikos:*
Having sent down upon me the gifts of thy healings, O holy one, heal my heart diseased with the corruption of sin by thy prayers, that I may now bring unto thee a word of song from my defiled lips, and may hymn thy sufferings which thou didst show forth, O hieromartyr, by a good and blessed repentance, drawing near unto God; for upheld by His hand, thou didst direct thyself as upon a ladder unto heavenly things, unceasingly praying that our souls may be saved.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Thou Who savedst Thy youths of Abraham in the fire and slewest the Chaldeans whom justice righteously caught: O praised Lord, God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Having entered into the high priesthood, thou didst show thyself as a most sacred model and rule unto high priests, crying: O praised Lord, God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
The change of the life-originating right hand having truly come upon thee, O father, made thee a wise divine speaker, singing: O praised Lord, God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Thou hast appeared unto us as an unconquerable weapon against the adversary, O blessed one thou hast appeared, refuting his deceits, unto those who sing: O praised Lord, God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Theotokion:*
The heavenly choirs and the super-celestial assemblies sing praises unto Him Who was born of thee, O Theotokos, crying: O praised Lord, God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Rejoice, O Jerusalem! Keep festival, ye who love Zion! For the Lord of hosts, reigning unto the ages, has come: let all the earth be in awe before His face, and let it cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Most wisely didst thou neglect the Hellenic wisdom, O glorious one; but crying aloud the divine utterance of the Apostles, healed by the Spirit, O father, and clearly proclaimed by fiery tongues, thou didst cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Thou hast ascended unto the high and supernal radiant dwellings, offered unto Christ by blood as an immaculate sacrifice, O father, a living, acceptable, and well-pleasing slaughter, singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Vanished by the grace that dwelt within thee, the demonic legions are driven away, and the passions flee from the sick, O God-wise one; while we the faithful are filled with divine light, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Those who in faith call thee the Theotokos make choir with the angelic powers over thy childbearing, O all-pure Mother of God; for thou didst give birth unto the Master and Redeemer for us, unto Whom we sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Eve, through the sickness of disobedience, brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Theotokos, through the budding forth of childbearing, didst make blessing blossom forth unto the world: therefore we all magnify thee.

*Troparion:*
From on high dost thou oversee with divine grace those who piously hymn thee, O speaker of divine things, by thy prayers; and crowning us with the shield of good-pleasure, grant us peace and salvation, as a most divine hierarch.

*Troparion:*
Thy dust abundantly renders a stream of healings unto those who love thee; and God-bearing women receive thee as a divine treasure, O Cyprian, and zealously reveal the hidden one, that we all may delight in thee.[^49]

*Troparion:*
Direct our journey toward Christ, O exceedingly rich one, by practice and a God-pleasing life and purest cleansing; entreat the essential divine gentleness, as a most compassionate hierarch.

*Theotokion:*
Strengthen our spiritual weakness, O Mother of God, by thy power strengthen our spiritual weakness, O Mother of God, by thy power; loose the heavy burden laid upon thy servants, O most holy one, who hast ineffably shone forth the Sun of Righteousness unto the world.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Же́ны оу҆слы́шите):*
Let us praise Cyprian, the mirror of sufferers, who among sorcerers was blameless and alone surpassed them, who changed his way of life and burned the books of deceit, and wonderfully received the crown of martyrdom.

*Theotokion:*
He who of old deceived me with the hope of divinity, the malicious one, is again most wisely deceived by the bodily presentation of Him Who shone forth from the Virgin; and thus the bodily condemnation was loosed by the flesh, death being put to death.

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos.*
*Glory, Tone 8:*
He who was formerly a strict champion of evil afterward appeared as a true teacher of the Church, and he who was a minister of the deceit of idols appeared as a hierarch of the faith, and from a wanton became chaste for the sake of the venerable virgin: by the prayers of them both, O Lord, save us.

*Now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
What is this visible sight which is seen by mine eyes, O Master? Thou Who upholdest all creation, lifted up upon the tree, and diest, giving life unto all!—the Theotokos weeping said, when she beheld God and man, Who ineffably shone forth from her, being lifted up upon the Cross.

*At the Divine Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Octoechos on 4; and of the Saints, Ode 3 on 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 7:* Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His saints. *Verse:* What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me?
*Epistle to Timothy, composite reading 280 [1 Tim. 1:12–17]:*[^50]
*Alleluia, Tone 2:* Thy priests shall clothe themselves with righteousness...
*Gospel according to John, composite reading 36 [10:9–16]:* The Lord said: I am the door...
*Communion Hymn:* In everlasting memory shall the righteous be...

*(Woodcut tailpiece)*[^51] *(Woodcut headpiece)*

*The service of Saint Andrew is sung at Compline.*[^52]

*Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
O most marvelous wonder!
