For in ecstasy of mind, spiritual wisdom was perfected in thee, O father Andrew; for thou didst count all things as refuse that thou mightest gain Christ[^53], and thou didst love the heavenly kingdom, which also thou hast obtained, now standing with the angels, O holy one: pray for those who diligently honor thy memory.

O most marvelous wonder! Unto him that was in the flesh, Thou didst reveal the bodiless ones, and didst disclose hidden mysteries unto Thy pleasing-one Andrew, who became a fool for Thy sake and endured earthly sufferings, taking up the cross and following Thee, O Blessed One; by whose prayers, O Christ, as Thou art merciful, save our souls!

O most marvelous wonder! Walking in a mortal body upon the earth, abiding as bodiless for Thy sake, O Lord, Thy pleasing-one Andrew acquired great poverty of possessions, having desired the things promised by Thee, which eye has not seen, nor ear heard, nor have entered into the heart of man, which Thou hast prepared for those who love Thee[^54]: by his prayers, as Thou art merciful, save our souls!

*Glory, Tone 6:*
Thou wast caught up unto heaven, and having heard ineffable words, thou didst behold invisible things; and seeing the image of the precious Cross, thou didst kiss it with love, and wast filled with divine sweetness; and the good things of paradise were revealed unto thee. Clothed in a robe woven of lightning, thou didst follow Christ, O holy Andrew, worthy of wonder![^55]

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Having heard the voice of Thine Apostle Paul saying: "We are fools for Christ's sake,"[^56] Thy servant Andrew became a fool upon earth for Thy sake, O Christ God; therefore, now honoring his memory, we pray unto Thee, O Lord: save our souls!

*Canon to the Saint, Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I shall pour forth a word unto the Queen Mother, and I shall appear radiantly keeping festival, and rejoicing I shall sing of her wonders.

*Troparion:*
Open my lips, stir up my tongue, direct my understanding, O God Who makest all things wise, that I may praise Thy pleasing-one Andrew, who became a fool upon earth for Thy sake.

*Troparion:*
O blessed Andrew, from youth thou didst love Christ, and with single-mindedness didst desire Him; how then shall we worthily praise thee, who didst arm thyself against the devil and didst conquer his wiles by the power of Christ?

*Troparion:*
Praying unto God with supplications and vexing the devil, though for a little while thou wast terrified by his manifold knocking at the door of thy hut, yet thou didst remain unmoved in mind, strengthened by the power of Christ.

*Theotokion:*
O most holy Lady, Virgin Theotokos, protect us with thy marvelous omophorion, and preserve thy city from tribulations and sorrows, even as the all-blessed Andrew saw thee praying in the Church of Blachernae.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Establish thy choir of singers, O Theotokos, living and inexhaustible fountain, who have assembled themselves spiritually; and in thy divine glory account them worthy of crowns of glory.

*Troparion:*
Wrestling with the Ethiopian demon, thou didst conquer him, having received boldness from Christ; thou didst cause great sorrow unto the demons, but unto the holy angels cheer and joy, with whom thou now dwellest.[^57]

*Troparion:*
In a dream thou didst formerly see Christ, strengthening thee against the demons, bestowing a crown upon thee, and instructing thee to wrestle with the Ethiopian; but now, seeing Him not in a vision of dreams, but in the ineffable light of His glory, pray for us.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst obey Christ Who commanded thee to become a fool and promised the heavenly kingdom; having followed Him, thou hast received His ineffable good things, O Andrew worthy of wonder.

*Theotokion:*
Come now in glory into thy church, O Theotokos, with the assemblies of the saints, even as holy Andrew saw thee praying for Christians in radiant air, and grant us great mercy, O Lady.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
Bound with fetters and irons, sitting in the church, O Andrew, thou didst endure many terrors from the demons, and didst find as a helper Christ's beloved Apostle John, who commanded thee to endure for Christ's sake; with whom now dwelling, pray for us who honor thy memory.[^58]

*Glory, and now and ever, Theotokion:*
Fallen into manifold tribulations from enemies visible and invisible, held fast by the tempest of countless sins: as unto my fervent intercession and my protection, O pure one, unto the haven of thy goodness do I flee. Therefore, O all-pure one, fervently pray Him Who was incarnate of thee without seed on behalf of all thy servants who unceasingly pray unto thee, O all-pure Theotokos, ever entreating Him to grant remission of sins unto those who worthily hymn thy glory.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb, the Shepherd, and the Redeemer upon the Cross, the Ewe-lamb exclaimed, weeping, and bitterly lamenting cried aloud: The world indeed rejoices, receiving through Thee redemption, but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of Thy loving-kindness, O long-suffering Lord! O abyss of mercy and inexhaustible fountain, take pity and grant remission of sins unto those who in faith sing of Thy divine passions!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Sitting in glory upon the throne of Godhead, upon a light cloud Jesus the Most Divine came with incorrupt palm, and saved those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst see Christ the King sitting upon a throne; having considered the image of this bitter life, which is more bitter than wormwood, thou didst thereafter receive the taste of incorrupt sweetness.

*Troparion:*
Then in a dream and in ecstasy didst thou see Christ, but now dwelling with the bodiless hosts, thou seest the incomprehensible and ineffable glory of God: remember us, O Andrew, who honor thy memory.

*Troparion:*
Let us the faithful praise Andrew, who was certified of the vision of understandings: a fool upon earth for Christ's sake, but in heaven now all-wise through spiritual grace, standing with the angels before the God of all.

*Theotokion:*
Who can declare the mystery of thy childbirth? Or who shall recount the power of thy prayer, hadst thou thyself not shown thy venerable protection unto Andrew the fool in the Church of Blachernae?

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
All things were amazed at thy divine glory; for thou, O Virgin who knewest not wedlock, didst hold in thy womb the God Who is over all, and gavest birth unto the timeless Son, Who grants peace unto all who hymn thee.

*Troparion:*
All who beheld Andrew's life were amazed: lying upon dunghills, and nakedness in the midst of the city, and trampling by chariots; yet all these things from God were the directing of His path into the heavenly kingdom.[^59]

*Troparion:*
All who beheld thy endurance were amazed; for the martyrs endured sufferings at one time, but thou didst endure evil tortures all the days of thy life, desiring to dwell in the heavenly kingdom: having obtained which now, thou rejoicest with unending joy.

*Troparion:*
Hidden things were revealed unto thee, and Thou didst see the bodiless beings of God as though in the flesh, for Thou didst love nothing of earthly things; therefore the gates of heaven were opened unto thee, and Thou didst enter into the ineffable joy of thy Lord.

*Theotokion:*
The angelic ranks sing of thee, and the generations of earth glorify thee; for thou, O Queen, didst appear unto us through Andrew, covering the people in the church with thine incorruptible veil, unceasingly praying for those who hymn thee as the Mother of God.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Celebrating this divine and all-venerable feast of the Mother of God, O ye godly-minded, come, let us clap our hands, and glorify God Who was born of her.

*Troparion:*
The voice of Paul was fulfilled in thee, O father: "For the preaching of the Cross is unto them that perish foolishness, but unto us who are saved it is the power of God,"[^60] O all-praised Andrew!

*Troparion:*
The voice of Christ spoken was fulfilled in thee: "Blessed are the poor in spirit, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven;"[^61] for thou didst become poor in spirit, and hast received the kingdom of heaven.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst guide Epiphanius when he was a youth onto the right path, unto whom also thou didst foretell that he would become a bishop and teach the people; for thou didst see all future things as though present before thine eyes.[^62]

*Theotokion:*
We sinners fall down before thee, O Theotokos, that even upon us thou mayest now spread thy invisible veil, and mayest bring thy prayers for us unto thy Son and our God, even as Andrew saw thee of old.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Having transformed thyself into foolishness of thy own will, thou didst utterly hate the beauty of this world; thou didst wither away carnal wisdom by fasting and thirst, and heat, and the cold of frost, never shrinking from rain and snow, and other harshness of the air; thou didst purify thyself like gold in the furnace, O blessed Andrew.

*Ikos:*

*Ikos:*
Come, ye lovers of Christ, come, ye lovers of the poor; let us praise the blessed sufferer, the all-wise Andrew, who became a fool for Christ's sake, who of his own will accounted the world and the things therein as refuse according to the Apostle,[^63] and eagerly ran after Christ; who subdued his flesh by fasting and thirst, and heat, and rain and snow, and the harshness of the air; who preserved his soul free from passions, and adorned it radiantly with virtues; who eternally inherited heavenly life with all the saints, and who prays unto Christ God for those who honor him with faith and love. Unto him let us cry aloud: Rejoice, O blessed Andrew, pleasing servant of Christ!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The godly-minded youths served not the creature rather than the Creator, but having manfully trampled down the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, O all-praised Lord and God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Having chosen to love nothing on earth more than the Creator, thou didst wander in the midst of the churches, singing: Blessed art Thou, O all-praised Lord and God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
He spared not his own flesh, but surrendered it unto ascetic struggles, labors, and harsh living; therefore the blessed Andrew manfully trampled down the wiles of the demons, singing: Blessed art Thou, O all-praised Lord and God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst desire nothing of earthly glory, but didst love Christ more, O blessed Andrew, and didst ascend unto Him rejoicing, receiving the recompense of thy sufferings, and ever singing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
O blessed Andrew, pray even now unto the Queen and Theotokos, whom thou didst see praying for the world, that she may shelter us with her invincible mercy; for by her protection are all we faithful preserved.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The Childbirth of the Theotokos saved the pious youths in the furnace; then prefigured, but now in reality fulfilled, it moves the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst once behold a rich man who died,[^64] who was escorted with visible honor, yet was invisibly dragged down evilly into the depths of hades, O blessed Andrew; and thou didst weep for him and didst pray unto the Lord.

*Troparion:*
The bodiless angel who grieved over the perdition of the rich man's soul rich man, who in this life had not repented, thou didst comfort in his grief, O blessed Andrew; with him thou singest: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst behold a monk who was a lover of money afflicted by an evil serpent, O blessed Andrew; and having healed him of the disease of avarice,[^65] thou didst instruct him to sing and supreme exalt the Lord unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*
Having learned of thy protection through the vision of Andrew, O Queen and Theotokos, we earnestly pray thee: Cover us with thy mercy, who hymn thy Son and supreme exalt Him unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy, enlightened by the Spirit; and let the nature of the bodiless minds celebrate, honoring the sacred festival of the Mother of God, and let them cry aloud: Rejoice, O all-blessed Theotokos, pure Ever-Virgin!

*Troparion:*
According to Christ's word, thou didst pray for those who struck thee, O holy one; therefore the heavenly gates opened, and thou didst behold the souls of the righteous flying in the likeness of swallows;[^66] with them thou dwellest now, O blessed Andrew.

*Troparion:*
Having truly loved Christ God, thou didst deny thyself and didst follow Him, even as He said: "He that loves to save his life shall lose it";[^67] therefore thou hast preserved thy life unto life eternal, O laudable holy Andrew!

*Troparion:*
Such great and glorious things have now come to pass for thee in the heavenly kingdom; though thou wast a fool unto the world, yet didst thou show thyself wise unto Christ, O laudable one! Look down from the circle of heaven upon us who honor thy memory.

*Theotokion:*
Every earthborn mortal is covered by thy veil, O most glorious Protectress and Theotokos, even as the blessed Andrew beheld thee praying for the world unto the Uncreated Essence, Who clothed Himself from thee in created nature.[^68]

*(Decorative Rococo woodcut tailpiece)*[^69] *(Baroque headpiece depicting the Divine Eye of Providence)*[^70]

*The same month, on the third day. The Holy Hieromartyr Dionysius the Areopagite:*

*At Lord, I Have Cried, Stichera on 6, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*

Having wisely entered into the innermost darkness of unapproachable light, thou didst learn the divine and unfathomable words, O holy disciple of Christ, Dionysius; and thou didst clearly explain the angelic ranks upon earth.

Having enlightened thy soul with the thrice-radiant ray, and by material fire exhausting the body, thou didst depart unto the immaterial radiance, O father; thou wast united with the angels, with whom pray unceasingly for us, that our souls may be saved.

Having pre-eminently excelled as a shepherd in the Church of God, thou didst make thy heart receptive unto the grace of the Spirit, O father; therefore unto us also, as waters from thy precious shrine, thou ever pourest forth healings, O Dionysius!

*Other Stichera, in the same Tone, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи):*

By the hook of grace, O wise one, the divine Paul, having preached to the multitude, caught thee, a sacredly revealed visionary of ineffable things, seeing thee to be a chosen vessel;[^71] with him pray, O eloquently speaking Dionysius, that He may save those who hymn thee in love!

Having made thy mind equal in honor unto the angels, O father Dionysius, through virtue, O all-wise one, thou didst sacredly recount the transcendent good order of the celestial hierarchy, whereby thou didst arrange the ecclesiastical institutions, imitating the heavenly ranks.

Most philosophically imitating God, as far as is possible, O blessed Dionysius, thou didst piously unfold thy mystic discourse concerning the Divine Names, O godly-minded one; having learned by superior union what is beyond thought, thou didst instruct the ends of the world.

*Glory, Tone 6. By Andrew of Jerusalem:*

Come, ye faithful, let us harmoniously praise the annual memory of the hierarchs, Dionysius and Cyprian! For the one, having spat upon the Stoic philosophers and having been instructed by the chosen vessel, became a knower of ineffable mysteries became a knower of ineffable mysteries; while the other, having had his mind enlightened for the sake of the good virgin Justina, fled from demonic delusion, and having burned his books of magic with fire, became a preacher of the Gospel.[^72] Therefore let us sinners also, glorifying the Savior Who glorified them, cry aloud: O Christ God, Who hast crowned Thy passion-bearers with glory, by their supplications save our souls!

*Now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Егда́ от дре́ва):*

When the undefiled Ewe beheld her Lamb of His own will being led unto the slaughter as a man, weeping, she said: Dost Thou hasten now to make me childless, O Christ, who gave Thee birth? What is this that Thou hast done, O Deliverer of all? Yet do I hymn and glorify Thine extreme goodness that is beyond mind and word, O Lover of mankind!

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos. Glory, Tone 4:*

An unseen depth of heavenly knowledge shall we call thee, O most radiant martyr of Christ; for we hymn thee as an ally and firm champion of the Church, O wise one; for thou didst shine forth through most honorable fire, having been counted worthy to soldier together with the hosts on high, having clothed thyself in Christ as a radiant garment, and having illuminated thy mind with the brightness of the Holy Spirit, O Dionysius! Therefore we faithfully celebrate thy universal memory, glorifying Him Who glorified thee, O saint, even the Lord.

*Now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one noble" (Я́ко до́бля):*

When the Ewe who bore Thee beheld Thee upon the Tree, the Lamb and Shepherd, she wept and maternally said unto Thee: O beloved Son, how art Thou suspended upon the Tree of the Cross, O longsuffering One? How were Thy hands and feet nailed by the lawless, O Word, and Thou didst pour out Thy blood, O Master?

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Having learned goodness and being sober in all things, clothed with a good conscience as befits a sacred minister, thou didst draw ineffable mysteries from the chosen vessel; and having kept the faith, thou didst finish an equal course, O hieromartyr Dionysius:[^73] pray unto Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*Glory, now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion:*
*At Matins, at "God is the Lord", the Troparion of the Saint, twice. Glory, now and ever, its Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion. Both Canons of the Octoechos with the Heirmos on 8, without the Martyria, and of the Saint on 6. Whose acrostic is: I praise the mind of wise teachings. By Theophanes.[^74] Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Having passed through the water as if it were dry land, and having escaped from the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!

*Troparion:*
Having been instructed by the divine Paul, the beholder of heavenly things, O Dionysius, thou wast revealed as a heavenly initiate of mysteries and a proclaimer of God.

*Troparion:*
Having been illuminated by the grace of God, O blessed one, enlighten the souls of those who hymn thee, that they may understand thy God-inspired teachings, O Dionysius.

*Troparion:*
With a most purified mind and soul, thou wast counted worthy to perceive the radiance of the heavenly adornments and their choirs, O Dionysius.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst appear as a light-bearing bridal chamber of the Incarnation of the Creator of all, which is beyond mind; for from thee, O Mother of God, He clothed Himself in our flesh.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Lord, Supreme Architect of the heavenly vault and Builder of the Church, do Thou establish me in Thy love, O summit of desires, confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Having surpassed the heavenly spheres, O father, through hidden words and all-wise teachings thou didst explain unto all the ranks, the eminences, and the radiances of the transcendent powers.[^75]

*Troparion:*
Having been filled with exalted contemplation, O venerable one, of the Divine Names,[^76] thou didst theologize upon the divine understanding; having become an earnest theologian by His grace, O blessed Dionysius.

*Troparion:*
Having mortified thyself unto the present through desire toward God, and having firmly learned philosophy, through life-creating gifts which are beyond mind, thou wast a God-bearing instrument, O father.

*Theotokion:*
By the gift of the all-creating Spirit, O Virgin, as Theotokos thou didst bear fruit for God, even a body wherein the sin of the world was condemned, and everlasting life gushed forth.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and the Word" (Прему́дрости):*
In the abyss of the wisdom of the mind, having plumbed the depths of the Spirit, thou didst proclaim the one Godhead of the Trinity, O venerable one, having written down all the angelic ranks and their mystic illuminations, O godly-minded one; therefore also thou didst lead the reality of the earthly hierarchy unto the one Unity, O hierarch Dionysius! Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who honor thy holy memory with love. *(Twice)*

*Glory, now and ever, its Theotokion:*
O cloud of the spiritual Sun, golden-wrought lamp of divine Light, undefiled, spotless, all-blameless Lady, by the radiance of dispassion enlighten my soul darkened by the blindness of passions, I pray thee; and wash my defiled heart with streams of compunction and tears of repentance, and cleanse me from the mire of my deeds, that in love I may cry unto thee: O Theotokos Ever-Virgin, pray unto Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto me, for I, thy servant, have placed my hope in thee!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb, the Shepherd, and Deliverer upon the Cross, the Ewe wept aloud, and weeping bitterly, cried: The world truly rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee, but my inward parts burn as I behold Thy crucifixion, which Thou endurest for the sake of tender mercy, O longsuffering Lord! O abyss of mercy and inexhaustible spring, have compassion, and grant remission of transgressions unto those who hymn Thy divine sufferings with faith!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing; Who, without leaving the bosom of the Father, didst visit our poverty; therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind![^77]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst adorn the good order of the Church; for in her, O father, thou didst depict sensible images in fitting place by sacred writing of the formless powers,[^78] whereby all the assemblies of the faithful are mystically initiated and enlightened, O godly-minded one!

*Troparion:*
The streams of thy theology, O God-receiver, water the spiritual meadows that ever blossom abundantly and are planted upon the rectitude of theology; for thou didst proclaim the unity of essence in three Persons, the venerable single Sovereign Principle.

*Troparion:*
The intense desire and love toward God of the angelic celestial hierarchy, and their unswerving inclination toward the divine and incomprehensible height, thou didst teach in a godly-minded manner unto those who cry with faith: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Theotokion:*
Thy Son became the sacred Performer of salvation for all who hymn thee, and a guide, an enlightener, righteousness, and redemption, O Virgin; therefore, as truly and sovereignly the Theotokos, we the faithful glorify thee with one accord.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy face, O unwaning Light, and an alien darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my paths toward the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee!

*Troparion:*
The proclamation of Thine angels, borne like a heavenly sound, the godless struck down the godless minds with thunder. By the word of grace, and by the brightest rays of divine commands, enlighten the hearts of the faithful!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast likened unto a merchant seeking goodly pearls, O all-rich one; and having found the one pearl that is truly of great price,[^79] and having marveled at its divine, radiant casting of light, O holy hierarch, thou didst confess God.

*Troparion:*
Most philosophically, O father, didst thou love the Spring of wisdom; and possessed by longing and love for it, thou becamest great, and thou ever pourest forth the streams of divine commands, O proclaimer of God!

*Theotokion:*
As one full of grace, thou didst receive the beneficent beauty of Him Who by a single will created all things; Who by divine goodness and by the illumination of true faith hath adorned us, O Mother of God.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are my iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and give ear unto me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparion:*
Philosophizing lawfully, thou didst receive the gift of wisdom; divinely speaking of God, thou didst leave behind the Orthodox dogmas, O all-blessed one, enjoying which, we the faithful call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
Adorned with God-befitting understanding, O godly-minded one, thou didst incline thine ear unto the divine teacher, and didst learn from him transcendent wisdom, O Dionysius!

*Troparion:*
Thy life was marvelous, and thy word yet more admirable; thy tongue was radiant with light, thy lips fire-breathing and inspired, and thy mind tested, O father blessed of God!

*Theotokion:*
With joy we the faithful cry aloud unto thee the voice of Gabriel, O Virgin; for from thee ineffably came forth unto us the sovereign Creator of life, and mingled Himself with mankind, the Lover of mankind.

*Kontakion, Tone 8, Podoben: "To thee, the champion leader" (Взбра́нной):*
Having passed through the gates of heaven in spirit as a disciple of the Apostle who attained unto the third heaven, O Dionysius,[^80] thou wast enriched with all understanding of ineffable things, and didst enlighten those who sat in the darkness of ignorance; therefore we cry: Rejoice, O universal father!

*Ikos:*
The great Dionysius, having become an angel among men through his virtues, as though winged in his whole being, learned heavenly understanding with his mind; therefore in hymns let us honor him as an angel honoring him, crying aloud unto him such things as these:[^81]
Rejoice, thou who didst come to know Christ through Paul!
Rejoice, thou who didst turn many unto Christ!
Rejoice, destroyer of the temples of polytheism!
Rejoice, beholder of the God-comprehending counsel!
Rejoice, God-inscribed book of hidden treasures!
Rejoice, table of divine vision and heavenly mirror!
Rejoice, for thou didst behold the Passion of the Lord!
Rejoice, for thou wast eagerly slaughtered for His sake!
Rejoice, spring pouring forth spiritual understanding!
Rejoice, drop that quenches godlessness!
Rejoice, guileless path of salvation!
Rejoice, barrier against the assault of the ungodly!
Rejoice, O universal father!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
At the condescension of God, the fire was put to shame in Babylon of old; therefore the youths in the furnace, dancing with joyful foot as in a meadow of flowers, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Like a heavenly shower upon a thirsty land, thy teachings ever moisten the longing hearts of the faithful, and teach them insatiably to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
With God-wise understanding thou didst describe the bodiless minds, and having become divine, O all-blessed one, thou didst instruct the Church to sing their hymnody: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst appear as a well-appointed receptacle of the All-Holy Spirit; and prophetically proclaiming His inspirations and revelations, O wise Dionysius, thou didst joyfully cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Behold, now is fulfilled the prophecy of Isaiah who spoke of God; for the Virgin held God the Word in her womb, and gave birth unto the Giver of life, unto Whom we all cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The Chaldean tyrant furiously heated the furnace sevenfold for the pious;[^82] but having seen them saved by a higher power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having become bright as a mirror, O Dionysius, and having made thy mind through virtue a vessel of pure revelations, O all-rich one, receiving the rays of the radiance that is beyond mind, thou didst cry aloud: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Most abundant grace was poured out upon thy lips; therefore thou didst set forth divine teachings, and their understanding stretching out unto us understanding beyond intellect, more clearly and radiantly opening it unto those who sing in faith: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Through thee, O Dionysius, the all-glorious metropolis of Athens became most renowned, having offered thee as an all-sacred first-fruit unto Christ the King of all, ever singing: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
All-blameless Mother of God, destroy the scabs of my soul and the wounds of my sins; for thou didst bear a Son Who has power to easily loose from unbreakable bonds those bound by the cords of sin, the only Benefactor and the only Giver of life.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Heaven was dismayed thereat, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the hierarchies of angels and men magnify thee as the Theotokos!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast accounted worthy to be a beholder of the holy Apostles, and their fellow-sufferer, and didst become a partaker of their glory; and with them thou didst hasten unto the contemplation of the body of the truly life-originating and only Theotokos, most honorable,[^83] whom worthily we magnify.

*Troparion:*
Thou becamest an heir of the divine Kingdom in the heavens, O father, as a lawful high priest; as an invincible athlete, with the chrism of the priesthood thou didst commingle the blood of martyrdom; therefore worthily wast thou accounted worthy of a twofold crown, O sacred initiate of mysteries, Dionysius![^84]

*Troparion:*
As one having boldness toward Christ, O wise hierarch Dionysius, ever pray that those who piously hymn thee may be delivered from temptation, O disciple of ineffable visions, as an initiate and sacred revealer of the divine goodness.

*Theotokion:*
Having valiantly conquered heresies, show forth the honorable Church which Christ, Thine authentic Son, in His goodness acquired by His precious blood, O full of grace; and save us from afflictions and the surrounding distress that encompasses us, O Lady!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "While the disciples looked on" (Ученико́мъ зря́щымъ):*
Ascending by virtues, thou didst rise unto the heavens, O God-receiver; and thou didst come to know the ranks of angels and the stations of all the powers on high, having learned the thrice-radiant ray from Paul.

*Theotokion:*
The most craftily wicked enemy, having of old in paradise envied me my divine and blessed life, through malicious cunning, who drove me out of Eden, is slain by thy childbirth, O Theotokos!

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos. Glory of the Saint, Tone 8. By Germanus:*

Having pre-eminently excelled among saints and martyrs, O venerable one, thou didst show thyself a faithful shepherd, and didst drink the cup of Christ; therefore, having pleased Him in both, pray for all of us, being now established with the ministers on high in the light!

*Now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 8, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Прехва́льнїи му́ченицы):*

The undefiled Heifer, seeing the Young Bull of His own will nailed to the Tree, lamenting piteously, cried: Woe is me, O most beloved Child! What has the thankless Jewish gathering rendered unto Thee, wishing to make me childless of Thee, O All-Beloved?

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Octoechos on 4, and of the Saint, Ode 3 on 4. Prokimenon, Tone 7: The saints shall boast in glory, and they shall rejoice upon their beds.[^85] Verse: Sing unto the Lord a new song. Epistle: Acts, pericope 40 from the half.[^86] Alleluia, Tone 2. Verse: Thy priests shall clothe themselves with righteousness, and Thy righteous shall rejoice. Gospel: Matthew, pericope 55 from the half. Communion Hymn: In everlasting memory shall the righteous be; he shall not fear evil tidings.*

*(Decorative Rococo woodcut tailpiece)*[^87]

*The same month, on the fourth day. The Holy Hieromartyr Hierotheus, Bishop of Athens:[^88]*

*At Vespers, at Lord, I Have Cried, Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ еси́ зна́менїе):*

Having received the grace of the All-Holy Spirit, O all-glorious Hierotheus, thou hast now traversed the whole universe, teaching men to honor the Unity in three Hypostases: the unoriginate Father, the Source of the Godhead, with Whom also the Son as enthroned together with the Father and consubstantial, born of the Virgin, bearing flesh as a man.

A sacredly divine gift didst thou offer thy soul, as acceptable; and thou didst surrender thy body sacredly as a divine and sacredly performing, which pours forth salvation for the faithful unto Almighty God, souls of those who believe; therefore do we call thy memory and burial blessed, and we love thy miracles, and proclaim thy words.

Thou didst behold the number of the twelve Apostles at the most glorious Dormition of the Ever-Virgin and all-pure Theotokos, the Mother of the Lord, and Dionysius was with thee, who set down in writing the divine ranks;[^89] with them, we the faithful call thy most festive and holy solemnity blessed, O glorious Hierotheus!

*Glory, Tone 6:*
Standing before the divine Dormition of the Mother of God, O proclaimer of God, by divine utterances thou didst gladden the hearts of the faithful, O blessed one, and didst delight the sacred assembly of the God-proclaiming Apostles, singing the mysteries of God in most divine ecstasy;[^90] therefore, having obtained boldness toward Christ, O hierarch Hierotheus, pray for our souls!

*Now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
When the undefiled Ewe beheld her Lamb of His own will being led unto the slaughter as a man, weeping, she said: Dost Thou hasten now to make me childless, O Christ, who gave Thee birth? What is this that Thou hast done, O Deliverer of all? Yet do I hymn and glorify Thine extreme goodness that is beyond mind and word, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Having learned goodness and being sober in all things, clothed with a good conscience as befits a sacred minister, thou didst draw ineffable mysteries from the chosen vessel; and having kept the faith, thou didst finish an equal course, O hieromartyr Hierotheus:[^91] pray unto Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*Glory, now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion:*
*At Matins, both Canons of the Octoechos, and the Canon of the Saint on 4. Whose acrostic is: We honor thy light-bearing memory, O all-blessed one. A composition of Theophanes. Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^92]
Having passed through the water as if it were dry land, and having escaped from the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!

*Troparion:*
Having as teacher the heavenly disciple and mystagogue, God-receiver Paul, the fisher of the world, thou becamest a disciple of vision on high.

*Troparion:*
The tongue of Paul, speaking the mysteries of God, embraced thee in fitting measure, O blessed one, and made thee a proclaimer of God.

*Troparion:*
Wounded by spiritual love and by divine words, unto the Master of all in the knowledge of all things, O all-sacred one, thou didst dedicate sacred words.

*Theotokion:*
Having become the God-befitting receptacle of the unwaning Light, O pure Virgin: cleanse me from the defilements of every sin and from corruptions, as thou art good.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Lord, Supreme Architect of the heavenly vault and Builder of the Church, do Thou establish me in Thy love, O summit of desires, confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
As one who sacredly interprets the sacred Scriptures and proclaims the word of piety, having joyfully assembled around our teacher, we all glorify thy sacred memory.

*Troparion:*
By strength and divine nod, and by divine desire stretching thyself out toward the Benefactor of all, thou didst illuminate thy mind with rays from thence, and didst enlighten thy soul, O all-blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Suitably according to thy name wast thou called;[^93] for thou didst show thyself a sacred divine and holy God-wise offering unto the Almighty and Creator of the ages, and didst adorn His temple.

*Theotokion:*
He Who spread out the earth and holds its circle, O pure one, and in every way by the nature of the Godhead, was bodily held in thine embrace, and showed thee forth as confirmation unto all the faithful.

*Kontakion, Tone 8, Podoben: "To thee, the champion leader" (Взбра́нной):*
We praise thee as the hierarch of Athens, for through thee we have been initiated into marvelous and ineffable things; for thou wast revealed as a God-accepted singer of hymns. But pray, O all-blessed Hierotheus, that we may be delivered from all falls into sin, that we may cry: Rejoice, O God-wise father!

*Then the Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*
Having catechized thy mind with Hellenic learning, and having pastured before the assembly of the faithful,[^94] thou didst offer a pure sacrifice unto the King and Creator, Who was incarnate for our sake of the Virgin Theotokos; and having confessed Him Who is risen in glory, O Hierotheus.

*Glory, now and ever, Theotokion:*
Fervent protectress and invincible, sure and unashamed hope, rampart and shelter and haven of those who flee unto thee, O pure Ever-Virgin: with the angels pray unto thy Son and God, to grant peace unto the world, and salvation, and great mercy.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee upon the Cross, O Christ, hanging of Thine own will in the midst of thieves, Thy Mother, maternally torn in her inward parts, cried aloud: O sinless Son, how art Thou unjustly nailed to the Cross as an evildoer, wishing as the tender-hearted One to make alive the race of mankind?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing; Who, without leaving the bosom of the Father, didst visit our poverty; therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind![^95]

*Troparion:*
With eloquent divine proclamations, O blessed Hierotheus, thou didst adorn the choirs of the faithful, and in a God-wise manner thou didst gladden the sacred assembly of the Apostles, acting with divine wonder and singing: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast wholly consecrated unto God, O blessed one, departing in God-like manner unto Him with theological sacred discourses, revealed as a sacred minister, O God-receiver, a divine composer of hymns, and a wise proclaimer of God, judged prudently by those who sat with thee.

*Troparion:*
Clearly beholding divine things most brightly, O father, far surpassing all the most sacred riddles and shadows on earth, as the truest visions of divine radiances: pray that those who sing in faith may be saved: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Theotokion:*
Thou, O all-pure one, didst give birth unto the uncontainable God Who contained Himself within thy womb; as a pure maiden adorned with virginal brightnesses, O all-blessed one; therefore as the true Theotokos do we piously praise thee, and we all glorify thy childbirth.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy face, O unwaning Light, and an alien darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my paths toward the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee!

*Troparion:*
Dwelling together with the assembly of the Apostles, O father, with them thou didst stand before the divine Dormition, O voice of God, of the all-holy and pure Mother of God, who gave birth for us in the flesh unto the Giver of life.

*Troparion:*
Supernaturally understanding, and as an elder holding visions beyond words, he declared unto all who receive in faith the radiance, and thy contemplative theologies, O all-blessed one, unto those who love to understand in all-valiant manner.

*Troparion:*
Shining with most abundant rays by supernatural illumination, by the assurance of divine revelations, and by purity of life thou didst hold many fast through weighty testing, and by contemplation being taught the wisest divine things.

*Theotokion:*
Without knowing man, O Virgin, thou didst conceive from virginal blood the Word Who is uncircumscribed by the nature of the Godhead, Who for the sake of us men contained Himself, fashioned beyond word and law and nature, O Maiden.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are my iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and give ear unto me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparion:*
Being taught divine knowledge by God-befitting instruction, O father, thou hast left unto me thy words as second words of sacred knowledge,[^96] enlightening our souls.

*Troparion:*
Blessed truly wast thou, O God-blessed one, with a purified soul; and true blessedness and the only Desired One didst thou love: the beauties of the Savior, O Hierotheus!

*Troparion:*
Gazing upon the all-pure rays, O God-receiver, with intellectual flashings of divine splendors: thou wast revealed as a partaker of their enjoyment and illumination, O blessed one!

*Theotokion:*
Joyfully and truly do we glorify thee, O Theotokos; for being truly the Mother of God, thou didst ineffably give birth unto Him Who is hypostatically united in the flesh, O all-holy Virgin Mother!

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йся на Крⷭ҇тъ во́лею):*
As an unshakable pillar, O Hierotheus, thou wast not terrified by the threats of the enemies; but thou didst demolish the fortress of delusion, showing thyself as a precious stone, O father; therefore the Master crowns thee for thy bravery with His life-originating and divine right hand; pray unto Him for all of us![^97]

*Ikos:*
Obeying Thy law, O Christ, Thy disciples, proclaiming unto all among the Gentiles the word of Thy divine coming, ordain the faithful as pastors and preachers in all the churches; therefore they appointed him also as pastor, whom Thou didst choose from infancy, and having enlightened him as blameless before Thee, and having willed to raise him unto this, and having entrusted Thy flock unto him, Thou didst show him forth as a witness of Thy Kingdom, praying for all of us.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
At the condescension of God, the fire was put to shame in Babylon of old; therefore the youths in the furnace, dancing with joyful foot as in a meadow of flowers, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Striving to behold the Holy of Holies, O God-blessed one, through purity of life and exalted divine vision, thou didst wisely acquire it, and singing didst cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
The utterance of thy words and the eloquence of thy writings gladden those who read them with love, and instruct them unto hymnody, teaching them to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Known as a Unity in the Godhead, thou didst theologize upon the Trinity in Persons, praised in three Hypostases, O God-wise one; and singing didst cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Beyond the laws of nature, O Virgin Mother, thou didst give birth unto the Sovereign of all things visible and intelligible, O pure one, Whom we hymn, crying in harmony: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The Chaldean tyrant furiously heated the furnace sevenfold for the pious;[^98] but having seen them saved by a higher power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
The all-powerful operation of the Holy Spirit, dwelling in thy most pure soul, showed thee forth as a God-accepted preacher and divine speaker, sacredly hymning the Deliverer and praising Him in faith: Ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having examined with pious quest the ineffable dispensations of Christ's mysteries, Thou didst clearly explain them to be, working through theology; and by the sweetest composition of thine utterances crying unceasingly: Ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Most abundant grace filled thee with most divine inspiration and tested love of God; therefore thou didst pour forth teachings like rivers, gladdening the choirs of the Apostles; with whom thou now singest: Ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
My soul, slain by sins and harmed by venomous biting, O all-pure one, do thou raise up to cry aloud, having without wedlock and beyond nature given birth unto God Who is able to make all things alive: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Heaven was dismayed thereat, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the hierarchies of angels and men magnify thee as the Theotokos!

*Troparion:*
Having been a lover of the wisdom of the All-Wise, thou hast now doubly received from her a graceful and well-favored crown; and having become a heavenly dancer before the Creator of divine things: pray that those who praise thee, O Hierotheus, may be delivered from tribulations!

*Troparion:*
Thou hast taken up thy dwelling in the supreme divine courts, and an heir of eternal mansions, thou now celebratest with the angelic hosts and with the apostolic ranks, rejoicing gladly with them; with them also ask for freedom and divine peace for those who honor thee in faith!

*Troparion:*
Having received sure recompenses, whose manifestations thou didst acquire upon earth, gazing with constant love toward God: now fervently pray, O father Hierotheus, that mercy and cleansing may be granted unto those who celebrate in faith thy light-bearing and all-sacred memory!

*Theotokion:*
As Mother of the Creator of all, with thy maternal boldness implore thy Son to save from the tyrant's violence thy captive servants, and to bring to naught the slander of deceitful men; and to grant peace and salvation unto those who hymn thee!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ оу҆слы́шите):*
Thou didst subdue the worse unto the better, O God-receiver, setting the all-wise mind above the passions of God; on every side, O Hierotheus, thou wast an instrument of theology and an Apostle of Christ, O wise hierarch; pray for the world!

*Theotokion:*
Having arrived at thy most pure Dormition, O Maiden, Hierotheus with the rest of the Apostles sang a hymn unto thee, strange and ineffable, being divinely transported, as one truly God-accepted; show us forth also to be imitators of him, O Virgin Mother of God!

*(Decorative Rococo woodcut tailpiece)*[^99] *(Baroque woodcut headpiece)*[^100]

*The same month, on the fifth day. The Holy Martyr Charitina:[^101]*

*At Lord, I Have Cried, Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one noble" (Я҆́кѡ до́бля):*

As one named after grace, with joy thou hast passed over unto the all-honorable heavenly bridal chamber, where it was thy desire to acquire dwelling; having truly endured painful torture by fire, and the tearing out of thy teeth and all thy nails, O passion-bearer Charitina, much-suffering virgin martyr!

Thou didst break the jaws of the lions, O much-suffering one, enduring the crushing of thy jaws; and thou didst valiantly endure the tearing out of thy nails, uprooting the cruel ferocities of delusion; and cast into the depths of the sea, thou didst drown the malice of the cunning adversary.

Cast into the sea after thy blessed end, O glorious one, thy foster-father received thee,[^102] crying in amazement: How greatly art thou now graced, my most tender and beloved child, through the torments of honorable suffering; and thou hast made true the name prefigured by grace!

*Glory, now and ever, Theotokion:*
With the rays of the All-Holy Spirit, O all-pure one, dew my mind, O thou who didst give birth unto the unreckonable Drop, Christ, Who by mercies washed away the measureless iniquities of men: dry up the spring of my passions, and count me worthy ever of the torrent of living food, by thy prayers!

*Stavrotheotokion, the same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
When the Ewe who bore Thee beheld Thee upon the Tree, the Lamb and Shepherd, she wailed and maternally said unto Thee: O beloved Son, how art Thou suspended upon the Tree of the Cross, O longsuffering One? How were Thy hands and feet nailed by the lawless, O Word, and Thou didst pour out Thy blood, O Master?

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy lamb Charitina, O Jesus, cries aloud with a great voice: Thee, my Bridegroom, do I love, and seeking Thee do I suffer, and am crucified and buried together with Thy baptism, and I suffer for Thy sake, that I may reign in Thee; and I die for Thee, that I may also live with Thee; but as a blameless sacrifice accept me who have lovingly sacrificed myself unto Thee; by her prayers, as Thou art merciful, save our souls![^103]

*At Matins, both Canons of the Octoechos, and the Canon of the Saint on 4. Whose acrostic is: With love I sing the grace of Charitina. Joseph. Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^104]
Having traversed the watery deep of the Red Sea with dryshod feet, ancient Israel of old, by the cross-shaped hands of Moses, conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.[^105]

*Troparion:*
By thy gifts, O Charitina, grace my mind, having been graced by sacred sufferings, sacredly to hymn thee as a passion-bearer and martyr, shining with virginity.

*Troparion:*
The grace of the All-Holy Spirit having endowed Thee, adorned Thee with joy-bearing wreaths, and strengthened Thee to inherit everlasting joy, having suffered in a manful-minded manner.

*Troparion:*
Knowing Thee, O Master, to be lifted up upon the Cross, Thy glorious martyr endures painful wounds, strengthened by Thy power, Who didst strengthen our nature.

*Theotokion:*
A spacious dwelling of the divine nature wast thou, O pure Mother of God; therefore Charitina, loving thee and having virgin-like followed after thee, is brought unto thy Son.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and confirmation!

*Troparion:*
The divine desire that burned in thy soul extinguished the kindlings of the flesh and the cruel coals of torments.

*Troparion:*
Seeing the enemy broken in pieces before thy martyr's feet by the grace of Christ, we offer praises unto Him.

*Troparion:*
By all-efficacious strength, thou didst crush the enemy's head, having valiantly endured the scorching of thine own head, O martyr.

*Theotokion:*
Loving thee, O pure one, as the only blessed among women, the martyr Charitina was glorified by thy good gifts of grace.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (Оу҆диви́ся і҆ѡ́сифъ):*
Having graced thy soul with God-given virtues, thou didst truly shine forth through thine honorable martyrdom, and wast betrothed as a bride unto thy Creator, remaining incorrupt in purity abiding in purity, O Charitina; therefore thou didst trample down the enemy, having triumphantly endured manifold sufferings and pains; therefore in faith do we celebrate thy most holy memory.

*Glory, now and ever, Theotokion:*
Joseph marveled, beholding that which was beyond nature, and he pondered in his mind the rain upon the fleece in thy seedless conception, O Theotokos, the bush unconsumed by fire, the budding rod of Aaron;[^106] and bearing witness, thy betrothed and guardian cried aloud unto the priests: A Virgin gives birth, and after childbirth remains Virgin again!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding upon the Cross the Lamb born seedlessly of her, pierced with a spear, the Virgin and Ewe, wounded with the arrows of grief, cried out, exclaiming painfully: What is this new mystery? How dost Thou die, Who alone art the Lord of life? Therefore arise, raising up the fallen forefather!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her order, worthily crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^107]

*Troparion:*
As an unblemished sacrifice for slaughter, O passion-bearing martyr, the tormentors snatched thee from thy father's arms, thee who wast greatly beloved of the heavenly Father.

*Troparion:*
Having followed the wise virgins, O all-glorious one, thou didst richly supply the lamp of thy soul with blood;[^108] and thou hast inherited the divine bridal chamber, O martyr named after joy!

*Troparion:*
Thy heart being inflamed with desire for the Bridegroom, O valiant-minded one, by the outpouring of thy blood thou didst extinguish the coals of delusion, and didst dry up the sea of polytheism.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst give birth unto the incarnate Word of God, O pure Mother of God; Whom Charitina having most warmly loved, set herself in battle array against fire and manifold torments.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou, my Lord, art come into the world as a light, a holy Light turning from the dark gloom of ignorance those who hymn Thee in faith!

*Troparion:*
Though grieving thee, O God-wise one, by shearing thy head and burning it with coals, they in no wise shook the strength of thy mind.[^109]

*Troparion:*
Prefiguring Thy nails and slaughter, O Lord, I endure the pain of heated spikes, cried aloud the martyr.[^110]

...by the tearing off of the nails of thy hands and feet, O pure maiden, with thy flowing precious blood thou didst sanctify the faithful; and like Christ thou didst cleanse the world of demonic blood.

*Theotokion:*
An earthly mind cannot understand thy supernatural conception, O Virgin Mother and Maiden; for thou didst give birth unto God.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side![^111]

*Troparion:*
With graces and signs Christ crowned thy sacred head, for Whose sake thou didst choose to suffer, O passion-bearer named after divine joy!

*Troparion:*
He Who was delivered from the deep saves thee also alive when cast into the surge of the sea, O God-wise one;[^112] and with the depth of thy blood He drowned the enemy.

*Troparion:*
With the weapon of endurance thou didst put the enemies to death, and enduring the utter tearing out of thy teeth, O glorious one, thou didst break the jaws of the bodiless lions.

*Theotokion:*
Having found thee, O Virgin, to be her strength and confirmation, Charitina the passion-bearer valiantly endures torments, and rejoicing after thee is brought unto the Master of all.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щя̀):*
Having confirmed thy soul with faith by a strong mind, O Charitina, thou didst openly put to shame the adversary that taunted thee, and didst stand before Christ, O all-blessed one; wearing a purple robe of thy blood, and ever rejoicing with the angels, pray for us, O passion-bearer![^113]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace, the Abrahamic youths, scorched rather by love of piety than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord![^114]

*Troparion:*
He Who delivered the three young youths from the furnace saves thee, O martyr, from the fiery wheel,[^115] as thou gratefully singest: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
As a light-bearer thou ascendest unto heavenly realms with the virgins, having valiantly endured burning torches while singing: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
The most lawless one, convicted by thine endurance, binds thy hands and feet, and casts thee into the depths as thou singest: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
By thy divine word thou didst convict the unreason of the enemies; by sufferings, O martyr, thou didst fill up the lack of the life-creating Passions,[^116] crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord my God!

*Theotokion:*
As alone pure and incorrupt unto God, the pure Word loved thee, and being incarnate of thee, O Maiden, He refashioned the whole man, having glorified Charitina with wonders.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Stretching out his hands, Daniel stopped the mouths of the lions in the pit;[^117] and girded with virtue, the youths, lovers of piety, quenched the power of the fire, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Comely wast thou in the beauty of thy wounds, and remaining an incorrupt virgin, O all-honored one, thou wast joined unto the beautiful Word, Christ incarnate of a Virgin for our sake, unto Whom we cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
By all means having guided the assaults of those who tormented thee, valiantly didst thou endure the burning fury of the wheel, the searing of the goads, the casting into the deep, and the tearing out of thy precious teeth and nails, magnifying Christ unto ages.

*Troparion:*
Thy feet, O glorious one, were adorned with the beauty of thy pains, therefore walking upon the waters they were not wetted, for a glorious angel of God was abiding with thee, O virgin martyr; with whom thou didst cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
A wonder astonishing every mind is seen after thy repose, how the deep that received thee gave back thy relics unto thy foster-father,[^118] who buried them with fatherly care; he treasures up grace concerning thee, O Charitina, having received the heavenly kingdom.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown to be higher than the heavens, having given birth unto the heavenly God, Who purified the whole compound of earthborn men, O most pure one, and Who enlightened the honored memory of Charitina, unto Whom we sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
A cornerstone was cut out from thee, the unhewn mountain, O Virgin, not cut by hands of man;[^119] even Christ, Who united separated natures. Therefore, rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Theotokos!

*Troparion:*
Having obtained an incorrupt crown of glory, O virgin martyr, thou didst endure the tribulations of every manner of tortures, rejoicing in spirit and in firmness of understanding.

*Troparion:*
As an adorned, comely, and God-pleasing virgin, as an unconquerable martyr, and a blameless bride of the Master, on wings of gold thou didst fly up unto heavenly realms.

*Troparion:*
Thou, O radiant-looking passion-bearer, didst shine forth with beams, driving away the gloom of the malice of demons and of passions by the God-given wonders ever granted unto thee, O praiseworthy one.

*Troparion:*
Thy joyful day gathers together rejoicing peoples to chant thy valors, thy struggles and wrestlings, and thy manly suffering, O great-martyr, named after joy![^120]

*Theotokion:*
Light shone forth upon us from thy womb unwedded, O Virgin; enlightened divinely by His rays, she who is named after joy became light, O unwedded Mother of God.

*(Woodcut: Christ the Teacher framed by baroque foliage)*[^121]

*Month of the same, the 6th Day. The Holy and Glorious Apostle Thomas:*

*At Vespers, we sing: "Blessed is the man", the first antiphon.*

*At Lord, I Have Cried, stichera on 8, Tone 4. Podoben: "As one valiant" (Я́кѡ до́блѧ):*

*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Я́кѡ до́блѧ):*
By the touching of the side, O all-blessed Thomas, thou didst attain unto the summit of good things; for like a sponge thou didst draw water thence, the fountain of blessings and life eternal, and thou didst give drink unto hearts parched with ignorance, pouring forth the dogmas of divine knowledge of God.[^122] *(Thrice)*

*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Я́кѡ до́блѧ):*
By thy faithful unbelief thou didst confirm the faithful; beginning to theologize Him as God and Lord of all creation, Who bore flesh for the sake of us who are earthborn, and endured the Cross and death, and the piercing of the nails, and the spearing of His side, from which we draw life.[^123] *(Thrice)*

*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Я́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Thomas the marvelous opened a fountain of dogmas unto the prudent; for touching Thy side, he was taught fitly concerning the twofold operation in the twofold natures,[^124] and cried aloud: Thou art my God and Master! Thou art also the Lord of glory, Who for my sake became flesh! *(Twice) Glory, Tone 6. By the Studite (Студі́тово):*[^125]
As a servant of the Word and of His unutterable incarnation, thou didst draw the depth of wisdom, O Apostle Thomas; for with the rod of the Cross, lifting souls up from the deep of delusion, thou didst catch them; and with the net of thy dogmas thou didst enlighten the whole universe, and with the light of knowledge thou didst illumine the darkened souls of the Indians.[^126] Therefore, enjoying the radiant glory of Christ, pray Him to have mercy on our souls.

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
Who will not call thee blessed:

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day:*

*Three Readings:*

*Reading from the Universal Epistle of John (Chapter 1):*
That which was from the beginning, which we have heard, which we have seen with our eyes, which we have looked upon, and our hands have handled, of the Word of life; (for the life was manifested, and we have seen it, and bear witness, and declare unto you that eternal life, which was with the Father, and was manifested unto us;) that which we have seen and heard declare we unto you, that ye also may have fellowship with us; and truly our fellowship is with the Father, and with His Son Jesus Christ. And these things write we unto you, that your joy may be full. This then is the message which we have heard of Him, and declare unto you, that God is light, and in Him is no darkness at all. If we say that we have fellowship with Him, and walk in darkness, we lie, and do not the truth; but if we walk in the light, as He is in the light, we have fellowship one with another, and the blood of Jesus Christ His Son cleanses us from all sin.

*Reading from the Universal Epistle of James (Chapter 1):*
James, a servant of God and of the Lord Jesus Christ, to the twelve tribes which are scattered abroad, greeting. My brethren, count it all joy when ye fall into diverse temptations; knowing this, that the trying of your faith produces patience. But let patience have her perfect work, that ye may be perfect and entire, wanting nothing. If any of you lack wisdom, let him ask of God, Who gives to all men liberally, and upbraids not; and it shall be given him. But let him ask in faith, nothing doubting; for he that doubts is like a wave of the sea driven with the wind and tossed. For let not that man think that he shall receive any thing of the Lord. A double-minded man is unstable in all his ways. Let the brother of low degree rejoice in that he is exalted:

...in that he is exalted, but the rich, in that he is made low; because as the flower of the grass he shall pass away. For the sun is no sooner risen with a burning heat, but it withers the grass, and the flower thereof falls, and the grace of the fashion of it perishes; so also shall the rich man fade away in his ways. Blessed is the man that endures temptation; for when he is tried, he shall receive the crown of life, which the Lord has promised to them that love Him.

*Reading from the Universal Epistle of Jude (Chapter 1):*
Jude, the servant of Jesus Christ, and brother of James, to them that are sanctified by God the Father, and preserved in Jesus Christ, and called: Mercy unto you, and peace, and love, be multiplied. Beloved, when I gave all diligence to write unto you of our common salvation, it was needful for me to write unto you, and exhort you that ye should earnestly contend for the faith which was once delivered unto the saints. For there are certain men crept in unawares, who were before of old ordained to this condemnation, ungodly men, turning the grace of our God into lasciviousness, and denying the only Master God, and our Lord Jesus Christ. I will therefore put you in remembrance, though ye once knew this, how that the Lord, having saved the people out of the land of Egypt, afterward destroyed them that believed not. And the angels which kept not their first estate, but left their own habitation, He has reserved in everlasting chains under darkness unto the judgment of the great day. Even as Sodom and Gomorrah, and the cities about them in like manner, giving themselves over to fornication, and going after strange flesh, are set forth for an example, suffering the vengeance of eternal fire. But, beloved, remember ye the words which were spoken before of the apostles of our Lord Jesus Christ; how that they told you there should be mockers in the last time, who should walk after their own ungodly lusts. These be they who separate themselves from the unity of faith, sensual, having not the Spirit. But ye, beloved, building up yourselves on your most holy faith, praying in the Holy Spirit, keep yourselves in the love of God, looking for the mercy of our Lord Jesus Christ unto eternal life. And of some have compassion, making a difference; and others save with fear, pulling them out of the fire; hating even the garment spotted by the flesh.[^127] Now unto Him that is able to keep you without sin and without blemish, and to present you faultless before the presence of His glory with exceeding joy, to the only wise God our Savior, through Jesus Christ our Lord, be glory and majesty, dominion and power, before all ages, and now, and ever, and unto all ages. Amen.

*If the superior wishes to sing the Litiya: the sticheron of the temple, and of the apostle, Tone 4. Podoben: "As one valiant" (Я́кѡ до́блѧ):*

*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Я́кѡ до́блѧ):*
With the rod of grace, thou didst draw men up from the deep of vanity, O praiseworthy one; having obeyed the nod of the Teacher in all things, Who enlightened thine understanding in every way, and showed thee, O all-blessed one, to be an apostle and an honorable proclaimer of God, speaking of His unsearchable Godhead.

*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Я́кѡ до́блѧ):*
The radiance of the Spirit came down upon thee in the form of fire,[^128] and made thee, O blessed one, a divine receptacle; swiftly driving away the demonic gloom, and enlightening the world with the radiance of thy most wise words, O revealer of mysteries, adornment of the apostles, blessed eyewitness of Christ!

*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Я́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Enlightening with the lightnings of preaching those sitting in the darkness of ignorance, O glorious one, by faith thou didst show them to be sons of the Master and God; whose Passions and death thou didst emulate, and becamest an heir of glory, as one wise and eloquent of God, as a disciple of truth.

*Glory, Tone 2:*
Leaving earthly things, thou didst follow Christ, and having been sealed by the breathing of the Holy Spirit,[^129] thou wast sent by Him unto the lost nations, to turn men unto the light of the knowledge of God, O Apostle Thomas; and having finished the struggles of thy divine passion and of manifold tortures, thou didst commit thy soul unto Christ; pray Him, O all-blessed one, to grant us great mercy.

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
All my hope I place in thee, O Mother of God; preserve me under thy protection.

*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*

*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
As a pearl from the deep of worldly confusion, Christ the Lover of mankind took thee; through thee having enriched those impoverished and held fast by the destitution of terrible evils, O Apostle Thomas. Therefore we call thee blessed, and in praise we glorify thy most festive memory, piously honoring thee.

*Verse: Their sound has gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.*[^130]

*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Thou didst enlighten the land of India, O most sacred and God-beholding apostle; for having enlightened all, thou didst make them sons of the light and day, by their faith, O God-wise one; having cast down the temples of idols, through grace thou didst raise up churches unto the praise of God in all things, O intercessor for our souls!

*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands.*[^131]

*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
With an inquisitive hand thou didst examine the divine side and the wounds of the nails, which the Immortal One offered for our sake; and having gladly turned unbelief into good belief, O Thomas, thou didst cry aloud: Thou art God and Lord, and I glorify Thee, O Lover of mankind, Who by Thy Passions hast poured forth dispassion unto the faithful![^132]

*Glory, Tone 6. By Anatolius (А҆нато́лїево):*[^133]
Seeing the mystical things of Christ's wisdom, thou wast mystically shown to be an intellectual bowl, O Apostle Thomas, wherein the souls of the faithful rejoice; for with the divine net of the Spirit thou didst draw up the people from the deep of ignorance; therefore from Sion, like a river of grace, thou didst go forth, pouring out thy divine dogmas into all creation; therefore, conforming thyself unto Christ's Passions, thou wast pierced in the side,[^134] entering into the gloom of incorruption; pray Him to save our souls.

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
O Theotokos, thou art the vine:

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
O holy Apostle Thomas, entreat the merciful God, that He grant remission of transgressions unto our souls.

*Or this, Tone 2:*
Having been a disciple of Christ and a partaker of the divine apostolic choir; for by unbelief having confirmed the Resurrection of Christ, and by touching having certified His most pure Passion, O all-praised Thomas, now also entreat peace and great mercy for us.

*Glory, now and ever, Theotokion.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord", the Troparion twice; Glory, now and ever, Resurrection Theotokion.*

*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5. Podoben: "Let us, the faithful, praise" (Собезна́чальное сло́во):*

*Tone 5, Podoben: "Let us, the faithful, praise" (Собезна́чальное сло́во):*
Let us all praise with hymns the apostle and disciple of Christ in his memory; for in a manner befitting God, having touched the wounds of the nails, and having sought out certain faith, he confirmed our thoughts in the world, and entreats the Savior that our souls may find mercy. *(Twice)*

*Glory, now and ever, Theotokion:*
Ever entreat God and Lord, Who was incarnate of thee for our sake who are corrupted by sins, O pure one, that He may show mercy unto us and turn away His wrath and anger from those who unceasingly cry aloud with faith: Great are thy wonders, O full of grace!

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Lifted up" (Вознесы́йся):*

*Tone 4, Podoben: "Lifted up" (Вознесы́йся):*
Having touched the Savior's side and drawn divine understanding thence, O glorious apostle, by thy faithful unbelief thou didst draw earthborn men unto faith; and pierced by lances all over thy body,[^135] thou enlightenest the souls of all who praise thy holy falling asleep. *(Twice)*

*Glory, now and ever, Theotokion:*
O unashamed hope of those who trust in thee, who alone didst give birth in the flesh beyond nature unto Christ our God: with the holy apostles entreat Him to grant cleansing of transgressions unto the universe, and unto us all amendment of life before the end.

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3. Podoben: "Of divine faith" (Бж҃їя вѣ́рѣ):*

*Tone 3, Podoben: "Of divine faith" (Бж҃їя вѣ́рѣ):*
By the light-beaming radiance of the Spirit of God, thou didst destroy the darkness of polytheism, and didst enlighten the hearts of the faithful, chanting saving commandments, O all-wise Apostle Thomas; entreat Christ God to grant us great mercy. *(Twice)*

*Glory, now and ever, Theotokion:*
Whithersoever anyone is saved, thither does he rightly flee for refuge; and what other such refuge is there that shelters our souls as thou, O Theotokos?

*Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Their sound has gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.*[^136]

*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands.*[^137]

*Let every breath praise the Lord:*

*Gospel from John, pericope 67 (John 20:19–31).*[^138]

*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 4:*
Christ, the great Sun, sends thee, a much-radiant beam, unto India, O revealer of mysteries, swiftly driving away the murky deceit, O all-wise Apostle Thomas, and enlightening the people to honor the divine Trinity, by Whom we all are preserved.

*Canon of the Theotokos with the Heirmos on 6, and of the Apostle on 8, whereof the Acrostic is: "I marvel at Thomas who is full of wonders. By Theophanes, Tone 4."*[^139]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin, drown, I pray Thee, the three-part power of my soul in the deep of dispassion,[^140] as Thou of old didst drown the valiant captains; that in the mortification of the body I may chant a song of victory unto Thee as upon a timbrel![^141]

*Troparion:*
Entreat the unoriginate Son of God, God the Word, Who appeared upon the earth in a form like unto ours, Whom thou didst see, O glorious one, and Whose side thou didst touch with the palm of thy hand, to save thy flock!

*Troparion:*
My God Jesus, the Knower of secret things, Who searches the reins and tries the hearts,[^142] received thee, O all-blessed Thomas, as a minister and witness of His theophany, and as a divine apostle.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast shown to be a river of mystical waters, watering the face of the earth, O Thomas; and with the streams of the knowledge of God and with the sound of the Spirit,[^143] thou didst drive away the assemblies of the adversary and the polytheistic deceit.

*Theotokion:*
Being the destroyer of death and corruption, having given birth unto Christ, the fountain of incorruption, O all-hymned Theotokos, Who by grace adorned human nature with immortality, O most pure one!

*Katavasia: "I shall open my mouth:"*[^144]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
We boast not in wisdom, nor in power, nor in wealth, but in Thee, O Christ, the hypostatic Wisdom of the Father; for there is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind![^145]

*Troparion:*
Being adorned with the comeliness of virtue, thou didst become like Him, O eyewitness; and by wonders illuminating with divine light the malicious ignorance of the Indians, through faith thou didst enlighten their gloom.

*Troparion:*
Kindled with the radiance of the Spirit's creative power, thou wast sent as a God-illumined arrow, O God-receiver, and by wonders thou didst enlighten the world, O Thomas.

*Troparion:*
Initiated into the dogmas of divine wisdom, passing through like the swiftness of lightning, O Apostle Thomas, thou didst enlighten the ends of the world with light.

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth in the flesh unto God without knowing wedlock, strengthen me when I am shaken by the assaults of passions; for there is no helper, O most pure one, save thee!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Прему́дрости):*

*Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Прему́дрости):*
With the net of divine words having caught rational fish, thou didst offer them as first-fruits unto our God; and desiring to be clothed in the wounds of Christ, thou wast shown to be an imitator of His Passion; therefore having come together, we dutifully honor thy most festive memory, O glorious apostle, and with one accord we cry unto thee: Entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love! *(Twice)*

*Glory, now and ever, Theotokion:*
As a Virgin and alone among women, who didst give birth unto God in the flesh without seed, all generations of mankind call thee blessed; for the fire of the Godhead made its abode in thee, and as an infant thou didst nurse the Creator and Lord; therefore both the angelic and human race worthily glorify thy most holy birthgiving, and with one accord we cry unto thee: Entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who worship thy most pure birthgiving with faith!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
He that sits in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, Jesus the most divine, came upon a light cloud, and by an incorrupt hand saved those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ![^146]

*Troparion:*
Beginning with the most glorious manifestation of wonders, and brightly setting forth the glory of divine dogmas, thou didst encompass the universe with preaching that cries aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!

*Troparion:*
As an eyewitness of the Word, and His fervent minister, and a witness of His glory and majesty, and as a faithful steward and apostle, we praise thee, O blessed and all-honored Thomas!

*Troparion:*
Having fixed thy foundation upon the cornerstone, as a bridal chamber and a heavenly tabernacle, thou didst build up those who cry, O blessed and ever-memorable one: Glory to Thy power, O Christ![^147]

*Theotokion:*
We name thee, the all-holy Virgin, the untilled land that brought forth the Ear of life,[^148] and the light cloud that poured down the shower of the Giver of life, crying: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
The ungodly shall not see Thy glory, O Christ; but waking early out of the night, we chant unto Thee, the Only-begotten, the brightness of the Father's glory of the Godhead, O Lover of mankind![^149]

*Troparion:*
Illumined with the glory of Christ, O apostle, and having touched the life-creating side, thou didst draw the unfathomable deep of theology, and didst enrich the world.

*Troparion:*
Not having come at Thy first entrance, Thomas believed not the disciples; but afterward believing, he cried aloud rejoicing, proclaiming Thee to be God and Lord, O most merciful One![^150]

*Troparion:*
As an animate heaven declaring Thy glory, O Christ,[^151] with spiritual thunder Thomas cried aloud, and confessed Thee as God and Lord, preaching good tidings unto the faithful.

*Theotokion:*
Show my mind to be conqueror of passions and thoughts, O all-immaculate one; subdue their assaults, O Bride of God, who didst give birth unto God, the Savior of our souls!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I shall sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, cleansed from demonic blood by the blood which for mercy's sake flowed from Thy side![^152]

*Troparion:*
Thy doubting, O blessed one, heals the soul-destroying unbelief of all; for without doubt thou didst examine the wounds of the nails and of the spear!

*Troparion:*
As a mighty One, O Christ, Thou didst send forth the apostle as a sharpened arrow into the hearts of Thine enemies, O Giver of life, and he brought unto Thee an abundance of their spoils!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst abolish the idolatrous sacrifices, O compassionate One, being slain upon the Tree; and the holy slaughters of the God-proclaiming apostles, who imitated Thy Passions, brought an end to them.

*Theotokion:*
O wonder, newest of all wonders! For without knowing man, a virgin conceived in her womb Him that upholds all things, and contained Him without constraint!
