*Kontakion, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Яви́лся є҆сѝ дне́сь):*
Full of the grace of wisdom, the apostle of Christ and true minister cried out in repentance unto Thee: Thou art my God and Lord!

*Ikos:*
Peter, great in proclaiming God, cried out to the chief disciple and great revealer of mysteries, Thomas: We have seen the Lord! But he said: If I see not the prints of the nails in His hands, I will not believe! He that made the oath cried aloud: Then the Master of all came as a servant, desiring to save all, and says unto Thomas: Handle My hands and side, and be not faithless, for I am thy Lord and God! Then he cried out in repentance: Thou art my God and Lord![^153]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace, the Abrahamic youths, scorched rather by love of piety than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord![^154]

*Troparion:*
With the choir-stands on high, as an apostle rejoicing thou dancest in the heavens, O blessed one; and rejoicing thou singest: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Unto the ends of the earth ran forth thy proclamation, and the exposition of thy divine words, teaching those who cry: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast a temple of the all-hymned Trinity, O all-praised one; therefore thou didst destroy the temples of idols and their statues, O apostle, and didst erect temples unto the glory and praise of Christ God!

*Theotokion:*
From the bondage of the Law we were delivered by thy birthgiving; for thou, O Theotokos, didst bear for us the Deliverer, without knowing man. Blessed art thou among women, O all-blameless Sovereign Lady!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The birthgiving of the Theotokos saved the pious youths in the furnace: then prefigured, but now in operation, it moves the whole universe to chant: Praise the Lord, ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages![^155]

*Troparion:*
Conversing with God Who became incarnate, forgetting the flesh, thou wast shown to be a converser with God, O blessed one; and thou wast enriched with supramundane deification, being deified by drawing near unto God, and crying: Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
United in love unto Christ, thou didst love death with Him, O blessed one, and didst say unto thy fellow apostle: Let us also go, that we may die with Him;[^156] crying: Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Christ the Savior showed the apostles to be clouds of voluntary rain, drowning the churches of the all-wise in showers of divine knowledge, and watering those who chant: Praise the Lord, ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Pure of blemishes and defilements, and a temple of every holiness, thou didst give birth unto the all-sacred Word of God, co-unoriginate with the Father; therefore we hymn thee, O Virgin, and exalt thee above all unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
A cornerstone was cut out from thee, the unhewn mountain, O Virgin, even Christ, Who joined together the sundered natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Theotokos![^157]

*Troparion:*
Thou wast shown to be a light-bearing chariot of the seated Word, borne upon thy most comely and God-bearing soul, O glorious one, guiding unto salvation those who praise thee.

*Troparion:*
Celebrating thy most blessed memory, we pray: Deliver us from every tempest through the intercession of thy boldness, which thou hast as an apostle of Christ, O all-honored seer of God!

*Troparion:*
Today we celebrate thy memory, O God-proclaiming Thomas; illumine and enlighten us with faith, delivering us through thy prayers from temptations, tribulations, and all passions!

*Theotokion:*
A fair ear of grain sprouted from the virginal soil: the Word of God, whereon we the faithful feed, and in Whom we live and exist; therefore, having given birth unto Him, we magnify thee as the Theotokos!

*Exapostilarion. Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ оуслы́шите):*
O eyewitness and apostle of Christ the all-compassionate: Entreat Him to grant peace unto all the universe, and victories unto our Christ-loving king;[^158] and remission of transgressions and deliverance from diseases unto those who praise thee, O Thomas, as a God-speaking disciple!

*Glory, Another, Podoben: "In the Spirit" (Дх҃омъ):*
As a most radiant sun of the East, thou wast sent unto the Indians, O Apostle Thomas, thou praise of the apostles; and thou didst enlighten their darkness with the light of thy words and with Trinitarian faith; therefore we celebrate thy holy and divine memory.

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
We the faithful dutifully bring thee forward as a suppliant and fervent protectress, thy most pure Mother; make Thy servants worthy to partake of Thy glory, O Christ, and of the inheritance of the righteous, and of the delights of Paradise, and grant the kingdom of heaven.

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Idiomela. Tone 1. By Anatolius:*
Thou didst follow Christ, O Apostle Thomas, and didst not look back upon the world; and having hidden His teachings in thy treasure-house, thou wast shown to be an apostle; therefore being among the Gentiles, thou didst enlighten by the Savior's commandments their souls darkened by deceit and passions, to believe in the consubstantial Trinity. *(Twice)*

*Tone 4:*
By the divine fire of understanding thou didst enlighten thy soul; in all things thou wast an imitator of the Master as a lawful disciple, O Apostle Thomas; for with the reed of the Cross thou didst draw up the souls of the ungodly from the depth of ignorance; therefore thou didst catch with the fishhook of the Holy Spirit the darkened conscience of the Indians; ever entreat Christ God that we may be delivered from the dark countenance of the enemy, and that our souls may be saved.

*Tone 4:*
Having left the depth of fishing, by faith thou didst hasten unto the reed of the Cross, and thereby thou didst catch all men, O Apostle Thomas; therefore having touched the most pure side of the Word with thy hand, thou wast deemed worthy to behold with thy finger the wounds of the pierced nails; ever fervently entreat Christ God to save our souls.

*Glory, Tone 6:*
Ascending upon the chariot of virtues, thou wast shown to be a charioteer as Elijah, being kindled by the fire of the Holy Spirit, O Apostle Thomas; for searching the depth of the rational sea, thou didst catch fish, bringing them from death unto life; therefore enlightening the souls of the faithful, by the reed of the Cross thou didst lead them from death-bearing deceit unto life; and standing before Christ with the bodiless hosts, fervently entreat Him for the salvation of our souls.

*Now and ever, Theotokion. Great Doxology, and Dismissal. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from Ode 3 and Ode 6, on 8.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 8: Their sound has gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.*[^159]

*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands.*[^160]

*Epistle to the Corinthians, pericope 131 (1 Cor. 4:9–16).*[^161]

*Alleluia* Tone 1: The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord.*[^162]

*Gospel from John, pericope 65 (John 20:19–31).*[^163]

*Communion Verse: Their sound has gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.*[^164]

*On the same month, on the seventh day. The Holy Martyrs Sergius and Bacchus.*

*At Lord, I Have Cried, Stichera on 6, Tone 6. Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*

Having despised all earthly might, and having hated the flesh, ye loved the holy God; and wearing shameful clothing in mockery, from heaven ye received a garment of light and an incorruptible robe; wherein rejoicing, O venerable crown-bearers, be ye a shelter and clothing unto us all, who have been stripped of life through the deception of the serpent!

O most glorious Sergius and all-praised Bacchus, vessels of the Spirit, streams pouring out healings unto all, our guardians and strong champions of the Church, voluntary sacrifices and honorable offerings, luminaries illuminating the ends of the earth with the light of piety: dispel the darkness that lies upon our souls, and drive away all the malicious gloom!

Unwaning mansions, and rational joy, and unsetting light, and life sorrowless and imperishable, ye received upon departing from the corruptible body, O glorious sufferers; but pray that we also, having slain the passions and turned away from sin, may attain these, O Sergius and Bacchus, protectors and havens of the faithful, praying with boldness for our souls!

*Glory, Tone 4:*
Sergius and Bacchus, the radiant and twofold dawn of martyrs: for ye cast down the savagery of the torturers, and abolished the idolatrous deceit, and with a radiant voice speaking on high, ye proclaimed the perfect mystery of the knowledge of God; by their prayers, O Christ the Son, Lawgiver, Arbiter of the contest, and Crowner, make us also worthy powerfully to crown ourselves with mastery against the unseen and wicked powers![^165]

*Now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion. Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Я́кѡ до́бля):*
Beholding Christ, the Lover of mankind, crucified and His side pierced with a lance, the most pure one wept and cried aloud: What is this, O my Son? What have the ungrateful people rendered unto Thee for all the good things Thou didst do for them? And dost Thou hasten to leave me childless, O most beloved One? I marvel, O compassionate One, at Thy voluntary crucifixion!

*At the Aposticha from the Octoechos.*

*Glory, Tone 8:*
The array of the martyrs of Christ was strengthened against the enemies; among whom, like earthly luminaries, shine the glorious and most excellent passion-bearers Sergius and Bacchus; before them the wicked demonic host turned its back; at them the torturers were amazed, and the angels marveled, beholding the bodiless trampled by the bodily; and the Church of the faithful, celebrating an all-festive feast and worldly joy, cries aloud: O Thou Who by the weakness of the flesh didst bind the strong one, by the prayers of Thy saints save our souls!

*Now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion. Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
What is this sight that is beheld, which is seen by mine eyes, O Master? He that upholds all creation is lifted up upon the Tree, and Thou diest Who givest life unto all! the Theotokos weeping said, when she beheld Him that was borne upon the Cross, God and man Who ineffably shone forth from her.

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received imperishable crowns from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, they cast down the torturers and shattered the feeble boldness of the demons; by their prayers, save our souls!

*Or this, Tone 5:*
O adornment of the passion-bearers of Christ, and eyes of the Church of Christ, enlighten the eyes of our souls, O much-suffering Sergius and all-glorious Bacchus; entreat the Lord that we may flee the darkness of sin, and through your prayers, O holy ones, may be shown to be partakers of the unsetting light!

*At Matins, the Canon of the Saints on 6, whereof the Acrostic is: "I sing of Sergius the passion-bearer, and of the ever-memorable Bacchus. By Theophanes, Tone 4."*[^166]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Thy victorious right hand has been glorified in strength in a manner befitting God; for it, O Immortal One, as all-powerful, shattered the adversaries fashioning anew for the Israelites a path through the deep![^167]

*Troparion:*
O Sergius, servant of Christ, having received as fellow suppliant and helper the ever-memorable Bacchus, with whom thou didst piously accomplish thy sufferings: grant a word of wisdom unto me who sing of you!

*Troparion:*
Having laid your foundation upon the immovable rock of faith in Christ, O holy martyrs, established upon a firm step, ye were shown to be a rampart and pillars of piety.

*Troparion:*
Having believed the words of Christ, and gazing steadfastly toward Him, ye spat upon all passing renown, O holy ones, and were wounded by the love of the everlasting glory.

*Troparion:*
Stripped for Christ's sake of noble birth, homeland, and honor, ye rejoiced; and being clad in womanly apparel, ye were radiantly arrayed in the garment of incorruption with manly wisdom.

*Theotokion:*
Entreat the Word Whom thou didst bear, Who came unto us in the flesh, to be merciful and kindly unto the faithful, O Mother of Him; for we have acquired thee as our protectress and salvation.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Thou Who alone knowest the weakness of human nature, and didst mercifully take form therein: gird me with power from on high, that I may cry unto Thee, O Holy One: The animate temple of Thine ineffable glory, O Lover of mankind![^168]

*Troparion:*
He that alone as God knows all things before their being, now beholding your purpose toward Him, abundantly filled you with the word of wisdom, and divine knowledge, and firm understanding, as His own warriors, O blessed ones.

*Troparion:*
Lawfully choosing to suffer for Christ, O servants, with God-wise understanding the passion-bearing martyrs hated the corruptible and fleeting glory of all the world and of the prince of the world, through love of the Creator.

*Troparion:*
Standing before the Lord with most pure applications of the mind, and being filled with the radiant beams from thence, and now enjoying ineffable blessedness, deliver from misfortunes those who honor you, O passion-bearers!

*Theotokion:*
Adam, who of old was held fast by death, is now delivered by thy birthgiving, O only Bride of God; for thou, O pure one, beyond nature and understanding, didst bear the hypostatic Life, united in flesh according to hypostasis.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*

Come, ye lovers of martyrs, let us honor Sergius and Bacchus, the holy rational pearls of the Lord and passion-bearers, as having manfully trampled underfoot the deceit of the enemy, and having destroyed all the strength of idols; therefore having worthily received from heaven crowns of victory, they dance with the angels; unto whom let us all cry aloud: Entreat Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who honor your holy memory with love! *(Twice)*

*Glory, now and ever, Theotokion:*
Falling into many-woven tribulations from enemies visible and invisible, held fast by the tempest of my countless transgressions: as unto my fervent protection and shelter, O pure one, I flee unto the haven of thy goodness; therefore, O most pure one, fervently entreat Him Who was incarnate from thee without seed, for all thy servants who unceasingly pray unto thee, O all-immaculate Theotokos, ever imploring Him to grant remission of transgressions unto those who worthily hymn thy glory!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The Ewe-lamb, beholding the Lamb, Shepherd, and Deliverer upon the Cross, cried aloud weeping, and bitterly lamenting, cried: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee, but my womb burns, beholding Thy Crucifixion, which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of compassion, O long-suffering Lord, abyss of mercy, inexhaustible fountain! Be compassionate, and grant remission of transgressions unto those who with faith chant Thy divine Sufferings!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Habakkuk, with foresighted eyes looking upon thee as the mountain overshadowed by the grace of God, foretold that from thee should come forth the Holy One of Israel, for our salvation and renewal![^169]

*Troparion:*
Two luminaries having shone forth from the west, making their way contrary unto the ungodly who then held sway over the earth, came unto Thy light-bearing and saving Orient, O Christ.

*Troparion:*
Neither fire, nor sword, nor persecutions, nor wounds separated you from pious understanding in God; for ye despised this transitory life for His sake, O praiseworthy ones, and received blessed and imperishable delight.

*Troparion:*
Truly unsetting luminaries illuminate the firmament of the Church of Christ with the light of divine radiance, and gladden the souls of the pious, sending forth rays of wonders.

*Troparion:*
Divine Bacchus and most excellent Sergius, by the yearly return, summon the lovers of piety and lovers of martyrs unto a spiritual banquet, setting forth their valiant deeds.

*Theotokion:*
The sword that guards the gates of Eden now yields unto the faithful; and beholding them marked by the precious blood of Him that was born of thy womb, O all-immaculate one, and by grace, it joyfully receives them.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
O Christ Who didst enlighten the ends of the world with the brightness of Thy coming, and didst illumine them with Thy Cross: enlighten with the light of Thy divine knowledge the hearts of those who orthodoxly hymn Thee!

*Troparion:*
Having driven away deceit with the weapon of truth, and manfully endured the wounds of the torturers, ye became victors, being crowned by Christ, and now ye worthily rejoice.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by the invincible power and grace of the Trinity, the pair of martyrs cast down the prince of darkness and those enslaved unto him; therefore they are piously called blessed.

*Troparion:*
By endurance, and courage of soul, and fortitude of thought, the glorious pair of martyrs surpassed all the bitter rage of the persecutors, and takes up abode with the angels.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst shine forth, O Mother of Him, as the dawn, bearing upon thy hands the unsetting Sun of true righteousness, united in flesh according to hypostasis; therefore we all glorify thee.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
The deepest abyss has surrounded us, and there is none to deliver; we are counted as sheep for the slaughter; save Thy people, O our God, for Thou art the strength and restoration of the weak![^170]

*Troparion:*
The relics of the martyrs richly pour forth fountains of healings; let us therefore, O faithful, fervently draw forth, and call the passion-bearers blessed, even glorious Sergius and ever-renowned Bacchus.

*Troparion:*
Hating the cohabitation of the lawless, and doing away with deceit, Sergius and Bacchus journeyed steadfastly upon the heavenly path, and reached the infallible haven of Christ.

*Troparion:*
The heavenly gates are opened unto the passion-bearing martyrs, who revere the honorable suffering, which resembles that of our gracious God and drives away demonic hosts.

*Theotokion:*
Truly acknowledging thee as the Mother of God, O most pure one, we proclaim it with understanding; for beyond the ages thou didst bear the unoriginate, only-begotten Son Who shone forth from the Father ineffably, O Virgin.

*Kontakion, Tone 2. Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Въ вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Having manfully armed your mind against the enemies, ye destroyed all their deceit; and having received victory from on high, O all-lauded martyrs, with one mind ye cried aloud: Good and pleasant it is to be with God![^171]

*Ikos:*
Living in the heavens, O Christ, Sergius and Bacchus, and being filled with divine light from Thee: hasten quickly to forestall me who walk in the darkness of ignorance, and rescue me from passions, O Thou Who alone art immortal; having sent down upon me the garment of repentance, that with a radiant mind I may sing of their light-bearing feast, and cry aloud rejoicing: Good and pleasant it is to be with God!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
We the faithful behold thee as a spiritual furnace, O Theotokos; for as the exalted One saved the three youths, so He wholly renewed the world in thy womb, the praised and most glorious God of our fathers![^172]

*Troparion:*
Rejoice, glorious pair of passion-bearers! Rejoice, ye who radiantly conquered the threats of the torturers! Rejoice, ye who accomplished the blessed course! Rejoice, O food ever remaining within! Rejoice, ye who stand before God, O God-blessed ones!

*Troparion:*
Walking most radiantly upon the earth, O martyrs, ye darkened the demonic eyes and the faces of the persecutors by the light of grace and the rays of suffering, as ye sang of the praiseworthy and all-glorious God.

*Troparion:*
Having become living animate temples of God, and living as sufferers, being truly strengthened by the power of God, ye conquered the hosts of enemies, O all-blessed ones, praising in all songs Him that is strong in battle!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast a most pure temple and a spiritual ark, having received the uncontainable and incomprehensible Creator in thy womb, the only-begotten God the Word, O blessed, all-immaculate Theotokos!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
In the furnace the children of Israel, as in a crucible, shone more purely than gold with the beauty of piety, saying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, chant and exalt Him above all unto all ages![^173]

*Troparion:*
O Thou Who of old didst cool the flame with dew for the youths, strengthen Thy martyrs standing in the contests, as they sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, chant and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
With understanding the passion-bearers repulsed the poisonous and soul-harming flatteries of the torturers, crying unto Christ and saying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, chant and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Christ showed Sergius and Bacchus to be effective guardians unto us, truly crowned victors, for those who keep their memory and chant: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, chant and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Direct my path of virtues, O Master Christ, by the prayers of the martyrs, and deem me worthy easily to cry unto Thee: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, chant and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
I bless thee as the true Mother of our God, and with the angel I bring unto thee: Rejoice, O pure, all-immaculate Theotokos; for unceasingly all the works of the Lord sing of thee and exalt thee above all unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The fiery bush that burned yet was unconsumed showed forth a type of thy pure birthgiving; and now we pray thee to quench the furnace of temptations raging against us, that we may unceasingly magnify thee, O Theotokos![^174]

*Troparion:*
Ye acquired Christ entirely, for for His sake ye left all the world; and now, O martyrs, ye take up abode in the heavens, beholding the choirs of the hosts on high, and the splendors of blessed men.

*Troparion:*
O most excellent couple of soul, entreat the only Compassionate One to grant grace from on high and remission of transgressions unto those who bring praise unto you with love, striving toward Him: despise not those who are of lower degree.

*Troparion:*
Enlightened by the radiance of the tri-radiant Godhead, the invincible martyrs cast away the polytheistic, deceitful godlessness and the fear of the torturers; and now they enjoy imperishable delights of paradise.

*Troparion:*
Having finished the good course and taken care to keep the pious faith, ye were deemed worthy to receive the unshakable kingdom, O all-glorious martyrs, crowned with a diadem of goodness and majesty.[^175]

*Theotokion:*
O marvels of thine beyond understanding! For beyond nature, O Virgin Mother, thou alone didst give birth unto the Word of God Who became flesh, Who by His divine will wisely upholds and guides all things.

*Exapostilarion. Podoben: "Let us assemble with the disciples" (Оу҆ченикѡ́мъ):*

Let us praise the light-radiant pair of passion-bearers, Sergius and Bacchus; for they endured the onslaught of wounds, and cast down the pride of the all-malicious torturer by Trinitarian power.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown to be more glorious than the Cherubim and higher than the Seraphim, having given birth unto God incarnate; and thou didst show unto earthborn men the straight paths to walk in, leading unto salvation.

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Idiomela. Tone 1. By Germanus:*
The pair of martyrs Sergius and Bacchus cried aloud: Behold now, what is so good or what so pleasant, as for brethren to dwell together in unity,[^176] not bound by love of nature, but by the manner of faith! Therefore the holy ones trampled the enemy, and having taken up the Cross followed Christ; and they pray unto the King and God to grant great mercy unto our souls. *(Twice)*

*Tone 4:*
Having come together, let us prophetically cry aloud a song unto the saints: Behold now, what is so good or what so pleasant, as for brethren to abide together in unity,[^177] not by succession of kinship, but by the conjunction of faith in the Holy Spirit! For having cast away all corruptible things, and having taken the Cross upon their shoulders, Sergius and Bacchus, the valiant martyrs, followed Christ; and having boldness in the heavens, they ever pray for us, to grant us great mercy.

Having shod himself in the preparation of the Gospel of peace,[^178] with shod nails, with blood flowing from his feet, Sergius blinded the serpent that watches for our heel,[^179] and received the crown of martyrdom; therefore by their prayers, O Christ God, nail our flesh to Thy fear,[^180] and have mercy on us!

*Glory, Tone 3:*
How good and how pleasant was the brotherly mind of Thy martyrs, O Lord![^181] For those whom bodily nature made not to be brethren, this brother-loving faith made to be of one mind unto blood; by their prayers, O Christ, have mercy on us!

*Now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion. Tone 3, Podoben: "Great is the power of Thy martyrs" (Ве́лїя мꙋ́ченникъ):*
A sword pierced thy heart, O most pure one, when thou didst behold thy Son upon the Cross, and didst cry aloud: Show me not to be childless, O my Son and my God, Who didst preserve me a Virgin after birthgiving![^182]

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos. Glory, of the Saints, Tone 2. By Anatolius:*
Beholding of old your contests, O martyrs of the whole world, the Church of God is today radiantly adorned, and faithfully celebrates in your memory, wearing the shame that was mockingly laid upon your divine bodies as church adornment; for which ye were deemed worthy of heavenly glory and unending blessedness.

*Now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion:*
When the undefiled Ewe-lamb beheld her Lamb willingly drawn to the slaughter as a man,[^183] weeping she said: Dost Thou now hasten to make me who bare Thee childless, O Christ? What is this that Thou hast done, O Deliverer of all? Yet I hymn and glorify Thine extreme goodness that is beyond mind and word, O Lover of mankind!

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Octoechos, and of the Saints, Ode 3, on 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Unto the saints that are in His earth, the Lord hath made wonderful all His desires in them.*[^184]

*Verse: I foresaw the Lord always before me, for He is at my right hand, that I may not be shaken.*[^185]

*Epistle to the Hebrews, pericope 331 (Heb. 11:33–40).*[^186]

*Alleluia, Tone 2: Behold now, what is so good or what so pleasant, as for brethren to dwell together in unity.*[^187]

*Gospel from Luke, pericope 76.*[^188]

*Communion Verse: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous.*[^189]

*On the same month, on the eighth day. Our Venerable Mother Pelagia.*

*At Lord, I Have Cried, Stichera, Tone 8. Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*

O most marvelous wonder! Having taken courage as a man, a woman trampled the enemy, and quenched the bodily passions by much abstinence; and having fervently taken the Cross of the Lord upon her shoulders, Pelagia cast aside the burden of worldly wealth, and by the teachings of wise Nonnus followed Christ.

A new Thecla thou wast shown unto us;[^190] for having found the teacher Nonnus as another Paul, and having emulated his teachings, O glorious one, having fervently applied thyself unto repentance, and united thyself unto Christ by abstinence, O most blessed and all-honorable Pelagia: therefore unceasingly pray unto Christ, that we may be saved 

Rejoice, all-honorable Pelagia! For kindled with divine zeal, O all-honorable one, thou didst cast aside prodigal pleasure, O most blessed one, and hastening unto Nonnus didst cry aloud: Thou shalt give an answer for my deeds, unless thou signest me with the sign of Christ, O glorious one, clothing me in the clean garments of baptism!

*Glory, Tone 4. By John the Monk:*
Where sin abounded, grace did much more abound,[^191] as Paul teaches; for in prayers and tears, O Pelagia, thou didst dry up the abyss of many transgressions, and at the end brought acceptable repentance unto the Lord, and in this thou prayest for our souls.

*Now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion. Podoben: "As one valiant" (Я́кѡ до́блѧ):*
When the Ewe-lamb that bare Thee beheld Thee upon the Tree as the Lamb and Shepherd, she wept and maternally spoke unto Thee: O beloved Son, how art Thou suspended upon the Tree of the Cross, O long-suffering One? How were Thy hands and feet pierced with nails by the lawless, O Word? And Thou hast poured out Thy blood, O Master!

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos.*

*Troparion, Tone 8:*
In thee, O mother, that which was according to the image was surely saved; for taking up the Cross, thou didst follow Christ, and by deed didst teach to despise the flesh, for it passes away, and to care for the soul, a thing immortal; therefore together with the angels thy spirit rejoices, O venerable Pelagia!

*At Matins, both canons of the Octoechos, and of the Saint on 4. Whose acrostic is: With love I honor the wise Pelagia.*[^192] *Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The Israel of old, having traversed the deep of the Red Sea with dryshod feet, by Moses' crosswise-stretched hands conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.[^193]

*Troparion:*
Having mystically set forth today her divine memory as a sumptuous feast unto the whole world, Pelagia commands all to be nourished with the food of her ascetic struggles.

*Troparion:*
Desire that is beyond all craving, having taken up abode within thy soul, O honorable one, illumined thy mind, and the flame of the divine Spirit enlightened thee and consumed the mire of passions.

*Troparion:*
Having escaped the surging of the sinful sea, O honorable one, unto the quiet haven of Christ Thou didst come, O Pelagia; therefore by thy repentance Thou didst inherit the land of the meek.[^194]

*Theotokion:*
O pure one, the calm of those who keep themselves from earthly things in this life, thou art a firm and strong anchor, and a haven and pilot, ever guiding and saving those who run unto thee.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and foundation![^195]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst soar unto the heavens, O dove of Christ, upon the wings of abstinence, being lifted up by many labors.

*Troparion:*
Having washed away the foul stench of the mire of passions with the water of Christ, O honorable one, Thou wast shown to be sweet myrrh unto Him, O Pelagia.

*Troparion:*
Thy honorable inflamed love and longing toward God truly became a death-bearing weapon unto the enemy.

*Theotokion:*
As morning dew dripping down thy gladness, O pure one, it ever quenches the furnace of passions for those who sing of thee.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having quenched the flame of passions with the dew of repentance, thou didst commit Thy life unto Christ and Thy Savior; and having fled the world for His sake, thou didst dwell alone in the wilderness, having attained the angelic life; therefore also having found an end revered by God with great glory, for thou wast made known unto the ends of the earth, O Pelagia equal to the angels: entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who with love celebrate Thy holy memory!

*Glory, both now and ever, Theotokion:*
Having fallen into manifold temptations from enemies visible and invisible, held fast by the tempest of my numberless transgressions: as unto my fervent intercession and protection, O pure one, I run unto the haven of thy goodness; therefore, O all-pure one, fervently pray unto Him that was incarnate of thee without seed for all thy servants who unceasingly pray unto thee, O most pure Theotokos, ever beseeching Him to grant remission of transgressions unto those who worthily hymn thy glory!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The Ewe-lamb, beholding the Lamb, Shepherd, and Deliverer unrighteously lifted up upon the Cross, weeping bitterly cried aloud: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee, but my womb burns, beholding Thy Crucifixion, which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of compassion, O all-good, sinless God Lord; unto whom let us faithfully cry aloud: Show Thy tender mercy upon us, O Virgin, and grant remission of transgressions unto those who venerate His Sufferings!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Beholding Thee, the Sun of Righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her order, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^196]

*Troparion:*
As most honorable incense, O Pelagia, consumed by the ember of abstinence, thou wast a sweet-smelling whole burnt-offering unto Christ our God, running after the fragrance of His myrrh.[^197]

*Troparion:*
The bodily beauty, O Pelagia, that formerly belonged to the mire of sin, thou didst wholly transform into undefiled comeliness, which Christ Thy Bridegroom desired.[^198]

*Troparion:*
The grace of the Spirit having taken up abode in thy soul, O Pelagia, as a spark of the Word, kindled a high flame of faith and burned up sin.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst bud forth as a rod, O Theotokos, from which grew the spiritual Flower, Christ our God, Who filled all creation with divine fragrance, being Himself inexhaustible and precious myrrh.[^199]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou, my Lord, hast come as a Light into the world, a holy Light turning from the gloom of ignorance those who chant unto Thee with faith![^200]

*Troparion:*
Being anointed with the mystical waters of the Spirit, thou didst blossom in the faith with the fruitful abundance of virtues and the labors of fasting.

*Troparion:*
Thou, O Lord, art the Light of men lying in darkness; Thou didst shine into the heart of the venerable one the radiance of divine understanding.[^201]

*Troparion:*
Thy labors, O honorable Pelagia, sown with tears, were gathered in the heavens with gladness and joy of abundance.[^202]

*Theotokion:*
Truly marvelous was thy grace shown to be, O most pure one, working powers and wonders gloriously throughout all the earth.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side![^203]

*Troparion:*
The unsetting dawn of Christ our God having shone forth in the soul of Pelagia, the prince of darkness was driven away; and the grace of the Spirit and faith in Him took up abode.

*Troparion:*
She that of old was ensnared by the serpent became a snare unto him, holding his power and trampling his deceit by the power of God.

*Troparion:*
Having loved Christ God the Bridegroom, thou didst adorn thyself with virtues, and having beautifully adorned thyself with divine labors, thou wast deemed worthy of His bridal chamber, O Pelagia.

*Theotokion:*
To behold thy ineffable glory, O Theotokos, of old all the divine prophets desired; but in these last times thou wast revealed unto us who love thee, greater than all creation!

*Kontakion, Tone 2. Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Having worn out thy body with fasts, by vigilant prayers thou didst entreat the Creator concerning thy deeds, that thou mightest receive complete remission; which also thou didst manifestly find, O mother, having shown forth the path of repentance.

*Ikos:*
All ye that in this life have defiled yourselves in sins, come with me, and with haste let us emulate the repentance, tears, and ascetic labors of our venerable mother Pelagia, that we may swiftly receive forgiveness from God, even as the blessed one, while yet alive, washed away the defilement of sin, and received from God complete forgiveness, having shown forth the path of repentance!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace, the Abrahamite youths, consumed by love of piety rather than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord![^204]

*Troparion:*
Thou wast truly shown to be an abyss of divine achievements, drowning in it all the strength of the enemy; therefore thou didst sing: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
In the furnace of abstinence having divinely refined body and soul as pure gold, thou didst display the primal beauty more brightly, O honorable Pelagia, through extreme abstinence.

*Troparion:*
Having divinely stripped off by the laver the former garment of passions and of the corrupt old man, thou didst put on the new man, O glorious one, being perfected in Christ.[^205]

*Theotokion:*
He that sits upon the Father's throne, the Word of God, dreadfully and marvelously took up abode in thee, O Theotokos, and exalted our clay, lifting it up in Himself, as He is full of tender mercy.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Daniel, having stretched out his hands, stopped the mouths of the lions in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^206]

*Troparion:*
The rays of thy wonders shine brightly as the sun, and illumine all the radiance and the divine faith shining in thee, and cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, chant and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Bound faithfully unto Christ by an invisible bond, Thou didst cleave unto Him, and remained inseparable by the union of His love; unto Whom Thou wast spiritually joined by faith in Him, O Pelagia, remaining inseparably with Him.

*Troparion:*
Having received true understanding in thy heart, manifestly in the Spirit thou didst forsake all worldly vanity; therefore, O honorable one, thou didst astonish earthborn men by thy sudden conversion, and didst fill the heavens with gladness!

*Theotokion:*
The noetic hosts sing of thy greatness, O most pure Virgin; all the patriarchs and prophets clearly proclaim it with the apostles, as also the choirs of martyrs, and the whole multitude of the venerable: with whom we worship thee.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
A cornerstone was cut out from thee, the unhewn mountain, O Virgin, not by hands: Christ, Who joined together divided natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Theotokos![^207]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst truly desire to behold the beauty of Christ the Lover, for Whose sake Thou didst burn with zeal against the world, and didst dry up the fair flower of the body, O venerable one, and didst hate worldly love.

*Troparion:*
Having laid aside all bodily heaviness by abstinence and labors, O Pelagia, thou didst ascend unto the heavenly mansions, wherein thou enjoyest the beauty beloved of thee, O glorious one.

*Troparion:*
Now remembering all those who honor thee with love, O venerable one, and celebrate this thy yearly memory: pray unto thy Creator and Maker, that we also may be partakers of His glory.

*Theotokion:*
I hymn thy grace, O Virgin, I magnify thy glory, for thou art truly the enlightenment of my soul, and comfort, and swift helper, and salvation, and protection.

*On the same month, on the ninth day. The Holy Apostle James, son of Alphaeus; and our Venerable Father Andronicus, and his wife Athanasia.*

*At Vespers we chant "Blessed is the man", the first antiphon. At Lord, I Have Cried, Stichera of the Apostle, on 8, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*

Thou didst shine forth, O Apostle, light-bearing as the sun by the rays of the Spirit, and didst illumine all the world, O blessed one, with the knowledge of God, and didst drive away the polytheistic gloom by divine preaching; therefore today, celebrating thy most radiant and praiseworthy triumph, we piously glorify thee. *(Three times)*

Thou didst thunder forth unto the universe, O all-wise one, sacred and saving teachings, and didst cleanse all creation of the idolatrous madness, O God-wise one, and didst enlighten the peoples with the radiance of the knowledge of God; and having destroyed the idol shrines by grace, thou didst raise up churches unto the praise of our God. *(Three times)*

Sitting upon the height of the knowledge of God, O James, thou didst receive the grace of the Spirit that appeared unto thee in the likeness of fire; and with a fiery tongue thou didst manifestly burn up all the polytheistic mire of impiety;[^208] therefore we praise thee as an Apostle and speaker of God, celebrating today thy holy festival. *(Twice)*

*Glory, Tone 4, Idiomelon. By Theophanes:*
Having manifestly received the grace of the divine Spirit, thou wast numbered among the most sacred apostolic choir, O James; therefore also when the mighty rushing wind of their coming came upon them,[^209] having enlightened thee with a fiery tongue, thou didst burn up the thorn-bearing heathen godlessness; entreat Christ God, O God-proclaimer, to save our souls!

*Now and ever, Theotokion: "He that for thy sake" (И҆́же тебѐ ра́ди): Entrance, Prokeimenon of the day. Three Readings, and Litiya, as on the 6th day of this month.*[^210]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*

Thou didst receive invincible authority and power over demons, O Apostle, in the Name of Christ, to drive away these princes of darkness; and thou didst pass through the earth, enlightening as the sun, and teaching all the lands, and didst preach the first saving coming of Christ, O glorious one!

*Verse: Their sound has gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.*[^211]

*Sticheron:*
Conforming thyself unto the primal Goodness, and the natural and most divine life, thou wast a good man, O James; being called a son by nature and by divine goodness, through the goodness of thy manners and the purity of thy mind, thou wast shown to be a genuine disciple of Christ.

*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands.*[^212]

*Sticheron:*
Having become a good instrument fashioned by divine guidance, O Apostle James, thou wast entrusted with the calling of the nations, guiding them unto the knowledge of Christ by words and deeds; and thou didst enlighten all to confess the true Godhead of Jesus, the Savior of our souls.

*Glory, Tone 6:*
Faithfully do we celebrate the most honorable day of thy memory, O glorious James, honoring thee not merely as the son of Alphaeus, but as an Apostle of Christ and proclaimer of His ineffable Incarnation; therefore standing ever before the throne with the bodiless hosts, the apostles, and the choirs of martyrs, fervently pray unto our Teacher and God to save us!

*Now and ever, Theotokion: "Thou art a temple and a door" (Хра́мъ и҆ две́рь є҆сѝ):*

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
O holy Apostle James, entreat the merciful God to grant remission of transgressions unto our souls!

*Glory, both now and ever, Resurrection Theotokion. And unto the venerable Andronicus we chant a canon at Compline, Tone 8.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The wonder-working rod of Moses, of old striking crosswise and dividing the sea, drowned the chariot-driving Pharaoh, but saved the fugitive Israel, who journeyed on foot, singing a song unto God![^213]

*Troparion:*
Having pre-cleansed thy soul, O Andronicus, with valiant virtues, and being kindled with the ember of divine love, thou didst turn away from bodily burnings; for thou didst have the divine dew from on high ever bedewing thee.

*Troparion:*
Obeying the blessed commandments, as the patriarch Abraham of old, O father, thou didst leave thy land,[^214] and disregarded thy kinsmen; and having departed far from thy wife and riches, O blessed one, thou didst dwell alone in the wilderness 

*Troparion:*
Thou didst fulfill the commandment of the Lord, having sold all Thy possessions and given to the poor,[^215] through which thou didst find the buying of the precious pearl;[^216] and having received this, thou wast richly enriched with the abundant outpouring of myrrh.

*Theotokion:*
By His own will the Fashioner of Adam is framed in thy womb, and He that holds all things in His palm, O Theotokos, is held by thy hands. O fearful mystery! And by His nod He appears as the Lord of all the saints!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Thou Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens with understanding, and didst found the earth upon the waters: establish me upon the rock of Thy commandments, O Christ, for there is none holy save Thee, O Thou Who alone art the Lover of mankind![^217]

*Troparion:*
Adam was cast out of Eden by the counsel of Eve;[^218] but thou, having believed the counsel of thy spouse, O wise one, wast a servant within, and with her rejoicest evermore, O blessed Andronicus!

*Troparion:*
He that mystically and wisely orders all things well, the Word, guiding earthborn men unto salvation, translated from earth unto heaven your two beloved children, showing you the straight path.

*Troparion:*
Disregarding brief and corruptible love, and leaving it unto those that are upon earth, ye bound yourselves with spiritual love, O blessed ones; therefore now ye dwell where are the mansions of the righteous.

*Theotokion:*
Without condemnation, as higher than the Cherubim and Seraphim, having ineffably given birth unto God and turned the curse into blessing for earthborn men, let her ever be honored with hymns; for she is our hope!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I considered Thy works, and glorified Thy Godhead![^219]

*Troparion:*
The sweats of thy labors dripping from thy flesh, O most sacred Andronicus, were transformed into the fragrance of myrrh.

*Troparion:*
As a high-growing palm tree thou didst blossom in the wilderness, O father, and didst bear as fruit unto God the sweetest labors of thy toil.[^220]

*Troparion:*
With rains of tears thou didst water the desert land, and didst show thy soul to be fruitful by ascetic labors.

*Theotokion:*
The Lord, not departing from the Father's bosom, took up abode in thy most pure womb, O Maiden, and renewed all the world!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Rising early at dawn we cry aloud unto Thee, O Lord: save us, for Thou art our God; besides Thee we know no other![^221]

*Troparion:*
Let us all the faithful hymn Andronicus with songs, and let us glorify his spouse Athanasia!

*Troparion:*
As it is written: Thou gavest not sleep unto thine eyes, nor slumber unto thy eyelids, O Andronicus.[^222]

*Troparion:*
The Lord glorified thee with the wise, O blessed one, accepting thy labors as a truly acceptable sacrifice.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst dreadfully bear One of the Trinity, O Maiden, and didst nourish with milk Him that nourishes the nature of earthborn men!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Grant me a robe of light, O Thou that deckest Thyself with light as with a garment, O most merciful Christ our God![^223]

*Troparion:*
Natural weakness in no wise blunted the resolve which thou didst possess toward the toils of suffering, O venerable one!

*Troparion:*
Having changed thy clothing, thou didst manifestly conceal thy nature; therefore without doubt thou didst accomplish the path of salvation.

*Troparion:*
Having learned of the path whereon thy spouse was journeying, thou didst follow after him, in no wise speaking anything unto him.

*Theotokion:*
We now bring unto thee the voice of Gabriel, O Maiden, crying unto thee: Rejoice, the Lord is with thee, O blessed one![^224]

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having with all thy soul taken the light yoke of the Lord upon thy shoulders,[^225] thou didst follow Him, O God-bearing Andronicus; and having rightly hated worldly tumults, O blessed one, thou didst zealously dwell in the wilderness, where without blemish thou didst finish the ascetic course, O father, uniting thyself unto God; therefore we cry aloud unto thee: Entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Hebrew youths boldly trampled the flame in the furnace, and changed the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages![^226]

*Troparion:*
Him that of old boasted immeasurably, the author of evil, that he held all creation, thou didst cast down as a trifling bird,[^227] having protected thyself with the power of the Spirit and the whole armor of the Cross.

*Troparion:*
Bearing thy cross upon thy shoulders, thou didst follow after the Lord with all thy soul; and thou didst obtain His divine glory, O Andronicus, as a doer of His commandments.

*Troparion:*
Thy boldness toward the Lord pours out the grace of myrrh, for resembling the pouring forth of waters, it is ever shed abroad for the salvation of the faithful.

*Theotokion:*
That He might deify my nature, the All-Perfect One ineffably becomes an infant, and proceeds forth from thee, O pure one, He Who by word brought all things into being, and Who sustains and strengthens them.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Him Whom the angelic hosts hymn, the King of heaven, praise and exalt above all unto all ages![^228]

*Troparion:*
Having watered the furrow of thy soul with streams of tears, thou didst reap a rich reward from wonderworking.[^229]

*Troparion:*
Heal my soul, wounded with transgressions, by the divine medicine of thy prayers, O pleasure of the Lord.

*Trinity Hymn:*
Faithfully let us piously hymn the all-holy Trinity, pre-unoriginate, Who brought all things from non-existence.

*Theotokion:*
Daniel foresaw thee, O Maiden, as the uncut mountain, from which was cut out the stone that crushed the temples of idols.[^230]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Truly do we confess thee as Theotokos, saved through thee, O pure Virgin, with the bodiless choirs magnifying thee.

*Troparion:*
Thou pourest out an inexhaustible grace of myrrh, and faithfully bestowest upon all who have recourse [to thee] deliverance from sicknesses, O father Andronicus.

*Troparion:*
Rejoicing, thou didst pass from temporal things unto the highest, and art glad with the holy fathers; ever remember us who celebrate thy memory, O father.

*Troparion:*
Receive my singing, O couple of the venerable, and grant me remission, to deliver me from transgressions by your prayer unto God.

*Theotokion:*
The three youths of old depicted thy womb, O pure Maiden, which the furnace did not burn; for unburnt didst thou receive the fire of the Godhead.[^231]

*Stichera, Tone 6. Podoben: "Having laid aside all" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*

Having laid aside all worldly torment, and hated wealth, and despised passing pleasure, ye took up the Cross as a divine yoke, and followed Christ, and marvelously settled in His rest; wherein making merry together, remember us who remember you in faith, and who reverently accomplish your sacred and honorable memory with love, O saints!

Greatly desiring the Master with all her soul, she ran after Thee she ran, the ever-memorable couple, united in the yoke of marriage, and broke through all fair things and the unbreakable bond as a spider's web; therefore also did she obtain Thy immortal kingdom. Therefore, O Bountiful One, by their prayers grant us cleansing of transgressions, and show us superior to passions that ever trouble our souls, O Good One!

*Sticheron:*
Ye were bereft of your children, O lovely couple, by a greater ascetic contest, remaining disconsolate in life; ye fulfilled the most valiant voice of Job, crying aloud: The Lord gave, and the Lord again took away![^232] Therefore also ye easily accomplished the work desired by you, and with joy passed unto the most beloved holy places, changing your dwelling, O most sacred couple!

*Glory, both now and ever, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, shoot from the root; rejoice, divine rod of Aaron that budded forth the unfading flower; fruitful paradise, living tree that cleanly blossomed forth from the root of Jesse, O Maiden, nourishing earthborn men with the noetic Bread; rejoice, all-honorable purple robe of the King of all, O Bride of God, radiant adornment, splendor of high priests; rejoice, thou who alone art all-pure![^233]

*Stavrotheotokion. Podoben the same (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
When the undefiled ewe-lamb of old, the blameless Mistress, beheld upon the wood of the Cross her Lamb, as a mother she cried aloud, and wondering cried out: O sweetest Child, what is this new and most glorious sight? How has the ungrateful assembly delivered Thee unto the judgment seat of Pilate, and condemns unto death the Life of all? Yet I hymn Thine ineffable condescension, O Word![^234]

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord", Troparion of the Apostle, twice:*

*Glory, both now and ever, Theotokion: "That which mediated" (Та̀ хода́тайствовавшꙋю):*

*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*
Having caught the nations, O glorious Apostle, and taught the ends of the earth to worship Thee together with the Father and the Spirit, O Christ God; therefore establish Thy Church, and send down Thy blessing upon the faithful, O Thou Who alone art merciful and the Lover of mankind. *(Twice)*

*Glory, both now and ever, Theotokion:*
We have become partakers of the divine nature through thee, O Theotokos, Ever-Virgin; for thou didst give birth unto God incarnate for us. Therefore, as is due, we all piously magnify thee.[^235]

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Go Thou quickly before" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
As a ray Christ, the Sun of Righteousness, sent thee forth to enlighten[^236] to enlighten all the earth, O glorious Apostle James; thou dost illumine with thy divine prayers, and with divine never-waning light dost enlighten all who accomplish thy holy memory with faith. *(Twice)*

*Glory, both now and ever, Theotokion:*
O unashamed hope of those who hope in thee, who alone didst give birth beyond nature in the flesh unto Christ our God; entreat Him together with the holy Apostles, to grant unto the universe cleansing of transgressions, and unto all of us amendment of life before the end.

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Catching noetic fish with the net of divine words, thou didst bring them as firstfruits unto our God; and desiring to be clothed in the wounds of Christ, thou wast shown to be an emulator of His sufferings; therefore having assembled, as is due, we honor thee, O glorious Apostle, and harmoniously cry aloud unto thee: Entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love! *(Twice)*[^237]

*Glory, both now and ever, Theotokion:*
O noetic gate of life, all-pure Theotokos, deliver from afflictions those who have recourse unto thee in faith: that we may glorify thine all-holy childbearing, unto the salvation of our souls.[^238]

*The Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of the 4th Tone. Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
*Their sound has gone out into all the earth. Verse: The heavens declare.*
*Let every breath.*
*Gospel according to John, pericope 67.*[^239]

*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 4. Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ оуслы́шите):*
As an initiate of mysteries and Apostle, and an excellent minister of Christ the only God, O James, proclaimer of God: entreat Him to grant victorious trophies unto the King,[^240] and unto those who celebrate thine all-holy memory, O all-praised one, cleansing of sins.

*Canon of the Theotokos with the Heirmos on 6, and of the Apostle on 8, the acrostic whereof is: "I honor the disciple of Christ, the son of Alphaeus." A composition of Theophanes, Tone 6.*[^241]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Traversing the deep on foot as on dry land, Israel, seeing Pharaoh the pursuer drowned, cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God![^242]

*Troparion:*
Give me thy hand, O God-seer and disciple of Christ, James, as I strive to praise thy venerable festival, and cause light to shine upon my heart by thy prayers.

*Troparion:*
Nourished with the streams of divine wisdom, as a flowing river flowing forth from the paradise of delights, and thou didst truly and manifestly water the face of the earth with streams of piety.[^243]

*Troparion:*
Thou wast numbered with the most sacred choir of Christ's disciples, O James, fulfilling the number of the Twelve; standing with them before the Master, remember us.

*Theotokion:*
The Lord, Who stretched out the heavens and founded the earth, revealed thee unto us, O all-pure Virgin, as an earthly heaven, having passed forth from thee in the flesh.[^244]

*Katavasia: "I will open my mouth" (Ѿве́рзꙋ оу҆ста̀ моя̀):*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.[^245]

*Troparion:*
Manifestly receiving the radiance of the Divine Spirit, thou didst appear as bright morning unto those in darkness, proclaiming unto all the epiphany of the noetic Sun.

*Troparion:*
Thy foot stood in uprightness, O blessed one, dancing divinely before the upright and good King, and traversing the heavenly path.[^246]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst have as a guileless teacher of mysteries, teaching thee the knowledge of truth, the unoriginate Son of God, Who makes all things by His divine authority, O James.

*Theotokion:*
O Mary, hope of all who place their hope in thee, who didst give birth unto the Word incarnate for our sake: deliver me, O all-pure one, from various assaults and afflictions.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Truly having as thy teacher Wisdom, teaching thee things beyond understanding, Thou didst make foolish the Hellenic wisdom, O God-inspired one; and Thou wast a divine luminary unto the nations, guiding the foolish with words of piety. Therefore, delivered through thee from delusion, we worthily hymn thee, and faithfully call thee blessed, O James, speaker of God: Entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love! *(Twice)*[^247]

*Glory, both now and ever, Theotokion:*
O cloud of the noetic Sun, golden lampstand of the divine Light: undefiled, unblemished, all-immaculate Lady, illumine, I pray thee, my darkened soul with the ray of dispassion, blinded by passions; and wash my defiled heart with streams of compunction, and with tears of repentance, and cleanse me from the mire of my deeds, that with love I may cry unto thee: O Theotokos Ever-Virgin, entreat Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto me; for I, thy servant, have thee as my hope.[^248]

...grant remission unto me; for I, thy servant, have thee as my hope.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Christ is my power, God and Lord, the honorable Church divinely sings, crying aloud from a pure understanding, keeping festival in the Lord.[^249]

*Troparion:*
The Lord Himself, the only Holy One, joined thee, O blessed one, unto the honorable choir of disciples, having foreseen the honorable and radiant life of thy conversation.

*Troparion:*
Being filled with the wonders of the Divine Spirit, O proclaimer of God, thou dost work miracles, driving away sicknesses, and didst free men from the malice of evil spirits.[^250]

*Troparion:*
The divine illumination coming upon thy heart, O blessed one, made it radiant, and rendered it receptive of gifts beyond understanding.

*Theotokion:*
By thy divine childbearing, O all-pure Virgin, the angelic ranks and the assemblies of earthborn men were joined together; for thou didst give birth unto Christ, the Prince of peace and Savior of all.[^251]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
With Thy divine light, O Good One, illumine in love, I pray Thee, the souls of those who rise early at dawn unto Thee, to know Thee, O Word of God, the true God, Who callest them from the gloom of sins.[^252]

*Troparion:*
Having become a converser with the noetic Sun, O blessed one, receiving rays from thence: what He is primarily by essence, thou wast secondarily by grace, O glorious one.[^253]

*Troparion:*
Possessing a most pure mind, and having acquired a radiant and pure heart, thou didst behold the incarnate God, incomprehensible unto hearing.[^254]

*Troparion:*
All prophecy and all legislation yield unto thee, O disciple of God, O most rich one; for Him Who was preached of old by them, thou wast vouchsafed to behold as an eyewitness, O blessed one.[^255]

*Theotokion:*
Behold, O Virgin, according to prophecy thou didst have in thy womb the Master and Lord Who is over all, and didst ineffably give birth unto Him, O pure one, remaining incorrupt after childbirth.[^256]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Bring up my life from corruption, O Most Merciful One![^257]

*Troparion:*
Thy feet were beautiful, guilelessly announcing peace, as proclaiming a supernatural peace and a most wise understanding unto those that exist, O all-honorable disciple of Christ.[^258]

*Troparion:*
Thou wast vouchsafed, O James, to understand the mystery, and to proclaim the awesome dispensation of the divine incarnation dispensation of the divine Incarnation, O James, receiving the primary rays from the Most High Himself, O ever-memorable one.

*Troparion:*
Shining worthily with the radiances of the ineffable Light, O foremost Apostle of Christ: fervently pray for those who accomplish thy most honorable memory, O glorious one.

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth unto Him Who rules over all creatures, as the good God, utterly destroy the wounds of my transgressions, O all-pure one, rendering Him Who was born of thee compassionate.

*Kontakion, Tone 2. Podoben: "The firm and God-proclaiming preachers" (Тве́рдыѧ):*
Him who firmly planted the dogmas of wisdom in the souls of the pious, let us all praise with eulogies as the proclaimer of God, James; for he stands before the throne of the Master's glory, and rejoices with all the angels, praying unceasingly for all of us.[^259]

*Ikos:*
As a disciple and friend of Christ, abiding ever with the immaterial choirs, and illuminated by the light of the never-waning bestowal of light, dispel the darkness of my mind, O all-blessed one: that I may hymn thy light-bearing memory, which all creation hymns, keeping festival today in piety; for thou wast the guide and luminary of all the faithful, O James, praying unceasingly for all of us.[^260]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The youths in Babylon feared not the flame of the furnace, but being cast into the midst of the flame, bedewed they sang: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers![^261]

*Troparion:*
Illumined with the radiances and brightnesses of the Spirit, the multitude of the nations names thee the divine seed, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers![^262]

*Troparion:*
Adorned with the wisdom given thee by God, thou didst destroy worldly wisdom, crying in godly wisdom, O Apostle: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Their sound has gone out into all the earth, O Apostle James: for like thunder thou didst announce unto all lands, O most wise one, worthily preaching the incarnate Word.[^263]

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast holier than the holy, O Virgin Mother, having ineffably given birth unto God, Whom hymning unceasingly we cry: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers![^264]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
For the sake of the laws of their fathers, the blessed youths in Babylon, braving danger, mocked the tyrant's decree spat upon the tyrant's senseless decree, and being united together they were not burnt by the fire, but sang a worthy hymn unto the Sovereign: Hymn the Lord, ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages![^265]

*Troparion:*
As a sacred disciple truly instructed in heavenly mysteries, O blessed one, thou didst traverse all the universe, O Apostle, proclaiming loudly the word of the faith of Christ, and teaching with ineffable grace: Hymn the Lord, ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast rich and blessed, vouchsafed every beatitude, having become a converser and co-dweller with the Master; and being truly and excellently enriched unto the end, rejoicing thou didst sing unto the Word: Hymn the Lord, ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Stand before the Savior as an excellent intercessor for the world, O blessed Apostle, asking from on high abundant peace for the Churches, and for those who praise thee in faith remission of transgressions, and salvation for our souls, crying aloud: Hymn the Lord, ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast revealed as the mediatress of salvation for all, O Mother of God, who alone art all-praised, having given birth unto the Savior, God the Word of God, Who delivered us from the ancient curse and granted blessing unto those who cry aloud in faith: Hymn the Lord, ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages![^266]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Weep not for Me, O Mother, beholding in the tomb the Son Whom thou didst conceive in the womb without seed; for I shall arise and be glorified, and as God I shall unceasingly exalt in glory those who magnify thee with faith and love.[^267]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst appear unto the world passing through as lightning, drawing those who sat of old in darkness and the shadow of ignorance unto the never-setting Light of the incarnate Only-Begotten Son, Whose eyewitness and active minister thou wast, O blessed one: Whom we magnify.[^268]

*Troparion:*
Receiving the rushing wind borne from heaven, O blessed one, enriched upon thy tongue inspired with fire, thou burnest up godless worldly wisdom as briars, shining with the luminous rays of Christ's preaching, and dispelling the gloom, O God-blessed one.[^269]

*Troparion:*
Receive, O proclaimer of God, Apostle James, the suppliant song which we offer unto thee, celebrating thy most honorable and radiant memory: deliver thy servants from sins, O foremost Apostle of Christ, fervently praying, that worthily we may magnify thee.

*Theotokion:*
Having received the Word in thy womb without knowing man, thou didst remain incorrupt, O all-pure Virgin, and didst give birth for us unto Emmanuel, Who is God and Man; knowing Him in both natures, we magnify thee.[^270]

*Exapostilarion. Podoben: "In spirit in the sanctuary" (Дх҃омъ во ст҃или́щи):*
Not as the son of Alphaeus, but as a disciple of the Word do we understand thee, O James, glory of the Apostles; for thou didst manifestly receive the divine radiance, and the grace of miracles, to drive away sicknesses from men who accomplish thine all-sacred memory with faith. *(Twice)*[^271]

*Glory, both now and ever, Theotokion:*
As possessing compassion and great mercy, O Virgin Theotokos, look down, O all-blameless one, upon my lowliness, and dispel the tempest of passions and assaults of sorrows, O pure one, and deliver me from the fire of Gehenna by thy prayers.

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 4. Podoben: "As noble" (Я́кѡ до́блѧ):*

With the rod of grace, from the depth of vanity thou didst draw forth men, O wonderfully praised one, submitting unto the nod of the Teacher, O James, Who enlightened thy understanding in all things, and revealed thee, O all-blessed one, as an Apostle and honorable speaker of God of His incomprehensible Godhead. *(Twice)*[^272]

The radiance of the Spirit came down upon thee in the appearance of fire, and made thee, O blessed one, a divine receptacle: quickly driving away the gloom of godlessness, and enlightening the world with the radiance of thy all-wise words, O speaker of mysteries, adornment of the Apostles, blessed eyewitness of Christ.

By the lightnings of preaching having enlightened those who sat in the darkness of ignorance, O glorious one, thou didst show them to be sons of the Master and God through faith; Whose sufferings and death thou didst emulate, and of Whose glory thou wast heir as wise and speaking of God, as a most genuine disciple.[^273]

*Glory, Tone 2:*
Forsaking earthly things, thou didst follow after Christ, and being sealed by the inspiration of the Holy Spirit, thou wast sent by Him unto the lost nations, to turn men unto the light of the knowledge of God, O Apostle James; and having finished the contests of thy divine passion, and of various torments, thou didst commend thy soul unto Christ; entreat Him, O all-blessed one, to grant us great mercy![^274]

*Now and ever, Theotokion: "All my hope" (Всѐ оу҆пова́нїе моѐ):*

*Great Doxology* Great Doxology, and Dismissal.

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Apostle, Odes 3 and 6. Prokeimenon, Tone 8: Their sound has gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world. Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God. Epistle to the Corinthians, pericope 131.[^275] Alleluia, Tone 2: The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord. Gospel according to Luke, pericope 51.[^276] Communion Hymn: Their sound has gone out into all the earth.*[^277]

*On the same month, on the tenth day. The Holy Martyrs Eulampius and Eulampia.*

*At Lord, I Have Cried, Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "As noble" (Я́кѡ до́блѧ):*

Illumined by the Holy Spirit, O all-praised martyrs, ye set yourselves in array against the wiles of the ruler of the world, and having manfully conquered him, by divine assistance ye valiantly triumphed over him; therefore today, celebrating your radiant memory, as is due we honor your sufferings with praises, O most glorious ones![^278]

Mingled with fire and torments, O saints, ye remained unyielding in mind; and as divine lamps, by the warmth of the Spirit ye illumined the hearts of the faithful with grace; therefore every age and generation celebrates your holy memory, singing praises unto the Lord.

The God-wise Eulampius and the all-honorable Eulampia, a divine pair, having shone forth as most bright stars, enlighten creation with the radiances of their ascetic contest and the brightnesses of miracles; celebrating their all-sacred memory today, with gladness we bow down before the shrine of your relics.

*Glory, Tone 4. By John the Monk:*
Brotherly love being commingled with bearing the same name, and purity joined with dispassion, unharmedly did ye preserve your virtuous good-heartedness; for where God is desired, all the world is disregarded. O wonder! The serpent was put to death, and he who spoke unrighteousness against the height, the subterranean one, was cast down by the martyrdom of the siblings Eulampius the wise and Eulampia: unto whom let us cry aloud with spiritual songs[^279] crying aloud: O ye who have well finished the course in Christ, ask peace for the world, and for our souls great mercy![^280]

*Now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion. Podoben: "As noble" (Я́кѡ до́блѧ):*
When the ewe-lamb who gave birth unto Thee beheld Thee upon the wood as Lamb and Shepherd, she wept and cried out to Thee as a mother: O beloved Son, how art Thou suspended upon the wood of the Cross, O Long-suffering One? How were Thy hands and feet nailed by the lawless, O Word? And how hast Thou poured out Thy blood, O Master?[^281]

*At the Aposticha, the Octoechos. Glory of the Martyrs, Tone 4, Idiomelon:*
Illumined by the light of the Trinity, the brother-and-sister pair cast down the cruelty of the torturers; therefore having trampled the flame, they danced and sang: Behold now, what is good or what is pleasant, but for brethren to dwell together?[^282] And looking toward divine glory, they were vouchsafed heavenly glory, ever entreating Christ God on our behalf, that our souls may be saved.

*Now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion. Podoben: "As noble" (Я́кѡ до́блѧ):*
When the ewe-lamb and Thy Mother beheld Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Lord, she was astonished and cried aloud: What is this sight, O beloved Son? Did the unfaithful and lawless assembly repay Thee with this, having enjoyed Thy many wonders? But glory be to Thine ineffable descent, O Master!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received incorruptible crowns from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, they cast down the torturers, and crushed the powerless boldness of demons: by their prayers save our souls![^283]

*At Matins, both Canons of the Octoechos; and the Canon of the Saints on 4, the acrostic whereof is: "I sing of the brother-and-sister pair." A composition of Theophanes, Tone 4.*[^284]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Traversing the Red Sea's deep on foot with unwetted feet, ancient Israel of old by Moses' hands stretched out crosswise conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.[^285]

*Troparion:*
Kindled with the love of Christ, O martyr Eulampius, thou didst cast away worldly darkness; and fervently didst thou draw near in wisdom unto the heavenly, immaterial light, O all-blessed one.

*Love Troparion:*
Thy longing toward God, O all-wise martyr Eulampius, brought to an end the onslaught of tortures; and by abstinence and steadfastness, thou didst truly burn away the demonic battle-arrays, O highly praised one.

Having strengthened the army of martyrs with thy God-proclaiming tongue, thou didst bring unto Christ a divine company, an exceedingly rich haul,[^286] receiving with them unfading crowns, O most rich one.

*Theotokion:*
Having come from on high into thy womb, O most pure one, God truly came forth having become flesh, raising up to Himself the fallen, and by grace binding up those on earth as sheaves,[^287] O all-hymned pure Virgin.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and my refuge and my firm support.[^288]

*Troparia:*
The enemy soul-destroyingly desired to exalt the deceitful decree of delusion; but him hast Thou, O God, cast down into the abyss.[^289]

Kindled with brotherly love, O maiden, thou didst openly run unto the beauty of Christ, O martyr Eulampia, ever worthy of praise.

*Theotokion:*
Having received without seed the divine Word in thy womb, O maiden, inconfusedly beyond all understanding hast thou, O most pure one, given birth unto Him for us.[^290]

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Guiding unto wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^291]
Having patiently traversed the dark clouds of tortures, O sufferers, ye shone forth like the sun through the grace of the Savior; and divinely bound by brotherly love, ye were brightly adorned with like sufferings. Therefore after your repose ye received endless grace from on high to heal infirmities, O pleaders of the Lord: entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto them that with love honor your holy memory.

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
Having fallen into many-entangled tribulations from enemies visible and invisible, held fast by the tempest of mine innumerable sins, unto thy warm protection and my shelter, O pure one, I flee as unto the haven of thy goodness. Therefore, O most pure one, fervently entreat Him Who was incarnate from thee without seed on behalf of all thy servants, who unceasingly pray unto thee, O most pure Theotokos, ever imploring Him to grant remission of sins unto them that worthily sing thy glory.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

The Ewe-lamb, beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross,[^292] cried out weeping, and bitterly lamenting, exclaimed: The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through Thee; but my womb burns, beholding Thy Crucifixion, which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of compassion, O longsuffering Lord, abyss of mercy and inexhaustible fountain: take pity and grant remission of sins unto them that with faith hymn Thy divine Passion.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Beholding Thee, the Sun of Righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her station, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord.[^293]

*Troparia:*
For the sake of Thee, the Creator, O passion-bearers, truly scorning all worldly deceit, ye desired Christ alone, crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord.

Stepping upon the flame of the furnace and trampling it underfoot, O martyrs, ye were divinely enriched with dew from heaven,[^294] crying aloud unto Christ: Glory to Thy power, O Lord.

Illumined by the radiance of the Trinity, the noble pair of martyrs dispelled the darkness of idolatry, crying aloud unto Christ: Glory to Thy power, O Lord.

*Theotokion:*
Having incorruptibly given birth unto Christ, O Virgin Mary, thou didst stay the course of mortal death, joining the generations of earth unto the bodiless ranks, O all-immaculate one.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou, O my Lord, hast come into the world as a light, a holy light turning from the gloom of ignorance those that sing Thy praises in faith.[^295]

*Troparia:*
The assaults of tortures did not shake you, O steadfast ones; for upon the rock of the Savior ye firmly established the feet of your souls.[^296]

Mightily conquering the primeval author of deceit, ye abolished the bloody sacrifices of idols by the outpouring of your blood, O glorious ones.

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast taken away the curse,[^297] having given birth unto Joy Who hath deified earthly beings, bearing God in thy hands, O Mother, Bride of God.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the demonic blood by the blood that for mercy's sake flowed from Thy side.[^298]

*Troparion:*
Your blood hath been shown forth as healing of soul-destroying passions, O passion-bearers,[^299] unto all who in faith have recourse unto the divine and all-radiant shrine, O most blessed ones.

Having lawfully suffered for Christ, O glorious ones, ye were enriched with an incorruptible crown,[^300] truly all-blessed ones, and received the heavenly kingdom and divine delight.

*Theotokion:*
Having found thee amidst thorns as a most pure lily and a flower of the valleys,[^301] O Mother of God, the Bridegroom became flesh in thy womb, O all-immaculate one.

*Kontakion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дв҃а дне́сь):*[^302]
Let us honor the noble martyrs and brethren according to the flesh, the wise Eulampius and Eulampia; for they put to shame the crafty devices of the most lawless by the power of Him Who was crucified. Therefore they were shown forth as the glory and boast of martyrs.

*Ikos:*
Let us the faithful today in sacred songs and hymns praise the pair of passion-bearers, for they cast down the deceit of idols, burned to ashes the fire of polytheism, and put the demons to shame; and they feared not the fury of the torturers, nor were they afraid of sword and fire, nor the onslaught of wild beasts, having gallantly contended well: the glorious Eulampius with the honorable Eulampia and their fellow sufferers, shown forth as the glory and boast of martyrs.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace, the children of Abraham, scorched rather by love of piety than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord.[^303]

*Troparia:*
Enriched with divine understanding, O ever-memorable martyr Eulampius, thou didst truly confound all the torment of the impious, singing: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord.

Having hastened unto the love of Him Who truly fashioned you, O sufferers, ye were divinely united in spirit even as in the flesh, singing: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord.

*Theotokion:*
In a manner marvelously strange, O Virgin, didst thou conceive the Word in thy womb, O most pure one, and bear Him in the flesh, unto those who cry: Blessed art thou among women, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady.[^304]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
