Daniel, stretching forth his hands, stopped the mouths of the lions in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.[^305]

*Troparia:*
Neither the multiplication of tortures, nor fire nor wounds, O all-wise ones, nor boiling cauldrons, nor the edge of the sword could separate you from the love of Christ, O glorious sufferers;[^306] but ye conquered, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

Now in place of timely sufferings, ye have been enriched with the Kingdom of God, O glorious sufferers: Eulampius singing praises together with the honorable Eulampia, the pair of martyrs, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Theotokion:*
As the only one who hast borne God, O unwedded Mother, pure in thy seedless childbearing: make prayer unto Him, O Sovereign Lady, that thy flock may be preserved from the temptation of enemies, as it cries unto Christ: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
A cornerstone was cut out without hands from an uncut mountain, even from thee, O Virgin: Christ, Who hath joined together the separated natures.[^307] Therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Theotokos.

*Troparia:*
By the streams of your blood, O passion-bearing martyrs, ye destroyed the deceit of idols, receiving as the reward of your martyrdom a heavenly habitation and abiding rest.

Lo, instead of darkness, fire, and wounds, O passion-bearers, the ever-radiant light is yours, and spaciousness and joyful refreshment; and ye readily grant the graces of profitable things, O glorious and most rich martyrs.

Having finished the divine course of your honorable contest, ye take up your dwelling in the divine splendors, where are the choirs of angelic ranks, and the multitudes of the righteous, and the assemblies of the martyrs, O ever-memorable ones.

*Theotokion:*
The prophetic predictions have now received their fulfillment, O Virgin Maiden, for thou, O all-pure one, hast borne for us the flesh-bearing God.[^308] Therefore with the angel we cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Sovereign Lady![^309]

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ оу҆слы́шите):*[^310]
Fittingly the harmonious pair of passion-bearers of the same womb, the godly-wise Eulampius and honorable Eulampia, were enriched in their calling by divine foreknowledge; for they illumine creation with the rays of their martyrdom and the beams of their healings.

*Theotokion:*
From thee, O pure maiden, the transcendent God through His great mercy clothed Himself in the compound of human nature, without confusing the properties of the two natures;[^311] but He remained one Son in both natures, even after His birth.

*In the same month, on the 11th day: The Holy Apostle Philip, one of the seven deacons; and our venerable father Theophanes the Confessor, Bishop of Nicaea.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Apostle, Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*[^312]
Numbered among the seven deacons, as one full of wisdom and divine grace, O blessed Philip, thou wast chosen to minister together with Stephen unto the needs of the saints.[^313] Therefore, having seen his murder, by divine command thou didst go up unto Samaria, preaching the Word of God, O glorious one,[^314] and enlighting them that previously sat in darkness, whom through the divine Spirit thou didst make sons of the day, O all-wise one.[^315]

Beholding the divine signs wrought by thy hand, the restoration of sight to the blind, the healing of the paralyzed, and the casting out of unclean spirits from where they dwelt, the people marveled, O Philip, and came unto the saving cleansing, being turned suddenly from unbelief to faith; therefore the divine choir of apostles, hearing thereof, rejoiced that the multitude of Samaria was also brought unto God.[^316]

Being a chariot of the Word, O speaker of divine things, seeing one traveling upon a chariot, asking questions and in perplexity, thou didst overtake the marvelous Candace-official;[^317] and showing him the explanation and revelation, thou didst make known the salvation he desired; for believing, he asked for divine cleansing, which having received, the Fashioner of all made him a divine preacher of God unto Ethiopia, and chief among martyrs.

*Other Stichera of the Venerable Father, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*[^318]
Thou didst shine forth, O Theophanes, in the forms of virtues as a most bright star on the firmament of faith, brightly, O glorious one: driving away with thy rays all heretical dark gloom, and enlightening through thy divine teachings those who with faith and love have recourse unto thee. Therefore we celebrate thy light-bearing feast in love: pray thou with boldness on behalf of our souls.

Having armed thy soul with ascetic labors, thou didst wither up the carnal stirrings, O most sacred father; and thy mind thou didst gloriously make a dwelling of the divine Trinity; and thou wast lifted up whither are the armies of martyrs, the inscrutable beauty, and the assembly of hierarchs, with whom now rejoicing, thou standest in glory before Christ, praying with boldness on behalf of our souls.

With the divine streams of thy tears, O hierarch, thou didst drown all the demonic battle-array as feeble; and by thy constant inclinings toward God and lofty ascents, thou didst cast down to the earth the proud exaltation, and wast borne up unto heavenly and God-befitting habitations, wherein taking up thy dwelling with the angels, thou standest before Christ, praying with boldness on behalf of our souls.

*Glory, and now, Theotokion, in the same tone and melody:*
Having stumbled through evil ways, and being enslaved by crafty deceit, O Bride of God, I, wretched one, flee unto thy most marvelous mercy and warm protection, O most holy maiden: deliver me from the bond of misfortunes and sorrows, and save me, O all-immaculate one, from demonic assaults, that I may glorify thee, sing of thee in love, bow down before thee, and magnify thee, O ever-blessed Theotokos.

*Stavrotheotokion, in the same melody:*
The undefiled Ewe-lamb of old, the immaculate Theotokos, when she beheld her Lamb upon the wood of the Cross,[^319] cried out maternally, and wondering, exclaimed: O sweetest Child, what is this new and glorious sight? How hath the graceless assembly delivered Thee unto the tribunal of Pilate, and condemned Thee unto death, Who art the Life of all?[^320] Yet I hymn Thine ineffable condescension, O Word!

*Troparion of the Apostle, Tone 3:*
O holy Apostle Philip, entreat the merciful God, that He grant remission of transgressions unto our souls.[^321]

*Glory, of the Venerable One, Tone 8:*
Guide of Orthodoxy, teacher of piety and purity, light of the universe, divinely inspired adornment of high priests, O all-wise Theophanes: by thy teachings thou hast enlightened all, O spiritual lyre; entreat Christ God, that our souls may be saved.[^322]

*And now, Theotokion:*
*At Matins, one canon of the Octoechos, and two of the saints. The canon of the apostle, the acrostic whereof is: "I will hymn the divine glory of Philip." By Joseph. Tone 6.*[^323]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

*Heirmos:*
When Israel walked upon the deep as upon dry land on foot, beholding Pharaoh the pursuer drowned, they cried aloud: Let us sing unto God a song of victory![^324]

*Troparia:*
Illumined with the most bright radiances of Christ through communion with the Godhead, O wise one, thou wast shown forth as a second light, holily guiding the faithful into light, O glorious Philip.[^325]

The transcendent Word, having given substance unto all our nature, holily presents thee as a holy disciple, O wise one, delivering the nations from unholy darkness.[^326]

By the laying on of the hands of the holy disciples, thou wast chosen together with Stephen and the rest as a holy deacon, as being full of the Spirit of God, O all-wise Philip.[^327]

*Theotokion:*
He Who brought all things into being out of nothingness had thee as the cause of His Incarnation, O pure Mother of God; therefore as is meet, we the faithful bless thee.[^328]

*Another Canon of the Venerable Father, in the same tone and melody:*
*Troparia:*
Drive away by thy prayers the darkness of my mind, O hierarch of Christ, that I may gloriously hymn thy holy and radiant memory with songs.[^329]

Like a river thou didst rush forth from Zion, O glorious hierarch, and plentifully water with divine streams the assemblies of the faithful, crying aloud: Let us sing unto God a song of victory![^330]

Having illumined thy soul with divine radiances, O Theophanes, thou didst burn away the pleasures of passions with the fire of abstinence, singing in truth: Let us sing a song unto Christ!

*Theotokion:*
With the most bright rays of thy Son, O Theotokos, enlighten my darkened soul, and by thy mediation, O pure one, calm the turmoil of passions.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.[^331]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst make sons of light of those who sat in the darkness of ignorance, O blessed one, declaring the glory of the Word in awesome signs, like unto the high heaven.[^332]

Holily proclaiming Christ Who shone forth from the tribe of Judah, O blessed one, thou didst show Him forth by the light of grace, Whom Moses of old and the prophets foreannounced.[^333]

By thy honey-flowing words thou dost persuade those who were cast away far away through lack of understanding, to flee the soul-destroying venom and receive saving grace, O divinely inspired Philip.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown forth as a mystical lampstand, O Maiden, truly bearing the Lamp that by fair goodness hath enlightened those who were previously held fast in the night of ignorance.[^334]

*Another Canon of the Venerable Father, in the same melody:*
*Troparia:*
Being full of the divine Spirit, O wise one, thou wast shown forth, richly nourishing with the sweetness of piety by the life-giving word the hearts of them that look unto thee.

Flashing forth by the commands of divine radiance, O all-blessed one, thou didst drive away all the heretical mist, singing unto Christ the Giver of Life: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

Having ascended the mountain of divine virtues, O hierarch, and entered into the thick darkness, thou didst become united with God,[^335] and didst receive the completion of understanding, the law of grace.[^336]

*Theotokion:*
Lo, the ladder of Jacob, which he once saw set up, whereon God was established:[^337] the light-bearing pure Theotokos hath been shown forth working actively.

*Kontakion of Theophanes, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking things on high" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щя̀):*[^338]
Having thundered forth the divine Incarnation of Christ, thou didst utterly expose the bodiless enemies, O marvelous Theophanes. Therefore we all cry out unto thee in faith and piety: Pray unceasingly on behalf of us all!

*Sessional Hymn of the Apostle, Tone 8, Podoben: "Guiding unto wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^339]
Shown forth as a driver away of demons, and having given light as a beacon unto them that were in darkness, thou didst make known the Sun that shone forth from the Virgin; and having shattered the temples of idols, thou didst raise up churches to the glory of our God, O blessed one.[^340] Therefore we honor thee, and brightly celebrate thy divine memory, crying out unto thee with one accord: O Apostle Philip, entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto them that with love honor thy holy memory.

*Glory, of the Venerable Father, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Яви́лся є҆сѝ дне́сь):*[^341]
Thou hast appeared unto the Church as another sun, O venerable Theophanes, disciple of the Lord, illuminating her with the lightnings of thy divine words.

*And now, Theotokion:*
Stretching forth thy most pure hands, O Virgin Mother, shelter those who place their trust in thee and cry unto thy Son: Grant unto all Thy mercies, O Christ![^342]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thy Son lifted up upon the wood, O most pure one, torn in thy maternal womb, sorrowfully she sorrowfully cried out: Woe is me! How hast Thou set, O my ageless Light?[^343]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Christ is my power, God and Lord, the honorable Church divinely sings, crying aloud from a pure mind, keeping feast in the Lord.[^344]

*Troparia:*
Beholding thee working miracles and great signs, Simon hypocritically was baptized, O Philip; not fleeing wickedness, but giving himself over unto destruction.[^345]

Beholding thee working many miracles, they that dwelt in the city of Samaria, O preacher of Christ Philip, with rejoicing received the light of divine knowledge.[^346]

An angel of the Lord said unto thee: Go, O Philip, directing the way of the sacred man, that he might know the path of the Incarnation of the Word.[^347]

The most honorable Candace-official, beholding thee instructing him, was baptized by thy hand, O Philip; and shown forth full of the Holy Spirit, he became a divine preacher.[^348]

*Theotokion:*
Thy Son, having become incarnate and born of thy pure blood, O all-immaculate one, made the sons of men sons of God;[^349] therefore do we glorify thee.

*Another Canon of the Venerable Father, in the same melody:*
*Troparia:*
Having cleansed thyself through fasting, O all-wise father Theophanes, thou wast wholly filled with light, wholly capable of containing God, wholly a partaker of the Holy Spirit.

Melting with cold, cast into grievous exile, suffering, O all-blessed one, thou didst endure like a martyr, rejoicing before the Lord in a pure conscience.

Having shone forth in righteous deeds, O glorious one, thou wast shown forth sharpened as the sword of Christ, cutting down heretical subtleties, being strengthened by the divine Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast been shown forth as an all-radiant lampstand, a divine table, a habitation of God, an ark, and a rod that hath blossomed forth Christ the Flower in the world, O Virgin Mother.[^350]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
With Thy divine light, O Good One, illumine, I pray Thee, the souls of them that rise early unto Thee with love, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, the true God, Who callest them from the darkness of sin.[^351]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst not fear the fury of the lawless, but truly walkedst like a lamb amidst wolves, O Philip, turning them unto piety by the word of grace, O most blessed one.[^352]

Emmanuel was led as a lamb to the slaughter, even as the glorious Isaiah says;[^353] which things the honorable Candace-official, being unable to understand, learned from thee, O Philip.[^354]

We know thee as a holy-speaking disciple, one chosen among the seven deacons, a divine foundation of the Church, and a beacon unto all who are in darkness, O Philip.[^355]

*Theotokion:*
Confessing thee in truth from the soul as Theotokos, we are delivered from temptations and misfortunes by thy mediations unto the God and Lord of all, O Virgin full of the grace of God.[^356]

*Another Canon of the Venerable Father, in the same melody:*
*Troparia:*
By thy light-giving flashings, O most sacred father, thou didst destroy the gloomy darkness of the heretics, illuminating with the light of the knowledge of God and enlightening the ends of the world.

The champions of the impious wounded thee, the marvelous one, with grievous stripes; whom the angelic ranks embraced as a hierarch of Christ and a divine martyr.[^357]

Thou wast a luminous guide unto the wandering, O father, a pilot unto those that sail, a firm support and immovable wall unto those shaken by the assaults of bitter afflictions.

*Theotokion:*
The beauty of Jacob, which God loved and chose to make His abode:[^358] the glory of earthly beings and refuge of sinners, hast thou been shown forth, O Theotokos.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Beholding the sea of life surging with the storm of temptations, and fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry aloud unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Greatly Merciful One![^359]

*Troparia:*
Filled with spiritual waters, thou didst go forth like a river of divine Eden, flooding the ends of the earth with a gentle wave and watering the hearts of the faithful, O marvelous one.[^360]

Thou didst fill thy mouth to overflowing with spiritual understanding, O disciple; and having opened the gullet of the crafty one, thou didst snatch away innumerable multitudes of those swallowed up, and didst save them, O apostle.[^361]

The might of the invisible enemy was cast down and utterly perished, when by the power of the Spirit thou, O Philip, didst preach Christ Who was crucified and calls the nations.[^362]

*Theotokion:*
Apart from natural laws did thy holy and most pure Mother bear Thee, O Christ, the Fashioner of creation; and beyond nature she truly remained an undefiled virgin.[^363]

*Another Canon of the Venerable Father, in the same melody:*
*Troparion:*
Being like the sun, O most blessed one, thou didst send forth divinely radiant rays praising the martyrs' sufferings, stripes and wounds, and marvelous exploits, O hierarch of Christ.[^364]

*Troparia:*
Praising the most pure Sovereign Lady in purity with thy tongue, O divinely wise one, thou didst chant radiant words, anointing the assemblies of the faithful and the hearts of the wise.

By the inscription of thy most honorable and pure face, O wise one, the writings of the godless were blotted out, together with their god-detested and ungodly decrees, while thou didst proclaim that the icon of Christ is to be venerated.[^365]

*Theotokion:*
God, Who by His will constituted and upholds all things, O most pure one, is borne in the circumscription of the body upon thine embrace, though in His divine essence He is uncircumscribable.[^366]

*Kontakion of the Apostle, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йся):*
The marvelous fisher of the nations and confidant of Christ's disciples, the pre-chosen of the apostles, Philip, today gives unto the world the wealth of healings, and shelters from afflictions them that praise him.[^367] Therefore with one accord we cry out unto him: Save all by thy prayers, O apostle!

*Ikos:*
Having declared the glory of God like the heavens, O eyewitness,[^368] thou didst draw distant nations unto faith, that they might be near unto God; therefore, like Moses the lawgiver, thou dost enrich thy flock, O most praised one. He of old parted the sea and led the people across, and afterwards nourished them in the wilderness with bread from heaven;[^369] but thou, having delivered the desiring creature from the cruel sweet-poison of unbelief, didst lead them on high unto the supernatural heavenly realms, as they cry aloud: Save all by thy prayers, O apostle!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The angel indeed made the furnace produce dew for the venerable youths, but the command of God, consuming the Chaldeans, persuaded the tormentor to cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^370]

*Troparia:*
The most divine One set thee forth as a steed, troubling the sea of the nations, O Philip, shattering the heads of the enemies, and singing unto Him in song: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^371]

He revealed the mystery hidden before all ages; and having become a speaker of mysteries, O wise Philip, thou didst instruct nations and peoples, and cleanse them from their abominations.[^372]

Thou wast shown forth as the greatest star, O glorious Philip, and the adornment of Tralles, wherein thy precious body lies, working most glorious miracles, O wise one, for the enlightenment of them that piously bless thee.[^373]

*Theotokion:*
We name thee the Birthgiver of God, O most pure Virgin Mary, being saved by thee day and night from the harmful assaults of the enemy, and from all temptations and defilements of soul.

*Another Heirmos:*
The youths in Babylon feared not the furnace flame, but cast into the midst of the flame, being bedewed, they sang: Blessed art Thou, O Lord, the God of our fathers![^374]

*Troparia:*
Rightly dividing the word of truth, thou didst endure the testing of wounds; and in exile, O blessed hierarch Theophanes, thou didst sing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord, the God of our fathers![^375]

The streams of thy dogmas drowned the clamors of the impious who cry not aloud unto Christ our God, O wise one: Blessed art Thou, O Lord, the God of our fathers!

The rivers of thy honey-flowing words, O Theophanes, filled the souls of men with divine gladness, as they hymn in songs God Who is supremely glorified.

*Theotokion:*
In thee, O Virgin Mother, a great mystery was accomplished: the Son of God, having become incarnate from thy womb, hath now appeared; unto Whom we sing, rejoicing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Out of the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the venerable youths, and by water Thou didst consume the sacrifice of the righteous one; for Thou, O Christ, makest all things by Thine only willing: Thee do we exalt supremely unto all ages![^376]

*Troparia:*
Having seasoned them with the divine word as with salt, O all-blessed one, thou didst cleanse away the foul stench of deception and heal the hearts of men to honor and glorify the Trinity unto the ages.[^377]

The feet of the lame were made firm by thy word, O wise one, while the abominable and unclean demons wailed, unable to endure the grace given unto thee.[^378]

They that dwelt of old in Samaria received from thee the ray of grace, O Philip, beholding thee work many miracles by the power of the Spirit, the fountain of grace.[^379]

*Theotokion:*
Strengthen my soul that totters through the malice of the adversary, O pure Virgin, having received Him Who moves all things by His divine regard, whilst thou thyself art unmoved.

*Another Heirmos:*
Courageously braving danger for the ancestral laws, the blessed youths in Babylon spat upon the senseless decree of the reigning king; and joined together in the fire wherein they were not consumed worthily sang a hymn unto Him Who holds dominion: Sing unto the Lord, ye works, and exalt Him supremely unto all ages![^380]

*Troparia:*
Truly bearing the ancestral law, O all-blessed one, thou didst spit upon the senseless decree of the most lawless reigning emperor, who destroyed them that piously kiss the depiction of the Lord's form, which He took upon Himself when He appeared in the world from the Virgin Maiden, Whom we exalt supremely unto the ages.[^381]

A divine mind, a most sweet harp of the All-Holy Spirit, a melodiously singing nightingale, the summit of the faithful, a pillar and foundation of Orthodoxy, O wise one, a firm bulwark of the Church, the praise of monastics, O Theophanes, the glory and strength of the fathers wast thou shown forth, hymning Christ unto the ages.[^382]

Thou wast shown forth as an alabaster vessel of the All-Holy Spirit's myrrh, O father, by the outpouring of thy myrrh-breathing and wise teachings destroying the most foul-smelling corruption of deception; and by thy character thou makest fragrant the honorable Church, crying unceasingly: With songs we exalt Thee supremely, O Christ, unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*
The fertile mountain of God, which forefather David prefigured of old chanting prophetically, hast thou been shown forth, O Virgin; from which was cut out the cornerstone Christ, and He shattered all the idolaters' might.[^383] Him do we unceasingly hymn and exalt supremely unto the ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
It is not possible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-immaculate one, the Word Incarnate became visible unto mortals: Whom magnifying together with the heavenly hosts, we bless thee.[^384]

*Troparia:*
Beholding of old the things hoped for by thee, and receiving unshakable divine authority, whereof thou didst instruct men, O divinely speaking Philip: exult and make merry, delighting in divine enlightenment and consolation.

Like a light, like a star, like a great sun, thou didst encompass the universe with radiant beams, heralding the divine preaching; and having come to Tralles, a city of Asia, whereof thou wast leader, O blessed one, therein thou didst fall asleep.[^385]

Thou didst herald the Cross, death, and resurrection of the Deliverer unto those near and far off, having become a star-studded heaven flashing with divine splendors, O Apostle of Christ Philip, worthy of marvel.[^386]

The earth covered thy much-suffering body that pours forth rays of healings; but heaven while heaven hath thy holy spirit rejoicing with the apostles; together with them remember us also, O wise one, who praise thee.

*Theotokion:*
Every breath brings unto thee a thankful voice, crying joyfully and saying: Rejoice, fiery throne of the Most High; rejoice, spacious tabernacle of God; rejoice, O pure one, comely crown of the apostles![^387]

*Another Heirmos:*
Inconceivable is the birth of the seedless conception, incorrupt the fruit of the unwedded mother; for the birth of God renews natures. Therefore do all generations Orthodoxly magnify thee as the Mother unwedded of God.[^388]

*Troparia:*
The manslayer, unable to bear seeing thee submissive unto the Church of Christ, stretched forth temptations against thee; nevertheless thou didst remain unbending, manfully resisting the impious emperors with valiant courage, persuading them to bow down before the icon of Christ, O Theophanes.[^389]

Adorned with the ineffable beauty of the Trinity, O divinely wise one, and patterned with splendors, thou shinest; and joyfully, O richly endowed one, thou truly standest about the throne of the Creator and God, where the voice of them that keep feast resounds, O God-bearing father Theophanes.[^390]

Thou hast worthily received the honors of thy struggles, and as a crown-bearer now in heaven thou rejoicest with the angels, where is the assembly of the prophets, the apostles, the innumerable hosts of martyrs in truth, and the choirs of patriarchs: remember us, O father Theophanes!

*Theotokion:*
Subjecting the barbarians in wars unto faithful kings, grant victories, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, blessed and supremely glorified, pure unwedded Mother, sheltering us from every injury of the enemy: that chanting unto thy Son, we may magnify thee.

*Exapostilarion of the Apostle, Tone 3, Podoben: "O Thou Who hast adorned heaven with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*
O Apostle of Christ, into all the earth went forth thy proclamation; thou didst preach God unto us:[^391] Him entreat thou on our behalf, that the spiritual light may shine forth upon us.

*Glory, of the Venerable Father, Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Же́ны оу҆слы́шите):*
As a divine light, O Theophanes, wast thou shown forth unto the Church, illuminating her with the brightness of thy songs and with the light of thy face, adorned as thou wast bearing the marks of Christ upon it:[^392] unto Whom cease not to pray on behalf of thy flock, O father!

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*

The robe granted me of old by the font of baptism have I defiled with passionate wanderings; and thinking upon the hour of the divine and truly dread judgment, I, wretched one, am terrified: stand by me then, O all-immaculate Virgin, and deliver me from the fearful condemnation![^393]

*At the Liturgy, the Prokeimenon of the Apostle, Tone 8:* His sound is gone out into all the earth, and his words unto the ends of the world.[^394] *Verse:* The heavens declare the glory of God.[^395] *Epistle: Acts, Pericope 20 [Acts 8:26–39]. Alleluia, Tone 1:* The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord [LXX Ps. 88:6]. *Gospel from Luke, Pericope 50 [Lk. 10:1–15]. Communion Hymn:* His sound is gone out into all the earth [LXX Ps. 18:5].

*Be it known: that after the 11th day of this month, on the coming Sunday, we celebrate the memory of the Holy Fathers who assembled at the Seventh Ecumenical Council in Nicaea for the second time, the three hundred and sixty-five Holy Fathers,[^396] for the deposition and destruction of the ungodly teachings of the Christ-hating and Christian-slandering Iconoclasts, Copronymus,[^397] and those who held like mindedness with him, the wretched bishops and unconsecrated hierarchs, and of their godless and defiled pseudo-council.[^398]*

*And if the present 11th day falls on a Sunday, the service of the Holy Fathers of the Seventh Council is sung on that same day.*

*If on Wednesday, their service is sung on the preceding Sunday; but if on Thursday, their service is sung on the coming Sunday.[^399]*

*The service of the saint of the day is omitted, and is sung when the Ecclesiarch shall determine.*

*On Saturday at Small Vespers, the Sunday stichera and Theotokia according to custom.*

*At Great Vespers, at Lord, I Have Cried, stichera of the Octoechos: three of the Resurrection, and one Anatolian.*

*And of the Holy Fathers on 6, Tone 6, Podoben: "Despaired of" (Ѿча́яннаѧ):*

The honorable councils of the fathers, convened at diverse times, did Germanus the New Patriarch very well gather together into one by one and the same canon, writing them down,[^400] and likewise holding fast their dogmas; who also presents these watchful intercessors for salvation unto the Lord, and unto the flock together with its pastors.

The scripture of the Law appointed the venerable week for the Hebrew children, as they held fast unto shadows and served therein;[^401] which week the fathers, assembling at the Seventh Council by the nod of God Who in six days finished all this universe and blessed the seventh,[^402] made yet more honorable, setting forth the definition of faith.[^403]

Unto the Trinity, the cause of the world's existence out of all things, ye clearly yielded first place, O thrice-blessed fathers: for having laid down three and four councils by a most mystical word, ye were shown forth as vindicators of the Orthodox word; four being the elements, ye also elucidated the Trinity That created them and fashioned the world.[^404]

The ministry of the Prophet Elisha was indeed powerful enough to breathe life once into the prostrate son of the Shunammite woman; nevertheless he turned seven times and bowed down over him,[^405] foreannouncing as a seer your seven councils, whereby ye raised up the slaying of the divine Word, putting to death Arius and his fellows.[^406]

Who tore Thy garment, O Savior? Arius, Thou saidst: who severed the Trinity's equally honored origin into divisions; he denied that Thou art One of the Trinity; he also teaches Nestorius not to call her Theotokos.[^407] But the Council which was in Nicaea proclaimed Thee the Son of God, O Lord, enthroned together with the Father and the Spirit.[^408]

*Glory, Tone 6:* Let us today praise the mystical trumpets of the Spirit, the God-bearing fathers, who chanted in the midst of the Church a harmonious song of theology: one immutable Trinity, one essence and Godhead; the overthrowers of Arius and champions of the Orthodox: who ever pray unto the Lord that our souls may be saved!

*Now and ever, the first Theotokion of the tone.*

*After the Entrance, the Prokeimenon of the day, and three readings.*

*The Reading from Genesis [Gen. 14:14–20]:*
Having heard that Lot his brother's son was taken captive, Abram numbered his own household servants, three hundred and eighteen,[^409] and pursued after them unto Dan. And he fell upon them by night, he and his servants with him with him, and smote them, and pursued them unto Hobah, which is on the left hand of Damascus. And he brought back all the cavalry of Sodom, and brought back Lot his brother's son, and all his goods, and the women, and the people. And the king of Sodom went out to meet him after his return from the slaughter of Chedorlaomer and of the kings that were with him, at the valley of Shaveh, which is the King's Dale. And Melchizedek king of Salem brought forth bread and wine: and he was the priest of the Most High God. And he blessed Abram, and said: Blessed be Abram of the Most High God, Who created heaven and earth: and blessed be the Most High God, Who hath delivered thine enemies into thy hand.[^410]

*The Reading from Deuteronomy [Deut. 1:8–17]:*
In those days, Moses said unto the sons of Israel: Behold, I have delivered the land before your face: go in and possess the land which the Lord sware unto your fathers, Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob, to give unto them and to their seed after them. And I spake unto you at that time, saying: I am not able to lead you alone. The Lord your God hath multiplied you, and, behold, ye are this day as the stars of heaven for multitude. May the Lord God of your fathers add unto you a thousand times more than ye are, and bless you, as He hath said unto you! And I took from among you wise men, and known, and prudent, and made them rulers over you, captains over thousands, and captains over hundreds, and captains over fifties, and captains over tens, and officers among your judges. And I charged your judges at that time, saying: Hear the causes between your brethren, and judge righteously between a man and his brother, and the stranger that is with him. Ye shall not respect persons in judgment; thou shalt judge the small as well as the great; thou shalt not be afraid of the face of man; for the judgment is God's.[^411]

*The Reading from Deuteronomy [Deut. 10:14–21]:*
In those days, Moses said unto the sons of Israel: Behold, the heaven and the heaven of heavens is the Lord's thy God, the earth also, with all that therein is. Only the Lord had a delight in thy fathers to love them, and He chose their seed after them, even you above all nations, as it is this day. Circumcise therefore the hardness of your heart, and be no more stiff-necked. For the Lord our God, He is God of gods, and Lord of lords, a great God, a mighty, and a terrible, Who regards not persons, nor takes reward. He executes the judgment of the stranger, the fatherless, and the widow and loves the stranger, in giving him food and raiment. Love ye therefore the stranger: for ye were strangers in the land of Egypt. Thou shalt fear the Lord thy God; Him shalt thou serve, and to Him shalt thou cleave, and swear by His name. He is thy praise, and He is thy God, That hath done for thee these great and glorious things, which thine eyes have seen.[^412]

*At the Litiya, the sticheron of the temple.*

*Glory, Tone 3:* Ye became sure guardians of apostolic traditions, O holy fathers; for having Orthodoxly taught the consubstantiality of the Holy Trinity, ye conciliary cast down the blasphemy of Arius; together with him having exposed Macedonius the Spirit-fighter, ye condemned Nestorius, Eutyches and Dioscorus, and Sabellius, and Severus the Acephalite.[^413] From whose deception entreat ye that we may be delivered, praying that our life may be preserved undefiled in the faith!

*Now and ever, Theotokion: Without seed from the divine Spirit...*

*At the Aposticha, stichera of the Octoechos.*

*Glory, of the Fathers, Tone 4:* The suppliant memory of the God-bearing fathers who assembled from the whole universe in the radiant city of Nicaea, the assemblies of the Orthodox, do we faithfully celebrate today in piety. For they with pious wisdom cast down the godless decree of cruel Arius, and conciliary banished him from the Catholic Church; and they clearly taught all to confess the Son of God as consubstantial and co-eternal, existing before the ages, setting this forth with certainty and piety in the Symbol of Faith.[^414] Therefore, following their divine commandments, believing with certainty, we worship the Son together with the Father and the All-Holy Spirit in one Godhead, the Consubstantial Trinity!

*Now and ever, Theotokion: Look down upon the supplications...*

*At the Blessing of Loaves, the Troparion: O Birthgiver of God and Virgin: twice; and of the Fathers, Tone 8:*
Supremely glorified art Thou, O Christ our God, Who hast established our fathers as beacons upon the earth, and through them hast guided us all unto the true faith: O Supremely Compassionate One, glory be to Thee![^415]

*At Matins, at God is the Lord, the Troparion of the Resurrection twice. Glory, of the Fathers; and now and ever, Theotokion: Thou Who for our sake wast born...*

*The Resurrection canon with the Heirmos on 4; the Cross-Resurrection canon on 2; of the Theotokos on 2; and of the Saints on 6. The acrostic whereof is: "I sing the seventh assembly of the blessed." By Germanus. Tone 8.*

*Ode 1, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
The rod of Moses, working a wonder of old, striking crosswise and parting the sea, drowned Pharaoh the chariot-driver, and saved Israel the fugitive, who marched on foot, chanting a song unto God.[^416]

*Troparia:*
Unto me who desire now to hymn in songs the seventh assembly, the gathering of the sevenfold gifts, grant me the Paraclete, O Lord, Who made them wise in tongues of fire and silenced all blasphemous babbling of tongues![^417]

The number seven hath been most venerable from the beginning: for as the archetype of all divine creation, there was the all-perfect resting on the seventh day; and now there is the end of all heresies in the council equal in number.[^418]

Having conquered God-fighting Arius in Nicaea of old with the pastoral staff, the choir of fathers guided the Church to walk in Orthodox teachings; and now in this same place, as a champion, it hath put to shame the iconoclasts.[^419]

*Theotokion:*
Even as the fathers piously instructed, confessing faithfully that the virginal womb without pangs gave birth in the flesh unto the Bodiless One, we bow down before Him, depicting His image upon icons and honorably kissing it.[^420]

*Katavasia: I shall open my mouth...*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens with understanding, and didst found the earth upon the waters: establish me, O Christ, upon the rock of Thy commandments; for there is none holy save Thee, O Thou only Lover of mankind![^421]

*Troparia:*
Mystically instructed by Christ that His indestructible Church shall not be shaken,[^422] the divine chief pastors cast out from among the pious those who sought to shake her, as members of Antichrist.

The assembly of the fathers cleanses away the turbid and muddy streams, having drawn from the fountains of salvation;[^423] and with the streams of teachings it satisfies the thirsting people of Christ.

Against those who hate the truth and slander Christians, the seventh assembly of lovers of Christ took place in the radiant city of Nicaea, whereof Irene and Constantine were the protectors.[^424]

*Theotokion:*
Let the impious one be clothed in sackcloth,[^425] who reveres not the honorable icon of the Birthgiver of God, and proclaims her not as having given birth unto Christ in a divine-human manner: and let him be sent into the fire, to burn unconsumed!

*Kontakion and Ikos of the Resurrection. Then the Sessional Hymn of the Fathers, Tone 4, Podoben Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Most radiant beacons of the truth of Christ wast thou shown forth unto the world upon earth, O truly most blessed fathers, inspired of God; having dried up the heresies of blasphemous babblings, and extinguished the flame-stirring confusions of the heterodox.[^426] Therefore, as hierarchs of Christ, pray that we may be saved!

*Twice. Glory, and now and ever, Theotokion:*
Speedily anticipate us, O Virgin Mother pure; snatch us away from enemies that blaspheme against thee; shatter all the babblings of heresies; destroy their assaults by thy might: that they may know that thou art the only Mother of God, saving the congregation of the Orthodox by thy prayers!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art also my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who leaving not the bosom of the Father, didst visit our poverty. Therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry aloud unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind![^427]

*Troparia:*
Having the voice of God as an arrow and sword, the most divinely inspired fathers together slaughter all those of evil belief who, under the semblance of the Cross, honor not the depictions of Christ, of the Birthgiver of God, and of all the saints.[^428]

Even as by seven trumpets the walls of Jericho fell at the seventh circuit,[^429] so by these seven councils the entire horde that exalts itself against God was cast down into the abyss at the seventh assembly of the melodious trumpets of the Spirit.

Displaying youthful vigor and inflamed with divine zeal, the multitude of fathers slew the shameful priests like Nicanor;[^430] therefore with boldness it taught all affectionately to bow down before the icon of Christ.

*Theotokion:*
Thou art my hope, O all-immaculate one, thou art my song, thou art my haven, thou art my restoration: who unwedded didst give birth unto God the Word, co-eternal with the Father. Therefore do I bow down before thine icon without doubt, being strengthened by thy might.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy countenance, O unwaning Light, and an alien darkness hath covered me, wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my ways unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee![^431]

*Troparia:*
Having examined with exalted contemplation, the honorable fathers delivered the iconoclasts unto anathema,[^432] as those who held alien thoughts; and honor unto the icon of Christ they commanded to be fittingly rendered.

Now is the time of rejoicing, now hath the day of salvation appeared: let us rejoice, therefore, and joyfully cry aloud unto Christ: Grant us Thy peace, by the prayers of the fathers of the Seventh Council,[^433] O Lover of mankind!

*Theotokion:*
For the sake of the tender compassion of His mercy, the Son of God was born unwedded of the Virgin: bearing what was alien as His own, and by this form He is seen voluntarily circumscribed,[^434] Who according to His essence is uncircumscribed.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the abyss of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou give ear unto me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparia:*
Let the mountains drop down sweetness and rejoicing;[^435] for the heretical multitude is driven out, which poured forth bitter venom—the rejection of the holy icons.

Let heaven and earth together celebrate the majesty of the daughter of God;[^436] for she is magnified, casting away those that belittle her.

*Theotokion:*
The maternal Son, Who before with the Father was motherless, without a father was born in a manner befitting God, and hath regenerated me: therefore her that bare Him together with Him that was born, figuratively do I embrace.

*Kontakion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Disdaining the image made by hands" (Рꙋкопи́саннагѡ):*
The Son Who shone forth unutterably from the Father was born of a woman, twofold in nature; Whom seeing, we reject not the depiction of His form, but devoutly portraying this, we honor it faithfully; and therefore the Church, holding fast the true faith, reverently kisses the icon of Christ’s becoming man.[^437]

*Ikos:*
The all-bountiful God, willing ever to raise us up unto the most perfect remembrance of His becoming man, delivered this means unto men: to depict His form in the colors of honorable icons; that seeing these before our eyes, we may believe what we have heard by the word, clearly understanding the deed and the name, the portrayal and the sufferings of the holy men, and Christ the Crown-giver bestowing crowns upon the holy sufferers and martyrs; for the sake of which the Church, holding fast the true faith yet more clearly, reverently kisses the icon of Christ’s becoming man.[^438]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
At God’s condescension, the fire was ashamed in Babylon of old; therefore the children in the furnace, dancing with joyful foot as in a meadow of flowers, sang aloud:

...sang aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
The heresiarchs are vanquished by the dogmas of the God-speaking men, who rightly depict the honor of images as passing unto the prototypes,[^439] even as the Great Basil said:[^440] Blessed is the God of our fathers!

The temples are beautified, adorned with radiant icons this day; therefore they unite peace in the churches unto Him Who in beauty is fairer than all sons of men,[^441] as they sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

The Light hath shone forth, darkness is far removed, the impious are driven away. Therefore all things are filled with light, and with rejoicing they sing and say unto Christ the Giver of light: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
O all-immaculate Lady, only hope of salvation for all, who in fearful manner didst give birth unto Christ the King of kings: bearing Him as an infant in thine arms, thou art co-worshipped together in depiction,[^442] even as the fathers say.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Sevenfold did the Chaldean tyrant frantically kindle the furnace for the godly; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried aloud unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, sing praises; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages![^443]

*Troparia:*
Most excellently doth the assembly of fathers legislate for the godly the due veneration and erection of the sacred icon of Christ; and as lovers of piety and as children of their good obedience, we keep their yearly memory, and with love we reverently kiss the image of Christ.

Sevenfold did the proud-minded bow down before the humble, and cut off the strivings of those who love virtue, wherewith they slandered their divine deeds; but the fathers of the Seventh Council who assembled in Nicaea utterly tore down their pride sevenfold.[^444]

The shafts of infants and the fragments of those of youthful mind become wounds and stripes unto those who grow old in malice;[^445] but by divine power the many tongues of those who speak blasphemies against the height have grown feeble: for who among the godly knows not the Father, the Son, and the Spirit as one God, the Cause of all good and the One?[^446]

*Theotokion:*
The Creator is fashioned as an infant of His own will for mercy’s sake, from thy pure blood, preserving thee all-pure and most cleansed after childbirth, and cleansing the defiled image. Therefore upon the icons He is portrayed together with thee, having become man in nature, while remaining God being God by nature.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
At this the heaven was appalled, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God hath appeared in the flesh unto men, and that thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the hierarchies of angels and men magnify thee, the Birthgiver of God![^447]

*Troparia:*
Great in might and counsel, upholding all things, O incomprehensible only God, most sovereign Supreme Ruler, King of all: establish the Church, preserving her in Thy true faith, by the supplications of the all-glorious fathers who magnify Thy glory!

Of great honors were ye accounted worthy upon earth, O godly-minded holy ones, for ye lovingly revered the depiction of Christ; and now, having laid aside the shadow and the veil of the flesh, face-to-face ye behold Him in Person, being accounted worthy of greater things.[^448]

May we be chastised by the entire host of barbarian savagery which Thou hast permitted to come upon us; do Thou Thyself shatter their onslaught and audacity, and fight alongside the King who hopes in Thee, O Creator of all,[^449] through the bold prayers of the holy fathers whose memory we celebrate!

*Theotokion:*
To comprehend the mystery of thy dread childbirth no human mind is able, nor even the lofty angelic mind: for beyond nature didst thou give birth unto God incarnate. Therefore knowing thee to be the Birthgiver of God, and portraying thee together with Him, we magnify thee!

*Resurrectional Exapostilarion. Glory... of the Fathers, Tone 2, Podoben: "In the Spirit in the sanctuary" (Дх҃омъ во ст҃или́щи):*
O stormless fathers, who assembled at the Seventh Council: offer earnest prayer unto the Trinity, that we who sing of your divine assembly may be delivered from every heresy and from eternal judgment, and may obtain the heavenly kingdom!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
By the supplications of Thine all-good Mother, O Lord, and of the fathers who assembled at the seven councils, establish the Church and strengthen the faith, and make us all partakers of the heavenly kingdom when Thou shalt come upon earth to judge all creation![^450]

*Note: The Theotokion of the Resurrectional Exapostilarion is not said, but that of the Fathers.*

*At the Praises, the Resurrection stichera, 4; and of the Fathers, 4, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*
Having gathered all the spiritual skill, and examined through the Divine Spirit the heavenly and honorable Symbol of Faith,[^451] the revered fathers divinely inscribed and sketched inscribed: wherein most clearly they teach that the Word is co-unoriginate with Him that begat Him,[^452] and truly consubstantial, following manifestly the apostolic teaching, the all-glorious and most rich, truly godly-minded ones. *(Twice)*

*Verse:* Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers, and praised and glorified is Thy name unto the ages.

Having received all the spiritual radiance of the Holy Spirit, the most supernatural blessing, in brief words and with much understanding they divinely-inspiredly proclaimed, as preachers of Christ, blessed defenders of the evangelical doctrines and of the pious traditions, having manifestly received the revelation thereof from on high; and being enlightened, they set forth the God-taught faith.

*Verse:* Gather together unto Him His venerable ones.

Having gathered all pastoral skill, and having now stirred up most righteous indignation, they avengingly drove away the heavy and destructive wolves, casting them out from the fulness of the Church by the sling of the Spirit, as fallen unto death and as having fallen incurably sick, the divine pastors, as truest servants of Christ and most sacred initiates of the divine proclamation.

*Glory... Tone 8 (by George of Nicomedia):*
The choir of holy fathers, having come together from the ends of the inhabited earth, taught the one essence and nature of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, and clearly handed down the mystery of theology unto the Church: whom praising in faith, let us call blessed, saying: O divine host, God-speaking weapons of the camp of the Lord, far-shining stars of the spiritual firmament, unconquerable pillars of the mystical Zion, fragrant flowers of paradise, all-golden mouths of the Word, boast of Nicaea, adornment of the inhabited earth: pray ye earnestly for our souls!

*Both now:*
Thou art most blessed, O Virgin Birthgiver of God:

*Great Doxology. And dismissal. And the customary exit into the narthex. Glory, and now and ever, Gospel sticheron. At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Resurrection, on 6; and of the Fathers, Ode 3, on 4. After the Entrance, the troparion of the Resurrection, and of the Fathers; then the kontakion of the Resurrection; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... of the Birthgiver of God. Prokeimenon, first of the Resurrection; then the Song of the Fathers, Tone 4: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers, and praised and glorified is Thy name unto the ages. Verse: For Thou art righteous in all in all that Thou hast done for us.[^453]*
*Epistle of the day; and of the Fathers: to the Hebrews, section 334 [Heb. 13:7–16].[^454]*
*Alleluia of the Tone; and of the Fathers, Tone 1: The God of gods, the Lord, hath spoken... Verse: Gather together unto Him His venerable ones [Ps. 49:1, 5].*
*Gospel of the day; and of the Fathers: John, section 56 [Jn. 17:1–13].[^455]*
*Communion Hymn: Praise the Lord from the heavens [Ps. 148:1]. Another: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous [Ps. 32:1].*

*The same month, on the 12th day: The Holy Martyrs Tarachos, Probus, and Andronikos; and our Venerable Father Cosmas the Hagiopolite, Hymnographer, Bishop of Maiuma.*[^456]

*At "Lord, I have cried," the stichera of the Martyrs, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Smitten upon the face, and wounded on the neck, and provoked with rods, with hands burned, and the head, O martyr, and pierced cruelly with spits, with ears and lips cut off, and given vinegar to drink, and cast unto wild beasts, and beheaded with the sword, O martyr, thou didst rejoice, O Tarachos![^457]

Thou didst endure being beaten, and pierced with spits, and having thy calves cut, deprived of thine eyes, and given as food unto wild beasts, O all-blessed one, and drawn near unto the fire, and beheaded with the sword, O adornment of martyrs and confirmation of the Church, glorious sufferer Probus, equal in rank unto the angels!

Burned with fire, and beaten with ox sinews, scraped with scrapers, sliced across the calves, and having thy God-speaking tongue cut out, and thy teeth harshly uprooted, O martyr Andronikos, grappling with wild beasts, and finished off with swords, thou didst accomplish the contest, and didst receive the crown!

*Other stichera of the Venerable One, Tone 4, Podoben: "As noble among the martyrs" (Я҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
As the nourisher of wisdom and the fosterer of purity, the treasury of knowledge, O hierarch Cosmas, most renowned theologian, divine teacher, enlightener of the faithful: we praise thee, O blessed one, praying that through thee we all may be delivered from corruption, afflictions, and every harm by the Good One!

...we praise thee, O blessed one, praying that through thee we all may be delivered from corruption, afflictions, and every harm by the Good One!

In temperance didst thou live, in vigilance and fasting, in chaste wisdom and humility, O glorious one; in a manner befitting God wast thou enriched in understanding with earthly and temporal things, and with high, radiant ones; and clearly didst thou thunder forth the incomprehensible, dread, and great mystery of the dispensation of the King of all, O blessed one!

Humble-minded and meek, quiet and sweet of speech, affable and innocent, chaste-minded and righteous, most simple and whole didst thou become, O venerable Cosmas; a hierarch holy-manifest from God wast thou shown forth, and thou didst pasture thy flock upon the grass of waters of salvation, O godly-minded one; therefore now we call thee blessed!

*Glory... Tone 4 (by Anatolios):*[^458]
Having acquired Christ as a fire, ye left behind the fair things of earth; and taking up the Cross upon your shoulders, for the sake of manifold-woven tortures ye followed Him; and before many kings and tyrants ye denied Him not: angels adorned your heads with victorious crowns, O bold of soul, and radiantly did ye enter into the great bridal chamber! O Probus, boast of martyrs; Tarachos, destroyer of demons; and Andronikos, victory of the faithful: having boldness toward the Savior of all, pray ye for our souls!

*Both now, Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 4, Podoben: "As noble among the martyrs" (Я҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
When the ewe-lamb that bare Thee saw Thee, the Lamb and Shepherd, upon the Tree, she wailed and maternally exclaimed unto Thee: O Son beloved, how art Thou suspended upon the Tree of the Cross, O longsuffering One? How are Thy hands and Thy feet nailed by the lawless, O Word, and Thou hast poured forth Thy blood, O Master?

*At the Aposticha, of the Octoechos. Glory... of the Martyrs, Tone 4 (by Andrew of Jerusalem):*[^459]
A triple assembly, equal in number unto the Holy Trinity, having appeared as radiant stars from the east, they summon creation unto sacred solemnity: Probus and Tarachos, with the wise Andronikos; for they surrendered their flesh unto wild beasts, and having crimsoned the earth with their blood, crowned they exult in the heavens with the angels, asking of the Lover of mankind that He grant us remission of offenses and great mercy!

*Both now, Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*

Beholding Thy righteous slaughter, O Christ, the Virgin wept and cried aloud unto Thee: O sweetest Child, how diest Thou unjustly? How hangest Thou upon the Tree, Who didst suspend the whole earth upon the waters?[^460] Leave me not alone, O most merciful Benefactor, Thy mother and handmaid, I pray Thee!

*Troparion of the Martyrs, Tone 5:*
At the achievements of the holy martyrs the heavenly hosts were exceedingly amazed; for in mortal flesh, having struggled well by the power of the Cross, they invisibly vanquished the bodiless enemy; and they pray unto the Lord, that our souls may find mercy.

*Glory... Troparion of the Venerable One, Tone 8:*[^461]
Guide of Orthodoxy, teacher of piety and purity, light of the inhabited earth, divinely-inspired adornment of hierarchs, O Cosmas most wise: by thy teachings thou hast enlightened all, O spiritual lyre; pray unto Christ God that our souls may be saved!

*Both now, Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion. At Matins, Canon of the Octoechos, 1; and of the Saints, 2. Canon of the Martyrs: whereof the acrostic is: "I sing the glory of Thy martyrs, O Savior." A work of Joseph. Tone 8.[^462]*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
He that drove the charioteer Pharaoh plunged him into the deep of old, when the wonder-working rod of Moses struck the sea crosswise and divided it; but he saved Israel the fugitive, that went on foot, as he sang a song unto God.

*Troparia:*
Adorned with martyr graces, ye stand with love before Christ the Crown-giver in the heavens, O martyrs, illuminated by the light of the thrice-radiant effulgence; and ye enlighten all who glorify you in faith.

In the persecution of impiety did ye appear, driving away all the deceit of wickedness, O invincible martyrs, by valiant struggles, as good soldiers, as pillars of the Church, and as the stronghold of truth.

Having struck down the wicked heresy with prudent understanding, O all-wise ones, and the raging sea of impiety by the gentleness of the Spirit, rejoicing ye passed over unto the heavenly harbors.

Be courageous, O choir of martyrs! The choirs of angels truly rejoice together with you in the heavens this day, O blessed Probus, Andronikos, and Tarachos; for manfully did ye endure the pains of martyrdom, being glorified with divine victories!

*Theotokion:*
The Word, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Holy Spirit, willed to dwell within the virginal womb and without wedlock became flesh; and having suffered voluntarily, He ordered the divine armies of martyrs to suffer through faith.

*Another Canon of the Venerable One: whereof the acrostic is: "Wisely let us praise Cosmas the hymnographer." Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Traversing the Red Sea’s abyss with dry feet of old, Israel on foot vanquished the power of Amalek in the wilderness by Moses’ hands stretched out crosswise.

*Troparia:*
Having adorned the life of thy virtues with light, O father Cosmas, and shone with the flashes of the thrice-radiant Light, as thou standest before thy Master, pray that they who sing of thee may be enlightened!

Thy manner befittingly matches thee, bearing thy name: for an adornment of hierarchs wast thou shown forth, O wise one, and the confirmation of monastics, and thou wast the abode of wisdom![^463]

As a most wise lover of wisdom, wisely didst thou purify thy soul, O Cosmas of world renown, driving away the passions of worldly confusion like the mists of gloom.

*Theotokion:*
Let us sing of Mary the immaculate and pure Maiden, reverently and with one accord glorifying her as the Mother of God: Rejoice, O pure Bride of God, blessed Virgin Mother!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Lord, supreme Creator of the heavenly vault and Builder of the Church: do Thou establish me in Thy love, O summit of desires, firm support of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*
Firmly opposing the persecutor, O Tarachos, and most stoutly enduring the crushing of thy face, thou didst crush the jaws of the spiritual lions,[^464] having become valiant through the power of the Spirit!

With lofty-minded understanding didst thou endure the stripes upon thy back and belly, O martyr Probus, being stretched out and scorched with fire, and pierced in the breast with spits, and smitten on thy God-speaking mouth!

Lifted up like a lamb, thou didst endure the pains of cruel wounds, O martyr Andronikos: the burning of fire, the uprooting of teeth, and the cutting out of thy God-speaking tongue!

Thrice-radiant as stars of intellectual splendor, O glorious ones, ye enlighten all creation, driving away the darkness of the deceit of polytheism, O most radiant passion-bearing martyrs!

*Theotokion:*
As good, as fair, and as chosen, O pure one the Word Who brought all things into being chose thee, and the Superessential One willed to dwell within thy spotless and God-welcoming womb.

*Another. Heirmos:*
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, my refuge and confirmation!

*Troparia:*
With fair songs unto the hymnody of Christ didst Thou cause the Bride to rejoice, having clearly set forth the divine mysteries of Christ.

Nourished on the laws of Christ, O father Cosmas, thou didst pasture the flock committed unto thee with Orthodox ordinances.

Possessing in thyself a soul kindled by the fire of divine zeal, O venerable father Cosmas, thou didst drive away all heresies.

*Theotokion:*
The shadows have passed away, and Christ the Truth hath shone forth, having taken flesh of thy womb, O Mother of God, and He hath saved us.

*Kontakion of the Venerable One, Tone 8, Podoben: "To thee, the champion leader" (Взбра́нной):*
Adorned with virtues, O God-inspired Cosmas, thou wast an adornment of the Church of Christ: for with hymns didst thou adorn her, O blessed one; but pray unto the Lord, that we may be delivered from all the wiles of the adversary, who cry unto thee: Rejoice, O father thrice-rich![^465]

*Sessional Hymn of the Martyrs, Tone 8, Podoben: "The wisdom and Word of God" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Youthfully armed with the firm faith of the Holy Trinity, Andronikos, Probus, and all-wise Tarachos destroyed the might of the deceit of polytheism, and bound the people unto divine love; therefore having received the prizes of victory, with the choirs of the bodiless hosts they rejoice together; unto whom let us cry in faith: Pray ye unto Christ God, that He grant remission of offenses unto those who celebrate your holy memory with love![^466]

*Glory... of the Venerable One, in the same tone:*
Nourished in piety and faith, and having adorned thy mind and soul with wisdom, thou didst flee from all earthly cares; and living in solitude, having set thy mind upon ineffable things, thou wast accounted worthy of great grace. Therefore didst thou also adorn the divine Church of Christ with honey-flowing hymns and teachings, O our holy father Cosmas: pray unto Christ God that He grant remission of offenses unto those who celebrate thy holy memory with love!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Having unburnt conceived Wisdom and the Word in thy womb, O Mother of God, thou didst bear unto the world Him that upholds the world; and in thine arms didst thou bear Him Who bears all things, the Nourisher of all and Creator of creation. Therefore do I pray thee, O all-holy Virgin, and faithfully glorify thee, that I may be delivered from transgressions; and in the day of judgment, when I am to stand before the face of my Creator, O sovereign Lady, pure Virgin, then grant me thy help: for thou canst do all things that thou willest, O all-hymned one!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding upon the Cross the Lamb, Shepherd, and Deliverer, the Ewe-lamb exclaimed weeping, and bitterly wailing cried aloud: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee; but mine inward parts are consumed with fire, beholding Thy Crucifixion which Thou endurest for the tender compassion of Thy mercy, O longsuffering Lord! O Abyss of mercy and inexhaustible Wellspring, have compassion, and grant remission of offenses unto those who sing with faith of Thy divine passions![^467]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art also my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who leaving not the bosom of the Father, didst visit our poverty; therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry aloud unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind![^468]

*Troparia:*
Keeping Thy law, O Word, the martyrs hated the laws of the lawless, which commanded them to neglect Thee the Creator through demonic cunning; and having endured all the painful torment of tortures, they obtained heavenly serenity.

Loving to bear the yoke of Christ placed upon thy neck, whereof thou rejoicest when crushed, and having the Cross as the rod of sovereign power, O all-blessed Tarachos, thou didst endure the diverse increases of tortures, crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

Having been shown powerful through the Divine Spirit, O Probus, thou didst manfully endure the painful crushing of thy calves and the tearing out of thine eyes, being enlightened most perfectly by knowledge of the Trinity; and thou hast been joined unto the heavenly choirs, O martyr!

Youthfully didst thou stand before the tribunal, O martyr Andronikos, firmly opposing the enemy; therefore the cutting of thy calves made thee mightier, trampling down his head and preparing for thee a path into the heights!

*Theotokion:*
Alone dost thou bear in thy womb God incarnate, Who grants being unto all; Whom the divine martyrs steadfastly confessing in the arena, suffered.

...[for Thy sake], O Virgin Theotokos all-hymned, marvelous wonder of angels, and scourge of demons.

*Another [Canon of the Venerable One]. Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her station, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
Having thoroughly purified the eye of thy heart from passionate confusion, O hierarch Cosmas, thou didst sing a fair and saving song, and thou didst enlighten the Church.

Thy hymnographic delights, adorned with dogmas and the beauties of sweet melody, O Cosmas all-praised, become a healing of all despondency for those who love them.

The Church, heavily ailing from the blasphemous and wanton prattlings of Copronymus, hast thou healed, O all-blessed one, by the writings wherewith thou didst sweetly hymn the Lord![^469]

How marvelous are thy songs, and filled with all understanding, O father: whereby thou hast openly revealed unto all the mysteries of Christ, truly dread and most marvelous!

*Theotokion:*
With hymnody let us sing of the Theotokos, who bare the incarnate Word of God in two natures, yet in one single hypostasis, even as the divine Cosmas taught.[^470]

*Canon of the Martyrs. Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy face, O Light never-setting, and an alien darkness hath covered me, the wretched one? But turn me, and unto the light of Thy commandments direct my ways, I pray Thee![^471]

*Troparia:*
Unswerving travelers of the narrow and rough path did ye become, having traversed the multitude of afflictions like sea foam, O passion-bearers; for like unshaken pillars ye established your hearts upon the rock of the faith.

Confessing the divine truth and the saving teachings of the Theologian, thou didst endure the tearing apart of thy lips, O martyr Tarachos, by the power of thy mind stopping the mouth that spoke injurious blasphemy against God Who made him.

Stretched out on four sides, and held fast by the wounds of stripes, thou didst display a valiant uplifting of soul; therefore, crowned together with thy fellow sufferers, O Probus, thou standest before the King of all.

*Theotokion:*
Greater than the heavenly hosts art thou, O Virgin, having borne the Word of the Father, co-unoriginate and co-eternal; enlightened by Whose radiance, the passion-bearers passed through the night of godlessness.

*Another [Canon of the Venerable One]. Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou, my Lord, hast come into the world as a Light, a holy Light turning from gloomy ignorance those who praise Thee in faith![^472]

*Troparia:*
Thy utterance, O wise one, hath gone forth unto all the ends of the earth, gladdening with divine hymns those who praise thee, O Cosmas.

Surpassing the laws of nature, thou didst praise the most holy Theotokos, who bare the superessential Word; therefore we call thee blessed.

Singing marvelous songs with sweetness, thou didst most sacredly hymn the ineffable and pure Nativity of the Master from the Virgin.

*Theotokion:*
The lightnings of thy Nativity, O all-hymned one, have enlightened the whole inhabited earth, and the earth was moved from deceit unto faith in Him.

*Canon of the Martyrs. Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the abyss of evils, I pray Thee: for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou give ear unto me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparia:*
Having become weapons of the Mighty One through faith, ye manfully wounded the hearts of the evil-minded, valiantly rising up against them, O glorious passion-bearers.

Even as of old the beasts in the den were put to shame before Daniel, so now did they fear the stout armor-bearers of faith in the arena, being tamed by their honorable sufferings.

Emulating Thy pierced side, O Master, Tarachos likewise received piercing in the ribs; he was given vinegar to drink, and his head was cruelly scorched with fire.

*Theotokion:*
Having conceived the Pre-eternal One in thy womb without seed, O pure one, thou didst give birth in the flesh unto Him Who by nature is bodiless; and with milk didst thou nourish Him Who nourishes all, O Mother of God unwedded!

*Another [Canon of the Venerable One]. Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, having been cleansed from demonic blood by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side!

*Troparia:*
Thy words have appeared like sweet honeycomb, gladdening the hearts of the pious who sing of the Savior's Baptism, and Simeon's Meeting and dismissal.[^473]

Not in words alone, but in reality, O blessed one, didst thou set forth in songs the raising of Lazarus from the dead, and the praise of the children, and the envy of the Judeans.

Thou didst adorn the Exaltation of the thrice-blessed Tree, having sung a hymn filled with all joy and filled with existing gladness in the praise thereof, O all-blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
Following thy divinely-inspired words, O Maiden, all generations call thee blessed, O Virgin, as the blessed Mother of the blessed God.

*Kontakion of the Martyrs, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
The valiant soldiers and martyrs of Christ, Probus and Andronikos together with Tarachos, have shown forth the glory of the Trinity unto us; for they exposed all the godlessness of the tyrants, having valiantly suffered through faith.[^474]

*Ikos:*
Valiantly emulating the sufferings of Job, Probus and Andronikos together with wise Tarachos rejoiced as they suffered in the arena, exposing the godlessness of the lawless. Therefore we also, honoring their memory with love, praise them in hymns and faithfully proclaim their struggles: for marvelous is the Lord, Who hath glorified these luminaries in the world, who valiantly suffered through faith!

*Canon of the Martyrs. Ode 7. Heirmos:*
At the condescension of God, the fire was put to shame in Babylon of old; therefore the Children in the furnace, dancing with rejoicing foot as in a meadow, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^475]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst joyfully endure the cutting off of thine ears, O glorious Tarachos, as an obedient servant of Him Who inclined His most merciful ear unto thee and fulfilled thy prayers, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Stretched out, O martyrs, and tormented with the wounds of stripes, with firm purpose ye opposed the most lawless, and ye became victors, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Thy spirit was filled with divine dew and refreshment when they scorched thy hands and head with fire; and like the Children thou didst cry aloud, O Tarachos: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

By the sprinkling of your honorable blood, O holy ones, ye quenched the coals of deceit, pouring forth upon us the dew of divine grace, and shedding forth the waters of healings unto those who ever praise your memory.

*Theotokion:*
Without leaving the Father's bosom, the most perfect Word empties Himself within thy womb, and flesh is seen, and an Infant is born; unto Whom we sing, rejoicing, O Virgin: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Another [Canon of the Venerable One]. Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace, the children of Abraham, scorched rather by love of piety than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord![^476]

*Troparia:*
Every soul rejoices, reading thy hymnody, O God-wise father, and crying in faith: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Truly thy tongue became the pen of a swiftly writing scribe of the Holy Spirit, O father, which wrote of the great wonders of the Word![^477]

The rule of praises is vanquished concerning thee, O father; for neither hath it anything worthy to speak unto thy praise, since thou hast uttered the majesties of the Master.

*Theotokion:*
What earthly tongue can worthily call thee blessed, who didst bear God, O Bride of God? Therefore with faith we cry aloud: Rejoice, O pure Virgin; rejoice, O most glorified one!

*Canon of the Martyrs. Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried aloud unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, praise; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^478]

*Troparia:*
Illumined by wounds and by the fair beauties of well-doing and marvelous suffering, ye all stand before the Lord, crowned, being richly filled with the light that streams forth there, and singing with love unceasingly: Ye children, bless; ye priests, praise; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

Raging within himself, the most senseless tyrant, because he was vanquished by your resistance, straightway commands that ye be put to death by the sword, who sing unto the Maker and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, praise; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

Champion martyrs of goodly victory, who by the warmth of faith melted the winter of godlessness: the front-ranking pillars of the faithful, fair blossoms of the mental paradise, harbors in storm for the holy, unshakeable columns—Andronikos, Probus, and Tarachos—are honored!

*Theotokion:*
With the bodiless ranks, with the hosts of martyrs, with apostles and prophets, let all of us devoutly sing of the Sovereign Lady who reigns over all creatures, as holier than the cherubim and sole pure Mother of God, supremely exalting Christ unto all ages!

*Another [Canon of the Venerable One]. Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Daniel, stretching out his hands, stopped the mouths of the lions in the pit;

and the children, zealots of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: All ye works of the Lord, bless ye the Lord!

*Troparia:*
Strengthened by the staff of thy dogmas, O glorious one, we who praise thee turn away from every heretical malice, and devoutly sing unto Christ, crying: All ye works of the Lord, bless ye the Lord!

Having risen above all in mind, O Cosmas, thou didst describe the Supper, wherein the Lord delivered the life-bearing mysteries, and the dread betrayal and inhumanity of Judas, which thou hast also taught us to flee![^479]

Illumined by the rays of immaterial Light, O all-hymned Cosmas, thou didst describe the immaculate passions, and the burial, and the resurrection, and the Transfiguration of Christ, having taught all to sing: All ye works of the Lord, bless ye the Lord![^480]

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst sprout forth as a rod from the root of Jesse, O most pure Virgin, having borne the Flower, the Lord, Who hath filled us with sweet fragrance through the myrrh-breathing scents of His passions; therefore do all of us bless thee, O Mother, Bride of God![^481]

*Canon of the Martyrs. Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Every ear was struck with awe at the ineffable condescension of God, how the Most High voluntarily descended even unto the flesh, becoming man from the virginal womb; therefore, O most pure Theotokos, the faithful magnify thee!

*Troparia:*
In perishable bodies the valiant ones were able to achieve imperishable struggles; and they were likened unto the angels, having received the unshakable Kingdom of God, Probus, and Tarachos, and Andronikos, well-pleasing servants of Christ.

Ye were shown forth as coals, mystically kindled with immaterial fire, and ye burned up the material deceit, O great martyrs; and shining forth like light, ye illuminate creation, and deliver our souls from passions and afflictions.

This day the Church of Christ radiantly and joyfully keeps the yearly festival, this your divine solemnity; and the prophets, apostles, and all the martyrs, victorious passion-bearers of great renown, rejoice together with you!

Ye ascended into the light-bearing habitations, O passion-bearers, clad in a radiant robe which ye wove through manifold tortures; and ye stand before the throne of the Almighty, fervently praying for all!

*Theotokion:*
Spare me, O Savior Who wast born, and Who didst preserve her that bare Thee incorrupt after childbirth: when Thou shalt sit to judge all my deeds, overlook mine iniquities and sins, as sinless, merciful, as God and Lover of mankind!

*Another [Canon of the Venerable One]. Ode 9. Heirmos:*
A cornerstone cut without hand from an unhewn mountain, from thee, O Virgin, was cut off: even Christ, Who joined together the separated natures; therefore, rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Theotokos![^482]

*Troparia:*
Rich and noble didst thou become, O all-blessed one, shining with the rays of virtues, and enlightening the faithful who honor thee with praises, O Cosmas, and who praise thee orthodoxly: we magnify thee, O thrice-holy one!

Shining with divine laws, and thereby having guided thy flock unto life-bearing pasture, O blessed one, as was befitting, O venerable one, thou hast passed over unto the heavens, reaping the fruits of thy labors!

The never-setting Light was manifested unto thee, who hast now passed over from temporal things, as one purified beforehand, whereof enjoying more clearly and purely, remember those who honor thee, O worthy of glory!

*Theotokion:*
Glorifying thee, we call thee blessed, O most holy Virgin and more glorious than the seraphim, who didst bear Christ, O Mother of God, Who pre-eternally shone forth from the Father, even as it is written.

*Exapostilarion of the Octoechos:*
*And of the Saints, Podoben: "While the disciples looked on" (Оу҆чени́кѡмъ зрѧ́щымъ):*
The three-woven choir of passion-bearers, Tarachos and Probus, let us all crown with Andronikos in hymns of praise: for they endured scrapings, and cruel wounds, and afflictions, and they reign with Christ!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown forth more glorious than the cherubim, and appeared higher than the seraphim, as she that bare God incarnate, Who showed unto earthborn men straight paths to walk unto salvation for those who watch!

*At the Liturgy. Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
Unto the saints that are in His earth, the Lord hath made wonderful all His desires in them.
*Verse:* I beheld the Lord ever before me.
*Epistle to the Ephesians, Pericope 233 [6:10–17].*
*Alleluia, Tone 4:*
The righteous cried, and the Lord heard them.
*Gospel according to Luke, Pericope 106 [12:8–12].*
*Communion Verse:*
