Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous![^483]

*The same month, on the 13th day. The Holy Martyrs Carpus and Papylas.*[^484]

*At "Lord, I Have Cried," Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As noble among the martyrs" (Я҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Bound with fetters, and shut up in prison, and given over to wild beasts for food; suspended and scraped, and cast into the fire; and receiving divine help from heaven: ye remained unshaken through love for the Savior of all, O most wise Carpus and Papylas!

Having established thy mind upon the rock of the faith, O martyr Papylas, unswerving in understanding, thou didst rejoice when stoned with stones, emulating the leader of martyrs, the Archdeacon Stephen, as a minister and servant of the divine mysteries, as one numbered among the martyrs, and a companion of angels![^485]

Treading unswervingly the path of martyrdom, nailed unto iron shoes, ye firmly crushed the sting of the enemy and destroyer, O all-praised ones, and were crowned with victories; therefore the whole Church of Christ celebrates your holy, light-bearing, and illustrious memory!

*Glory... Tone 6:*
As one worthy among the beholders of God, O venerable one, thou didst behold the heavens opened, and the Lord sitting upon a throne, with cherubim and seraphim round about Him; and we who are again enlightened by thee, thy sacred flock, O Carpus, cry aloud unto thee: Pray for peace for the world, and great mercy for our souls![^486]

*Both now, Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Podoben: "The three-day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Beholding our Life hanging upon the Tree, the all-spotless Theotokos maternally lamented and cried aloud: O my Son and my God, save those who praise Thee with love!

*At the Aposticha, of the Octoechos.*
*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received imperishable crowns from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, they cast down the tyrants, and crushed the powerless insolence of demons. By their prayers, save our souls!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion.*
*At Matins, both Canons of the Octoechos; and the Canon of the Martyrs, on 4: whereof the acrostic is: "I crown Carpus and Papylas with hymns. Joseph's." Tone 4.[^487]*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Thy victorious right hand hath been glorified in strength in a manner befitting God; for it, O Immortal One, as all-powerful, shattered the adversaries, making a new way through the deep for the Israelites![^488]

*Troparia:*
Praising the all-sacred memory of Carpus and Papylas, the stout witnesses of the truth, let us in a manner befitting God sing unto the Lord of glory, Who made you victors.

Having first been tried by the fire of asceticism, by your suffering as martyrs ye secondly revealed the true testing of your souls, and shone brighter than gold with divine virtues.

Rushing with divine power toward wounds, toward suspension, toward fire and toward death, O glorious ones, ye overcame the strength of the persecutors, and were adorned with imperishable crowns.

*Theotokion:*
O Portal of the Light, that shone forth from thine incorrupt womb, shine upon me the illuminating rays of repentance, and drive away the darkness of my sins, O full of God's grace!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Thou Who alone knowest the weakness of human nature, and didst mercifully take form therein: gird me with power from on high, that I may cry aloud unto Thee, O holy One: O living temple of Thine ineffable glory, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*
A pleasing and most sacred fruit didst thou offer unto Christ, the sacred Agathodoros who was with thee, who endured stripes and wounds, and in the flesh overcame the bodiless enemy by the strength of knowledge.[^489]

Unjustly crushed, O wise one, by command of the judge, and scraped upon thy ribs, thou didst endure; and being again ignited with fire, by streams of blood thou didst quench the coals of deceit, O much-suffering Carpus!

Desiring Christ, he hangs on high; and with his breast ignited with fire, thou didst valiantly endure, O God-wise Papylas, visibly bedewed with the dew of the Spirit, and strengthened by divine hopes.

*Theotokion:*
An undefiled bridal chamber of God, an unhewn mountain, a holy ark, and a table do all of us, the faithful, proclaim thee, O pure one: and censer of the immaterial Coal, O most holy and all-spotless Virgin!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "The stone was sealed" (Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ):*
Having cultivated the fruits of knowledge of God by Thy labors, Thou didst uproot the thistles from the root of godlessness; and anointed with divinely-working ointment, Thou didst shepherd the sacred people[^490] people, O sacred hierarch; and having lawfully suffered, thou wast accounted worthy of twofold honors, O Carpus! Glory to Him Who gave thee strength! Glory to Him Who crowned thee! Glory to Him Who works healings through thee for all!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Thy divine hands, wherewith thou didst bear the Creator, O most holy Virgin, Who took flesh through goodness: stretch them out and implore Him to deliver us from slanders, and passions, and afflictions, who praise thee with love and cry aloud: Glory to Him Who dwelt within thee! Glory to Him Who came forth from thee! Glory to Him Who delivered us through thy childbirth!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb upon the Cross, crucified with the robber, O longsuffering Word, and pierced in the side with a spear, the Ewe-lamb cried aloud, maternally lamenting: What is this strange and terrible mystery, O my Jesus, ineffably accomplished? How art Thou covered with a tomb, O uncircumscribable God? Leave not me alone, who bare Thee, O my sweetest Son!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Perceiving with prophetic eyes thee, the mountain overshadowed by the grace of God, Habakkuk proclaimed that the Holy One of Israel would come forth from thee, unto our salvation and renewal![^491]

*Troparia:*
Bound with the indissoluble love of Him that made thee, by stoning with stones, O Papylas, martyr of Christ, thou didst openly vanquish those who cast them, through the patience bestowed upon thee by God, O all-praised one, valiantly contending against envy.

Arrayed against every kind of torment, O most valiant ones, when cast together unto wild beasts, ye vanquished them like Daniel through the divine forms of faith, O glorious martyrs Carpus and Papylas!

The all-evil contriver of malice, delivering the well-pleasing servants of Christ mercilessly unto unquenchable burning, thought to weaken their strength; but his wiles were turned to ashes!

*Theotokion:*
A haven of salvation do we have in thee, O Virgin, who are ever tempest-tossed in the deep of cruel things: unto thee do we flee, and in thee do we place our hope, that we may not fear the assault of invisible foes.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
O Christ Who didst enlighten the world by the radiance of Thy coming, and didst illumine the ends of the earth by Thy Cross: enlighten with the light of Thy divine knowledge the hearts of those who orthodoxly sing of Thee!

*Troparia:*
Neither affliction of persecution, nor famine, nor death could separate the God-wise ones from the love of Christ; but rather, looking forward unto the future recompense, they took no account of painful things.[^492]

Quickened by divine power, ye endured the cruel boring through of your feet, O all-praised ones, treading the path of martyrdom and crushing the sting of godlessness.

Carrying the unquenchable lamp of suffering, ye passed through the night of ignorance, O martyrs; and ye drew near unto the never-setting Light, as truly sons of the day through faith.

*Theotokion:*
Without knowledge of man didst thou conceive God Whom nothing can contain, and thou didst give birth unto the supernatural Creator of nature: a great mystery, an incomprehensible wonder, O sole all-spotless Virgin!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
The uttermost abyss hath surrounded us, and there is none to deliver; we are accounted as sheep for the slaughter: save Thy people, O our God, for Thou art the strength of the ailing and the restoration!

*Troparia:*
Your virtues breathe sweet fragrance unto the assemblies of men, openly driving away the foul stench of polytheism, O worthy of glory: for ye have now appeared as the sweet fragrance of God, O martyrs!

The burning flame did ye quench with the dew of faith, O blessed ones; and ye warmly kindled desire in Agathonike, who is sacred and glorious with you, with whom ye manfully accomplished your sufferings.[^493]

Steered by the addition of afflictions, O Carpus and Papylas, noble sufferers, being truly unarmored swords, ye cut down the hostile armies.

*Theotokion:*
Raise me up from the mire of passions and from the pit of falls, and guide thy servant, O pure one, who confesses thee to be the Theotokos: for thou art ever the restoration of the falling.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
As a precious treasure, and a wellspring pouring forth streams of healings, the Master hath granted your relics unto those upon earth: which take away the diverse diseases of passions, and unceasingly grant grace unto our souls; therefore with one accord and love, O divine martyrs, we celebrate your solemnity![^494]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
As a spiritual furnace do we, the faithful, contemplate thee, O Theotokos; for even as the Supremely Exalted One saved the three children, so within thy womb He renewed the whole world, the praised[^495] God of our fathers, and supremely glorious.

*Troparia:*
Quenching youthful passion's vapor with the dew of self-control, and the fire with the blood of suffering, ye martyrs reduced to ashes a second time the idol furnace, singing praise unto the God of our fathers, Who is supremely glorious.

Let us stand firm, behold the arena is opened, let us not grow slack! From on high Christ openly offers the crowns of endurance for martyrdom with His life-originating right hand! cried aloud the sufferers Carpus and Papylas with fervent soul.

Being openly divinized through all-form incline toward God, O holy ones, ye easily endured the wounds of the godless, remaining truly yourselves as though another were suffering, singing praise unto the God of our fathers, Who is supremely glorious.

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast openly undone the curse of Eve, our foremother, O pure one, having given birth unto the Firstfruit of blessing, O all-spotless Maiden unwedded, Whom the heavenly ranks bless: the praised God of our fathers, Who is supremely glorious.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
In the furnace the Israelite Children, as in a crucible, shone more brightly than gold with the beauty of piety, saying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^496]

*Troparia:*
The light-bearing memory of the martyrs this day, illuminating with the light of healings us who cry aloud, calls forth: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

Thou didst adorn thyself as a hierarch, as a divine prophet, as a witness of the sufferings of Christ, O all-blessed one, as a divine sign, singing, O all-blessed Carpus: Ye works, sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

The streams of thy suffering blood utterly drowned the torrents of godlessness, and were shown forth as a wellspring of healings unto those who cry aloud, O martyr Papylas: Ye works, sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
The sacred choir of divine prophets proclaims thee to have become the Mother of God, O all-spotless one, unto Whom all of us cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The unconsumed burning bush showed forth an image of thy pure childbirth; and now we pray thee to quench the furnace of temptations that rages against us, that[^497] that we may unceasingly magnify thee, O Theotokos!

*Troparia:*
As whole-burnt offerings, as perfect and blameless sacrifices, were ye offered unto the Creator, O all-blessed ones, having received the end by the sword; and now as crown-bearers, ye dwell in the heavens.

Unconquerable pillars of the Church and ramparts have ye appeared, O martyr sufferers, and wellsprings pouring forth waters of healings, and radiant and all-bright luminaries, delivering the pious from the night of sin.

Having already taken up your abode with Him Himself, illuminated by radiant and bright splendors and divine brightnesses, and filled with everlasting joy: O Papylas and Carpus, pray for us!

*Theotokion:*
Cherubim-like as a throne, O all-pure one, thou bearest in thine arms Him Who bears all things, Who for our sake clothed Himself in flesh like unto us, and is seen double yet simple; Whom ever implore on behalf of us who hymn thee.[^498]

*The same month, on the 14th day. The Holy Martyrs Nazarius, Gervasius, Protasius, and Celsus; and our Venerable Mother Parasceve.*[^499]

*Her service is written after this service.*

*At "Lord, I Have Cried," Stichera of the Saints, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Having accomplished the contest as witnesses of the faith, the valiant-minded Nazarius and great Protasius, with wise Celsus and God-wise Gervasius, received the crown of victory: whom let us hymn as noble warriors, as heirs of the Kingdom on high, and as voluntary whole-burnt offerings.

Illumined in mind by the light-beholding of the Spirit, O sacred Nazarius, by divine grace thou didst shine forth brighter than the sun unto souls that were in darkness: teaching, enlightening, most radiantly instructing to believe in and honor the one Lord, gloriously incarnate of the Virgin, Who left earthly things behind.

Together with Nazarius, ever-memorable Celsus, and glorious Protasius, and Gervasius shone forth, having suffered with valiant mind, and having manfully vanquished upon earth all the demonic hosts; therefore do they pour forth streams of healings, and richly water the hearts of the pious with divine grace.

Let Nazarius be praised, and let Protasius be honored, and divine Celsus and Gervasius, who gloriously suffered and diminished deceit, being sacredly numbered among the angelic choirs, and divinized by divine communion, receiving crowns of victory from the life-originating right hand.

Having finished the course and kept the faith, ye were adorned with crowns of suffering and were joined unto the angels, having brightly shown forth bravery and trampled underfoot in Christ the deceitful foe, O divine martyrs; therefore, having come together in harmony, we celebrate your light-bearing memory with rejoicing.

By divine preaching the God-wise Nazarius enlightened cities as he passed through them; whom Celsus emulated, and as a divine sufferer gained glory, having trampled the deceiver underfoot. All of us honor them with praises as stars and luminaries unto those in darkness, and physicians of our souls.

*Glory... Tone 8:*
Let us praise the spiritual sacrifices, Nazarius, Gervasius, Protasius, and Celsus: for they brought to nought all the fury of the torturers together with the idols. Therefore, by their prayers, O Christ God, grant peace unto the world, and great mercy unto our souls!

*Both now, Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 8, Podoben: "Martyrs of the Lord" (Мч҃ницы гдⷭ҇ни):*
I endure not, O Child, beholding Thee asleep upon the Tree, Who bestowest wakefulness upon all: that unto those who of old fell into the sleep of perdition through the fruit of transgression, Thou mightest grant divine and saving wakefulness! cried aloud the weeping Virgin, whom we magnify.[^500]

*At the Aposticha, of the Octoechos.*

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received imperishable crowns from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, they cast down the tyrants, and crushed the powerless insolence of demons. By their prayers, save our souls!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion. If it be Wednesday or Friday, Stavrotheotokion.*

*At Matins, both Canons of the Octoechos; and the Canon of the Saints, on 4: whereof the acrostic is: "Faithfully do I hymn the crown-bearing martyrs. Joseph's." Tone 4.[^501]*

*Faithfully do I hymn the crown-bearing martyrs. Joseph's. Tone 4.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
I will open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I will utter a word unto the Queen Mother; and I will appear radiantly keeping festival, and rejoicing I will sing her wonders.[^502]

*Troparia:*
Having faithfully suffered and finished the contest, ye were adorned with the crown of victory, O divine martyrs; therefore do we celebrate your divine memory with rejoicing.

Winged with the sail of the divine Spirit, dry-shod ye traversed the deep of the tortures of martyrdom, O glorious martyrs; and now ye have taken up your abode at the divine haven of the light that hath appeared.

Clothed with Christ as with a breastplate, O blessed ones, in the body ye struck down the bodiless foe, having endured manifold tortures and an unjust death.

*Theotokion:*
Let us sing of the all-spotless Maiden, who alone contained the pre-eternal Lord within her womb: for she became wider than the heavens, having given flesh unto God through goodness.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Theotokos, living and ungrudging fountain: strengthen those who spiritually assemble together in choir, who sing thy praises; and in thy divine glory vouchsafe them crowns of glory![^503]

*Troparia:*
As divine clusters of the divine vine in truth did ye become, O martyrs, pouring forth for us the wine of suffering, which spiritually gladdens the hearts of all through grace.

Firmly didst thou oppose the persecutor, O Nazarius, martyr of Christ, when he commanded thee to sacrifice unto abominable gods, O exceedingly wealthy one: for like a lamb thou wast ready for slaughter, O glorious one.

In agreement of praise let all of us faithfully sing today of Nazarius, Gervasius, Protasius, and Celsus, who became witnesses of the truth in both deeds and words.

*Theotokion:*
The tabernacle of the law prefigured thee who wast to be the sacred dwelling-place of God, O pure one: from whence came forth our cleansing, granting mercy unto all of us.[^504]

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Honoring the holy struggles and persecutions of Nazarius, the pains and wounds of Protasius, we praise Gervasius and Celsus, honorable vessels of the Holy Trinity: radiant stars, protectors of the faithful, true destroyers of impiety, and pillars of the faith, and an unshakable foundation; unto whom let us cry aloud in faith: Pray ye unto Christ God, to grant forgiveness of sins to grant forgiveness of sins unto those who honor your holy memory with love!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
As a Virgin and sole among women, who barest God in the flesh without seed, all generations of men call thee blessed: for the fire of the Godhead made its abode within thee, and as an infant thou didst nurse the Creator and Lord. Therefore do both angelic and human race worthily glorify thy most holy childbirth, and with one accord cry aloud unto thee: Implore Christ God to grant forgiveness of sins unto those who worship thy most holy childbirth in faith!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The Ewe-lamb, beholding the Lamb, the Shepherd and Deliverer, upon the Cross, cried out weeping, and bitterly lamenting exclaimed: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance; but my womb burns within me as I behold Thy crucifixion, which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of Thy lovingkindness, O longsuffering Lord! O abyss of mercy and inexhaustible fountain: take pity, and grant forgiveness of sins unto those who sing in faith of Thy divine passions!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Sitting in glory upon the throne of the Godhead upon a light cloud, Jesus the supremely divine came by an incorrupt hand, and saved those who cry aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Christ![^505]

*Troparia:*
Beyond understanding is the love of Thy holy martyrs: it surpasses all thoughts, O Word—the combats, the manly struggles, and the endurances which Thou gavest unto those who lawfully suffered.

Being wounded in different bodies, the warriors of Christ exceedingly manifested one single mind: they confessed the one God in three Suns, and openly destroyed the deceit of polytheism.

The angels marveled at the combat of the honorable martyrs Nazarius, Protasius, Gervasius, and Celsus: how, being in the flesh, they wrestled with the bodiless foe, and cast him down unto the ground most manfully.

As noble soldiers, as unconquerable martyrs, as armor-bearers of Christ, and as obedient servants of piety, ye inherited the heavenly mansions, ever rejoicing with the angelic choirs.

*Theotokion:*
Without leaving the Father's bosom, O all-pure Theotokos, the Supremely Divine One sits within thy most pure womb, through goodness preparing a divine seat and an ever-abiding glory for all.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
All things were amazed at thy divine glory; for thou, O Virgin unwedded[^506] for thou, O Virgin unwedded, didst hold in thy womb God Who is over all, and didst give birth unto the timeless Son, granting peace unto all who sing thy praises!

*Troparia:*
Thy body, O Nazarius, was delivered over unto wounds, unto the bonds of manifold tortures; therefore it pours forth sweet-smelling myrrh for us, imparting sweet fragrance unto the souls of those who honor thee with unhesitating faith, O all-praised one.

With drops of thy blood, O passion-bearer, thou didst quench the coals of polytheism; and with the fire of divine wonders, O all-wise one, thou burnest up the mire of cruel passions through the accompanying grace of God, O Nazarius.

As blossoms breathing forth knowledge of God, lush meadows of divine knowledge, fruitful trees of paradise, let us honor the visible and radiant sufferings, and faithfully call them blessed.

Let Nazarius be sung, let Celsus be magnified, together with Protasius let noble Gervasius be praised in faith: who became unshakable pillars and indestructible foundations of the Church!

*Theotokion:*
The sword that of old guarded Eden, O pure one, turns its back before the faithful who are sealed with the honorable blood that flowed of old from the side, pierced by the spear, of Him Who shone forth from thee.[^507]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Celebrating this divine and all-honorable festival of the Mother of God, O ye God-wise: come, let us clap our hands, and glorify God Who was born of her![^508]

*Troparia:*
Thy paths were in the waters, O all-blessed Nazarius, and thy footsteps were not known unto them; by Whose power thou didst put to shame all the foes.[^509]

The incurable afflictions of bodies hast thou healed by divine grace, O martyr Nazarius; and thou pourest forth sweet-smelling myrrh unto those who draw near unto thine honorable relics.

Divine Protasius and great Gervasius with him, having trampled underfoot temporal wealth and power, were vouchsafed eternal glory.

*Theotokion:*
Thou alone hast renewed the laws of nature, O Virgin, having given birth unto the immortal God, Who clothed Himself in mortal flesh that He might make alive the dead through goodness.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Bright luminaries have ye appeared, O divine martyrs, illuminating creation with the light of wonders, dispelling ever the deep night of diseases, and unto Christ, the one God praying unceasingly unto the one God for all of us!

*Ikos:*
As radiant and divine luminaries, standing ever before the great Light, O God-blessed martyrs, and always enlightened and divinized by the radiances of the never-setting Godhead emitted thence: spiritually enlighten those who celebrate your memory in faith, and deliver them from darkening, and passions, and perils, and malice, praying unceasingly for all of us!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The God-wise ones worshiped not the creature rather than the Creator, but manfully trampling underfoot the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, O supremely praised Lord and God of our fathers![^510]

*Troparia:*
Having established your steps upon the rock of divine knowledge, O glorious ones, ye turned not your hearts away through the enemy's crafty wiles, singing: Blessed art Thou, O supremely praised Lord and God of our fathers!

A never-setting Light shone forth upon you who suffered in blood, and gladness openly received you, O great martyrs, as ye sang: Blessed art Thou, O supremely praised Lord and God of our fathers!

Zealously passing through cities, O blessed ones, ye cleansed them, confessing Christ before the torturers, and granting healings unto all who had need, by the grace of the All-Holy Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
Having found thy womb to be a light-bearing palace, God made His abode therein, making sons of light of those who cry aloud, O all-spotless one: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The Childbirth of the Theotokos saved the pious Children in the furnace: then prefigured, but now in reality, it rouses the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Ye works, sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^511]

*Troparia:*
Rising early at dawn unto the great Sun that shone forth gloriously from the Virgin, O unconquerable martyrs, ye became light, and by grace ye destroyed the darkness of deceit, crying aloud: Ye works, sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

Rome of old proclaims thy struggles, O much-suffering Nazarius: Constantinople sings of thy wonders, keeping thine honorable relics in faith, crying aloud: Ye works, sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^512]

*Troparia:*
Divine Nazarius, pouring forth the myrrh of grace, commands all who hasten in faith to draw it, unto enlightenment and cleansing, as they cry aloud: Ye works, sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

Having the valiant passion-bearers—Nazarius, Celsus, Protasius, and again Gervasius—as most mighty intercessors with the Lord, with love we cry aloud: Ye works, sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Having united Himself unto thee in the flesh according to essence, He Who bestows being upon all: renew me wholly, who am wholly grown old through transgressions, O all-pure one! Therefore do we sing of thee as Virgin, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Eve indeed through the disease of disobedience brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Theotokos, through the sprout of childbirth didst blossom forth blessing unto the world; therefore do all of us magnify thee![^513]

*Troparia:*
Strengthened by the power of Christ, Who destroyed the malice of the destroyer, having bowed your neck unto Him, ye received the end by the sword, being released from the flesh, O great of renown; therefore are ye worthily glorified!

Fair were ye with bodily wounds, and were likened unto the angels; and now ye stand rejoicing ever before the beauty of all, openly adorned by communion therewith, most fairly, O all-honorable ones!

With signs and wonders did He strengthen you, both before the end, O all-ever-memorable ones, and after the end of the age, O Nazarius, Protasius, Celsus, and Gervasius, unconquerable martyrs; therefore are ye worthily called blessed!

All things were cleansed by your love, as they celebrate your memory today: the martyrs rejoice, and all the prophets keep festival with us; with whom pray unto the Benefactor for us, O all-blessed ones!

*Theotokion:*
We offer unto thee the greeting of divine Gabriel, rejoicing, O all-pure Maiden: Rejoice, O full of grace, the boast of martyrs and apostles, and salvation of all the faithful, O Virgin Mother unwedded![^514]

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "In the Spirit in the sanctuary" (Дх҃омъ во ст҃и́лищи):*
As though bodiless didst thou endure unbearable tortures, O blessed Nazarius, glory of martyrs, with wise Gervasius, Protasius together, and divine Celsus; therefore in faith do we honor your all-festal and honorable memory!

*Theotokion:*
Beyond understanding is the most glorious wonder of thy childbirth *Theotokion:*
Beyond understanding is the most glorious wonder of thy childbirth, it strikes with amazement every mind, both of earthborn and of angels: for being a Virgin thou didst give birth, and a Virgin didst thou remain, even as before childbirth, O Virgin! O fearful mystery! O marvelous childbirth! O most glorious pregnancy![^515]

*Service of our Venerable Mother Parasceve.*[^516]

*At Vespers, the customary Kathisma. At "Lord, I Have Cried," Stichera on 6; and at Matins, the Canon on 6.*

*If the Superior desires, or where a temple of the Venerable Parasceve is situated, then we sing: "Blessed is the man"; and Stichera on 8; and the Aposticha; and at Matins we sing even as for the other saints that have a Polyeleos.*[^517]

*Stichera, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*
Thou didst shine forth, O Parasceve, by virtues and contemplation, like a faithful faster upon the firmament, with all-bright and glorious rays: thou drivest away all heretical darkenings, and by faith and love thou enlightenest those who attend diligently unto thy teachings. Therefore with love do we celebrate thy light-bearing festival: pray thou with boldness unto Christ for our souls!

By ascetic labors thou didst arm thy soul, and didst wither the bodily surgings, and thou didst make thy mind, O glorious one, a dwelling-place of the divine Trinity; and thou wast lifted up, O Parasceve, unto where there is incorruption and the rest of the righteous, with whom thou now rejoicest, and in glory standest before Christ, praying with boldness for our souls!

By the streams of thy divine tears, O Parasceve, Thou didst utterly drown the demonic array, as being powerless, through thine incline ever toward God and mighty ascents: and Thou didst cast down unto the ground their proud soaring, and wast lifted up unto the heavenly and beauteous mansions, wherein Thou dwellest together with the angels: standing before Christ, pray Thou with boldness for our souls!

*Other Stichera, Tone 6, Podoben the same (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*

*Tone and Podoben the same (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже).*
Living after death, O blessed Parasceve, thou visitest all who with faith flee unto thy shrine: from cruel passions thou ever shelterest them, and from afflictions thou deliverest them, and by thy prayers thou bestowest healings, and savest from demonic assault those who honor thy holy memory, asking of God that He grant forgiveness of transgressions.

An earthly angel upon earth wast thou shown forth, a bodiless life didst thou display in an earthly body: for fleeing far from father and mother, thou wast joined unto Christ thy Bridegroom in the heavenly bridal chamber; living purely for Whom, in Kallikrateia thou hast a strong habitation, where thou bestowest healings upon those who flee unto thee in faith, and pourest forth the grace of wonders upon all.[^518]

Of old indeed God rained down manna from heaven upon the Israelites; but today unto those who possess thine honorable name, wheat was doubled for distribution unto the needy: and vouchsafe us also now at thy memory, O venerable Parasceve, to receive the grace of the Heavenly Bread, unto thanksgiving for thy wonders, that we may obtain joy and salvation at thy holy memory![^519]

*Glory... Tone 4:*
Having quenched the kindling of bodily passions by the outpourings of tearful streams, and having kindled the fire of divine love, thou wast illumined with the immortality of Christ the King: and unto Him wast thou united, O good virgin, and with Him into the spiritual bridal chamber didst thou enter, O Parasceve: pray for our souls!

*Both now, Theotokion according to the Tone; or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Прехва́льнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*
Beholding Thy wrongful slaughter, O Christ, the weeping Virgin cried aloud unto Thee: O sweetest Child, how art Thou dying without justice? How hangest Thou upon the Tree, Who didst suspend all the earth upon the waters? Leave Me not alone, O most merciful Benefactor, Thy mother and handmaid, I pray Thee![^520]

*And if thou singest the Polyeleos; Both now, Theotokion: "The glory of all the world." Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. Three Readings.*

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon [Chapter 5:15–6:3]:*
The righteous live for evermore; their reward also is with the Lord, and the care of them is with the Most High. Therefore shall they receive a glorious kingdom, and a beautiful crown from the Lord's hand: for with His right hand shall He cover them[^521] shall He cover them, and with His arm shall He shield them. He shall take His jealousy for complete armor, and make the creature His weapon for vengeance upon the foes. He shall put on righteousness as a breastplate, and shall put on unfeigned judgment as a helmet. He shall take holiness as an invincible shield: and His severe wrath shall He sharpen for a sword: and the world shall fight with Him against the senseless. Right-aiming shafts of lightnings shall go forth, and from the well-curved bow of the clouds they shall fly unto the mark. And hailstones full of wrath shall be cast as out of a stone-bow; the water of the sea shall rage against them, and the rivers shall harshly drown them. Yea, a mighty wind shall stand up against them, and like a whirlwind shall sift them. And iniquity shall lay waste the whole earth, and ill-doing shall overturn the thrones of the mighty. Hear therefore, O ye kings, and understand; learn, ye that are judges of the ends of the earth. Give ear, ye that rule multitudes, and boast yourselves in peoples of nations: for power is given you of the Lord, and sovereignty from the Most High.[^522]

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon [Chapter 3:1–9]:*
The souls of the righteous are in the hand of God, and there shall no torment touch them. In the eyes of the foolish they seemed to die, and their departure was taken for misery, and their going from us to be utter destruction: but they are in peace. For though they be punished in the sight of men, yet is their hope full of immortality. And having been a little chastised, they shall be greatly rewarded: for God tested them, and found them worthy of Himself. As gold in the furnace did He test them, and as a whole burnt-offering did He accept them. And in the time of their visitation they shall shine, and run to and fro like sparks among the stubble. They shall judge nations, and have dominion over peoples, and their Lord shall reign unto the ages. They that put their trust in Him shall understand the truth, and such as be faithful in love shall abide with Him: for grace and mercy are upon His holy ones, and He hath visitation among His chosen.[^523]

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon [Chapter 4:7–15]:*
Though the righteous happen to die early, yet shall he be in rest. For honorable age is not that which consists in length of time, nor is it measured by number of years. But wisdom is gray hair unto men, and an unspotted life is ripe old age. Having pleased God, he was beloved of Him; and living among sinners he was translated; he was caught away, lest malice should alter his understanding, or deceit beguile his soul. For the fascination of wickedness obscures good things[^524] good things, and the roving of desire undermines the innocent mind. Being made perfect in a short time, he fulfilled a long time: for his soul pleased the Lord; therefore hastened He to take him away from among the wicked: but the peoples saw it, and understood it not, neither laid they this up in their mind: That His grace and mercy is with His holy ones, and that He hath visitation among His chosen.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*
Wounded with divine desire, O venerable mother Parasceve, hating all the bright things of this deceitful life, thou didst follow Christ with a burning desire of the Spirit: having mortified carnal wisdom by abstinence, and true compunction, and perfect humility, thou didst receive from God the gift of ineffable wonderworking: Whom pray thou to save and enlighten our souls![^525]

*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*
Having desired the angelic life, O venerable mother Parasceve, this fleeting life didst thou count as a dream: laboring in abstinence, and diligent prayers, and lying on the ground, and vigilance, and purity, thou wast shown forth as an all-pure vessel of the divine Spirit: and from God didst thou receive the gift of radiant and ineffable wonders: Whom pray thou, O all-blessed mother, to save and enlighten our souls!

*Verse: Blessed are all they that fear the Lord, that walk in His ways.*
All the things of this present life, seductive, short-lived, and quickly fleeting, didst thou cast away, and didst quench the flame of passionate fire by unceasing compunction, and perfect humility, and diligent prayer: thou didst attain unto the ranks of the bodiless hosts, and with unveiled face thou beholdest the glory of God: Whom pray thou, O venerable Parasceve, to save and enlighten our souls!

*Glory... Tone 2:*
A rose blossomed forth from a good root, who from thy childhood didst love Christ, O Parasceve; having been nourished by grace rather than milk, and standing upon a rock, thou didst waste away thy flesh; and having been lifted up in understanding unto God, thou wast formed by virtues into a dwelling-place of the Lord; and thou rejoicest with the heavenly hosts, having become the habitation of Christ: pray thou that our souls may be saved![^526]

*Both now, Theotokion; or Stavrotheotokion:*

*Stavrotheotokion, Podoben: "When from the tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
When the spotless Ewe-lamb beheld her Lamb willingly led to the slaughter as a man, weeping she said: Dost Thou hasten now to leave Me childless, O Christ, Who gave Thee birth? What is this that Thou hast done, O Redeemer of all? Yet do I hymn and glorify Thine utter goodness which surpasses mind and word, O Lover of mankind![^527]

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Having loved a wilderness and solitary life, and zealously run after Christ Thy Bridegroom, and having taken His easy yoke in Thy youth, arming thyself manfully with the sign of the cross against the spiritual foes, by ascetic struggles—with fasting, and prayers, and drops of tears—Thou didst quench the coals of passions, O worthy of all praise, Parasceve; and now standing in the heavenly bridal chamber with the wise virgins before Christ, pray Thou for us who honor Thy precious memory![^528]

*Glory... Both now, Theotokion: "The mystery hidden from the ages."*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the saint, twice.*
*Glory... Both now, Theotokion.*

*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Having loved the Bridegroom, the Creator and God, Thou didst leave behind a corruptible bridegroom, O precious Parasceve, and rejoicing didst endure ascetic labors; therefore, wearing crowns, Thou didst ascend unto the divine bridal chamber, entreating for all the remission of sins. (Twice)[^529]

*Glory... Both now, Theotokion:*
O all-immaculate Virgin, who didst give birth unto the most radiant God: together with the venerable woman pray Him unceasingly to grant us remission of transgressions and amendment of life before the end, unto us who with faith and love hymn thee as is meet, O only all-hymned one!

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
The blameless and precious ewe-lamb was mystically united unto the Lord in a pure life, having adorned herself with God-given beauty; therefore did she receive the grace of healings, curing the passions of those who flee unto her, by the power of the Spirit. (Twice)

*Glory... Both now, Theotokion:*
We entreat thee as the Mother of God, who wast shown forth as truly a Virgin even after childbirth, who with love flee unto thy goodness: for we sinners have thee as an intercessor, and we have acquired thee as salvation in tribulations salvation, the only all-immaculate one.

*Thereafter: "Praise the name of the Lord." Selected Psalm of the Venerable. Refrain:*
We magnify thee, O venerable mother Parasceve, and we honor thy holy memory; for thou prayest for us unto Christ our God.[^530]

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
Forechosen of God, O venerable mother Parasceve, having escaped the billows of life and the worldly turmoil of passionate thoughts, Thou didst reach the haven of life; and hymning with the angels Christ God the Redeemer, pray Thou unceasingly for us, that He may grant grace and mercy unto us who honor thy holy memory with love. (Twice)[^531]

*Glory... Both now, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, thou that receivedst the joy of the world from the angel! Rejoice, thou that gavest birth unto thy Creator and Lord! Rejoice, thou that wast vouchsafed to become the Mother of God!

*Then the Graduals, the first Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon:*
*God is marvelous in His saints, the God of Israel.*
*Verse: In the congregations bless ye God, the Lord from the springs of Israel.*
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 104 [25:1–13].*
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 8:*
Let every tongue be moved unto the praising of the fair bride of Christ, the pure dove gilded by the Holy Spirit: for having loved the wilderness life, she became a converser with angels, the boast of virgins, whom the angels praised, and we, the human generations, calling her blessed, cry aloud: Rejoice, O protectress of those in perils, and haven of the tempest-tossed, O all-blessed Parasceve! For thou didst follow Christ thy Bridegroom, Who grants great mercy unto the world.[^532]

*Canon of the Theotokos with the Heirmos on 6; and two canons of the venerable woman on 8.*

*Tone 6. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Having traversed the deep on foot as on dry land, Israel, beholding the pursuer Pharaoh engulfed, cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God![^533]

*Troparia:*
Drive away the darkness of my understanding by thy prayers, O venerable Parasceve, that I may hymn with songs thy glorious, light-bearing, and blessed memory!

Like a river rushing forth from Zion, O glorious Parasceve, thou didst abundantly water the assemblies of the faithful with divine streams, crying aloud: Let us sing a new song unto the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Having illumined thy soul with divine radiances, O Parasceve, thou didst burn up the voluptuousness of the flesh with the fire of abstinence, singing a true song unto Christ.

*Theotokion:*
With the radiant beams of Thy Son, O Birthgiver of God, enlighten my darkened soul, and by thy mediations, O pure one, calm the turmoil of passions.

*Second Canon, Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Having traversed the water as dry land, and escaped the Egyptian malice, the Israelite cried aloud: Unto our Redeemer and God let us sing![^534]

*Troparia:*
Rejoicing in the heavens, O mother, and standing ever before Christ thy Bridegroom, with the angels, and prophets, and apostles, and with venerable women, pray thou for us!

Having loved fasting and pure humility, O virgin, thou didst make the true God glad: unceasingly pray Him for us!

A joyous voice of Christ came unto Thee as Thou didst make supplication upon earth, O honorable mother, and Thou wast filled with joy, praying for us with songs unto Him!

*Theotokion:*
The angelic ranks and the human generations praise thee unceasingly, O unwedded Virgin: for their Creator hast thou borne upon thine arms as an infant.

*Katavasia: "I shall open my mouth."*

*Canon 1. Ode 3. Heirmos:*
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.[^535]

*Troparia:*
Thou wast filled with the divine Spirit, O wise mother Parasceve, and wast shown forth feeding richly with the sweetness of piety, by the living word, those who gaze upon thee.

With the divine radiance of the sacred commandments thou didst enlighten all, O blessed Parasceve, having driven away all heretical malice, singing unto Christ the Life-giver: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

Thou didst ascend unto the mountain of divine virtues, O Parasceve, and having entered into the cloud of visions, thou wast united unto God, receiving the spiritual tablets of the law of grace.

*Theotokion:*
Behold, the ladder of Jacob, which he once saw set up, upon which God was confirmed, was shown forth light-bearing and pure, being a Virgin.

*Canon 2. Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou art the establishment of those that flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee.[^536]

*Troparia:*
Ask, O glorious Parasceve, bride of Christ, that remission of sins be granted unto those who praise thee in faith.

Fleeing, thou didst depart far off, and didst dwell ever in the wilderness, and in mountains and caves, pleasing God.

Thou didst find Him Whom thou didst love from thy soul, O most honorable Parasceve: a hidden treasure in the breadth of the wilderness.

*Theotokion:*
We Christians have all acquired thee as a refuge and our wall, and unceasingly do we glorify thee, O unwedded Mother.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
Thou didst appear unto the Church like another sun, O venerable Parasceve, illuminating her with the lightnings of thy wonders, and enlightening those who cry aloud unto thee in faith: Rejoice, boast of ascetics, and ornament of virgins! (Twice)[^537]

*Glory... Both now, Theotokion:*
Stretching forth thine all-pure hands, O Virgin Mother, shelter those who hope in thee, and cry aloud unto thy Son: Grant unto all Thy mercies, O Christ!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thy Son lifted up upon the Tree, O all-pure one, torn in maternal womb, sorrowfully didst thou cry aloud: Woe is me! How hast Thou set, O my timeless Light?[^538]

*Canon 1. Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church godly sings, crying aloud from a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord.[^539]

*Troparia:*
Having cleansed thyself through fasting, O venerable Parasceve, thou wast wholly filled with light, and wast wholly shown forth receptive of God, and wholly didst appear a partaker of the Spirit.

Cruelly mortifying with hunger the wicked frolic of youth, O blessed Parasceve, thou didst endure the pains of fasting, rejoicing from conscience in the Lord.

Shining with the righteous deeds of life, O glorious one, thou wast shown forth sharpened like a sword of Christ, cutting down demonic wiles, being strengthened by the divine Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
An all-radiant lampstand, a divine table, a dwelling of God, an ark, and a rod that blossomed forth Christ unto the world, hast thou appeared, O Virgin Mother.

*Canon 2. Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I considered Thy works, and glorified Thy Godhead.[^540]

*Troparion:*
Having made thyself an immaculate mirror and pure in thought, like a luminary shining ever among the ascetics, O glorious one in bodily nakedness, thou wast burned by the heat of the sun for love of thy Bridegroom, and didst endure the coldness of frost.

By all-night vigilance, and fervent prayers and groanings, thou didst wither the motions of the flesh, O all-blessed mother.

*Theotokion:*
Christ, Whom thou didst bear, O all-pure Virgin: pray Him to grant remission of transgressions unto thy servants.

*Canon 1. Ode 5. Heirmos:*
With Thy divine light, O Good One, enlighten with love the souls of those who rise early at dawn unto Thee, I pray Thee, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, as the true God, Who callest them from the darkness of sin.[^541]

*Troparia:*
With thy light-bestowing flashes, O Parasceve, Thou didst destroy the dark gloom of heresy; and being illumined by the light of the knowledge of God, Thou enlightenest the ends of the world.

Having thee all as protectress, worthily do we glorify thee, O Parasceve: for thou wast shown forth a converser with angels, and the adornment of virgins, O venerable mother.

Thou wast shown forth, O mother Parasceve, radiant with light: a guide of the wandering, a pilot of the seafaring, a foundation and unshakable wall of those shaken in perils.

*Theotokion:*
The beauty of Jacob, which God loved and chose to dwell in, the glory of earthborn and refuge of sinners, hast thou appeared, O Birthgiver of God.

*Canon 2. Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Rising early at dawn, we cry aloud unto Thee: O Lord, save us! For Thou art our God, besides Thee we know no other.[^542]

*Troparia:*
Fleeing the things of this day and world that are opposed, thou didst not desire to love the lustful demon's desires.

Thinking to overcome thy love and purity, the all-crafty one became a laughing-stock with his wiles.

They buried thy body as incorruptible: for as one bodiless didst thou live, O bride of God!

*Theotokion:*
We hymn thee as Virgin after childbirth, O Birthgiver of God: for in the flesh thou didst bear God unto the world.

*Canon 1. Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven I cry aloud unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Most Merciful One![^543]

*Troparion:*
Thou shinest like the sun, emitting God-illumined beams, O all-blessed Parasceve O Parasceve, pouring forth wonders unto those who praise thy labors of fasting and marvelous struggles!

Praising the all-pure, good Birthgiver of God honorably with thy tongue, O God-wise Parasceve, singing radiant words, thou didst wisely move the hearts of the assemblies of the faithful to compunction.

The inscribed, all-honorable, and holy icon dost thou lovingly kiss; and the writings of the godless dost thou destroy, spitting upon their God-hated and godless decrees, proclaiming the honor and veneration of the icon of Christ.[^544]

*Theotokion:*
God, Who by His will constituted all things and sustains the universe, is held in the depiction of the body in thine arms, O all-pure one, He Who by His divine essence is uncircumscribed.

*Canon 2. Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I declare my griefs; for my soul is filled with evils, and my life has drawn near unto Hades; and like Jonah I pray: Lead me up from corruption, O God![^545]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst trample underfoot worldly turmoils, and standest before the Creator of the world, having taken flight on gold-adorned wings from the labors of fasting and prayers, O mother blessed of God!

Because of thy God-pleasing life, O most honorable Parasceve, thou art granted unto thy homeland as a consecrated gift, having come unto the West, O all-glorious one![^546]

Having desired an ineffable life from thy youth, hidden invisible in the earth, coming forth, O venerable one, thou wast a marvel, God having revealed Thee!

*Theotokion:*
May we escape from cruel transgressions by thy prayers, O pure Birthgiver of God, and may we attain the divine radiance of the Son of God, Who was ineffably incarnate of thee!

*Kontakion, Tone 6:*
Let us all piously hymn the holy protectress of those in perils, the all-honorable Parasceve: for leaving behind corruptible life, she received incorruptible life unto the ages; therefore did she find glory, and the grace of wonders by the divine decree.[^547]

*Ikos:*
Come, ye assembly of ascetics; come, ye lovers of purity; come, ye feast-lovers of every age: with spiritual songs let us praise the wilderness-loving turtledove, the boast of ascetics, the adornment of virgins, the beauty of hesychasts, the consoler of the sorrowing, the visitor of the sick, and the speedy helper of those in perils, the God-wise Parasceve, who pours forth healings unto all[^548] healings: for she has found the grace of wonders by the divine decree!

*Canon 1. Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Angel made the furnace pour forth dew for the holy Children, but the command of God consuming the Chaldeans persuaded the tyrant to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^549]

*Troparia:*
Fulfilling the word of Christ the Bridegroom, thou didst follow Him with irrevocable desire, O Parasceve, named for the Lord, crying aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

Perishable beauties didst thou despise, O venerable Parasceve, looking unto the recompense that is yonder; and thou didst receive ageless glory, divine beauty and gladness.

With the rivers of thy honey-flowing words, O Parasceve, thou fillest with divine gladness the souls of men who glorify the all-praised God with hymns.

*Theotokion:*
A great mystery was wrought in thee, O Virgin Mother: the Son of God was incarnate of thy womb and appeared unto all; unto Whom rejoicing we sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Canon 2. Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Hebrew Children boldly trampled upon the flame in the furnace and changed the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages![^550]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst set like the sun, O venerable mother, yet thou enlightenest by wonders those who sing with warm faith and love: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

All lands were filled with thy sweet fragrance at the finding of thy relics, whereunto thou commandest us to sing in faith, O venerable one: Blessed is the God of our fathers![^551]

Thou art found to be a restoration of the lame, O mother: for he who limped in his leg, leaping, cried aloud, shouting unceasingly: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Behold, her whom the great Isaiah foretold through the Holy Spirit: having conceived God in thy womb, thou didst give birth, and blessed is the fruit of thy womb, O all-pure one!

*Canon 1. Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Out of the flame didst Thou pour forth dew for the venerable ones, and by water didst Thou burn up the sacrifice of the righteous: for Thou doest all things, O Christ, simply by willing them; Thee do we supremely exalt unto all ages![^552]`

*Troparia:*
Keeping in truth the ancestral law before thine eyes, O all-blessed one, thou didst spit upon the faces of demons, piously kissing the representation of the Lord, having received which, thou wast shown forth in the world as a virgin maiden: Whom we supremely exalt unto all ages!

A divine mind, a well-tuned psaltery of the All-Holy Spirit, a melodious nightingale, the supreme boast of the faithful, a pillar of Orthodoxy and wise support, a canon of the Church, glory of monastics, and beauty of all the venerable wast thou shown forth, singing unto Christ unto the ages.

A vessel of the myrrh of the All-Holy Spirit didst Thou appear, O mother, by the pouring out of the myrrh of thy fragrant and wise teachings, destroying the foul corruption of deceit, and perfuming the precious Church by thy character, O Parasceve, calling out unceasingly: With songs we supremely exalt Thee, O Christ, unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
A fertile mountain of God, which the Prophet David anciently made clear under prophetic inspiration, wast thou shown forth, O Virgin, from which was cut out the cornerstone Christ, and He shattered the power of idols: Whom unceasingly we hymn and supremely exalt unto all ages.

*Canon 2. Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The Heavenly King, Whom the angelic hosts hymn, praise ye and supremely exalt unto all ages![^553]

*Troparia:*
Having strengthened thyself in mind through desire of the bodiless ones, thou didst surrender thy body unto fasting, and thou livest unto the ages.

Surpassing rich among women didst thou appear, O blessed Parasceve, singing unto the Creator: Thee do we supremely exalt unto all ages!

Unto him that was paralyzed for many years, the precious one granted healing of the infirmity; therefore did he hymn thee, singing unto all ages.[^554]

*Theotokion:*
The Lord Who descended from heaven and dwelt in the virginal womb: hymn and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Canon 1. Ode 9. Heirmos:*
It is impossible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the Word Incarnate was shown forth unto men: Whom magnifying with the heavenly hosts, we call thee blessed.[^555]

*Troparia:*
The murderer of men, unable to bear thee standing firm for the Church of Christ, extended temptations unto thee: yet remaining unyielding, thou didst valiantly resist the spiritual foes, venerating the icon of Christ, O God-wise Parasceve!

Adorned with ineffable valor, O God-wise one, and arrayed with the brightest radiances, joyfully, O all-blessed one, dost thou stand before the throne of God the Creator, where the sound of those that keep festival voice of those that keep festival is heard, O God-wise Parasceve!

Thou hast received the worthy honor of thy struggles, and now crowned in the heavens, rejoicing with the angels, where is the assembly of the prophets, and of the apostles, and in truth the many armies of the martyrs, where are the choirs of the patriarchs: remember us, O venerable mother Parasceve!

*Theotokion:*
Fighting on behalf of faithful rulers in battles, grant victories unto our King over adversaries, O Birthgiver of God, shielding us from every assault of enemies, O pure Mother of God the Word that knewest not wedlock: that singing unto Thy Son, we may magnify thee.[^556]

*Canon 2. Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Truly we confess thee as the Birthgiver of God, saved through thee, O pure Virgin, with the bodiless choirs magnifying thee.[^557]

*Troparia:*
Cease not praying unto Christ, O venerable mother, to deliver us Thy servants from perils, who from the soul hymn and magnify thee!

Thou wast shown forth as the beauty of virgins, O venerable one, for thou didst emulate the angelic life: cease not praying for thy homeland!

Thou hast been united with the bodiless choirs, and standest rejoicing before the Master with the wise virgins, praying unto Him for the world.

*Theotokion:*
Fill me with joy and gladness, O Virgin, who didst receive the Cause of joy: for thou hast poured forth rejoicing unto all.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жєны̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите):*
As divine light, O Parasceve, thou didst hymnically appear unto the Church, having illumined this beauty with the light of thy countenance, O most good bride of Christ: pray unceasingly unto Him for all of us! (Twice)

*Glory... Both now, Theotokion:*
Having defiled the good garment of baptism anciently granted unto me with passionate serving, I am terrified, wretch that I am, truly contemplating the hour of divine judgment, O all-immaculate Virgin: stand by me then, and deliver me from the dreadful rebuke![^558]

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
Come, all ye God-wise ones from a far country, touching the reliquary of the venerable one, offering your petitions from the soul: receive deliverance from diverse passions, remission of sins, appropriation unto God, and every benefit unto us for salvation! (Twice)

Having undertaken the path of fasting, fleeing the worldly tempests, O glorious Parasceve: on the one hand, with the wise virgins, light-bearing didst thou enter into the courts of thy Lord; and on the other hand, having been betrothed unto Him, thou pourest forth healings unto those who hasten unto thee, and deliverest from soul-afflicting infirmities by thy prayers unto God!

Having prevailed through hope in Christ and through faith, leaving behind the possessions of thy parents, thou makest merry with the bodiless hosts, O venerable mother, glorious Parasceve! Therefore dost thou pour forth healing after death: for thy relics were shown forth full of every fragrance, which Christ granted unto thee, Whom thou didst love, Whom thou didst desire; and He glorified thee, O venerable one!

*Glory... Tone 8:*
Leaving behind thy homeland, and kin, and possessions, O Parasceve, and hating all worldly impieties, thou didst love God alone, unto Whom by fasting thou wast betrothed, and thou becamest a dwelling-place of the Holy Spirit: enlightened by Whose radiance, pray thou to Him to save our souls![^559]

*Both now, Theotokion:*
O Sovereign Lady, receive the prayer of Thy servants.[^560]

*The Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*
*If we do not chant the Polyeleos, then we chant the Aposticha in the Oktoechos. Glory to the venerable one, Tone 2:*
O venerable mother Parasceve, having from thy youth entrusted all thy care unto Christ God, and strengthened by Him, thou didst subdue the carnal passions, and didst enslave the worse unto the better: therefore hast thou received a threefold-woven crown; pray thou that our souls may be saved!

*Both now, Theotokion; or Stavrotheotokion, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
When the spotless Ewe-lamb beheld her Lamb willingly led to the slaughter as a man, weeping she said: Dost Thou hasten now to leave Me childless, O Christ, Who gave Thee birth? What is this that Thou hast done, O Redeemer of all? Yet do I hymn and glorify Thine utter goodness which surpasses mind and word, O Lover of mankind![^561]

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes, from the first canon, Ode 3; and from the second, Ode 6. Prokeimenon, Tone 4: "Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel."*
*Epistle to the Galatians, section 208 [Gal. 3:23–29]:* Brethren Before faith came...[^562] Alleluia: With patience I waited for the Lord, and He gave ear unto me.[^563] Gospel according to Matthew, section 104 [Mt. 25:1–13]: The Lord said this parable: The kingdom of heaven is likened unto ten virgins... Communion Hymn: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous![^564]

*In the same month, on the 15th day. The Venerable Father our Euthymius the New; and the Holy Venerable-Martyr Lucian, Presbyter of Great Antioch:*[^565]

*At "Lord, I Have Cried," Stichera of the venerable one, Tone 1, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Along the narrow path didst thou walk with an untroubled spirit, crossing the waters unto life: for thou wast the boast of ascetics, O Euthymius, patiently casting down the snares of demons; therefore didst thou attain to become an heir of the heavenly kingdom, and thou delightest in unceasing beauty.

Thy most radiant life astonished angels, and openly terrified the fierce rage of demons: and it radiantly adorned the assemblies of the faithful, commanding them to walk always toward Christ's pure dwelling: Whom entreat thou to deliver from corruption and perils those who in faith observe thy most honorable memory!

Singing of hymns and praise didst thou offer daily unto Christ, O father, dwelling in the wilderness; having committed thy soul and thought unto God alone, like great Moses thou didst enter into the impenetrable darkness; therefore, having conquered the invisible foe, thou becamest a pure receptacle and an instrument of the Divine Spirit.

*Other Stichera of the martyr, Tone the same [Tone 1], Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Richly adorned with divine knowledge, enriched with faith, thou didst strengthen the faithful boldly to endure intricately-woven tortures and the rage of the tormentor for the sake of the future life and incorruptible joy: therefore do we call thee blessed, O worthy of glory, Lucian and today we celebrate thy divine festival!

A long imprisonment and a most violent death didst thou endure, O venerable one: bound with bonds, and pierced with sharp potsherds, O blessed one, tormented by cruel hunger and protracted thirst; therefore did the radiant food openly receive thee as an invincible martyr, O valiant passion-bearer!

The bosom of the sea received thee, O glorious one, yet after thirty days cast thee forth, as Jonah anciently, serving toward thy godly burial, O divine outpouring of healings, excellent leader of martyrs, confirmation of the Church, intercessor for our souls, O most wealthy God-bearer![^566]

*Glory... Tone 6:*
O venerable father, into all the earth has the sound of thy right deeds gone forth; therefore hast thou found in the heavens the reward of thy labors; thou didst destroy legions of demons and attained unto the angelic ranks, whose blameless life thou didst emulate: having boldness before Christ God, entreat peace for our souls![^567]

*Both now, Theotokion; or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́венъ):*
Beholding Thee crucified, O Christ, she who gave Thee birth cried aloud: What strange mystery is this that I behold, O my Son? How dost Thou die in the flesh, hanging upon the tree, O Giver of life?[^568]

*At the Aposticha, of the Oktoechos. Glory... Tone 5:*
O venerable father, thou gavest no sleep unto thine eyes, nor slumber unto thine eyelids, until thou didst free soul and body from passions, and preparedst thyself as a dwelling of the Spirit: for Christ, coming with the Father, made an abode in thee; and thou wast a servant of the Consubstantial Trinity, O great-preacher Euthymius: pray for our souls![^569]

*Both now, Theotokion; or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ):*
The Ewe-lamb, once beholding her Lamb hastening unto the slaughter, diligently followed, crying aloud these things unto Him: Whither goest Thou, my sweetest Child, O Christ? Why dost Thou run this swift course, O Long-suffering One, unsluggishly? O Jesus most desired, sinless, most merciful Lord: grant a word unto Thy handmaid, O my most beloved Son; pass me not by in silence, O Compassionate One, who marvelously gave Thee birth: O all-compassionate God, grant great mercy unto the world![^570]

*Troparion of the venerable one, Tone 8:*
In thee, O father, was preserved with exactness that which is according to the image: for taking up the cross, thou didst follow Christ, and by thy deeds didst teach us to overlook the flesh, for it passes away, but to care for the soul, an immortal thing; therefore, O venerable Euthymius, thy spirit rejoices together with the angels.[^571]

*Glory... Troparion of the martyr, Tone 4:*
Thy martyr, O Lord, Lucian, in his suffering received an incorruptible crown from Thee, our God: for having Thy strength, he cast down the tormentors and shattered the powerless insolence of demons: by his supplications save our souls![^572]

*Both now, Theotokion.*
*At Matins, one Canon of the Oktoechos, and two of the Saints.*
*Canon of the venerable one, whose acrostic is: "Rejoice, divine glory of monastics." Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Come, ye peoples, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He led forth from the bondage of Egypt, for He is glorified![^573]

*Troparia:*
Rejoice, O fount welling up with a saving stream, from which we all draw healing grace for transgressions, who with love come unto thee, O ever-memorable one!

Having received holiness from Christ God from the womb, O glorious one, thou wast shown forth as an elect vessel, receiving divine grace of the Spirit, O father Euthymius!

That thou mightest appear a partaker of incorruptible life, O most wise father, thou didst despise temporal life; and approaching unto God, thou entreatest unceasingly that we all may be saved.

*Theotokion:*
Following the wise utterance of thy lips, rejoicing we call thee blessed, O all-immaculate one, who becamest for us the cause of ineffable blessedness.

*Another Canon of the martyr, whose acrostic is: "Thy glory, O all-blessed Lucian, do I sing." The composition of Joseph. Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
I will sing unto Thee, O Lord my God, for Thou didst lead forth the people from the bondage of Egypt, and didst cover the chariots of Pharaoh and his power.[^574]

*Troparia:*
Shining forth with wisdom, and grace, and spiritual power, O Lucian, thou didst enlighten the people, and didst guide them unto the knowledge of Christ, O martyr!

Shielding thee with the weapon of true faith, He Who bore our infirmity as compassionate made thee invincible, casting down the roots of the enemies unto the heights, from the depths of heresy, O sufferer, thou didst draw up those that were fallen, by the nets of thy divine words, O worthy of marvel!

*Theotokion:*
The co-eternal Word of the Father, beyond cause and speech, by the Word didst thou conceive without seed, O pure one, and gavest birth unto Him for our deliverance.

*Canon 1. Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Establishing me upon the rock of faith, Thou hast enlarged my mouth against my enemies; for my spirit rejoiced in singing: There is none holy as our God, and none righteous besides Thee, O Lord![^575]

*Troparia:*
A most radiant pillar wast thou shown forth, O God-wise one, guiding the ranks of monastics: a cloud overshadowing the portion of God, and darkening the faces of invisible enemies by the grace of the King of all.

Thou standest before the face of thy Master, with unslacking nod entreating that thy flock be delivered from the bondage of darkness, O venerable one, and from the torment of passions; and God listens to thy prayers.

Having put to death the motions of thy flesh by steadfast fasting, O God-proclaimer, thou becamest a dwelling of the Master: entreat Him to deliver from afflictions and the disturbance of passions those who hasten unto thee!

*Theotokion:*
Isaiah foresaw thee as a scroll, wherein the Word was bodily inscribed, tearing up the handwriting of the first-created which he signed through transgression, and granted us remission from bondage.

*Canon 2. Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The bow of the mighty has grown weak, and the infirm have girded themselves with strength; therefore is my heart established in the Lord.[^576]

*Troparia:*
Through the radiance of virtues, O martyr, thou didst perfect thy soul as a house for God, and didst tear down the temples of idols by thy prayer.

Encompassed by tribulations, O martyr, and afflicted by the pains of torments, thou didst hymn Him Who deemed thee worthy of such good things as a Benefactor.

Surrendering thy body unto torments, O martyr, thou didst preserve thy soul unstumbling: thou didst offer thyself as a blameless sacrifice unto the Master, O all-honorable one!

*Theotokion:*
An Infant is born of the Virgin, He Who by His will created the ages: Whom we hymn as the Birthgiver of God, and righteously call blessed.

*Kontakion of the martyr, Tone 2, Podoben: "In prayers" (Въ мл҃твахъ):*

...Shining forth first in fasting, and secondly radiant in suffering, let us all gloriously honor Lucian with hymns as a most radiant beacon, who prays unceasingly for all of us![^577]

*Sessional Hymn of the venerable one, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having renounced the flesh for the sake of Christ, and having appeared as a spiritual leader of the people, thou didst hear the words of secret teachings: thou wast shown forth as a rule of uprightness for monks, and didst steadfastly trample underfoot all passions; therefore didst thou utterly burn to ashes the craftiness of demons, looking ever unto Him, O God-bearer Euthymius: pray thou unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love![^578]

*Glory... Sessional Hymn of the martyr, Tone the same [Tone 8]:*
Having anointed the hearts of the faithful with the word of grace, O martyr Lucian, thou didst dispose them to suffer and to conquer the tormentor; and having well finished the divine course, after the end thou wast deemed worthy of the glory of martyrs: therefore, having come together, we honor thy holy end, glorifying the Savior, O all-praised passion-bearer: pray thou unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
O cloud of the spiritual Sun, golden-wrought lamp of the Divine Light: spotless, unstumbling, all-immaculate Sovereign Lady, I pray thee, enlighten with the beam of dispassion my soul that is darkened by the blindness of passions; and wash my defiled heart with streams of compunction and tears of repentance, and cleanse me from the filth of my deeds: that with love I may cry unto thee: O Ever-Virgin Birthgiver of God, pray thou unto Christ God to grant me remission of transgressions, for I thy servant place my hope in thee.[^579]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The Ewe-lamb, beholding upon the Cross the Lamb, and Shepherd, and Redeemer, exclaimed weeping, and bitterly lamenting cried aloud: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee, but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of Thy loving-kindness, O Long-suffering Lord! O Abyss of mercy and inexhaustible Fount: have compassion, and grant remission of transgressions unto those who sing in faith of Thy divine passions![^580]

*Canon 1. Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I sing unto Thee, for by report I heard of Thee, O Lord, and was afraid; for unto me didst Thou come, seeking me that was gone astray: therefore do I glorify Thy great condescension toward me, O Most Merciful One![^581]

*Troparia:*
Divine desire inflamed thee, warming thy heart, and thou didst attain unto desired things to the end, O God-wise father, leaving behind attachment to earthly things, and becoming a fellow-dweller of the supreme ministers.

The flatteries of thy spouse didst thou trample underfoot in every way, O venerable one: for the love of the Creator entered warmly into thee, O God-wise one, and utterly cast away the dark desires of created things.[^582]

Unto a pure pasture didst thou lead the flock of thy self-control, O father, and broughtest it unto the untroubled fountain of dispassion, conquering spiritual wild beasts, which save thou even now by thy prayers!

*Theotokion:*
Behold, generations of generations glorify thee, as thou didst foretell, O Maiden: for thou wast the palace and divine temple of the Creator, wherein the Most High dwelt, wrapping Himself in flesh, that He might save us.

*Canon 2. Ode 4. Heirmos:*
He Who sits in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, upon a light cloud, Jesus the most divine came with incorrupt hand, and saved those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ![^583]

*Troparia:*
Thou wast shown forth steadfast against deceit, O blessed one, and ever a teacher of many: and through the patient endurance of torments, O martyr, thou didst humble the powerless strength of the proud one.

Stretched upon a tree and racked with thongs, tormented with hunger and thirst for a long time, and pierced with fine potsherds, thou didst endure, O worthy of glory, martyr of Christ!

Proclaiming the divine law, thou didst turn the lawless, and didst show them how to drink the cup of martyrdom eagerly: with whom thou hast taken up dwelling in the divine courts, being filled from the tree of life, O ever-memorable one!

*Theotokion:*
Shining forth from thy womb, the divine Sun enlightened those in the darkness of polytheism, O Maiden, and illuminated those who were in the shadow of death: unto Whom we cry aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!

*Canon 1. Ode 5. Heirmos:*
O Christ my Savior, the Enlightenment of those lying in darkness, the Salvation of the despairing: rising early at dawn unto Thee, O King of peace, enlighten me with Thy radiance: for another God besides Thee I know not.[^584]

Wholly clinging unto God, O father, in the wildernesses thou didst valiantly endure nocturnal frost, and bearing the burning heat of the day, thou wast a house of God's wisdom, adorned in diverse ways with the radiance of virtues.

Youthfully trampling upon lusts, thou didst not accept the serpent as a counselor of gluttony, O father: but by fasting and unceasing prayers, and by purity being united unto God, thou becamest a partaker of the better sweetness.

Ascending upon the clouds of virtues, O blessed one, thou didst receive the tablets written by the hand of God; and thou didst teach multitudes of monastics to refrain from all material things, leading them up unto the mountain of divine knowledge.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst appear as a pure ladder, O Birthgiver of God, higher than the heavens, having the Creator seated upon thee: for from thee God was shown forth incarnate, and united what before was separated; therefore do we faithful glorify thee as the Birthgiver of God.

*Canon 2. Ode 5. Heirmos:*
The ungodly shall not see Thy glory, O Christ, but rising early at dawn from the night, we hymn Thee, O Only-begotten One, Radiance of the Father's glory of the Godhead, O Lover of mankind![^585]

*Troparia:*
Having consumed the thorns of passions, O venerable one, with the sweat of fasts: with the streams of thy labors thou didst valiantly extinguish the furnace of impiety, O all-blessed one!

Remaining unyielding when encompassed by frequent afflictions, looking unto God Who is able to save, O holy one, into His hands thou didst commit thy spirit rejoicing!

Thou didst rebuke the ill-believing who had estranged themselves from God, and encompassed by long and cruel infirmities, O glorious one, thou drewest near unto Christ, Whose passion thou didst depict!

*Theotokion:*
After childbirth thou didst remain incorrupt, O pure one, and didst deliver the human race from corruption, as having given birth unto God: therefore in faith do we orthodoxly call thee blessed.

*Canon 1. Ode 6. Heirmos:*
From the whale Jonah cried aloud unto the Lord: Lead me up from the depth of Hades, I pray Thee, that unto Thee as Deliverer, with the voice of praise and the spirit of truth, I may sacrifice![^586]

*Troparia:*
Urged on by the love of the Master, thou didst leave behind all sensible things, and followedst Him, taking His light yoke manfully upon thy shoulders, O namesake of benevolence![^587]

Instructed in the knowledge of ineffable things, O blessed one, thou didst teach thy flock to mind heavenly things, driving away the assaults of passions by thy teachings.

Thy life, O father, was like unto the supreme powers; it gave thee boldness, which thou didst hold fast always: pray thou unto Christ to deliver thy flock from passions!

*Theotokion:*
Understanding thine ineffable mystery, O pure one, Habakkuk foretold thee as an overshadowed mountain: for from thee the Creator, being incarnate through tender mercy, took away the ancient sentence.[^588]

*Canon 2. Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that for mercy's sake flowed from Thy side.[^589]

*Troparia:*
Dry-shod didst thou traverse the deep of torments: and by the all-powerful right hand thy body was saved from the depth, visibly unhurt for those that love thee, O blessed of God![^590]

He Who delivered Jonah from the belly of the whale after three days, the sea-beasts serving Him, also saves thee from the depths of the sea after thirty days!

Inflamed with the love of Christ, O glorious one, unto death didst thou stand against the tormentors, O all-wise one: therefore wast thou deemed worthy of the heavenly kingdom, rejoicing!

*Theotokion:*
The most pure Word found thee as a pure dove, and the only undefiled one, and was well-pleased to be born of thee, O all-holy Mother of God, all-immaculate Virgin.

*Kontakion of the venerable one, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Passing unstumblingly through the multitude of surging billows, with the streams of thy tears thou didst steadfastly drown the bodiless foes, O God-wise Euthymius, venerable one: and having received the gift of wonders, thou healest all manner of passions: pray unceasingly for all of us![^591]

*Ikos:*
Open my mouth, O thrice-blessed one, by thy acceptable prayers, standing before God, O all-glorious Euthymius: that I may hymn thy divine life, and worthily tell of thy virtues, which Thou didst accomplish on earth for the sake of Christ God, O ascetic! For Thou wast shown forth as a most radiant pillar and rule of monks; having acquired an angelic life, Thou didst truly take up dwelling in the impenetrable darkness: pray unceasingly for all of us![^592]

*Canon 1. Ode 7. Heirmos:*
