The unburned bush upon the mountain, and the dew-bearing Chaldean furnace, clearly depicted thee beforehand, O Bride of God: for the divine and immaterial Fire in a material womb[^593] womb, thou didst receive the Fire unconsumed; therefore unto Him Who was born of thee do we sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^594]

*Troparia:*
Having acquired the character of Abraham, O all-wise one, thou becamest an exile from thy land, when thou didst behold Him Who promised thee a rich reward; therefore, arising, thou didst fervently sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Inflamed by the Spirit, O venerable one, thou didst destroy the snares of the enemy, and didst gain the mastery of victory, being shielded by the weapon of Him Who for our sake was nailed again to the Cross; therefore with gladness thou didst fervently sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Thou didst draw near unto the mountain of prayer, and having entered into the cloud of the Spirit, thou didst see the back parts of God,[^595] and receiving the glory of discernment, thou becamest, O father, an unshakable pillar; therefore diligently pray that they who sing to thee may be delivered from the temptation of the enemy!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst give birth to the Fulfiller of the Law, the Lord of all things before the ages, the Son, both Wisdom and Power: in the latter times, O most pure one, supernaturally in the flesh for the renewal of earthly beings; therefore piously, O pure Virgin, do we call Him blessed Who was born of thee: O God of our fathers!

*Canon 2. Ode 7. Heirmos:*
He Who saved Thy Abrahamic children in the fire, and slew the Chaldeans whom justice righteously snared: O praised Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou![^596]

*Troparia:*
The persecutor, unable to endure the rushing torrent of thy wise words, O martyr, puts thee to death and casts thee into the depth of the sea, mediating for thee the highest things, as well as the immutable kingdom, O valiantly wise one, and divine glory.[^597]

Having neither bowed down unto graven images nor bent thy knees unto idols, cast into the fire of afflictions, thou didst enter into the furnace of torment, dewed by the Spirit with dew-giving power, O God-wise martyr!

Thou didst draw forth by thy life-giving words the people who were deadened and engulfed in the abyss of Hellenic godlessness, O wondrously worthy chanter: O praised Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou![^598]

*Theotokion:*
Blessed is the Fruit of thy blessed womb, Whom the heavenly powers and the assemblies of the earthly bless, Who delivered us from the ancient curse, O blessed one!

*Canon 1. Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Unto God Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew youths, and changed the flame into dew: sing of Him, all ye works, as the Lord, and exalt Him supreme unto all ages![^599]

*Troparia:*
With the streams of thy tears thou didst burn to ashes fiery pleasures, and didst drive away the fear of the Gehenna flame, O wise father, having blended with the fear of thy Creator in perfect love unto all ages.

Translated unto God even as Enoch,[^600] thou wast received by Him, O father; and dwelling together with the heavenly choirs, thou singest: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him supreme unto all ages!

Now enjoying incorruptible gifts and being filled with the triple-radiant splendor, O blessed one: deliver thy singers from passions and darkness by thy prayers, that we may worthily glorify Christ!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst loose the ancient curse of earthly beings, O all-pure one, having conceived by the ineffable Word God incarnate from thee through tender mercy, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady, Whom we exalt supreme unto all ages!

*Canon 2. Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The Offspring of the Birthgiver of God saved the pious youths in the furnace: then indeed prefigured, but now in truth accomplished, He moves the whole world to sing unto Thee: Sing unto the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him supreme unto all ages![^601]

*Troparia:*
Those wasted away with spiritual hunger thou didst strengthen with the bread of thy words, teaching them to honor the only Lord, the Bread of our life, O glorious one; unto Whom we all sing: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him supreme unto all ages!

Having trampled underfoot the cruel machinations of the deceiving enemy by thy suffering, thou didst ascend unto glorious heights, O worthy of glory, and didst attain unto the angelic ranks, crying aloud: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him supreme unto all ages!

O the marvelous deeds accomplished in thee, O most patient Lucian! For the bosom of the sea, having received thy holy body for a sufficient time, harmed it not, but yields it whole unto dry land, pouring forth streams of healings unto those who love thee![^602]

*Theotokion:*
With tongue and heart, O Virgin, do we proclaim thee pure Mother of God: for thou didst give birth unto the Lord, robed in flesh and known in two natures; Whom all the heavenly powers glorify unto all ages!

*Canon 1. Ode 9. Heirmos:*

God the Word of God, Who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam, who had grievously fallen into corruption through eating: Who for our sake was ineffably incarnate of the Holy Virgin, we the faithful magnify with songs in one accord![^603]

*Troparia:*
Illumined with the beauty of fasting, O father, and clothed with the robe of incorruption, O Euthymius, and crowned with a garland of gifts passing understanding, thou standest rejoicing before the face of all!

Thou didst take up dwelling in the ever-living mansions, O blessed one, where is the assembly of the Apostles, the multitude of martyrs, the gathering of the venerable, and the choirs of the prophets: worthily receiving from God the recompense of thy afflictions, O God-wise and worthy of glory!

Thou becamest a beacon, O speaker of God, brightly illuminated by the beams of the Triple-sun Light; entreat remission of sins, deliverance from afflictions, and estrangement from evils for all who run with love unto thee!

*Theotokion:*
The Master of all was ineffably incarnate of thee, O Birthgiver of God Mary, having become perfect Man; and He was born, even as before childbirth He preserved thee a pure Virgin: Whom entreat to save our souls from perils!

*Canon 2. Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Eve indeed through the disease of disobedience brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Mother of God, through the flourishing of thy pregnancy, didst blossom forth blessing upon the world: therefore do we all magnify thee![^604]

*Troparia:*
Thy sacred memory shines forth, O wise one, driving away the cloud of passions by the beams of the Spirit, illuminating the assemblies of the faithful, and consuming the demonic hosts: preserve us who celebrate it!

Thou wast loosed from bodily bonds, O blessed one, and rejoicing didst take flight unto the heavenly height, and appearedst adorned with the beauties of wounds before God the Creator; before Whom thou standest rejoicing with the martyrs!

Lucian the martyr hath gathered us together today to praise his sufferings, his radiant struggles, his courage unto the end, and the abundant grace of wonders: him do we all bless in one accord!

*Theotokion:*
A vessel of transcendent wisdom surpassing understanding wast thou shown forth, O pure Maiden: both an ensouled throne and a gate; therefore do the assemblies of men and the angelic ranks on high glorify thee, the only one surpassing all!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben (Подо́бенъ):*

*Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Же́ны ᲂу҆слы́шите):*
Having fled worldly attachment as grievous, and having withered the flesh by poverty of possessions: thou didst renew the strength of thy soul, O venerable one, and wast enriched with heavenly glory, O ever-memorable one; therefore, cease not to pray unto the Lord for us![^605]

*Theotokion:*
Awe-inspiring is thy intercession, O Mary adorned of God, and dreadful is thy glory to all the earth, O Birthgiver of God: for in thee now do we boast, and having thee as mediatress unto thy Son and Creator, by thy sleepless prayer may we all be saved!

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos.*

*Glory... Tone 2:*
Venerable father, having diligently practiced virtue from infancy, thou wast an instrument of the Holy Spirit: and having received from Him the working of wonders, thou didst persuade men to disregard pleasant things; and now being more purely illumined by the divine Light, enlighten also our thoughts, O father Euthymius![^606]

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
Beholding Thee nailed unto the Tree of the Cross, O Jesus, she that knew not wedlock, weeping, said: O sweet Child, why hast Thou left me alone who gave birth to Thee, O unapproachable Light of the Father without beginning? Hasten and be glorified, that they who glorify Thy divine sufferings may receive divine glory![^607]

*On the same month, on the 16th day. The Holy Martyr Longinus the Centurion, who stood by the Cross of the Lord:*[^608]

*At "Lord, I have cried", Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
O all-lauded martyr Longinus, by the purple dyeing of thy blood thou didst color for thyself a saving garment; by painful sufferings having stripped naked the multi-scheming one, thou dwellest now in the kingdom on high, crowned with a victorious crown as conqueror; therefore do we honor thy glorious memory!

O all-lauded martyr Longinus, having beheld upon the Cross the Son of God suffering willingly and tasting of death, when thou didst also see the sun darkening its light and the mountains trembling: then didst thou cry aloud unto the assembly of the Jews: Truly this is the Son of God, and the Lord and King of all![^609]

The Jewish people, the most lawless synagogue, an ungrateful nation, cut off thy head with a sword and cast it into a dunghill; and a blind woman running thither and taking it up receives the light; O blessed and all-lauded Longinus, pray thou unto the Lord for us, that we may be saved![^610]

*Glory... Tone 6, by John the Monk:*
At Thy Passion, O Christ, Longinus the centurion, beholding the temple veil rent in twain, proclaimed unto the Jews that Thou art the Son of God; therefore the cruel ones, having cut off his divine head with a sword, cast it into a dunghill; but a woman having found it opened the shut pupils of her eyes; with her we also cry aloud unto Thee: O Thou Who didst crown him who suffered for Thee, by his prayers enlighten also the eyes of our heart to glorify Thee as God, Who wast nailed to the Cross in the flesh in order to save us![^611]

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having set aside all" (Все ѿло́жше):*
When the spotless Ewe-lamb of old and immaculate Sovereign Lady beheld her Lamb upon the Tree of the Cross, crying out with maternal voice and marveling, she cried aloud: O most sweet Child, what is this new and most glorious sight? How hath the ungrateful gathering delivered Thee unto the tribunal of Pilate, and condemned unto death the Life of all? Yet I hymn Thy ineffable condescension, O Word![^612]

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos.*

*Glory... of the martyr, Tone 6, by John the Monk:*
Standing by the Cross and observing what was taking place, seeing God and Man crucified upon the Tree, thou didst cry aloud unto Him: Remember me, O Lord, in Thy kingdom! Therefore also the Savior called out unto thee: Blessed art thou, Longinus, and thy memory is unto generation and generation![^613]

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Lamenting Thee crucified, O Christ, she that gave birth unto Thee cried aloud: What is this strange mystery that I see, O my Son? How diest Thou upon the Tree, suspended in the flesh, O Giver of life?[^614]

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyr, O Lord, Longinus, in his suffering received an incorruptible crown from Thee, our God: for having Thy strength, he cast down the tormentors and crushed the powerless insolence of demons: by his prayers save our souls![^615]

*At Matins, both canons of the Octoechos, and the canon of the saint, whose acrostic is: "I will hymn the great glory of Longinus. Joseph." Tone 4.*[^616]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The Israel of old, traversing the deep of the Red Sea with unwetted feet, conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness through the cross-shaped hands of Moses.[^617]

*Troparia:*
Illumined by divine radiance, O glorious one, and standing before Christ God with the angelic ranks, enlighten those who celebrate thy light-bearing memory!

Nothing separated thee from union with God, O martyr, since by faith thou didst cut off all carnal pleasures: and thou purely enjoyest the utmost of desire!

Serving the living God with purity of mind, O all-wise one, thou didst in no wise offer worship unto dead idols, O blessed one; therefore, put to death, thou hast passed over unto unending life!

*Theotokion:*
In thy womb, by a word, thou didst conceive the Word of the Father, Who in His goodness was pleased to deliver earthly beings from unreason, O pure Virgin Mother, blessed and all-immaculate!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and my refuge and foundation![^618]

*Troparia:*
Having recognized upon the Cross the Son of God, the true Lord, suffering for our sake, in Him with all thy soul didst thou believe.[^619]

Thy soul, like a well-watered earth, having received the rational seeds, O wise one, hath brought forth a ripe ear of divine piety!

Having crossed the tempestuous deep of tortures with the sail of the Cross, O glorious one, rejoicing thou didst reach calm havens!

*Theotokion:*
Heal my soul, sickened by the assaults of passions, O all-pure Mother of God, and direct it unto salvation!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Thou didst behold the Redeemer crucified, and being illuminated by His rays, thou didst flee the deepest darkness of folly, and didst unite thyself unto the divine understanding, believing that He Who was visible is God; therefore also having suffered, O blessed Longinus, thou wast numbered rejoicing among the ranks of martyrs: with them ever pray unto Christ God, O glorious one, to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love![^620]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Having fallen into multifaceted perils from enemies visible and invisible, held fast by the tempest of mine unnumbered offenses, I flee unto the haven of thy goodness as unto my warm protection and shelter, O pure one; therefore, O all-pure one, pray diligently unto Him Who was incarnate of thee without seed, on behalf of all thy servants who unceasingly pray unto thee, O all-pure Mother of God, entreating Him ever to grant remission of offenses unto those who worthily sing thy glory!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding upon the Cross the Lamb, and Shepherd, and Redeemer, the Ewe-lamb exclaimed weeping, and bitterly lamenting cried aloud: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee; but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of Thy loving-kindness, O Long-suffering Lord! O Abyss of mercy and inexhaustible Fount: have compassion, and grant remission of transgressions unto those who sing in faith of Thy divine passions![^621]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her order, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^622]

*Troparia:*
Having acquired a most stalwart mind in the faith, O blessed one, thou didst destroy the unsteadfast firmness of delusion, and rejoicing didst cry aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

Beholding the impassible Lord of glory lifted up and nailed unto the Tree, O glorious one, thou didst confess Him to be the Son of God, suffering by His own will!

Seeing death withering away through the death of the Immortal One, thou didst hasten, O Longinus, to die for His sake, and to live after death with those who gloriously suffered!

*Theotokion:*
Having hypostatically united in thy womb, and become Man, O all-pure one, the Word beyond word didst thou bear, Who is manifested in two wills and energies![^623]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou, my Lord, camest as a Light into the world, a holy Light turning from gloomy ignorance those who sing unto Thee in faith![^624]

*Troparia:*
Truly becoming a stranger to all earth, thou wast adorned with the wounds of a passion-bearer, O Longinus, and didst settle in the land of the meek!

Strengthened by the power of the Cross, O passion-bearer Longinus, thou wast able to overcome the feeble madness of the tormentors!

Having established thy heart upon a steadfast foundation, O all-lauded one, thou didst remain unshaken by the disease of the wavering of temptations!

*Theotokion:*
Let us sing of the only Virgin who gave birth unto God without knowing man, by whom we have been saved, and let us bless her in faith!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that for mercy's sake flowed from Thy side.[^625]

*Troparia:*
Seeing the mountains and the earth trembling on the day of the Passion, thou wast shaken from the station of unbelief, O Longinus, and wast established in the faith of the Sufferer!

Thou wast able to receive true, perfect, and prudent understanding, seeing creation changing, and thou wast transformed unto divine knowledge, O Longinus!

Rejoicing, thou didst endure cruel torment, and didst bow thy neck unto the sword with unwavering resolve, cutting off the heads of enemies by faith!

*Theotokion:*
Dry up, O all-immaculate one, and pull up from the root the thorny thoughts of my heart, and show it forth fruitful through thine intercession, O all-immaculate Maiden!

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*
Rejoicing, the Church was made glad today in the memory of the ever-memorable[^626] passion-bearer Longinus, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Christ, and foundation![^627]

*Ikos:*
Seeing heaven obscured with much darkness, the earth shaking, the rocks splitting, and the veil of the temple rent in twain at the divine Passion of Christ, Longinus recognized the Son of God suffering in His tender mercy, yet impassible in His Godhead and glory, holding together and embracing all things with the Father and the Holy Spirit as true God and King; therefore the passion-bearer joyfully cried: Thou art my strength, O Christ, and foundation![^628]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace the Abrahamic youths, burned by love of piety rather than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord![^629]

*Troparia:*
Having lawfully suffered, O martyr, with a manly disposition of soul: thou didst put to shame all the counsels of the lawless, and wast lawfully crowned, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

When divine grace rested upon thy relics, it loosed every disease of those who piously approach thee, O blessed one, and proclaim thee the unconquered witness of truth!

Thou didst not spare thy mortal flesh for the sake of Christ, O Longinus; thou refusedst to sacrifice unto demons, and didst joyfully bring thyself as a blameless sacrifice unto God, beheaded with the sword, O worthy of wonder!

*Theotokion:*
Finding thee pure, the pure Word took up dwelling in thee, O Virgin, being well-pleased through thee to cleanse the defilements of men: for blessed is the Fruit of thy womb!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Daniel, having stretched forth his hands, shut the gaping mouths of lions in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^630]

*Troparia:*
Having first put to sleep bodily stirrings by vigils kept unto the Lord, O glorious one, thou fellest asleep with a sweet sleep, O blessed one, being put to death for the sake of Christ Who upon the Tree of the Cross by a divine sleep poured forth life unto the dead!

When thou didst behold pierced in the side with a spear, O most blessed one, Christ Who for tender mercy's sake became Man, pouring forth blood and water, thou wast wounded with sweet desire for Him[^631] singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^632]

Thou didst extinguish the hearth of idols with the streams of thy blood, O all-wise Longinus, and didst burn to ashes the graven images and frail shrines, having inflamed thy soul with divine zeal, and crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Having illumined the eyes of thy heart with divine rays, through the darkening of the sun's all-radiant shining thou didst earnestly leave behind the darkness of impiety: and thou wast shown forth, O wise one, as a star illuminating those who cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
With mouth, and tongue, and soul do I proclaim thee Birthgiver of God, and I pray thee, O all-pure one: illumine my heart with thy light, and deliver me from cruel darkness as I cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Christ the Cornerstone, Who united the separated natures, was cut out without hands from thee, the unhewn mountain, O Virgin; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Mother of God![^633]

*Troparia:*
My strength and song is the Word Who stretched out His hands upon the Cross, didst thou cry aloud unto the lawless, O martyr; depicting His all-blessed Passion, I willingly accept death!

As a most excellent passion-bearer, enriched with divine sufferings, thou wast shown forth all-beauteous; and thou standest before the beauteous Word, most richly receiving the recompense of thy afflictions!

We bless thee who wast manifested as an unsetting sun through the radiance of thy sufferings, illuminating the whole universe with the rays of healings, and driving away the darkness of folly!

Thou hast ascended unto the eternal mansions, and wast joined unto the choirs of all holy martyrs, with whom, O martyr Longinus, remember us who piously celebrate thy memory, O all-blessed one!

*Theotokion:*
A dwelling-place of the never-setting Light wast thou shown forth, O Birthgiver of God, whereby they that were in darkness and the shadow of sin beheld the light: therefore enlighten also the senses of my heart!

*Exapostilarion:*
When thou didst behold hanging upon the Cross Him Who upholds all things by a nod and by ineffable counsel, thou didst marvel at the miracle, and calledst Him the Son of the true God; of Whose glory thou wast truly made a worthy partaker according to likeness![^634]

*Theotokion:*
That I may glorify thine invincible grace, O all-pure one, with many[^635] transgressions, having fallen foolishly: come quickly with thy haste, and by thy strength snatch me away from falls into sin; for by thine unconquerable power thou savest whom thou wilt from all manner of afflictions.[^636]

*At the Liturgy: Prokeimenon, Tone 7: The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord. Epistle to Timothy, pericope 292. Alleluia, Tone 4. Verse: The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree. Gospel of Matthew, pericope 113. Communion Hymn: In everlasting memory.*

*The same month, on the 17th day.*
*The holy Prophet Hosea.*
*And the venerable-martyr Andrew of Crete.*

*At Lord, I Have Cried, Stichera, Tone 1.*
*Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*

The all-holy Spirit determined through thy tongue that the coming of Christ would take place in the last times for the renewal of creation; and now, seeing the fulfillment of the things foretold, O prophet, we honor thy memory!

Betrothing our nature which previously wandered away from Thee by its own will, O Lover of mankind, Thou didst deliver it from defilement, uniting it inseparably unto Thyself beyond understanding; and Thy prophet, having become an image of this, chastely took unto himself one who formerly strayed.[^637]

O God-proclaiming prophet, now seeing thy utterances filled with grace, and standing before the God of all, pray for all who celebrate thy memory in faith, entreating for them remission of sins, and peace, and great mercy!

*Other Stichera, of the Venerable One.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

Cleaving unto the Lord from thy youth, O blessed one, thou didst finish thy course even unto the end: for arming thyself with the Cross, thou didst put thy hands to the plow of the Lord's commandments, as it is written; entreat Him to deliver from corruption and perils[^638] those who in faith celebrate thine all-honorable memory![^639]

Another most precious stone wast thou through faith, suffering no shipwreck: for having smoothed the path of faith for those wandering upon earth, O glorious one, thou madest it easy to tread upon; and thou didst refute the opinions, O father, of Constantine's evil glory like a rotten foundation![^640]

Nourished by dogmas, O blessed one, and brought up in the teachings of the divine Fathers, thou didst honor the visible form of God Who was incarnate in sacred symbols and venerable images; therefore, having suffered for these, thou wast shown forth as a teacher of piety and the adornment of martyrs!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
O thou who didst contain in thy womb the uncontainable God Who in His love for mankind became Man, and taking our substance from thee manifestly deified it: despise me not, O all-pure one, who am now in sorrow, but have compassion quickly, and deliver me from diverse enmities and the harm of the evil one!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
When the Ewe-lamb who bore Thee beheld Thee, the Lamb and Shepherd, upon the Tree, she lamented and maternally cried aloud unto Thee: O beloved Son, how art Thou suspended upon the Tree of the Cross, O Long-suffering One? How were Thy hands and feet nailed by the lawless, O Word, and Thou pouredst forth Thy blood, O Master?

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Having previously practiced asceticism upon the mountain, thou didst destroy the spiritual hosts of enemies with the panoply of the Cross, O all-blessed one; and again thou didst manfully gird thyself for martyrdom, having slain Copronymus with the sword of faith; and for both thou wast crowned by God, O venerable-martyr Andrew, ever-memorable!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*
*At Matins, one canon of the Oktoechos, and two of the saints. Canon of the Prophet.*
*Whose acrostic is:*
*I sing the God-inspired words of Hosea. By Theophanes. Tone 1.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Unto God Who helped Moses in Egypt, and through him drowned Pharaoh with his whole army, let us sing a song of victory: for He hath been glorified![^641]

*Troparia:*
As one who truly spoke of God, as a prophet, O Hosea, standing before the Giver of life, pray diligently to deliver from temptations those who faithfully praise thee!

Having cleansed the visual faculty of thy soul from defilements, thou wast granted[^642] to foresee clearly things to come, and to foretell without falsehood, O glorious one, the things that were to come to pass![^643]

The wisdom of God, the Fount of foresight, showed forth the God-inspired Hosea as an inexhaustible river of prophecy, bubbling forth divine teachings!

*Theotokion:*
The Father manifestly called out of Egypt Christ Who appeared of the Virgin for our sake, as Hosea the most divine seer of old foretold.[^644]

*Another Canon of the Venerable One.*
*Whose acrostic is:*
*I sing with love of him who is named after courage. By Joseph. Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Of old the overwhelmingly armed power strewed the entire army of Pharaoh upon the deep; and the incarnate Word destroyed all-wicked sin: the all-glorified Lord, for He hath been gloriously glorified![^645]

*Troparia:*
Having manfully subdued the bodily passions, O father, with thoughts of courage as one who bears courage in thy name, thou didst do battle against the cruelty of the tormentors, and didst lawfully suffer; therefore, having come together in faith, we harmonize in blessing thee![^646]

Kindled spiritually like a coal with the warmth of the Holy Spirit, thou didst melt the ice of passions, and with the streams of thy blood thou didst extinguish the alien fire of impiety, O Andrew, God-blessed confessor!

Bound by the love of Christ, O father, having taken up thy cross rejoicing from thy youth, thou followedst Him, denying thyself through fasting and perfect suffering!

*Theotokion:*
Ever burdening my soul with the sleep of despondency, I flee unto the goodness of thy protection, O all-pure one: rouse me unto the vigilance of repentance, delivering me then from everlasting torment!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Establish me, O Christ, upon the immovable rock of Thy commandments, and enlighten me with the light of Thy countenance: for there is none holy above Thee, O Lover of mankind![^647]

*Troparia:*
Instructing with God-befitting teaching, O prophet, thou didst manifestly proclaim the calling of the Gentiles unto the salvation of God, being formed by the Holy Spirit!

Thou wast granted to foresee future things as present, O God-wise one, manifestly declaring the falling away of the Jews, and the abolition of the things of the Law!

Thou didst foretell that Israel should be saved, not by battle nor by bow nor by panoply, but in the Lord God Almighty, signifying unto us the incarnation of the Word![^648]

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O pure Virgin, who conceived the incarnate Word[^649] Who delivered us from delusion by His holy blood and body.[^650]

*Another [Canon]. Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Having established me upon the rock of faith, Thou hast enlarged my mouth against mine enemies; for my spirit was made glad to sing: There is none holy as our God, and there is none righteous above Thee, O Lord![^651]

*Troparia:*
Having established the steps of thine understanding, O father, upon the rock of Christ's love, thou wast not at all shaken by contrary winds, crying unto thy Creator: Thou art our God, and there is none righteous above Thee, O Lord!

Pouring forth healings both before thine end and after thine end, thou succorest those who are in infirmities, and drivest away unclean spirits, O venerable Andrew, crying unto thy Creator: Thou art our God, and there is none righteous above Thee, O Lord!

Washed with the tears of asceticism, thou wast adorned with martyric blood; and showing thyself forth all-beauteous, crying aloud, thou hast ascended unto the Desired One: Thou art our God, and there is none righteous above Thee, O Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst give birth, O all-blameless one, unto God the Word Who became in the likeness of flesh, consubstantial with the Father, remaining beyond the two natures even after childbirth; therefore in faith we call thee blessed!

*Kontakion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дв҃а дне́сь):*
Today the imperial city celebrates a most radiant festival of thy light-bearing memory, calling together every city and country; for it rejoices as having a great treasure, thy much-suffering body, O martyr Andrew, light of Orthodoxy![^652]

*Then Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having acquired a heart pure from passions, O prophet, thou wast truly a fitting instrument of the Spirit: being richly enlightened by Him, O wise one, and receiving knowledge of all things to come; therefore, having come together, we faithfully celebrate thy holy memory, glorifying the Savior, O Hosea ever-memorable! Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love![^653]

*Glory... of the Venerable One, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
From the daily fast and sufferings let us faithful worthily praise him as a preacher of piety and a steadfast champion; for without fear he opposed the one named of filth, and proclaimed that honor is due unto the image of Christ: as the adornment of all fast-keepers, and the image of the courage of martyrs![^654]

*Both now... Theotokion:*

...He Who sits upon the Cherubic throne and abides in the Father's bosom, sits as upon His holy throne, O Lady, in thy bosom: for God was truly incarnate, and reigned over all nations; and understandingly do we now sing unto Him; entreat Him that thy servants may be saved![^655]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Seeing Thee upon the Cross, O Christ, she who bore Thee in the flesh in the last times, Who was begotten of the unoriginate Father, cried aloud: Woe is me, O most beloved Jesus! How art Thou, Who art glorified as God by the angels, now willingly crucified by lawless men, O Son? I sing of Thee, O Long-suffering One!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I understood Thine economy, O Almighty One, and with fear I glorified Thee, O Savior![^656]

*Troparia:*
O Hosea, most God-seeing Tserkovne Oko: deliver from perils those who praise thee!

Most radiant and firmest receptacle of the Spirit: pray to save those who sing of thee!

Standing before the Judge of all as a divine prophet: deliver me from passions by thy prayers!

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast surpassed the angelic ranks, O Ever-virgin, having borne their Creator in thy womb!

*Another [Canon]. Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou camest forth from the Virgin, neither an ambassador nor an angel, but Thyself, O Lord, incarnate, and Thou savedst me, whole man; therefore I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^657]

*Troparia:*
Being obedient unto Christ's commandments, O father, thou didst venerate His image with honor; therefore the most lawless one put thee to death with multifaceted wounds!

Perfumed with the sweet fragrance of virtues, lying in the tomb thou sheddest fragrance upon the faces of those who invoke thee with unwavering heart, O venerable father![^658]

Him that was diseased, the all-wicked one, with the accursed heresy, O venerable one, thou didst boldly rebuke, having become a champion of the Orthodox, O Andrew worthy of wonder!

*Theotokion:*
As one fruitful, O all-hymned Maiden, thou didst bring forth the grain of grace which nourishes all who orthodoxly honor thee as the good one, and the Word incarnate of thee, O all-blameless one!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Rising early from the night, we sing unto Thee, O Christ God, Who becamest poor for our sake, and in the flesh enduredst the Cross and death![^659]

*Troparia:*
Unto those who are turbulently driven, thou hast taught us God's long-suffering and tender mercy[^660] and goodness, wherewith God spares, O God-proclaiming prophet![^661]

Christ, having put death to death, arose, as thy wise prophecy, O God-proclaiming voice, foretold, and granted life unto the souls that were in hades![^662]

Snatch me away from the depth of sins by thy prayers, O prophet Hosea, having blameless boldness before Christ, O God-wise and all-rich one!

*Theotokion:*
The Lord of all and King of all, co-unoriginate with the Father, was born under time of thee, O Virgin Mother of God, and was shown forth in the flesh!

*Another [Canon]. Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Enlightenment of those lying in darkness, salvation of the despairing, O Christ my Savior, unto Thee I rise early, O King of peace; enlighten me with Thy radiance, for I know no other God besides Thee![^663]

*Troparia:*
Having hated the darkening of sin, O father, thou wast shown forth all light by the splendor of dispassion; and by the rays of healings thou enlightenest the faithful who approach thy divine tomb, through the grace of God!

Thou wast shown forth entirely as a receptacle of God, having lived well and manfully suffered, O father; and now lying in the tomb thou remainest whole and incorrupt, O blessed one, loosing the passions of all the faithful!

Having youthfully acted valiantly, O all-blessed one, against demons and against men who held heresy, thou wast granted everlasting glorious praise and crowns, O venerable Andrew; therefore we faithful ever call thee blessed!

*Theotokion:*
Bearing in thine embrace Christ, Whom having ineffably borne, thou embracest the Word Who bowed down unto those who were wretchedly enslaved unto the evil one, O all-blameless Maiden; therefore we faithful ever call thee blessed!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Thou didst save the prophet from the whale, O Lover of mankind; and bring me up from the depth of transgressions, I pray Thee![^664]

*Troparia:*
Proclaiming things to come, O all-blessed Hosea, thou didst announce the descent of the Word unto men!

Christ, the immortal Life, brought up from the domains of hades those who had been put to death by sins, as thou didst foretell![^665]

Thou didst show forth the removal of the things of the Law, the falling away of the Jews, and the bringing in of the Gentiles, O prophet!

*Theotokion:*
From thy womb shone forth God the Word, O all-blameless one, ineffably wrapped in flesh for our sake as One full of tender mercy!

*Another [Canon]. Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Wallowing in the abyss of sin, the unfathomable[^666] Thy mercy: bring me up from corruption, O God![^667]

*Troparia:*
The lawless one cast thee into prison, O Andrew, who wast kept by goodness, and unalterably kept the divine will!

Thou tookest no account of thy body being torn to pieces and crushed by wounds, O blessed one; but as if another were suffering, thou didst endure, being strengthened divinely!

Like a hidden, precious treasure art thou manifested, O father, set forth before the face of the pious, and with wonders thou enrichest all who honor thee!

*Theotokion:*
Of old the prophet foresaw thee, O pure one, as an intelligible lampstand bearing the divine Lamp, Christ, by Whom all of us have been enlightened!

*Kontakion, Tone 8, Podoben: "To thee the Champion Leader" (Взбра́нной):*
Unto him who is named after courage let us, out of love, cry aloud songs of praise with thanksgiving, as unto a mystic speaker of piety, O God-blessed one; but as having boldness before the Lord, save us from all cruel dangers, that we may sing: Rejoice, O father ever-memorable![^668]

*Ikos:*
An angel of truth from Crete wast thou manifested, O father, having rebuked impiety; and seeing thee united unto theological voices, O all-wise one, I am in awe of thy suffering, crying and exclaiming unto thee such things as these:
Rejoice, mighty weapon of God!
Rejoice, faithful favorite of the Lord!
Rejoice, adversary of blasphemous enemies!
Rejoice, bridle of foul-speaking men!
Rejoice, for thou didst suffer for the image of Christ!
Rejoice, for thou didst trample down bodily pleasures!
Rejoice, for by fasting thou didst overcome the principalities of darkness!
Rejoice, for by suffering thou didst oppose Copronymus!
Rejoice, ever-flowing fount of miracles!
Rejoice, thou who madest the earth radiant by thy sufferings!
Rejoice, dwelling-place of the faithful, O father!
Rejoice, our advocate and deliverer!
Rejoice, O father ever-memorable![^669]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Abrahamic youths in the furnace, having prefigured the Trinity, changed the fiery flame into dew, and singing cried aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^670]

*Troparia:*
Having enlightened thy mind with illumination beyond nature, O Hosea, thou wast enriched with the knowledge of things to come, O blessed one, crying unto the Good One: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Thou wast likened in the hands of the prophets, O Savior, and didst multiply visions, O Immortal One, unto those who faithfully cry unto Thee in multifaceted ways: Blessed art Thou[^671] [^672] O God of our fathers![^673]

O Hosea, thou didst preach Christ unto us as a fount pouring forth waters of salvation, a peaceful river, unto Whom we cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Shining forth from the unoriginate Father, He was incarnate of thee, O Maiden Mother of God, for the sake of ineffable mercy, unto Whom singing we cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Another [Canon]. Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The God-opposing decree of the lawless tormentor raised the flame high; but Christ spread spiritual dew over the pious youths: He Who is blessed and supremely glorified![^674]

*Troparia:*
The rising waves of malice did not shake thine understanding, O glorious Andrew, as thou stoodest upon the rock of the knowledge of Christ; therefore thou becamest a stormless haven manifestly unto those tossed by tempest!

With foolish inquiries the one dark in understanding and wicked in mind sought to beguile thee who wast adorned with the brightness of theology; but he became worthy of laughter, O wise one, filled with shame by thy boldness![^675]

Thy mind being adorned with the most divine motions, O Andrew worthy of wonder, thou wast shown forth unto all as an all-radiant star, enlightening by miracles every one of the faithful who praise thee!

*Theotokion:*
A temple of the living God wast thou, O all-hymned Virgin, in which He Who dwells in the heavens ineffably made His abode, and for His goodness' sake was well-pleased to become Man!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Him Who preserved the children in the fiery flame of the burning furnace, and descended unto them in the form of an angel: sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^676]

*Troparia:*
Beyond comprehension is the foreknowing energy given unto the prophets from the divine gift, and the knowledge of things to come, to sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

Having acquired God-inspired lips and a God-speaking tongue, O Hosea, shining with the light of piety, thou singest: Sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

By words and prophetic deeds having rebuked the people that committed fornication, O all-rich one, thou didst figuratively teach them to sing: Sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^677]

*Theotokion:*
God the Word, co-eternal with the Father, appeared incarnate of the holy Virgin unto those who cry aloud in faith[^678] sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^679]

*Another [Canon]. Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Of old in Babylon the fiery furnace divided its action by the command of God, consuming the Chaldeans, but bedewing the faithful who sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^680]

*Troparia:*
Having mortified bodily passions beforehand through ascetic struggles, O wise one, thou didst suffer most stalwartly, honorably kissing the likeness of the Savior, of the Ever-virgin, O venerable one, and of the saints!

Raging in fury against Christ like fierce beasts, O father, they set about putting thee to death; therefore, dragging and beating thee, they cast thee into a chasm, in truth making thee a witness of truth!

With miracles thou waterest the faithful as with waters, and drivest away the spirits of malice, O all-blessed one, and cleansest diseases, having received the grace of healing from on high, from Christ our God!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown forth more spacious than the heavens, O Virgin, having contained in thy womb God Who cannot be contained by all; entreat Him, O pure Lady, to deliver from soul-corrupting constraint those who piously sing of thee!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Rejoice, praise of virginity; rejoice, all-pure Mother, whom all creation magnifies with divine songs![^681]

*Troparia:*
Thou receivedst honor as a prophet, O Hosea, having received a crown of comeliness from God, O all-blessed one!

Illumined with all the light of Christ, O all-surpassing one: by thy prayers grant that He be merciful unto those who sing of thee!

With praises, O Hosea, thou standest before the Master praising Him with the angelic ranks, having lived an angel-like life!

*Theotokion:*
Desiring to save man, the Savior and Lord was clothed in our flesh of the all-pure Virgin!

*Another [Canon]. Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The Son of the unoriginate Father, God and Lord, incarnate of the Virgin appeared unto us to enlighten the darkened and to gather together the dispersed; therefore we magnify the all-hymned Mother of God![^682]

*Troparia:*
Lo, O venerable father, having finished thy course, thou didst keep the Orthodox faith blameless; therefore, having lawfully suffered for its sake, thou receivedst from on high a twofold crown as an invincible martyr and unconquered ascetic!

Like a sweet-smelling lily and like a rose didst thou blossom forth, O father, in the flower-garden of the divine place of fasting, and[^683] thou sheddest sweet fragrance like a cedar, having blossomed martyrially, O all-praised Andrew, adornment of monastics![^684]

Manifestly adorned with signs and wonders, thou liest unharmed before the sight of all, O all-blessed Andrew, enlightening the souls of those who approach thee with warm faith and ever bless thee!

Celebrating today thy holy memory, a day of feast and filled with gladness, we cry aloud unto thee in faith, O father: by thy holy prayers unto God, deliver all of us from the distresses of life!

*Theotokion:*
Thy womb became a dwelling of the Light Who enlightened the world with the rays of Godhead, and utterly drove away the darkness of ignorance from the earth, O all-blameless Maiden; therefore we glorify thee!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Let us go up with the disciples" (Со ᲂу҆ченикѝ взы́демъ):*
Let us all praise the victorious Andrew, divinely celebrating his honorable memory, that we may be delivered from diseases; for he preached that the venerable depictions of the Savior and of all the saints are to be venerated with all piety; therefore, having received a crown in the heavens, he rejoices with the angels![^685]

*Theotokion:*
The most holy Mountain of God, and Lampstand, and Jar, and Table, and Bridge, and Rod, and Candle, and divine Throne, and Gate, and Temple, and Bridal Chamber, let us God-wise praise, the pure one and Virgin: of whom God, being incarnate without change and beyond nature, deified that which He assumed by an ineffable union![^686]

*Month of the same, on the 18th day: The Holy Apostle and Evangelist Luke.*[^687]

*At Vespers, we sing: "Blessed is the man", the 1st antiphon.*

*At "Lord, I Have Cried", stichera on 8, Tone 8, Podoben: "What shall we call you, O holy ones" (Что́ ва́съ нарече́мъ):*

What shall I now call thee, O apostle? Heaven, for thou hast told the glory of God; lightning, for thou illuminest the world with light; a cloud, raining down divine waters; a most divine cup of wisdom, pouring out for us wine that makes glad hearts: pray that our souls may be saved! *(Twice)*

What shall I now call thee, O God-receiver? A golden ark of the covenant, which Christ covenanted; a river flowing forth to us from Paradise; a luminary, radiating spiritual light; a lamp, illuminating the entire Church; bread of life of the divine table; a mixing bowl of spiritual drink: pray that our souls may be saved! *(Twice)*

What shall we now call thee, O beholder of God? A most active minister of the mysteries of Christ; a wise master-builder of the spiritual tabernacle, carving the tablets of grace; having written the newest law coming forth from Zion, and proclaimed by thee: pray that our souls may be saved!

What shall we now call thee, O glorious one? A firm treasure of heavenly gifts; a renowned physician of souls and bodies; a co-worker and companion of Paul, recounting the Acts of the Apostles: many names has virtue made for thee, O Luke: pray that our souls may be saved!

What shall I now utter unto thee, O God-proclaimer? A disciple, for thou hast announced the good tidings of Christ unto us; a physician, for thou healest the passions of souls; a lamp, having shone forth the spiritual light; the step and foundation of faith; for thou hast written for us the all-honorable Gospel: pray that our souls may be saved!

What shall I now call thee, O marvelous one? An unfailing beholder of the teachings of wisdom; a recorder of the apostolic teachings and acts; or an unshakable pillar of piety; an indestructible wall of the Church: many are thy names, O Luke, and greater are thy gifts: pray[^688] that our souls may be saved![^689]

*Glory, Tone 6, by Anatolios:*
O apostle of Christ and recorder of divine dogmas, and confirmation of the Church: thou in truth, when the hearts of those in the gloom of ignorance had gone down into the depth of despair, didst adorn them with good tidings, and didst save them as from a tempestuous storm of turmoil, having become a follower and disciple of the chosen vessel Paul. Therefore we beseech thee, O Luke worthy of wonder, adornment of the Antiochians: pray to the Savior and God for those who ever celebrate thy honorable memory in faith!

*Both now, Theotokion: "Who would not bless thee..."*

*Entrance, prokeimenon of the day, and three readings, and the Litiya: search for the 6th day of this month.*[^690]

*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O container of ascetics" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ по́стникѡвъ):*
Grace was poured out upon thy lips, O Luke, as a fiery tongue, O apostle, and thou wast shown forth as a fiery tongue, sending forth words of light by a preaching worthy of the light; and shooting burning arrows of darkness at those who desire it, writing and teaching the honorable Gospel; and thou wast revealed as a vital fragrance unto those truly desiring life, as Paul said, whom thou didst have as a teacher; but grant peace, life, light, and great mercy unto us who have not loved death again instead of life![^691]

*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands.*

We have known from thy words, even as thou hast said, the certainty of the words which thou didst speak, O divine initiate of mysteries; since thou didst begin to write unto us concerning the things whereof thou wast assured, even as they delivered them unto thee, who from the beginning were eyewitnesses; whereof thou also wast a servant and minister of the incarnation of the Word, Whom after the Resurrection thou didst behold in Emmaus, and with a burning heart didst eat bread with Cleopas: fill our souls also, who honor thee, with His divine warmth!

*Verse: Their sound has gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.*

Rejoice, thou who alone hast written for us "Rejoice, rejoice", the Annunciation of the Pure One; and this Mother of the Lord, as the Baptist said from the womb, whose conception thou didst also write, and the incarnation of the Word; and the temptations and wonders, the words and passions, the Cross and death, and the Resurrection which thou didst behold, and the ascension[^692] and the descent of the Spirit; a preacher of the Acts, and especially of Paul, whose companion thou wast shown to be; a physician and initiate of mysteries, and a lamp of the Church: which do thou ever preserve![^693]

*Glory, Tone 6:*
O all-wise fisher, holy disciple, laborer of the Savior, and preacher of His passions: who didst traverse creation in faith, and having gathered the nations from deceit and brought them unto God like a sweet fragrance, thou hast reached the heavens. Therefore, standing before the Judge of all, pray that we may be delivered from our iniquities, and in the day of judgment may be freed from torment!

*Both now, Resurrection Theotokion: "My Maker and Redeemer..."*

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
O holy apostle and evangelist Luke, entreat the merciful God, that He grant remission of transgressions unto our souls!

*Or this one, Tone 5:*
The narrator of the apostolic acts and writer of the Gospel of Christ, the luminary, the much-hymned Luke, who is inexhaustible unto Christ's Church: let us praise the holy apostle with sacred songs, as being a physician who heals human infirmities, natural sicknesses, and wounds of souls, and unceasingly prays for our souls!

*Glory, both now, Resurrection Theotokion. At Matins, at "God is the Lord", the troparion twice; Glory, both now, Theotokion. After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*
Come, all, let us praise the apostle Luke as a helmsman; for he abolished the deception of idols, and led us unto the light of life, and taught us to believe in the Trinity; therefore, today devoutly honoring him, we the faithful glorify Christ our God! *(Twice)*

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O Mary, Virgin Mother, holy mountain, garden of Eden, from whom was born Christ God, the Seedless Word, through Whom life sprouted forth unto the world!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*
Having journeyed after the Resurrection, O all-sacred Luke, with Cleopas unto the city of Emmaus, as Thou wast conversing, the Lord stood before you veiled in form; and straightway receiving the giving of bread, Thou didst recognize the Savior: Whom do Thou pray to grant us great mercy! *(Twice)*

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
As an uncultivated vine, O Virgin, thou didst bud forth the most beautiful Cluster, pouring out for us the wine of salvation, making glad the souls[^694] making glad both souls and bodies. Therefore, ever blessing thee as the cause of good things, with the angel we cry aloud unto thee: Rejoice, thou who art full of grace![^695]

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
With the angling rod of wisdom, drawing from the depth of the spiritual sea, out of the death-bearing depth thou didst catch alive the souls of the faithful, O all-blessed Luke. Therefore, taught by Paul, thou didst completely purify thy heart with the illumination of the Spirit; and with thy dogmas thou didst enlighten the nations; thou didst heal the infirmities of passions with wonders, ever entreating Christ God on our behalf, that our souls may be saved! *(Twice)*

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
Having thee as our hope and protection, O Mother of God, we shall not fear the assaults of the enemy, for thou savest our souls!

*The Graduals [Anavathmoi], the 1st antiphon of the 4th Tone. Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
*Their sound has gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world. Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God...*
*Let every breath praise the Lord. Gospel according to John, pericope 67 [Jn. 21:15–25].*
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 2:*
Having journeyed after the Resurrection, O all-sacred Luke, with Cleopas unto the city of Emmaus, as Thou wast conversing, the Lord stood before you, unrecognized in form; and straightway receiving the giving of bread, Thou didst recognize the Savior: Whom do Thou pray to grant us great mercy!

*Canon of the Theotokos with the Heirmos on 6, and of the Apostle on 8, Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us send up a hymn, O ye people, unto our marvelous God, Who freed Israel from bondage, singing a song of victory and crying aloud: Let us sing unto Thee, the only Master![^696]

*Troparia:*
Thou wast a most radiant chariot of God, bearing that which is all-holy before men, O all-honorable Luke, mightily drowning the chariots and captains of deception!

Having received the stream of life, when parched by the scorching heat of godlessness, thou didst give drink unto the world, O all-blessed Luke, with the showers of the knowledge of God trickling forth from thy belly!

Illumined with the lightning of the Spirit, O Luke, and by the purity of thy life, like lightning, O thrice-blessed one, thou didst divinely shine upon the entire universe, enlightening the hearts of the faithful!

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, all-holy temple, fleece bedewed of God, sealed fountain of the immortal stream: preserve thy flock, O Lady, uncaptured by all adversaries!

*Katavasia: "I shall open my mouth..."*
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

There is none holy as the Lord, and there is none righteous as our God, Whom all creation praises: there is none righteous above Thee, O Lord![^697]

*Troparia:*
Having shone forth like the sun by thy preaching, O Luke, thou didst adorn the firm support of the Church, melting the mist of deception by the warmth of faith, O all-blessed one!

Thou didst carve tablets fitting for God from the rock of God's incarnation, O most rich one, having thy divine tongue and God-inspired lips as a mason's pick!

Entering into the all-radiant cloud and covered by the cloud, O all-wise one, thou didst receive the new law written on thy heart by the finger of the Spirit!

*Theotokion:*
There is none blameless as thou, O Lady, and there is none all-pure above thee, O all-immaculate one; for in thy womb thou didst contain God the Word, the only One rich in mercy!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Having been shown to be a companion of Paul, thou didst patiently endure diverse tribulations, O all-praised apostle of the Lord; and having finished the course of faith with suffering, thou rejoicest with him in the heights, O blessed one; therefore, having also preached Christ's Gospel unto the world, thou didst enlighten the entire world under the sun, O all-honorable Luke: pray to Christ God that He grant remission of sins unto those who celebrate thy holy memory with love! *(Twice)*

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
O cloud of the spiritual Sun, golden lamp of the divine Light: unblemished, undefiled, all-immaculate Lady, I pray thee: enlighten my soul, darkened by the blindness of passions, with the beam of dispassion; and wash my defiled heart with streams of compunction and tears of repentance; and cleanse me from the mire of my deeds, that with love I may cry unto thee: O Mother of God, Ever-Virgin, pray to Christ God that He grant me remission of transgressions; for I, thy servant, have thee as my hope!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
From the overshadowing mountain, O Word, the prophet divinely foresaw Thee about to become incarnate of the only Mother of God, and with fear he glorified Thy power![^698]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst stir the sea of the Gentiles, O God-wise one, having become a horse of the King of all, sweetening the salty unbelief with divine salt, O ever-glorious one!

Having bent His bow, Christ sent thee forth, O Luke, as a brightly sharpened arrow[^699] shining with the rays of piety, and enlightening our souls![^700]

Heal the passions of souls together with those of bodies of those who sing of thee, O all-blessed one, with mystical surgery, cutting out the corruption of sin, O all-wise one!

*Theotokion:*
O Bride of God, Mary, Mother of God, deliver me from grievous transgressions and tribulations; and by thy prayers guide me unto the calm haven of salvation!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Rising early we cry aloud unto Thee: O Lord, save us; for Thou art our God, and we know none other beside Thee![^701]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst appear descending like dew from heaven, dissolving the burning heat of deception by thy words!

Having conceived the divine fear in thy womb, O apostle, thou didst bring forth salvation for those who have gone astray!

Ascending the mountain of virtues, O all-blessed one, thou didst proclaim the Gospel of the Kingdom unto all!

*Theotokion:*
O unwedded Virgin Mary, Mother of God, bring to vanity the hopes of our enemies, and make glad those who sing of thee!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Grant me a robe of light, O Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, O most merciful Christ our God![^702]

*Troparia:*
Having drunk the cup of wisdom, O all-wise Luke, thou didst give all to drink of the knowledge of the Savior Christ!

Chosen as a companion of Paul, O all-blessed Luke, thou didst catch the nations with the net of thy dogmas, O God-proclaimer!

Thou didst appear as a cloud raining down for us the shower of life, O apostle, flowing forth from the fountains of salvation!

*Theotokion:*
Thy childbearing, Mary, Bride of God, which was God the Destroyer of idols, is worshipped together with the Father and the Spirit!

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "The firm and God-proclaiming preachers" (Тве́рдыѧ):*
Let us praise the divine Luke, the preacher of true piety, the orator of ineffable mysteries, and the star of the Church; for the Word, Who alone knows the secrets of hearts, chose him together with the wise Paul as a teacher of the Gentiles![^703]

*Ikos:*
Enriched with knowledge from on high from the benevolent hand, thou wast entrusted with the portion of the Gentiles, O all-praised one. Therefore, having refined thy life by the word, as a lawgiver unto the nations[^704] thou didst show thyself, O Luke, as a lawgiver more than Moses. By the finger of God, as tablets inscribed by God composed by thee through the Spirit, thou didst elucidate the divine faith; therefore Christ also glorified thee, Who alone knows the secrets of hearts![^705]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The pious youths in Babylon bowed not down to the golden image, but bedewed in the midst of the fiery furnace, sang a hymn, saying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers and our God, Who art exalted above all![^706]

*Troparia:*
Having been a faithful disciple of the ineffable mysteries of Christ God, thou didst proclaim His divine Gospel unto the faithful, O all-wise one, who say: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers and our God, Who art exalted above all!

Adorned with the embellishments of virtues, thou didst appear, O all-blessed Luke, enlightened by the rays of divine comeliness, and crying unceasingly unto thy Creator: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers and our God, Who art exalted above all!

Sending forth light-bearing rays, appearing as dawn in the night, seeking devoutly, thou didst drive away the darkness of deception by the brightness of thy words: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers and our God, Who art exalted above all!

*Theotokion:*
Having a life enlightened by the word of wisdom, O Luke, beholder of God, thou didst clearly record the Virgin's childbearing, and didst lovingly depict it in icons: before which we faithfully prostrate, glorifying Christ!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Him Who was glorified on the holy mountain, and by fire in the bush revealed unto Moses the mystery of the Ever-Virgin: praise the Lord and exalt Him above all unto all ages![^707]

*Troparia:*
By the dew-giving wisdom of thy words and the showers of divine grace, thou didst extinguish the furnace of deception, having become a messenger and sacred preacher of the Lord, O God-wise one!

How beautiful are thy feet, O all-blessed one, who preachest the peace which surpasses all understanding in abundance: Whom we exalt above all and glorify unto all ages!

As the Light of the world, Christ, the Radiance of the Father's essence, the Lord of glory, revealed thee to enlighten those who sit in darkness, Whom we exalt above all unto ages!

*Theotokion:*
The Lord Who dwelt in the womb of the Virgin, and of her awesomely and beyond word renewed ancient Adam, let us sing and glorify unto ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Thee, the Mother of God the Most High, who knewest not wedlock: thee, who above mind didst truly give birth through the word unto God, higher than the all-pure powers, with unceasing glorifications do we magnify![^708]

*Troparia:*
Thou wast a preacher of universal salvation through the evangelical preaching; and thou wast revealed as a sacred and fair-sounding trumpet breathing with the inspiration of the Spirit, O all-honorable sacred proclaimer!

Adorned with the acts of the Savior's apostles, thou didst write these things concerning God for zealous believers, as a living image seen to endure always, O all-glorious sacred preacher!

Thou didst stand, O Luke, having reached the God-initiating summit of desires; for thou didst stand before Christ, being filled with the contemplation of God bestowed by Him; therefore we all call thee blessed!

*Theotokion:*
Thee who didst bear in thine arms the unapproachable God, Who is hymned in the heavens by every power and through thee ever grants us salvation, O Mother of God, do we magnify!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Let us go up with the disciples" (Со ᲂу҆ченикѝ):*
O Luke, apostle of Christ, initiate of ineffable things and teacher of the Gentiles, with divine Paul and the all-pure Mother of God, whose divine icon thou didst lovingly depict: pray, O beholder of God, for us who call thee blessed and celebrate thy sacred dormition, O all-wise speaker of mysteries![^709]

*Glory, another [Exapostilarion], Podoben: "In the spirit in the sanctuary" (Дх҃омъ во ст҃и́лищи):*
As a disciple of grace, and follower of Paul, and most excellent writer of the divine Gospel, let us praise [Luke]; for thou didst preach the incarnation, the enfleshment, and the passions of the Word, O blessed one; therefore, having gathered in faith, O Luke, we worthily honor thee!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Ineffable was the childbearing of the all-immaculate Bride, for she was a most honorable temple, O Mother of God; for by eternal counsel He took up His abode in thee, and thou didst bear God the Word; therefore, singing of thy childbearing, we worthily magnify thee, O all-immaculate one!

*At the Praises, stichera on 4, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thee, the cloud of light" (Ѻ҆́блакъ тѧ̀ свѣ́та):*
Let us the faithful honor with hymns the disciple of Christ, the most wise writer of the Gospel, the beautiful chosen vessel, the living image whose praise is in all the churches, the apostle Luke: for he preached the most glorious wonders of God, having enlightened those[^710] upon the earth with the rays of theology because of goodness![^711]

Having learned the art of healing souls rather than bodies, O wise one, thou wast shown to be all-good in both, inspired by the wisdom of God: healing by it both souls and bodies, O all-blessed Luke, thou summonest men unto knowledge, and liftest them on wings unto desire for God; thou ever leadest men up to heaven, and prayest for all who praise thee!

With the spiritual hook, those who had gone into the depth of ignorance like fish within, thou didst draw unto the knowledge of God; thou didst bring unto Christ a good food, O all-blessed one: due honor, incorruptible life, O most sacred Luke; therefore thou wast an apostle of Christ, a wise evangelist, a narrator of the active works of grace!

*Tone 4, by Leo the Master:*
Come, all creation, let us praise with hymns the true spiritual physician and disciple of the Savior, the ever-memorable Luke; for he healed the sickness of godlessness by the anointing of Christ's font, and with rivers of God-flowing waters from his mouth gave drink unto the living fields of the Gospel; therefore his sound prophetically went forth into the universe, and he prays that our souls may be saved!

*Glory, in the same Tone:*
Having gathered together as a people, O ye faithful, let us cry aloud in songs unto Luke, the initiate of the secret being of the Word: Thy tongue appeared as the pen of a scribe, of Christ the swift scribe, adorning faces unto the understanding of the nations in divine knowledge; wherein thou didst preach the Gospel, and likewise didst write the Acts of the Apostles; therefore, standing before the Trinity and God, pray for our souls!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
O Lady, accept the prayers of thy servants, and deliver us from every distress and sorrow!

*Great Doxology, and dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the canon of the apostle: Odes 3 and 6.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 8:*
*Their sound has gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.* (Ps. 18:4 LXX)
*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God...* (Ps. 18:1 LXX)

*Epistle to the Colossians, pericope 237 from the middle [Col. 4:5–9, 14, 18].*[^712]

*Alleluia, Tone 1: The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord...* (Ps. 88:5 LXX)

*Gospel according to Luke, pericope 51 [Lk. 10:16–21].*[^713]

*Communion Verse: Their sound has gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world. Alleluia.* (Ps. 18:4 LXX) *Month of the same, on the 19th day: The Holy Prophet Joel, and the Holy Martyr Varus.*[^714]

*Stichera of the prophet, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
The divine Spirit was poured out, even as the honorable Joel prophesying declared, moved by Him, upon us believers; and He reveals the manifestation of divine mysteries, and they prophesy who have received His operation, being enlightened by the divine beam and by divine grace![^715]

Filled with the proclamation of God, the marvelous Joel, like a fountain giving drink unto souls, goes forth from Thy house, O Master, and dropped sweetness for us, sweetening the thoughts, being high in spirit, lifted up by virtue unto divine height![^716]

By thy prophetic boldness, and by thy understanding approach unto God, O Joel, whereby Thou beholdest unseen visions: make Him merciful unto us who celebrate thy memory in faith, asking remission of transgressions, O glorious one, and communion in divine delights, and great mercy!

*Other stichera, of the martyr, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*
Having beheld the sufferings of the fair-victorious martyrs, and being filled with divine boldness, thou didst enter into the stadium, and didst proclaim Christ, Who for our sake became man, O Varus, not having feared death, nor the impending tortures; therefore, wounded and unmercifully broken to pieces, thou didst rejoice with a perfect mind, looking wisely upon the enduring good glory for those who have loved God![^717]

Seeing flesh falling away with blood, thou didst endure most valiantly, O sufferer, as though another were suffering; for bound to a tree, thou didst endure the tearings of the lawless most steadfastly; and thou didst commit thy spirit into the hands of God, having astounded those who beheld thee by thine unbending resistance; therefore we honor thee, who didst act most manly, O wise one, and didst cast down the tormentor, O Varus, by thy pains[^718] Anointing thee with myrrh, marvelous Cleopatra laid thee under the earth; and she built a temple as an abode for thee, celebrating a feast today, O Varus, and faithfully praying to thee; whose child thou didst number with the spiritual host, and didst adorn with glory, and didst unite unto the choir of the saints; of whose portion make us worthy, who devoutly celebrate thy solemn festival, O right-marvelous martyr![^719]

*Glory, both now, Theotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*
Rejoice, dewy root; rejoice, rod of Aaron that sprouted the unfading flower, and ever-living, fruitful paradise, tree of life, O pure maiden who didst blossom from the root of Jesse, and feedest the earthly with the bread of understanding! Rejoice, all-honorable purple robe, Bride of God of the King of all, all-radiant crown, comeliness of hierarchs! Rejoice, only all-pure one!

*Cross-Theotokion, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*
When the undefiled ewe-lamb of old, the immaculate Lady, beheld her Lamb stretched out upon the tree of the Cross, she cried out maternally, and wondering, wept: O my sweetest Child, what is this new and most marvelous sight? How has the ungrateful assembly delivered Thee unto the judgment-seat of Pilate, and condemns to death the Life of all? Yet I hymn Thine ineffable condescension, O Word!

*Troparion of the martyr, Tone 4:*
Thy martyr Varus, O Lord, in his suffering received an incorruptible crown from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, he cast down the tormentors, and crushed the powerless insolence of the demons: by his prayers save our souls![^720]

*Glory, both now, Theotokion or Cross-Theotokion.*

*At Matins, one canon from the Oktoechos, and two of the saints.*

*Canon of the Prophet.*
*Whose acrostic is: "I marvel at the prophecy of the Prophet Joel."*
*A composition of Theophanes, Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Come, O ye peoples, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He led forth from the bondage of Egypt, for He has been glorified![^721]

*Troparia:*
A prophetic choir shines brightly upon us; let us praise thee, O God-wise Joel, proclaimer of God: with them, pray that we may be saved, O blessed one!

Proclaiming the divine voices, O God-inspired one, thou didst foretell the fierce and righteous wrath which was to fall upon thy people who had fled from ungodliness, being bruised by these things.[^722]

The marvelous, light-bearing, and life-creating inspiration of the Spirit visited thee, and breathed into thee the knowledge of things to come, O most blessed prophet!

*Theotokion:*
The tabernacle of testimony prefigured thee, being depicted in manifold forms, revealing Him Who was immutably incarnate of thee, O all-pure Mother of God, Lady!

*Another canon, of the martyr.*
*Whose acrostic is: "I praise the passions of Varus, a great glory."*
*By Joseph, Tone 6.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
As Israel traversed the deep on foot as upon dry land with their feet, beholding their pursuer Pharaoh drowned, they cried: Let us sing unto God a song of victory![^723]

*Troparia:*
Thou hast appeared upon the height of Christ's Church, O Varus, like a bright sun, enlightening the fullness of the earth with the radiance of thy struggles, O blessed one!

Seized by the sweet desire of divine love, O all-blessed one, thou didst renounce thyself, and didst hasten unto every bodily torment, having valiantly contended!

Mentally having bound thyself with the bonds of the divine champions, O passion-bearer, thou didst destroy the deceptive bonds by the steadfast endurance of many torments!

*Theotokion:*
The transcendent Word took up His abode in thy womb beyond understanding, and being born in a manner befitting God, He brought the choirs of martyrs, O pure one: with whom we hymn thee in song!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the tree didst put sin to death; and plant the fear of Thee in the hearts of us who sing to Thee![^724]

*Troparia:*
By the wise dogmas of thy teachings, O prophet, we devoutly preach fasting and service, entering into the house of the Lord!

Following thy teachings with all our heart and mind, with weeping and prayers we seek God, O praiseworthy one!

Having revealed unto us the long-suffering of the Master, the abundant in mercy, O prophet, thou didst explain His divine goodness and grace!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst conceive God, O all-pure one, and didst bear the Word ineffably incarnate, Who spoke in the prophets and fulfilled their preachings!

*Another [canon, of the martyr]. Heirmos:*
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful[^725] O Good One, and hast established us upon the rock of Thy confession![^726]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst enter with steadfast mind into manly struggles, strengthened by the hopes of future blessings, and by the all-wise admonitions of the martyrs of Christ, O blessed one!

Beholding the army of holy passion-bearers suffering lawfully, thou didst show forth their manly strength, having joined thyself to manifold torments, O Varus!

In the sacred army, O Varus, thou wast found as a noble soldier, and by victories glorious didst cut down with the sword the multitude of the enemy's valor, O wise one!

*Theotokion:*
As the Mother of God, dispel the deepest darkness of my despondency and the relaxation of passions, I pray thee, by the illumination of the grace that is in thee!

*Sessional Hymn of the prophet, Tone 2, Podoben: "O passion-bearers of the Lord" (Страстоте́рпцы гдⷭ҇ни):*
O proclaimer of God, prophet, filled with the inspiration of the Comforter, thou didst explain the illumination of the Gentiles that was to come in the last times. Therefore, we now devoutly praise thee, and celebrating thy memory, cry out to thee: Pray to Christ God for our souls![^727]

*Glory, of the martyr, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
Having beheld the passions of the honorable martyrs, and having kissed their bonds with love, thou didst ascend unto the height of martyrdom, O all-marvelous one; for suspended upon a tree, thou didst endure wounds, until thou didst commit thy spirit into the hands of the Lord![^728]

*Both now, Theotokion:*
O hope of Christians, most holy Virgin, whom thou didst bear as God, beyond mind and word: unceasingly pray for those who sing of thee, to grant remission of all our sins and correction of life, unto us who ever glorify thee with faith and love!

*Cross-Theotokion:*
Beholding Thee, O Christ, stretched out dead upon the Cross, Thine all-immaculate Mother cried aloud: O my Son, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, what is this Thine ineffable dispensation, whereby Thou hast saved the creation of Thy most pure hands, O Merciful One!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the report of Thy dispensation, and I glorified Thee, the only Lover of mankind![^729]

*Troparia:*
Be merciful, O Lord, unto those who purely repent before Thee, for Thou alone art compassionate and plenteous in mercy!

Even as thou didst foretell, O glorious one, the Spirit from God was poured out upon all flesh of those who have believed!

The descent of the Spirit, having made wise the disciples of the Lord, fulfilled thy God-proclaimed words![^730]

*Theotokion:*
Having been delivered, O all-pure one, from the murky depths of Hades by thy divine childbearing, we call thee blessed!

*Another [canon, of the martyr]. Heirmos:*
Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church sings in a manner befitting God, crying aloud from a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord![^731]

*Troparia:*
Adorned with holy wounds, thou didst appear most beautiful, and beloved unto God Who crowned thy head, O Varus, right-marvelous one!

Torn upon the cross, O glorious one, and stretched out upon the earth, thou didst endure fierce pains. Therefore, rejoicing, thou didst inherit the life devoid of pain!

Beholding thee suffering lawfully, O blessed one, the divine martyrs strengthened thee with words of endurance: with whom, having completed long sufferings, thou wast called blessed!

With praises we crown the divine martyrs, whom divine grace crowned with divine diadems, having cast down the proud conceits of the enemy!

*Theotokion:*
Devoutly restrain the passions of the flesh, and passionate thoughts that alienate me from the only pure God, that in faith I may call thee blessed!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
The night having passed, the day is at hand, and light has shone upon the world. Therefore the angelic ranks praise Thee, and all things glorify Thee![^732]

*Troparia:*
The divine Joel clearly foretold the coming of Christ unto all: that He shall come and save the human race from the deception of idols!

Having risen entirely, O glorious prophet, unto the transcendent and unthinkable light, thou didst draw thence the grace of prophecy, and didst preach righteousness!

Pray for those who sing of thee, that they may be saved from every affliction, O most rich Joel, and entreat that we may be accounted worthy of divine grace and radiance!

*Theotokion:*
O most holy Virgin, goodness of the world, pray that those who confess thee as the Mother of God may be saved from countless sins and worldly tribulations!

*Another [canon, of the martyr]. Heirmos:*
With Thy divine light, O Good One, I pray Thee, lovingly illumine the souls of those who rise early unto Thee, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, the true[^733] God, who cry aloud from the gloom of sin![^734]

*Troparia:*
Thou art strengthened by divine beckonings, and with noble mind, O Varus, thou endurest unyielding tearings, tearing asunder the heart of the proud one who devised malice!

Beholding his own flesh falling to the earth, the glorious martyr rejoiced, contemplating beforehand the eternal things and divine rewards with the clarity of his mind!

I sing to Thee, O Lord God, and I glorify and worship Thee, being accounted worthy of the good things prepared for those who love Thee!—warmly cried the martyr in his suffering!

*Theotokion:*
