Having appeared as truly holier than the Cherubim and Seraphim, O all-immaculate one, sanctify all who now faithfully praise thee, and save them by thy mediation!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
A great abyss of sins surrounds me, and imitating the prophet, I cry to Thee: Lead my life up from corruption, O Lord![^735]

*Troparia:*
The fountains of thy words drip, pouring forth God-proclamations and soul-nourishing sweetness, O glorious one, giving drink unto those who approach in faith!

We know thee, O God-proclaimed prophet, as the mountain of prophecy pouring forth sweetness, and as the all-wise preacher of true piety, O Joel!

*Theotokion:*
O all-pure Lady, who didst bear the unwaning Light, illumine the hearts of those who faithfully glorify thee and Him Who was ineffably born of thee!

*Another [canon, of the martyr]. Heirmos:*
Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, I run to Thy calm haven and cry to Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Plenteous in mercy![^736]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst not heed the words of the most lawless enemy, and didst patiently endure the flame of intolerable pains, O much-suffering Varus, supplicant for those who praise thee in faith!

The most senseless tormentor did not cease crushing thee mercilessly with wounds, until thou didst commit thy sacred soul, radiant with the beauties of thy struggles, into the hands of God!

Thou wast lifted up, imitating Christ upon the tree; and hanging upon it, thou didst commit thy soul unto God Who crowns thee, O passion-bearer who didst contend lawfully and valiantly!

*Theotokion:*
Direct my humble soul, O Lady, ever tempest-tossed in the deep of evils and guide it unto a calm haven, as with unhesitating mind it calls thee blessed!

*Kontakion of the martyr, Tone 4:*
Following Christ, O martyr Varus, having drunk of His cup, thou wast crowned with the diadem of martyrdom, and dancest with the angels: unceasingly pray for our souls![^737]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Thou Who of old didst extinguish the all-burning furnace, and with spiritual dew didst bedew the youths: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^738]

*Troparia:*
The fountain going forth from the house of the Lord and giving drink has issued forth, even as thou didst foretell, O all-honorable prophet![^739]

Thou hast shown the torrent of Shittim to be a nourishing fountain bearing wheat, even as Joel foretold: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^740]

*Theotokion:*
He Who made all things, being born of the Virgin, saved the world that devoutly sings: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Another [canon, of the martyr]. Heirmos:*
The angel made the furnace bedewing for the venerable youths, but the burning command of God consumed the Chaldeans, persuading the tormentor to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^741]

*Troparia:*
Cleopatra, O Varus, in admirable manner attended thee, who wast enlightened by the additions of struggles, having found through thee a glory worthy of faith in this place, which she warmly displayed at thy decease!

Pouring out tears upon thee, and anointing thee with honorable myrrh, the all-honorable woman hides thee under the earth, who didst bury deceitful error, and from the grave dost enlighten us with radiance!

The ever-memorable one did not cease pleasing thee, O venerable servant of Christ, until thou didst appear as her advocate, O wise one, for the heavenly kingdom: of which she was accounted worthy through thee with her most beloved son!

*Theotokion:*
Crushed by countless transgressions, O Virgin, renew me by thy mediation, healing my soul, and granting me the word to cry: Blessed is she who gave birth to God in the flesh!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Him Who of old bedewed the Hebrew youths in the flame, and in it burned the Chaldeans most gloriously, let us praise the Lord, saying: Bless and exalt Him above all unto all ages![^742]

*Troparia:*
Thou hast clearly revealed images of repentance, O glorious one, showing the compassionate Lord Who receives through faith who approach Him in faith and sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Having become a servant of the Lord, O prophet Joel, thou wast entrusted to foretell His mysteries unto the people who praise and sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Rivers of wonders pour forth remission, which are the glorious apostles of the Lord, even as Joel prophesied, giving drink unto those who cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Thou, all-immaculate one, didst bear Christ, a composite Hypostasis of two natures, while remaining a pure virgin: singing unto Whom, we cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Another [canon, of the martyr]. Heirmos:*
Out of the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the venerable ones, and by water didst burn the sacrifice of the righteous man: for Thou doest all things, O Christ, only by willing: Thee do we exalt above all unto all ages![^743]

*Troparia:*
Having listened to the maternal supplications, O martyr, as a soldier shining brightly in the heavenly host, by Thine ineffable compassion thou didst show forth the honorable youth as never separated from thy glorious renown![^744]

Cleopatra, enriched by having brought thee from Egypt as a priceless treasure, O martyr, enriched all Palestine with the priceless gifts of healings, revering thee with love!

Contending, thou didst complete thy struggles, O noble soldier of the Almighty; and as a victor thou wast crowned by Him, O all-blessed Varus, great glory of martyrs!

*Theotokion:*
I add sins unto sins, and fear not the dreadful judgment to come; tell me, O pure one, the affliction of my soul, and having strengthened me by thy prayer, save me!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Blessed in the heavens and glorified on earth, rejoice, unwedded Bride![^745]

*Troparia:*
With the prophets as a prophet, thou rejoicest now gladly in the heavenly mansions!

From on high thou now watchest over us who devoutly glorify thee, O all-rich speaker of God!

Joel wisely showed the shortened path of salvation through repentance!

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast raised up us who had fallen, O Mother of God, having given birth to the compassionate God and Savior!

*Another [canon, of the martyr]. Heirmos:*
It is impossible for men to see God, upon Whom the angelic ranks dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, there appeared unto men[^746] the Word incarnate: Whom magnifying with the heavenly hosts, we call thee blessed![^747]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst cast down the strength of the adversary, being girded with divine power, O noble sufferer Varus. Therefore we praise thee, as the faithful draw abundant healing from thy relics!

Like light, like morning, like a great sun, thy memory has spread unto the ends of the earth, enlightening all with the light-bearing radiance of thy venerable struggles, O all-praised one, and driving away the gloom of hearts!

Beholding her son shining with thee in great glory, she who loved her child magnified the God of all; and she sincerely praised thee, O martyr, as her magnificent intercessor, having manifestly become rich!

The earth, upon which thy most patient body lies, O wise one, has been divinely sanctified; but by thy prayers grant us remission of sins, correction of life, O Varus, and deliverance from all distress!

*Theotokion:*
The Lord appeared from thee, O Virgin, as a bright cloud, and delivered the idols of Egypt unto divine destruction; and He accounted the multitude of passion-bearers worthy to bear this, imitating Him by His venerable sufferings![^748]

*Month of the same, on the 20th day: The Holy Great-Martyr Artemius.*[^749]

*At Lord, I Have Cried, stichera on 6, Tone 4, Podoben: "As noble among the martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Having given over thy much-suffering and most patient body to be punished with manifold torments of fierce wounds, O God-wise Artemius, Thou didst not deny Christ, nor didst Thou sacrifice unto idols; but Thou didst endure as though another were suffering, awaiting the future rewards and immortal renown![^750]

Suspended, scraped, crushed with stones, pierced through with iron on every side, and beheaded with the sword, thou didst remain unshaken having firmly set the feet of thy soul upon the spiritual rock, O glorious one. For this victory, thou hast joined the angels and martyrs, being filled with ineffable light!

Thy relics, O glorious one, ever pour forth the grace of healings unto those who love thee, and submerge passions and diseases, O Artemius, and put to flight the legions of malicious demons; and they water the hearts of the faithful, causing to sprout the divine fruits of virtues and the knowledge of piety!

*Glory, Tone 2, by the Studite:*
Let us honor the rational light of faith, Artemius; for he reproved the abominable emperor, and God crimsoned the Church with the blood of his martyrdom. Therefore he also received the ungrudging grace of healings, to heal the infirmities of those who in faith approach the shrine of his relics![^751]

*Both now, Theotokion or Cross-Theotokion, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
When the undefiled ewe-lamb beheld her Lamb willingly dragged to slaughter as a man, weeping, she said: Dost Thou now make me childless, O Christ, who gave Thee birth? What is this that Thou hast done, O Redeemer of all? Yet I hymn and glorify Thine extreme goodness, which surpasses mind and word, O Lover of mankind![^752]

*At the Aposticha of the Oktoechos.*
*Glory, Tone 6:*
Having lawfully suffered for Christ, O Artemius, and having cast down the spiritual enemy together with the visible emperor, thou didst worthily receive the crown from the Savior, the Judge of the contest; and now in the heavens thou rejoicest with the angelic ranks, clearly enjoying the celestial outpouring of light, and abundantly partaking of the Tri-hypostatic and Primal Light. Therefore we, the devout people and lovers of martyrs, cry out to thee in hymns: Rejoice, diamond invincible in soul, and stalwart warrior of Christ! Rejoice, destroyer of the idolatrous deceit, and radiant champion of the truth! But pray to the Lord that we also, through a life beloved of God, may attain with thee a standing before the throne of His majesty![^753]

*Both now, Theotokion or Cross-Theotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "On the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Beholding Thee crucified, O Christ, she who gave Thee birth cried aloud: What strange mystery is this which I see, O my Son? How dost Thou die in the flesh, suspended upon the tree, O Giver of life?[^754]

*Troparion of the martyr, Tone 4:*
Thy martyr Artemius, O Lord in his suffering received an incorruptible crown from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, he cast down the tormentors, and crushed the powerless insolence of the demons: by his prayers save our souls![^755]

*Glory, both now, Theotokion or Cross-Theotokion.*

*At Matins, both canons of the Oktoechos, without the martyria, and the canon of the saint on 6.*

*Canon of the Saint.*
*Whose acrostic is: "Grant me thy rich grace, O blessed one. Joseph."*
*Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
In the deep of old, the overwhelming power laid low the entire host of Pharaoh; but the incarnate Word destroyed all-wicked sin, the most glorious Lord, for He has been gloriously glorified![^756]

*Troparia:*
Having contended with the good fight, O martyr, and received radiant crowns, thou standest before the Light, enlightened by immaterial communion. Therefore we pray thee: illumine us by thy prayers!

Thy radiant festival, O martyr, today gladly called all unto the banquet, having set forth the struggles of thy sufferings, thy courage, and thy resistance: being filled with which, we call thee blessed in faith and love!

Having understood in the flesh the incomprehensible Lord of life, put to death and bowed down upon the Cross, O venerable one, thou didst love the voluntary death which comes from sufferings. Therefore thou didst receive immortal glory!

*Theotokion:*
Having taken flesh of thee in a manner befitting God, O all-pure one, through an abundance of love for mankind, the bodiless Word drew near unto sufferings, and poured forth dispassion unto men, the most glorious Lord: for He has been gloriously glorified!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The desert has blossomed like a lily, O Lord, the barren Church of the Gentiles, by Thy coming, wherein my heart has been established![^757]

*Troparia:*
Desiring to be adorned with crowns of martyrdom, O glorious one, thou didst endure wounds and the tearings of bitter pains by divine grace!

The most fierce addition of torments did not shake the pillar of thy heart, O all-wise one, for it was established upon the immovable spiritual rock!

Suspended upon the tree, and encircled on all sides with frequent tearings[^758] encircled, thou didst look with thine eyes toward Him Who is able to save, O glorious one!

*Theotokion:*
Having willingly taken flesh from thy most pure blood, O Birthgiver of God, the Lord saves mankind, and gathered assemblies of martyrs!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Pleasing the King of the ages, thou didst cast aside all counsel of the lawless emperor, O glorious one, and didst not sacrifice to graven idols; therefore thou didst offer thyself as a sacrifice to the Word Who was sacrificed, having suffered valiantly. Therefore thou also pourest forth streams of wonders, healing the passions of those who have recourse to thee in faith, O passion-bearer Artemius: pray to Christ God to grant remission of offenses unto those who with love honor thy holy memory![^759] *Twice.*

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
O cloud of the spiritual Sun, golden lampstand of the divine Light: undefiled, unbeguiled, all-immaculate Lady, illumine my soul darkened by the blindness of the passions with the radiance of dispassion, I pray thee; and wash my defiled heart with streams of compunction and tears of repentance, and cleanse me from the mire of my deeds, that in love I may cry unto thee: O Birthgiver of God, Ever-Virgin, pray to Christ God to grant me remission of transgressions; for I, thy servant, have thee as my hope![^760]

*Cross-Theotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross, the ewe-lamb wept and cried aloud, and bitterly lamenting, exclaimed: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee, but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion, which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of Thy loving-kindness, O longsuffering One, abyss of mercy and inexhaustible fountain! Show tender compassion, and grant remission of sins unto those who venerate Thy divine sufferings in faith![^761]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou didst come from the Virgin, neither an intercessor nor an angel, but Thyself the Lord incarnate, and didst save all of me, a man. Therefore I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^762]

*Troparia:*
Conformed willingly to the One crucified upon the tree, thou wast exalted while being stretched out, and most cruelly scraped, stripping off the garment of skins of mortality!

Thy bodily pains and the stretching out of cruel torments have mediated for thee, O martyr, a painless place of rest: being filled with which now, thou soothest every pain from our souls[^763] thou having become an equal of the angels by the splendors of thy sufferings, Christ our life, the heavenly Bread, sends thee food by an angel's hand, strengthening thee, O glorious martyr!

The grace of the Spirit adorned thee with rich gifts, O all-wise one; therefore thou drivest away every disease, O all-honored martyr, from those who have recourse to thee in faith!

*Theotokion:*
Christ, Who was well-pleased to take upon Himself all of me, a man, in thy womb by His extreme goodness, preserved thee an undefiled, incorrupt Virgin after giving birth, O immaculate one!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou hast become a Mediator between God and men, O Christ God; for through Thee, O Master, we have access from the night of ignorance unto Thy Father, the Source of light.[^764]

*Troparia:*
Thou wast seen through the Spirit to be a radiant beacon, O Artemius, enlightening the faithful, and driving away the knaveries of dark spirits filled with gloom!

Having planted the steps of thy soul upon the living Rock, when pressed down by stones and surrounded by pains, thou didst remain a true passion-bearer and unbending!

Armed with the spiritual weapons of the Spirit, O valiant-minded martyr, thou didst disregard the sharp edges of the tridents, wherein having been accounted worthy, thou didst trample down the stings of the enemy!

*Theotokion:*
Having piously endured the Cross and burial for our sake, the One born of thee, O Lady, made Artemius a most steadfast witness of His sufferings, as the strong One!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Wallowing in the abyss of sins, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy tender mercy: bring me up from corruption, O God![^765]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst offer thyself, O martyr Artemius, as a lamb and a whole-burnt offering unto Him Who received a voluntary crucifixion for our restoration!

While thy body was being crushed by exhausting wounds, O most patient Artemius, thou didst keep the nobility of thy soul uninjured through faith!

Deliver us from diseases, temptations, passions, and sorrows, O martyr Artemius, imitator of the Savior, who flee unto thee!

*Theotokion:*
Save thy servant, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, who am storm-tossed in the deep of sorrows, and overwhelmed by the assaults of surging waves!

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "The firm" (Тве́рдыѧ):*
Let us come together and worthily praise with hymns the pious and crown-bearing martyr, who took victory over the enemies[^766] Artemius, the greatest among martyrs and most bountiful giver of wonders: for he prays to the Lord for all of us!

*Ikos:*
Who can declare thy valiant struggles, O passion-bearer, or thy pains which thou didst endure manfully for the faith of the Lord, and the gifts of which thou wast accounted worthy? Human mouths cannot prevail; for adorned with wisdom and courage, having hated wealth and temporary dignity, thou didst show thyself a most faithful soldier, praying unto the Lord for all of us![^767]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The contrary command of the lawless tyrant raised a high flame, but Christ stretched out spiritual dew unto the pious youths: He Who is blessed and supremely glorified![^768]

*Troparia:*
Having acquired an invincible mind through the unbreakable steadfastness of thy soul, O Artemius, thou didst abolish the wiles of the enemy, O martyr, singing most excellently: Blessed and supremely glorified art Thou!

By the streams of thy blood, O blessed one, thou didst extinguish the high-flaming fire of torments, and being refreshed by the dew of the Spirit, thou didst cry out rejoicing: Blessed and supremely glorified art Thou!

Pouring forth gifts of healings, thou dost quench the inflammation of the passions by divine grace, driving away spirits by thine intercession, singing evermore: Blessed and supremely glorified art Thou!

*Theotokion:*
O all-immaculate Virgin, thou didst give birth in a manner befitting God unto Him Who poured forth immortality upon the dead: therefore pray Him, O pure one, to put to death our bodily passions, and to account us worthy of eternal life!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The furnace of fire in Babylon once divided its operation by the divine command, scorching the Chaldeans, but bedewing the faithful, who sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^769]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst endure thy body being exhausted by cruel wounds and tridents, looking unto the future reward which the God of all, the Master and Lord, grants unto Thee who criest, O glorious one!

The earth was sanctified by thy blood, and thy much-suffering body appeared as a medicine, ever driving away every disease, every affliction, and every harm of the demons from those who have recourse to thee in faith[^770] thou didst appear as an adornment of the sufferers, having piously rejected the things in the world, and having tested the transcendent rather than base things by thy precious blood, O all-praised one, singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth to the hypostatic Word, the Creator, O pure one, thou didst raise us up who had drained away through the bitter tasting of the tree, and who had fallen through slipping, O all-pure one; unto Whom we cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The Son of the unoriginate Father, God and Lord, having taken flesh of the Virgin, has appeared to us, to enlighten those in darkness, and to gather the scattered. Therefore we magnify the all-praised Birthgiver of God![^771]

*Troparia:*
Desiring to see heavenly beauties, thou didst pass beyond the visible beauties of the world, and having adorned thyself with the benefactions of sufferers, O valiant-minded martyr, thou didst receive the utmost of good things, rejoicing!

Like the dawn, like the sun, thy memory enlightens the hearts of the faithful, O blessed one of God; for thou hast appeared as a radiant heir of both the light and the day, dispelling the mist of our souls, O Artemius!

Let us leap for joy, O men, spiritually keeping festival, and singing the memory of Artemius, the divine martyr, unto God with praise, Who is marvelous in the saints, and Who sanctifies those who honor him in faith!

Thy shrine, continually pouring forth healings, summons the faithful to draw with love unto bodily health and true salvation of the soul, O all-blessed Artemius, intercessor for those who praise thee!

*Theotokion:*
Illumine me with the light of Thy countenance, O Lord, who am covered with the darkness of despondency; having as suppliants unto Thee, O Word, Thy true and pure Mother, and the angelic hosts, and the most glorious Artemius!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "O unchangeable Light" (Свѣ́те неизмѣ́нный):*
Light, having shone in thy heart, O passion-bearer Artemius, when thou didst manfully endure the most bitter torments, thou didst receive grace of healings from on high, ever to heal the infirm![^772]

*Theotokion:*
The Light Who shone forth timelessly from the Father before the ages, has now budded forth from thee in time at the end, O Virgin, for the salvation of the world: cease not to pray unto Him for the world!

*At the Aposticha, the stichera of the Oktoechos.*
*Glory, Tone 6:*
Today the whole universe is enlightened by the rays of the passion-bearer[^773] and the Church of God, adorned with flowers, O Artemius, cries aloud unto thee: O pleasing servant of Christ and fervent intercessor, cease not to pray for thy servants!

*Both now, Theotokion or Cross-Theotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "On the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Beholding Thee crucified, O Christ, she who gave Thee birth cried aloud: What strange mystery is this which I see, O my Son? How dost Thou die in the flesh, suspended upon the tree, O Giver of life?[^774]

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Oktoechos on 4; and of the martyr, Ode 3 on 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 7: The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord, and shall hope in Him. Verse: Give ear, O God, unto my prayer, when I pray unto Thee.[^775] The Epistle to Timothy, pericopa 292.[^776] Alleluia, Tone 4: The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree.[^777] Gospel of John, pericopa 52.[^778] Communion Hymn: The righteous man shall be in everlasting remembrance.[^779]*

*The same month, on the 21st day.*
*Our Venerable Father Hilarion the Great.*[^780]

*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera on 6, Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*
Meek in soul and heart, when divine love wounded thee, and by sacred ascents subdued thee to separate thyself from worldly turmoils, then, armed with the power of the Cross, thou didst rush against the demons in battle, O all-blessed one, and didst weave a diadem of victory! And now thou dwellest in the splendors of the saints, with whom do thou also ask for us enlightenment, peace, and deliverance from offenses!

By the light-giving rays of Thy healings, O father worthy of glory, having marvelously enlightened the world under the sun, dispelling the deepest darkness of diseases: therefore we recognize thee as another sun, the confirmation of monastics, and a guide of the saved by the Holy Spirit! And now we celebrate Thy radiant and saving memory, O Hilarion[^781] O Hilarion, receiving through thee the remission of transgressions!

By temperance, O father, thou didst subdue the bodily passions to the rational part of the soul; and adorned with the wings of dispassion, O venerable one, thou didst receive grace to heal the infirmities of men, and to drive away spirits of wickedness, and to save those who have recourse to thee. Therefore the choirs of monastics glorify thee, O worthy of glory, and all creation honors thy struggles, O Hilarion!

*Glory, Tone 6, by Anatolius:*
Bearing the wisdom of perfection in thy youth, thou didst lay upon thyself the yoke of Christ, O Hilarion; and thou didst emulate the life of the divine Anthony, likening thyself to him in equal measures of virtue. Having withered thy flesh which frisked like a colt, as was meet to subdue it to the soul, thou didst accomplish the course of fasting. But O most blessed father and wonderworking God-bearer, ask cleansing and great mercy for those who celebrate thy memory with love![^782]

*Both now, Theotokion or Cross-Theotokion, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
Beholding Thee nailed to the tree of the Cross, O Jesus, she who knew not wedlock cried weeping: O sweet Child, why hast Thou left me who gave Thee birth alone, O unapproachable Light of the Father Who is supremely without beginning? Make haste and be glorified, that those who glorify Thy divine passions may receive divine glory![^783]

*At the Aposticha, the stichera of the Oktoechos.*
*Glory, Tone 8, Idiomelon:*
Having been filled with the Holy Spirit, the venerable Hilarion abolished the wiles of demons; for having armed himself with the Cross, and being bold in Him, he healed all diseases by the word: the passions of souls and every plague of bodies. By his prayers, O Christ, send down peace upon us, as the Lover of mankind![^784]

*Both now, Theotokion or Cross-Theotokion, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder!" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ!):*
The undefiled heifer, beholding the Calf nailed to the tree willingly, lamented pitifully, crying: Woe is me, O most beloved Child! What has the thankless assembly of the Hebrews rendered unto Thee, wishing to make me childless of Thee, O all-beloved?[^785]

*Troparion, Tone 8:*
By the streams of thy tears thou didst cultivate the barren desert, and by the sighs from the depths thou didst bear fruit in thy labors a hundredfold, and becamest a beacon to the universe[^786] shining with wonders, O Hilarion our father, pray to Christ God that our souls may be saved!

*Glory, both now, Theotokion or Cross-Theotokion.*

*At Matins, both canons of the Oktoechos, without the martyria, and that of the saint on 6; whose acrostic is: "I sing the splendor of thy wonders, O all-blessed one. Theophanes." Tone 2.*[^787]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
In the deep of old, the overwhelming power laid low the entire host of Pharaoh; but the incarnate Word destroyed all-wicked sin, the most glorious Lord, for He has been gloriously glorified![^788]

*Troparia:*
Being eager to praise with great joy thy light-bearing and radiant life, O blessed one, I pray thee to illumine me with the quietness of thy brightness, and drive away from me the despondency of sin, O father!

Righteously, O blessed one, didst thou approach unto God rejoicing, having come to be outside flesh and the world; and thou didst preserve an untroubled mind, established by temperance, nailed on all sides to divine dispassion!

Illumined by the bright rays of the unoriginate and uncreated Godhead, O father, thou didst disregard evil belief, and turning away from its turbulent ignorance, thou wast united to the single Trinity!

*Theotokion:*
The Sun Who shone forth from thee, O Mother, Ever-Virgin, invisible from the Father before the ages, and visibly described in a body in most glorious manner, has enlightened mankind with the light of piety!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Having established me upon the rock of faith, Thou hast enlarged my mouth against mine enemies; for my spirit has rejoiced in singing: There is none holy as our God, and none righteous above Thee, O Lord![^789]

*Troparia:*
Bound with the chain of asceticism, thou didst wither the risings of poisonous passions, O ever-memorable one, crying aloud: There is none holy as our God, and none righteous above Thee, O Lord!

Having scraped off the garments of skins of mortality with the knife of temperance, thou didst weave a robe of salvation, crying to thy Creator: Thou art our God, and there is none holy above Thee, O Lord!

Strengthened by the hope of eternal things, and led unto God, O God-bearer, by the deeds of the divinely-wise Anthony, imitating whom thou didst become a beacon, shining with most glorious wonders, O venerable one[^790] O venerable one!

*Theotokion:*
He Who fashioned man by divine will, was well-pleased to be formed as a man, being created in thy womb, O Birthgiver of God; unto Whom we now cry: Thou art our God, and there is none righteous above Thee, O Lord!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Emulating spiritually the virtuous life of divine Anthony, thou didst take upon thy shoulders the Cross of thy Lord, having forsaken all worldly care; and having put to death the passions, thou didst live by the Spirit. Therefore thou didst also fill creation with most glorious wonders, O venerable one, by the grace of the Spirit, O Hilarion our father: pray to Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who with love honor thy holy memory![^791] *Twice.*

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
As the Virgin and only one among women who without seed gavest birth to God in the flesh, all generations of mankind call thee blessed; for the fire of the Godhead took up abode in thee, and thou sucklest the Creator and Lord as an infant. Therefore, O angelic and human race, we worthily glorify thy most holy childbearing, and with one accord cry unto thee: Pray to Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who venerate thy most holy childbearing in faith![^792]

*Cross-Theotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross, the ewe-lamb wept and cried aloud, and bitterly lamenting, exclaimed: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee, but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion, which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of Thy loving-kindness, O longsuffering Lord, abyss of mercy and inexhaustible fountain! Show tender compassion, and grant remission of sins unto those who praise Thy divine passions in faith![^793]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I sing to Thee, for by hearing I heard of Thee, O Lord, and was afraid; for Thou comest unto me, seeking me who have gone astray. Therefore I glorify Thy great condescension toward me, O most merciful One![^794]

*Troparia:*
Having put to death the wisdom of the flesh, the turmoil of the torrent of lawlessness, O venerable one, and escaped the wiles of demons, thou hast rested with the righteous, O father, in the quietness of the divine Spirit!

Rightly watered by streams of tears, O father, thou didst show forth the field of thy soul to be fertile through active cultivation, multiplying the assemblies of ascetics[^795] Strengthened by communion of the Holy Spirit, O God-bearer, Thou didst cast down the spirits of wickedness; and by the power of Christ Thou healest the infirm, having been enriched with prophetic purity!

*Theotokion:*
We understand thee to be a living temple and bridal chamber, an intellectual table, and a lampstand, O blessed Mother of God, from whom the Light shone forth upon those in darkness, the Radiance of the Father's essence!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
O Christ, my Savior, Enlightenment of those lying in darkness and Salvation of the despairing: unto Thee do I rise early, O King of peace; enlighten me with Thy brightness, for besides Thee I know no other God![^796]

*Troparia:*
Like lightning by the purity of thy life and conversation, imitating the bodiless hosts, having shone forth in the world, thou drivest away the prince of darkness, O divinely-blessed one, being enlightened by the light of piety!

Shining with all manner of wonderworking, O divinely-wise one, and illumined by divine grace, thou wast known to all as a treasure of healings; recognized by those who love thee, O God-welcoming father, as another prophet of God!

By the brightness of thy meek correct deeds, O father, thou didst drive away the dark enemy hosts; and having snatched those who flee unto thee from their rage, thou didst enlighten them unto faith by thy divine teachings!

*Theotokion:*
O only blessed one, thou didst ineffably give birth for us to the one Hypostasis visible in two natures: God the Word, Who took flesh for His ineffable mercy. Pray Him therefore to preserve those who praise thee!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Wallowing in the abyss of sins, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy tender mercy: bring me up from corruption, O God![^797]

*Troparia:*
Serving the monarchy of the unoriginate Godhead, thou didst refute polytheism, abolishing idolatrous impiety by grace, O divinely-wise one!

Thou didst flourish, O father, like a palm tree didst thou flourish in the Church, O all-blessed and divinely-wise one, gladdening the fullness of the faithful with the sweetness of thy fruits!

Thou didst appear as an invincible overthrower of evil spirits, O all-blessed one, having received enriching gifts from Christ, O blessed one!

*Theotokion:*
Without knowing man, O Virgin, thou didst give birth, and remainest a Virgin forever: manifesting the images of the true Godhead of thy Son and God!

*Kontakion, Tone 3, Podoben (Подо́бенъ):*[^798] *Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва дне́сь):*
As an unwaning light of the spiritual Sun, we, having assembled today, praise thee in hymns: for thou hast shone forth upon those in the darkness of ignorance, leading all up unto divine height, O Hilarion! Therefore we cry aloud: Rejoice, O father, foundation of all ascetics![^799]

*Ikos:*
Having loved the divine commandments of Christ, and having hated the enjoyment of the whole world, thou didst come eagerly unto Him, and becamest a beacon, enlightening all ends of the earth with the brilliance of the Spirit. Therefore falling down I pray thee: Enlighten also the eyes of my soul, to hymn thy struggles which thou didst display upon the earth for the sake of the life to come; enjoying which now, remember those who cry unto thee: Rejoice, O father, foundation of all ascetics![^800]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The contrary command of the lawless tyrant raised a high flame, but Christ stretched out spiritual dew unto the pious youths: He Who is blessed and supremely glorified![^801]

*Troparia:*
The proclamation of thy words and the divine grace of wonders went forth into all the earth, O all-blessed one, shining faithfully upon all with the radiance of piety, a calm harbor of healings!

Protected by the Cross of the Savior, thou didst remain unhurt when the demonic fury foolishly took up arms against thee, O all-wise one; for the Most High God knows how to preserve His pleasing servants!

Like an unshaken pillar, soaring to the heavens, like an immovable wall didst thou appear through fasting: enlightening with wonders, granting healings, and pouring forth divine doctrines from the ever-flowing fountain of divinely-inspired Scriptures!

*Theotokion:*
The cloud, the Virgin, gave birth to Thee, the Sun of righteousness, and remained a Virgin: Who didst partake of the grossness of flesh and becamest man through tender compassion and condescension; Whom we praise as supremely glorified!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The furnace of fire in Babylon once divided its operation by the divine command, scorching the Chaldeans, but bedewing the faithful, who sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^802]

*Troparia:*
Thou wast truly a pillar of spiritual perception, enlightened by divine understanding, and by God's command in a divinely-wise manner[^803] as one guided, and shining with virtue, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Thou didst appear as a mystical chariot bearing the name of the Lord, O venerable one, by the splendor of deeds, the manifestation of wonders, and the purity of life, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Adopted as a son by divine grace, thou becamest an heir of Christ, and didst receive incorruptible wealth and a kingdom that cannot be taken away, O speaker of God, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
As the noetic myrrh poured forth, O Virgin, thou didst bear in thy womb the named Son, Who with the incense of the Godhead perfumes the earthly with sweet fragrance, and delivers from corruption those who cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The Son of the unoriginate Father, God and Lord, having taken flesh of the Virgin, has appeared to us, to enlighten those in darkness, and to gather the scattered. Therefore we magnify the all-praised Birthgiver of God![^804]

*Troparia:*
Thou wast accounted worthy to pass into the dwellings of the saints, having completed thy life in holiness, O venerable one; and like seed, the thrice-radiant Light shone forth upon thee, the righteous one, and together with it, they received gladness.

Having received from Christ the recompense of thy labors, and manifestly partaken of eternal life, and being adorned beyond understanding with the beauties of divine comeliness, O father: pray unto the Birthgiver of God for those who praise thee!

Like a cedar of lofty top, exalted by virtue, thou wast planted in the courts of God, O God-bearer; and like an enclosed garden, like a fair-flowering paradise didst thou appear, and like a fountain bubbling forth the gifts of healings!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast a bridal chamber beyond understanding of the Word Who became incarnate, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, clothed and adorned with the glory of virtues. Therefore we proclaim thee, O all-immaculate one, to be the Birthgiver of God!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите):*
Thou didst turn away from worldly attachment as from something passionate, and having withered thy flesh with lack of food, thou renewest the furrows of those who hold fast, and wast enriched with glory, performing surpassing wonders, O summit of chief shepherds, divinely-welcome Hilarion![^805]

*Theotokion:*
In two wills and two natures, O all-immaculate one, yet in a single hypostasis, thou gavest birth ineffably unto God, Who for our sake willingly became poor even unto the Cross, and bestowed upon us the wealth of the Godhead, O Mother of God, Virgin!

*At the Aposticha, the stichera of the Oktoechos.*
*Glory, Tone 2. Idiomelon:*
Beholding thy quiet, pure, meek, and silent life, O father, Christ made His abode in thee, and thou becamest a divine dwelling-place. And for this cause thou dwellest with the angels in the heavens, O blessed one![^806]

*Both now, Theotokion or Cross-Theotokion, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
When the undefiled ewe-lamb beheld her Lamb willingly dragged to slaughter as a man, she wept and said: Thou hastenest now to make me childless of Thee, O Christ, who bare Thee! What is this which Thou hast done, O Deliverer of all? Yet I hymn and glorify Thine extreme goodness beyond understanding and word, O Lover of mankind![^807]

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Oktoechos on 4; and of the saint, Ode 3 on 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 7: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.[^808] Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me?[^809] The Epistle to the Corinthians, pericopa 188.[^810] Alleluia, Tone 6: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord.[^811] Gospel of Luke, pericopa 24.[^812] Communion Hymn: In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous man be.[^813]*

*The same month, on the 22nd day.*
*The Holy Equal-to-the-Apostles Abercius, Bishop of Hierapolis, the Wonderworker.*[^814]
*And the Holy Seven Youths of Ephesus.*[^815]

*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera of the saint, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
With the flashings of divine doctrines, thou didst dispel the polytheistic night, O father Abercius; and dawning as the morning, thou madest them sons of the day who were formerly in darkness, O hierarch, having displayed most glorious wonders. Therefore we all celebrate thy holy memory in faith, hymning thee, O divinely-inspired one!

Performing wonders in a single vessel, O father, thou didst combine wine and oil and another appearance, preserved when need arose unmixed by divine grace; and by prayer thou madest a gift of warm waters gush forth, healing all manner of diseases of those who come unto thee in faith and call thee blessed, O Abercius!

Cleaving entirely unto God, thou didst show thyself a fulfiller of divine doctrines, O Abercius; and having received grace from on high, O venerable one, thou didst drive away various diseases of men, and didst pull down the idol temples. Thou didst plant divine knowledge in those who formerly suffered wretchedly in ignorance, O blessed one!

*Other stichera of the martyrs, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Not submitting, the blessed youths disregarded the most lawless decrees, and being bound, they loosed the bond of deception; and having cast off worldly rank, they received glory which brought them to most glorious perfection and mediated for them the enjoyment of the heavenly kingdom!

Having valiantly trained yourselves for the struggles of suffering, O holy ones, ye hid yourselves in a cave, praying with unceasing supplications unto the Lord to grant strength and might; and by whatever judgments the Lover of mankind knows, He divinely commanded all of you to fall asleep in peace!

The divinely-gathered regiment, the blessed choir, the unconquerable assembly of martyrs, the fair triumph of the divine Church, the indivisible union, the invincible host, the unshakable foundation of piety: come, all ye, let us fervently hymn the witnesses of piety!

*Glory, Tone 6. By John the Monk:*
O venerable hierarch, all-blessed father, wonderworker, pleaser of Christ Abercius: having shone forth with prophetic life, thou wast accounted worthy of apostolic gifts, serving the Savior unceasingly with the angels; pray that our souls may be delivered from every menace![^816]

*Both now, Theotokion or Cross-Theotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Great is the power of Thy Cross" (Ве́лїя крⷭ҇та̀):*
A sword pierced thy heart, O all-pure one, when thou didst behold thy Son upon the Cross, and cried aloud: Show me not to be childless, O my Son and my God, Who didst preserve me a Virgin after childbearing![^817]

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
The truth of things revealed thee to thy flock as a rule of faith, an icon of meekness, and a teacher of temperance; therefore thou didst acquire high things through humility, and rich things through poverty, O father Abercius: pray to Christ God that our souls may be saved![^818]

...that our souls may be saved!

*And of the saints, Troparion, the same Tone:*
Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received incorruptible crowns from Thee, our God; for having Thine endurance, they laid low the tyrants, and shattered the powerless audacity of demons: through their prayers save our souls![^819]

*At Matins, one canon of the Oktoechos; and the canon of the hierarch on 4; whose acrostic is: "I sing the great glory of Abercius. Joseph." Tone 2.*[^820]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Come, ye peoples, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He brought forth from the bondage of Egypt, for He has been glorified![^821]

*Troparia:*
Illumined by the bright ray of the thrice-radiant Godhead, O Abercius, thou becamest light, enlightening those in darkness, and destroying all demonic gloom!

Thou didst have a heavenly life upon earth, O all-blessed one, having lived in the flesh as though fleshless, and having put to death the passions, and received the gift of priesthood!

Having in thee the speaking Word, O father, thou didst destroy the unreason of the vain-minded, having cast down the demonic temples and idols of deception!

*Theotokion:*
The tongues of orators lack power to speak of thy conception which is beyond word; for thou didst bear God in the flesh, O all-pure one, Who assimilated Himself to us out of goodness!

*Another canon of the martyrs, Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Of old the wonderworking staff of Moses, striking the sea crosswise and dividing it, plunged Pharaoh the charioteer into the deep; but it saved fleeing Israel as a foot-traveler, singing a song unto God![^822]

*Troparia:*
Ye holy seven youths honored by the number seven, radiant stars shining upon the firmament of the Church, who through testimony have settled into the heavens: remember those who honor you in faith!

Holding up the golden-beaming lamp with the sevenfold illumination of the Spirit, ye sevenfold martyrs diminished the gloom of deception, and with the light of piety now enlighten all sevenfold!

*Theotokion:*
In thee, O Virgin, I place the hope of my salvation; wash me entirely from the mire of sins, and make me clean, excellently pleasing thy Son, and doing His all-holy will!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the tree didst put sin to death; and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us who sing to Thee![^823]

*Troparia:*
With sacred words and manifestations of wonders, O Abercius, thou didst most gloriously guide those sailing the sea of deception unto the harbor of salvation!

Working healings, accomplishing surpassing wonders, driving away the fantasies of demons, thou becamest the cause of salvation for all, a protector of the rich!

Sacred word, divine life, character shining with wonders: thy life was glorious, O father, and thy death precious!

*Theotokion:*
God, Who before was invisible, became visible, O Virgin, being incarnate from thy womb through tender mercy; therefore in faith we call thee blessed!

*Another Heirmos:*
Thou Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens in wisdom and found the earth upon the waters: establish me, O Christ, upon the rock of Thy commandments; for there is none holy save Thee, the only Lover of mankind![^824]

*Troparia:*
Ye appeared upon earth as sevenfold-radiant luminaries of virtues; and having brightly enlightened with the ray of struggles and the flashings of wonders, ye poured forth the light of the knowledge of God upon all the world!

Having been committed to the Creator in maternal swaddling clothes, for His sake ye disregarded imperial glory and honor and passing wealth; therefore ye received the glory of incorruptible honors from Him!

*Theotokion:*
Raise up my deadened mind by the operation of Life Who appeared from thee unto the world, having consumed the wounds and images of sin, O only all-immaculate Birthgiver of God!

*Kontakion of the youths, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йся на Крⷭ҇тъ во́лею):*
Thou Who hast glorified Thy holy ones upon earth before Thy second and awesome coming, O Christ, by the most glorious rising of the youths, didst show the resurrection to those who knew it not, revealing incorruptible garments and bodies, and didst persuade the emperor to cry aloud: Truly there is a resurrection of the dead![^825]

*Ikos:*
Seeing of old the marvelous and lofty resurrection of the youths, the hierarch of Ephesus, being astonished in mind, wrote with diligence to the most faithful emperor concerning their rising: Deign, O master, to come here in thy majesty, that thou mayest see the power of thy God, which He has shown unto thee! And immediately the great trophy-bearer arose, and set forth making haste rejoicing, he appeared as a second chariot-rider Elijah, crying aloud: Truly there is a resurrection of the dead![^826]

*Sessional Hymn of the saint, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Thou didst appear as a hierarch clothed with honorable anointing, O father, in a divinely-working manner, and accomplishing all things by grace most sacredly; adorned with the splendor of divine gifts and wonders, by the Holy and Divine Spirit, O all-lauded one, healing diseases, burning demons, and turning back multitudes of the deceived, O Abercius! Therefore we cry unto thee: Pray to Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who with love honor thy holy memory![^827]

*Glory of the youths, Tone 4, Podoben: "Go before quickly" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Being as pillars of the Church of Christ, ye valiantly broke down the walls of unbelief, O seven brotherly martyrs! Therefore before death ye destroyed the pagan fury, and again after death the heretical belief, praying for our souls![^828]

*Both now, Theotokion:*
O pure, all-immaculate, and unwedded one, who alone in time didst bear the timeless Son and Word of God: with the holy and honorable patriarchs, martyrs, prophets, and venerable ones, pray Him to grant us cleansing and great mercy!

*Cross-Theotokion:*
O Virgin, all-immaculate Mother of Christ God, a sword pierced thy most holy soul when thou didst behold thy Son and God willingly crucified; Whom cease not to implore, O blessed one, to grant us forgiveness of transgressions![^829]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I sing to Thee; for by report, O Lord, I heard and was dismayed; for Thou comest unto me, seeking me who had gone astray. Therefore I glorify Thy great condescension toward me, O greatly merciful One![^830]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst adorn the seat of the throne, O venerable one, O hierarch, for thou didst excel with great wonders and with a radiant life. Therefore our most great God greatly glorified thee!

Knowing the Mother of the Lord's Parent, O God-bearer, thou didst fervently beseech Him to create the all-marvelous gushing forth of warm waters, for the healing and salvation of many!

By divine grace thou didst make the childless fruitful, and didst fulfill desires; for having given the seed of the word to the pious, O Abercius, like a good sower thou didst reap ears of grain a hundredfold, O blessed one!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst heal the crushing, O all-immaculate one, and the ancient wretchedness of earthborn men, having given birth to the Lord and King Christ, Who bore our infirmities through Thine ineffable tender mercy, O Bride of God!

*Another Heirmos:*
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art my power; Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who without leaving the bosom of the Father didst visit our poverty. Therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind![^831]

*Troparia:*
Joined together by a brotherly bond and inflamed with divine desire, by the harmony of true confession and faith, O martyrs of the Lord, ye put to shame the vain-minded reason of the tyrants and the polytheistic doctrine of deception!

Persecuted for Christ and with Christ, hiding in caves, mountains, and chasms of the earth: for His sake, in place of death, ye fell asleep with a life-bearing sleep for very many years, manifestly confirming the general resurrection of the earthborn!

*Theotokion:*
That Thou mightest renew the image buried by passions through the wealth of goodness, Thou didst dwell in the virginal womb, from which wisdom built the temple of God; through which Thou didst live with men, O compassionate One, and saved the ends of the world!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Enlightenment of those lying in darkness, salvation of the despairing, O Christ my Savior: unto Thee I rise early, O King of peace; enlighten me with Thine illumination; for I know no other God save Thee![^832]

*Troparia:*
Having set thy feet upon the rock of the heart, O divinely-wise one, thou didst teach those fallen into perdition and offering honor to stones, O blessed one, to honor with divine words the Rock, Jesus Christ, our God!

Let us honor Abercius, the sacred teacher: a physician of those suffering various diseases, a guide of all the deceived through good tidings, a depository of honorable gifts, a receptacle of the Holy Spirit!

Wine and oil, by thy command, together with other blessed appearances in a single vessel, at the proper time are poured out unmixed in most glorious manner, manifesting thine overflowing grace unto me, O sacred one!

*Theotokion:*
The laws of nature are most gloriously renewed in thee, O Virgin; for without seed thou barest God the Savior, and remainest still a Virgin, a strange wonder! Therefore we piously proclaim thee to be the Mother of Emmanuel!

*Another Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy face, O never-setting Light, and an alien darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my paths toward the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee![^833]

*Troparia:*
Having died unto the world, ye cast away all the beautiful things of life, committing your life unto the Upholder of life; having prolonged a life-bearing sleep for many years, as an icon of the general resurrection!

Ye showed yourselves sufferers in life, having endured long persecutions in hunger and thirst, in various perils, tribulations, and malice, enduring for Christ and being enriched with true rest!

*Theotokion:*
Having been enriched in thee as a wall, and preserved by thy protection, boasting in thy divine glory, we call thee blessed; for thou, O all-pure one, pourest forth gladness and joy upon our souls!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Wallowing in the abyss of sins, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy tender mercy: bring me up from corruption, O God![^834]

*Troparia:*
From the east unto the west thou didst come, O venerable one, and by the power of the Spirit drovest away demonic and cruel passions from the empress!

Thou didst appear a stranger to the world, O most sacred one, accomplishing strange and awesome wonders while walking in the body, and having departed hence, O Abercius!

Seen as lightning-visaged, O father, thou didst conquer the hosts of demons, who were unable to endure thy rebuke, O Abercius, divinely-inspired one!

*Theotokion:*
Through thee, O all-holy Bride of God, we who were slain have been accounted worthy of life, and we who were in bondage to corruption have been manifestly enriched with incorruption!

*Another Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for my transgressions are many; and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou give ear to me, O God of my salvation![^835]

*Troparia:*
Through love toward God, O wise ones of the fatherland of Ephesus, ye abandoned your radiant lineage, exceedingly bright wealth, and most honorable glory, choosing the heavenly inheritance!

The sevenfold choir of sufferers, divinely united, laid low the multitudinous host of enemies, and received the glory of confession and crowns of suffering!

*Theotokion:*
Comeliness shone forth from Sion on high by the assumption of flesh, through an ineffable union, being clothed from thee in human form and enlightened the world!

*Kontakion, Tone 8, Podoben: "As first-fruits of nature" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки):*
As a most great hierarch and companion of the apostles, the whole Church of the faithful honors thee, O Abercius: which by thy prayers preserve, O blessed one, unconquered and unassailable by any heresy, and undefiled, as one ever-memorable![^836]

*Ikos:*
Pondering the Light of all the world, estranged from the world, and imitating the sufferings of Christ, thou didst put to death the passions of the flesh, and taking up thy cross, O blessed one, didst follow thy Creator. And abiding with Him, pray now for those who honor thee in truth, as one ever-memorable![^837]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
When the golden image was worshipped on the plain of Dura, Thy three youths disregarded the godless decree; cast into the midst of the fire, bedewed they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^838]

*Troparia:*
As a divine pastor, as a worker of wonders with thine own hands, by prayer, O father, thou didst grant sight to the blind, hearing to the deaf, cleansing to the lepers, and good walking to the lame, who sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

The bubbling forth of warm waters and the pillar lying by thy tomb, which the evil spirit driven out by thee brought from the great city of Rome, preach thy wonderworking unto the age, O wise Abercius!

Thou wast a guide to humble people, lofty in visions, and in actions and powers thou didst appear most sacred, and a pleaser of the highest King; unto Whom we cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
He Whom the Father begat before the morning star was born of thee, O Maiden, known unto all in two essences and two wills as God and man, who cry in faith: Blessed art thou who barest God in the flesh!

*Another Heirmos:*
The fire was put to shame before God's condescension in Babylon of old; therefore the youths in the furnace, dancing with joyful step as in a meadow of flowers, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^839]

*Troparia:*
Obeying God, instructed by the divine law in right understanding, O youths, ye rebuked the most lawless decrees of the wicked tyrants, through death having acquired life and incorruption, being enriched with a dead body desiring faithfully to drink the cup of death for the Creator's sake, for the sake of immortality and incorruption, ye endured the burial of your bodies, explaining the resurrection by your most glorious rising, O martyrs!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst dawn as the morning, having the Sun of righteousness, Who snatches the world from the darkness of ignorance by the knowledge of God; unto Whom we sing, O all-pure one: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Hymn Him as Lord, ye works, Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew youths, and changed the flame into dew; and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^840]

*Troparia:*
Let Abercius be honored: the most glorious pastor, inexhaustible fountain of healings, pillar of the Church, confirmation of the faithful, great and brilliant luminary, sacred teacher!

Thou didst flourish like a palm tree according to the psalm in the courts of the living God; Thou didst multiply like a cedar, and didst appear like an olive tree, O wise one, anointing the faces of all in faith with the oil of thy deeds![^841]

Thou wast manifestly known as a city set upon the summits of the mountains by an active way of life; and thy city, which of old was submerged in deception, O venerable one, thou showest forth as an heir of the city on high![^842]

*Theotokion:*
Behold the bed of Solomon, which the splendors of the prophecies of divinely-inspired Scripture now surround, upon which Christ God rested by a divine incarnation, O blessed and full of grace![^843]

*Another Heirmos:*
In his rage the Chaldean tyrant heated the furnace sevenfold for the godly ones; but seeing them saved by a greater power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^844]

*Troparia:*
Sevenfold did your memory shine forth today after the likeness of the rays of the sun, O wise ones, enlightening that which is under the sun with the splendors of suffering, the flashings of wonderworking, and the rays of divine dogmas, whereby ye assure all of the rising of bodies and the incorruption of the dead!

God formerly established the preservation of Abimelech in the field, so that of old he saw not the desolation of the city; and He preserved you sleeping in a cave for very many years, for the confirmation of the resurrection of all, O youths, equal in number to the sevenfold ages![^845]

...O seven in number luminaries!

*Theotokion:*
Having been purified by the Spirit, O all-immaculate Birthgiver of God, thou truly didst receive in thy womb the unoriginate and co-eternal Son, incarnate of thee, O pure one, unto the benefaction of those who cry in faith: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
God the Word, Who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam, who by eating had grievously fallen into corruption, and Who for our sake was ineffably incarnate of the Holy Virgin: let us the faithful magnify Him with one accord in hymns![^846]

*Troparia:*
How radiant is thy life! How beautiful are thy feet![^847] How marvelous is thy prayerful boldness and closeness unto God! How exceedingly great is the grace given unto thee from on high, O wise one! Therefore in faith we honor thee, O Abercius!

Thee, as a fellow-dweller with all the holy martyrs, a zealous emulator of the divine disciples, an equal in rank of the patriarchs, a companion of the prophets and the venerable, and a converser with the heavenly ministers, we glorify in faith, O Abercius!

Thy most radiant memory, extending throughout the world, enlightens the souls of men with rich light in grace: celebrating it with love, deliver us from passions and the darkness of tribulations by thy prayers, we pray thee, O Abercius!

*Theotokion:*
As a radiant lampstand the prophet foresaw thee, truly bearing within thyself the divine Candle, O all-holy Virgin: the one transcendent God, Who for the tender compassions of His mercy was made man of thee![^848]

*Another Heirmos:*
Heaven was stricken with awe at this, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and that thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the ranks of angels and men magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God![^849]

*Troparia:*
Every earthborn was amazed at this, and every mind was astonished: that the dead, lying in a cave after a burial of many years, appeared as though they had fallen asleep at evening and arisen as usual at morning, confirming the common resurrection of the dead![^850]

O seven in number luminaries of the martyrs, ever-radiant beacons of Ephesus, intercessors for the whole world and fervent protectors, preachers of the common resurrection and guardians of the faithful: ask tranquility and salvation for us, and peace unto all the ends of the earth!

*Theotokion:*
As the Mother of Him Who suffered for us, deliver me from soul-corrupting passions. As tenderly compassionate, loose the bonds of transgressions; as good, soothe now my soul, mistreated by demonic assaults, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, that I may hymn thee, the all-lauded!

*Exapostilarion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите):*[^851]
The demon, the author of stumbling, who had wickedly entered into the emperor's daughter, compelled thee to hasten unto the city of Rome; from whom having cast out the dog, thou didst send him away laden, carrying a pillar to Hierapolis, O Abercius, divinely sounding!

*Glory, of the martyrs:*
Fenced about with the weapon of Thy precious Cross, O Word, Thy passion-bearers mightily conquered the opposing powers and put the tyrants to shame, and having suffered for Thee, they reign together with Thee, O Christ, my King Who art without master![^852]

*Both now, Theotokion:*
O thy mysteries! O thy wonders! How after childbirth didst thou remain a virgin, even as before childbirth? How didst thou contain God, Who is nowhere containable? Truly unutterable are all thy things, O blessed Mary!

*At the Liturgy:* *The Beatitudes from the Oktoechos for 4, and of the saint, Ode 3, for 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
My mouth shall speak wisdom, and the meditation of my heart shall be of understanding.[^853]
*Verse:* Hear these things, all ye nations; give ear, all ye that inhabit the world.[^854]
*Epistle to the Hebrews, pericopa 318.*[^855]
*Alleluia, Tone 2:* The mouth of the righteous shall meditate wisdom.[^856]
*Verse:* The law of His God is in His heart, and His steps shall not be tripped.[^857]
*Gospel of John, pericopa 36.*[^858]
*Communion Hymn:*
In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be; he shall not be afraid of evil tidings.[^859]

*The same month, on the 23rd day.*
*The Holy Apostle James, Brother of the Lord.*[^860]

*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera on 6. Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Come, ye godly-wise, let us sacredly honor the memory of the Brother of God; for having zealously received Christ's Gospel of His goodness, he was shown forth as a preacher of His kingdom, and confirms His ineffable dispensation. Therefore, O All-powerful One, grant cleansing unto us through him!

The sound of thy words hath resounded unto the ends of the universe, whereby we are enlightened in every form of divine virtue, and are guided in faith unto the knowledge of the Holy Trinity. Therefore we beseech thee as a hierarch: pray unto Jesus, the Lover of mankind, that He save our souls!

Foresighted from afar regarding thy life, O James, Christ the Lover of mankind, the most wise Foreseer, received thee as a brother; He appointed thee as a faithful consecrated minister of Jerusalem, and a chief shepherd and mystagogue, sacredly ministering ineffable mysteries: Whom even now beseech, that our souls may be saved!

*Glory, Tone 6. Idiomelon:*
With the blood of martyrdom hast thou adorned thy priesthood, O hieromartyr apostle; for standing upon the pinnacle of the sanctuary, thou didst proclaim God the Word to be the Creator of all.[^861] Therefore, cast down by the Jews, thou wast accounted worthy of the heavenly bridal chambers, O James, Brother of God: pray unto Christ God that our souls may be saved!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Who would not call thee blessed, O all-holy Virgin? Who would not hymn thy childbirth without pangs?[^862]

*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
The multitudes of the pious, now joyfully celebrating the sacred feast, let us all cry aloud, faithfully praising today in psalms and hymns the Brother of God and disciple of the Lord: for he unceasingly prays that we may be saved!

*Verse:* Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.[^863]

According to the flesh thou wast shown forth as the brother of the Lord of desires, O most wise one, a disciple and eyewitness of divine mysteries: fleeing with Him and being in Egypt with Joseph and the Mother of Jesus; with whom pray that our souls may be saved![^864]

...with Joseph and the Mother of Jesus; with whom pray that our souls may be saved!

*Verse:* The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands.[^865]

The apostolic choir chose thee to serve as priest first in Holy Zion unto Christ the Benefactor, as being His kin and brother according to the flesh, a fellow traveller and successor to His footsteps, O James!

*Glory, Tone 8:*
Thou wast a brother and successor of Christ the Chief Shepherd, and renowned among the apostles; thou didst love to die for Him, and wast not ashamed of martyrdom, O glorious James: unceasingly pray unto Him to save our souls!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Receive, O Birthgiver of God, the supplications of thy servants, delivering us from all adversity!

*Troparion, Tone 2:*
As the Lord's disciple, O righteous one, thou didst receive the Gospel; as a martyr thou hast that which is unwritten; boldness as the brother of the Lord; to pray as a hierarch: pray unto Christ God that our souls may be saved![^866]

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
All above understanding, all most glorious are thy mysteries, O Birthgiver of God: sealed in purity and preserved in virginity, thou wast known to be a mother without deceit, having given birth unto the true God; Whom beseech that our souls may be saved![^867]

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the troparion twice. Glory, both now, Theotokion.*
*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*[^868]
O apostle of Christ, eyewitness and martyr, all-wise James, blessed brother of the Lord: from the transgressions that hold us fast, deliver by thy prayers those who celebrate thy precious memory in faith, inheriting the heavens by the power of the Spirit! *Twice.*
*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
The bush which Moses saw unconsumed; the mountain of God, the holy cloud; the tabernacle undefiled, the God-bearing table; the palace of the transcendent King; the all-radiant and impassable door: as such, O Virgin, do we hymn thee!

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезначáльное):*[^869]
As a disciple of the Word and preacher of the truth, thou wast sent forth as an arrow, enlightening those who were in cruel darkness, O holy apostle; and driving away the gloom of godlessness from the earth, thou didst show forth all the faithful as sons of the day, O holy James, eyewitness of Christ! *Twice.*
*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
Having an unswerving hope in thee, O all-immaculate Virgin, who art our protection: from various tribulations, circumstances, and cruel dangers deliver us, praying unto thy Son with His apostles; and save all who sing praises unto thee!

...sing praises unto thee!

*Both canons of the Oktoechos, without the martyr-troparia, and the canon of the apostle on 6, by Theophanes, Tone 4.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Having traversed of old on foot the Red Sea's deep with unwetted feet, ancient Israel conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness by Moses' hands stretched out in the form of a cross.[^870]

*Troparia:*
Unto the good giving and the perfect gift to be bestowed upon men from the Father of lights,[^871] thou wast manifestly appointed, O James: pray that those who hymn thee, O apostle, may partake thereof!

Enlightened by the light of the noble and divine Spirit, and having set forth laws with Him: thou dost govern the Church of the nations as Christ's apostle, O all-blessed hieromystic!

Manifestly didst thou adorn the apostolic choir, O all-wise one, having become the first hierarch, anointed by the personal action of the Word as a disciple and brother of the Lord, a most sacred preacher!

*Theotokion:*
Having thee as a fountain of healings, we draw forth, as having given birth unto the Fountain of life, O pure Virgin Birthgiver of God: medicine for souls and renewal for bodies!

*Katavasia: I shall open my mouth...*[^872]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and confirmation![^873]

*Troparia:*
The tribe of Judah rejoices in Thee today with Christ's splendors, seeing Thee shining radiantly!

Having thee as a God-woven star and glorious preacher, the Church of the Gentiles is illumined with divine light!

Thou hast laid down the law of life for the Church of Christ, decreeing and speaking by the life-giving Spirit!

*Theotokion:*
Him Whom thou didst bear in the flesh, O Mother of God, Who shone forth from the Father: Him did James proclaim as the God of all, O pure one!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^874]
Thou wast a kinsman according to the flesh of Jesus, and from Him, O apostle, didst receive great grace, and unto all didst impart enlightenment and understanding, O all-wise James, and didst uproot the idolatrous deception from its root. Therefore the champions and deceivers of darkness unjustly slay thee as thou preachest the Savior. Therefore we cry unto thee: Pray unto Christ God to grant the remission of sins to grant the remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love! *Twice.*
*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
O cloud of the spiritual Sun, gold-wrought lampstand of the divine Light: immaculate, blameless, all-immaculate Sovereign Lady, enlighten my soul darkened by the blindness of passions with the radiance of dispassion, I pray thee; and wash my defiled heart with streams of compunction and tears of repentance, and cleanse me from the mire of my deeds, that with love I may cry unto thee: O Birthgiver of God, Ever-Virgin, pray unto Christ God to grant me remission of transgressions; for in thee have I, thy servant, placed my hope!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
For the sake of the love of Thine image, O Compassionate One, Thou didst stand upon Thy Cross, and the nations melted away; for Thou, O Lover of mankind, art my strength and praise![^875]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst lead Thine apostle-horses into the sea, O Savior, and they enlightened the nations, having drawn them unto Thy knowledge, O Master![^876]

Having appeared pure as a mirror, and having faithfully received the primordial rays of the uncreated Trinity, thou didst shine forth upon all!

Sacred prophecy sees thee as a stone cut out without hands, shattering the rotten vanity of deception by the power of the Spirit![^877]

*Theotokion:*
Entering into thy womb, O Mother of God, the pre-eternal Word, Who was ineffably born of the Father, manifests James as an apostle!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou, O my Lord, hast come as a light into the world, a holy light turning from gloomy ignorance those who hymn Thee in faith![^878]

*Troparia:*
Thou, O Lord, didst reveal the shining radiance of James as a light, transforming earthly ignorance and darkness!

Thee, O Lord, with a free voice did Thy righteous brother proclaim, and becoming an emulator of Thy passion, he was slain!

What earthly mind can offer songs of praise unto him who was enlightened with the graces of virtues, O James?

*Theotokion:*
Having found thee truly a divine dwelling-place, O Virgin, the Word Who provides for our salvation took up His abode in thee!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice to Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from demonic blood by the blood which flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side![^879]

*Troparia:*
He Who for mercy's sake received thee into brotherhood lawfully vested thee with a better vestment, and shows thee forth as a hierarch more honorable than Aaron of old![^880]

As an instrument struck by the operations of the Spirit, delighting all understanding, and bringing unto God those saved from cruelest bondage: Thou wast most eminent!

Thou didst drink the cooling waters of life, and pourest forth rivers flowing from thy divinely-speaking belly unto the universe by the divine Spirit!

*Theotokion:*
Eve through disobedience brought forth death, but the Birthgiver of God, having conceived Life, brought forth Him Who grows not old, and repaid the ancient debt for all!

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йся на Крⷭ҇тъ во́лею):*[^881]
God the Word, the Only-begotten of the Father, Who came unto us in the last days, O divine James: showed thee forth as the first shepherd and teacher of the people of Jerusalem, and a faithful steward of the spiritual mysteries. Therefore we all honor thee, O apostle!

*Ikos:*[^882]
Thee, the first hierarch of Jerusalem, O James, beholder of God, and brother of the Lord, let us faithfully hymn with songs of praise, and let us cry aloud unto thee: Grant us a perfect gift from the Father of lights, and drive away the affliction that comes upon us from the multitude of our transgressions; for the enemies have lifted up their heel against us, and the Ishmaelites have surrounded us: whose bows shatter quickly, O sacredly revealed one,[^883] that we all may honor thee, O apostle!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace the children of Abraham, burned by the love of piety rather than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord![^884]

*Troparia:*
As in a furnace, enlightened in the upper room by the divine coming of the Spirit,[^885] mystically didst thou cry aloud, O divinely speaking one: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

The violent rushing wind from on high prepared the apostle to declare the great works of God with fiery tongues: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

Setting forth the tablet of active contemplation as upon spiritual tablets, thou teachest men, crying: Blessed art Thou, O my God and Lord!

*Theotokion:*
He Who fashioned the world in thy womb, O Bride of God, shows forth James as the celebrant of divine service, crying: Blessed art thou among women, O all-immaculate Birthgiver of God!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Daniel, stretching out his hands, stopped the yawning mouths of lions in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, having girded themselves with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord;[^886] hymn and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
Stretching out Thy mighty hands, Thou didst bless Thine apostles, O Savior, as Thou didst ascend, O Master, unto Thine unoriginate Father, and didst prepare them to cry with rejoicing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^887]

Even as Thou didst truly promise Thine apostles, Thou didst send the Spirit; enlightened by His radiance, O Master, James manifestly enlightens the universe with lightnings, and cries unto Thee: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Thou didst have hypostatic Wisdom as thy Teacher, O wise James, instructing thee in ineffable and unutterable mysteries; and thou didst stir up people to call upon piety: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Manifestly as a spiritual mountain did the prophet see thee, O pure Virgin; for from thee was the most precious stone cut out, saving the universe that truly and divinely sings: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
A stone cut out without hands from an uncut mountain, O Virgin, even Christ the cornerstone, was cut out from thee, uniting separated natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God![^888]

*Troparia:*
Come, let us all magnify the first-enthroned, the luminary of the Church, with sacred hymns: James, as a hierarch and preacher of Christ, named Righteous after his very name![^889]

As having been shown forth as a partaker of the divine and unending kingdom, and named the brother of the Lord, verifying the name by thy manner of life, a hieromystic: thee do we magnify, O James!

Standing as a crown-bearer before the throne of the Trinity, O blessed one, pray unto thy Master, O James, well-pleasing hierarch, that those who honor thy memory in faith may enjoy incorruptible honors!

*Theotokion:*
Ineffably born of the Virgin, O divinely revealed one, by thy teachings thou didst manifestly teach men to honor the incarnate Word as God; in Whom we now believing, worthily magnify Him!

*Exapostilarion, Tone 3, Podoben: "In the spirit in the sanctuary" (Дх҃омъ во святи́лищи):*[^890]
As having been the brother of the Lord, O blessed, divinely-inspired James: we the faithful worthily praise thee, glorifying thy radiant, all-honorable, and light-bearing memory; pray fervently for those who honor thee! *Twice.*
*Glory* both now, Theotokion:*
Unto Thee do we the faithful worthily bring Thy precious Mother as an intercessor and fervent protector: account Thy servants worthy to become partakers of Thy glory, and of the portion of the righteous, and of heavenly delight, that we may attain unto the heavenly kingdom!

*At the Praises, stichera on 4. Tone 8, Podoben: "Lord, even if before the tribunal" (Гдⷭ҇и, а҆́ще и҆ на сꙋди́щи):*[^891]
O Lord, even if James stood upon the pinnacle of the sanctuary,[^892] yet crying aloud with boldness he proclaimed Thee as God the Word, the Creator of all, Who camest into the world: by whose prayers grant even now enlightenment unto our souls!
