O Lord, even if the apostle's head was shattered with a fuller's club,[^893] yet in paradise the tree of life was given unto him; and having been loosed from temporary things, he rejoices eternally: by whose prayers send down Thy peace upon the churches!

O Lord, having valiantly confessed Thee on earth as the Son of God Most High, the brother of the Lord, truly ever-memorable, put to shame and subdued the ill-famed enemies; therefore having also suffered, he stands before Thee rejoicing!

O Lord, even if the Jews cast down the righteous one from the height,[^894] yet was he joyfully accounted worthy of the heavenly chambers, and he worthily rejoices in the banquet of delight: therefore Thou dost bestow peace upon our souls!

*Glory, Tone 4. By Byzantios:*[^895]
Having been enlightened by the fiery radiance of the divine Spirit, thou wast shown forth as a divine zealot of piety, O James, brother of the Lord: therefore He Who for mercy's sake received thee into brotherhood vested thee with a vestment more honorable than that of ancient Aaron of the legal priesthood. Him beseech, O praise of the apostles, that our souls may be saved!

*Both now, Theotokion.*
*The Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy:* *The Beatitudes from the canon of the apostle, Odes 3 and 6. Prokeimenon, Tone 8:*
Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.[^896]
*Verse:* The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands.[^897]
*Epistle to the Galatians, pericopa 200.*[^898]
*Alleluia, Tone 1:* The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord.[^899]
*Verse:* God is glorified in the council of the saints.
*Gospel of Matthew, pericopa 56.*[^900]
*Communion Hymn:*
Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.

*The same month, on the 24th day.*
*The Holy Martyr Arethas and those with him.*[^901]

*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "All-praised martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мч҃ницы):*[^902]
Against Jewish fierceness,[^903] O all-praised martyr Arethas, thou didst oppose thy manly courage, and by divine grace thou wast shown forth as a victor, and didst lead a choir of martyrs soldiering for Christ, gathered from every age, O glorious one, and composed from every lineage!

Thou didst marshal, O all-glorious one, having assembled a beautiful choir of thy holy fellow-martyrs, with whom thou didst fight the good fight and didst finish the course of thy most courageous suffering: therefore pray unto Christ that He grant our souls peace and great mercy!

As godly-wise martyrs, inflamed with love for Christ, having forgotten natural weaknesses and being manifestly strengthened by divine power, they trampled underfoot the enemy's deception: they feared not the torment of sicknesses, and were not afraid of the fiery furnace!

*Glory, Tone 4. By Anatolius:*[^904]
Today let us the faithful sacredly honor with one mind the chief shepherd, the most glorious Arethas with those who were with him: for he rebuked the most lawless king, and poured out his blood for the confession of Christ; whence also a fiery cloud consumed upon the face of the earth, rebuking the impiety of the most lawless.[^905] Therefore, O Christ God, having strengthened Thy passion-bearers unto Thy praise, preserve us also, as Master of all, from temptations and afflictions, by the prayers of Thy saints!

*Both now, Theotokion or Cross-Theotokion, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́бля):*[^906]
When the ewe-lamb saw Thee, the Lamb and Shepherd, upon the Tree, she who bare Thee wept, and maternally cried unto Thee: O beloved Son, how art Thou suspended upon the wood of the Cross, O Long-suffering One? How are Thy hands and Thy feet, O Word, nailed by the lawless? And Thou hast poured out Thy blood, O Master!

*At the Aposticha of the Oktoechos. Glory, Tone 8. By Luke:*[^907]

Having understood the crafty and faithless evil counsel of the Jews,[^908] ye strove by the boldness of the Spirit to suffer for Christ. For as of old under the Law, they provoked Him Who saved them out of Egypt in the wilderness, and condemned Him to the Cross: so now also they proceeded in deed against you, having sickened with faithlessness, they condemned you to the fire.[^909] But ye, having suffered together in like-mindedness and manly endurance, were shown forth awesome among the martyrs: having boldness before God, pray that our souls be delivered from grievous transgressions!

*Both now, Theotokion or Cross-Theotokion, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^910]
"What is this spectacle seen, which is beheld by mine eyes, O Master, Who upholdest all creation? Being lifted up upon the Tree, and Thou diest, Who grantest life unto all!" The Birthgiver of God wept and said, when she beheld God and man, Who shone forth ineffably from her, lifted up upon the Cross.[^911]

*Troparion, Tone 4:*[^912]
Entreated, O Lord, by the sufferings which the saints endured for Thy sake, and heal all our sicknesses, O Lover of mankind, we pray Thee!

*Glory, both now, Theotokion or Cross-Theotokion.*
*At Matins, both canons of the Oktoechos, and of the martyrs, on 4.*
*Its acrostic: The choir of martyrs let us godly-fairly praise in hymns.*[^913]
*A composition of Theophanes. Tone 8.*[^914]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us send up a hymn, O ye people, unto our marvelous God, Who freed Israel from bondage, chanting a song of victory and crying: Let us sing unto Thee, the only Master![^915]

*Troparia:*
O martyr passion-bearer of Christ, Arethas all-blessed, by thy prayers and those of the martyrs who are with thee, enlighten my mind, that I may hymn your radiant and divine memory!

Having thy life on high, and having gained it in the heavens, O godly-blessed one, through the likeness of Christ thou didst surrender thyself, by desire of martyrdom, unto the lawless Jews, O godly-wise one!

A transgressor of the divine words, a most lawless and unrighteous Jew destroyed the godly-chosen flock of Christ:[^916] yet received he judgment by righteous and divine providence!

Thou didst walk unswervingly the path leading unto heaven, and didst bring unto Christ a choir of martyrs, O all-praised Arethas, gathered from every age and from every lineage!

*Theotokion:*

With hymns let us the faithful praise Mary the most holy Mother of God, who was full of grace, the divine protection, the calm harbor, the mediatress of all the faithful!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
O Lord, Creator of the vault of heaven, and Builder of the Church: do Thou confirm me in Thy love, O summit of desires, confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind![^917]

*Troparia:*
Beholding Thy Church strengthened by the grace of the Cross, the most lawless Hebrew youths, melting with envy, cruelly tormented with manifold wounds [those who confessed Thee], O only Lover of mankind!

Having a venom-shooting tongue, the evil-believing ones, blaspheming Thee Who didst deliver their forefather from ancient tyranny, slaughtered those who believe in Thee, O Savior![^918]

O Thou Who alone didst fashion human hearts by Thy divine nod,[^919] who can fathom the depth of Thy providence? For the flock which Thou didst gather, O Christ, Thou hast delivered unto wolves!

Thy godly-wise flock, being cut down by barbarian swords, fervently called upon Thee: Receive our spirits and our souls, O Savior, and guide them unto the light of Thy kingdom!

*Theotokion:*
Me, who was slain of old, thou hast led back again unto life, O most pure one, having borne the hypostatic Life, through whom the most hostile death was utterly dissolved and cruelly torn asunder!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^920]
Arming thyself with the power of the Cross, and having surrounded thy city with faith, by deeds thou didst put to shame the fury of the tyrant; and becoming an image of divine endurance, thou didst lead martyrs unto Christ. Therefore, having come together, we honor thee as is meet, O all-praised Arethas, and thy all-festal memory, O all-glorious passion-bearer! Pray unto Christ God, that He grant remission of sins unto those who honor with love thy holy memory!

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
Having fallen into manifold temptations from visible and invisible enemies, and held fast by the tempest of mine innumerable transgressions: I flee unto the haven of thy goodness, O pure one, as unto my warm protection and shelter. Therefore, O most pure one, pray fervently unto Him Who was incarnate from thee without seed, on behalf of all thy servants who unceasingly pray unto thee, O most pure Birthgiver of God: beseeching Him ever to grant remission of transgressions unto those who worthily hymn thy glory!

*Cross-Theotokion:*[^921]
When the ewe-lamb beheld the Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross, she cried out weeping, and bitterly lamenting, cried aloud: "The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through Thee; but my maternal heart is ablaze, beholding Thy crucifixion, which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of Thy loving-kindness, O Long-suffering Lord, Abyss of mercy, and Inexhaustible Fountain! Have compassion, and grant remission of transgressions unto those who chant in faith Thy divine passions!"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art also my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who hast not left the Father's bosom, and hast visited our poverty. Therefore, with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind![^922]

*Troparia:*
Strengthened by grace, and a divine and most radiant choir, a multitude suffering martyrlike, a victorious host, having Arethas as leader of the rank: let them be honorably hymned with love by those who sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

Held fast by thy wisdom, O godly-wise city of thine, O most wealthy one,[^923] shining with the honorable gray hairs [of thy elders], it contended manfully against the most lawless: and taking the victory, it chants unto the Savior: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

Surrounded by the new Law of the life-creating Spirit, trampling underfoot the law of the letter, the pious city surpassed in purity of soul and nobility of character, chanting unto Christ: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

Truly thou hast been shown forth as the city of God, O most honorable one, and the divine dwelling of martyrs; and of thee are sung brightly now most glorious, great, and marvelous things![^924] For bringing unto the Master the choir of those who have fallen asleep, [thou singest]: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Theotokion:*
Having budded forth from the royal lineage of David, O Virgin, thou hast given birth unto the Word of the all-reigning One, Who shone forth ineffably and beyond understanding before the ages from the Father: therefore, we the faithful in godly wisdom call thee blessed as the Birthgiver of God!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy face, O Light unwaning, and an alien darkness hath covered me, wretched one? But turn me back, and guide my paths unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee![^925]

The godly-called and godly-blessed choir contended, and warmly strengthening and assisting one another, the gloriously victorious ones chose to be slaughtered by the lawless for the sake of Christ!

Strengthened by faith and inflamed with zeal for piety, Thy favorites, O Christ, feared not the terror of the most lawless, and chastely bound by Thy love, they willingly surrendered themselves!

Casting their whole life upon the divine providence of God and the unavoidable judgments of His foreknowledge, the divine choir rushed with noble boldness against the foul murders of the lawless Jews![^926]

Hoping in Thee and believing now in Thy resurrection, Thy martyrs, O Savior, wept not neither did they cry aloud, being slaughtered by the lawless for Thy confession, O most valiant ones!

*Theotokion:*
Thou art the Word of God, co-eternal with the Father before the ages, Who through the riches of compassion didst dwell in thy womb, O Maiden, and wast manifest as a man upon earth, having become flesh, O all-hymned Virgin Birthgiver of God!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee: for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou give ear unto me, O God of my salvation![^927]

*Troparia:*
Marked with the blood of Christ, the holy and glorious martyrs fervently poured out their own blood, and are now counted worthy to reign with Him!

A word prophesying peace unto the nations that were dumb, did the speechless prattle of infants preach Christ:[^928] the godly-wise virgins brought themselves unto God for the sake of piety!

A light hath appeared unto thee, shining forth healings abundantly from Christ, O godly-eloquent Arethas: unto Whom thou didst depart, while yet dripping with the sweat of suffering!

A martyric host was divinely brought unto Thee, O Savior: the choirs of the saints dancing about Thee ask remission of sins for those who ever glorify them!

*Theotokion:*
The majesty of the Most High shone forth from Zion, through the assumption of flesh, in an ineffable union: clothed from thee, O unwedded one, He hath enlightened the world![^929]

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ дне́сь):*[^930]

The light-bearing feast of the passion-bearer hath arrived today as a mediator of joy unto us: celebrating it, we glorify the Lord Who is in the highest!

*Ikos:*
Enlighten my mind, O Christ, with the radiance of the contests of noble Arethas, and of all the holy ones who suffered with him. For he was shown forth as the first of all the faithful, manfully arraying himself against those who deny the Incarnation, [confessing] Thee Who beyond nature wast incarnate and born, that Thou mightest deliver us from deceit, and show without stumbling unto those who desire to walk the path of martyrdom, wherein the sufferers walked, chanting unto Thee, the Lord Who is in the highest![^931]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The fire in Babylon was once put to shame by the condescension of God: therefore the youths, dancing with joyous step in the furnace as in a meadow of flowers, chanted: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^932]

*Troparia:*
The streams of blood of Thy sufferers pour forth healings unto those who praise their memory with faith, and fervently cry unto Thee, the Creator: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

The victors completely broke the counsel of the lawless, for being adorned with divine understanding and keen wit, and rejoicing as they were slaughtered, they chanted: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Compassionate mothers, wounded with Thy love, O Christ, spared not their children: but consumed in the furnace, they cried out and chanted unto Thee, the Master: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^933]

Having brightly kindled the torches of suffering and virginity, the God-bearing ones rejoice with the wise virgins in the bridal chamber of God, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^934]

*Theotokion:*
Thou art the cleansing of souls and the mediatress of salvation unto those who proclaim thee with faith and love as the Mother of God, and cry in unison unto thy Son: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Sevenfold did the Chaldean tyrant madly kindle the furnace for the pious: but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, exalt Him above all unto all ages![^935]

*Troparia:*
"Fulfillment was not found in the Law, but salvation was given in Christ!" cried out the martyrs, saying unto the God-slayers: "Therefore, rejoicing, we are all slaughtered and die fervently, chanting unto Him: Ye people, exalt Christ above all unto the ages!"

An infant rays forth mysteries and teaches the senseless to look upon Thine all-pure image, O Christ, and to receive faith in Thine advent upon earth: and having escaped from the hands of the most lawless, he entered into the flame with his mother, chanting: Ye people, exalt Him above all unto the ages![^936]

Having piously contended upon earth, the martyrs joyfully departed and worthily received crowns from Christ in the heavens, where is the dwelling of those who make merry, who chant in faith and unceasingly cry aloud: Ye people, exalt Him above all unto the ages!

Having conquered the cruel ruler of the world and those who served his desire, ye passed over unto the Almighty and Lord of all with boldness and ineffable joy, crying aloud: Ye priests, hymn; ye people, exalt Him above all unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*
Pray for us unto thy Son and Lord, O most pure one, on behalf of those who cleanly and truly hymn thee, and confess thee as the Mother of God: asking forgiveness of sins, that those who chant in faith may obtain salvation: Ye priests, bless; ye people, exalt Him above all unto the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Every ear was terrified at the ineffable condescension of God, that the Most High willingly came down even unto flesh, having become man from a virginal womb: therefore, we the faithful magnify the all-pure Birthgiver of God![^937]

*Troparia:*
Partakers of Christ's glory, ye became companions in His death, O holy martyrs, and were brightly adorned with the diadem of the kingdom! And standing before God, do ye ask soul-saving deliverance for us, O praiseworthy ones!

Ye were shown forth as lawful soldiers of Christ and invincible martyrs, O all-wise Arethas, with those who fervently suffered with thee: with passion-bearing women and virgin maidens. Therefore, we pray, intercede diligently for us!

Direct my life, O Word of God, and pilot it, O Master, by the intercessions of Thy favorites who love Thee; and count me worthy to be a partaker with them, overlooking my sins, as Thou art good and the Lover of mankind!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast the dwelling of God, having contained in thy womb Him Whom nothing can contain, and having brought forth for us incarnate Him Who was formerly bodiless. Beseech Him now, O pure one, to grant remission of transgressions unto all who ever magnify thee in faith!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите):*[^938]
God was the fulfiller of Arethas' prophesying,[^939] having raised up the manly Elesbaan unto the overthrow of the impious Himyarite,[^940] pursuing and slaying the hosts of the ungodly, but exalting Najran and filling it with rejoicing![^941]

*Theotokion:*
We confess thee to be truly the Birthgiver of God, O Sovereign Lady, who have been saved through thee: for ineffably thou didst bear God, Who by the Cross dissolved death, and drew unto Himself multitudes of martyrs, with whom we praise thee, O Virgin!

*At the Liturgy: Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* Unto the saints that are in His earth.[^942]
*Verse:* I beheld the Lord ever before me.[^943]
*Epistle to the Hebrews, pericopa 331.*[^944]
*Alleluia, Tone 4:* The righteous cried, and the Lord heard them.[^945]
*Gospel of Matthew, pericopa 38.*[^946]
*Communion Hymn:*
Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous.[^947]

*The same month, on the 25th day.*
*The Holy Martyrs and Notaries Marcian and Martyrius.*[^948]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^949]
Having finished the course and kept the faith,[^950] ye were adorned with the crown of martyrdom, O Marcian and Martyrius, unshakable pillars of the Church: breasts pouring forth the milk of bridal youth, radiant pearls, luminaries enlightening creation with the splendors of piety!

Bringing forth a single mind and consenting in unity, O divine pair, ye destroyed the division of Arius: teaching all to honor the Son as co-eternal and co-unoriginate with the Father, and with the Spirit: the Trinity in Unity, and Unity in Trinity, one nature indivisible in three Persons![^951]

Disciples and of the same disposition, imitators and fellow-zealots of the sacred preacher Paul did ye show yourselves:[^952] and by the shedding of your blood ye drowned the enemies, and dried up the torrent of cruel heresies; and ye were manifest as a river of piety watering the Church of Christ, O Marcian and Martyrius!

*Glory, Tone 4. By Germanus:*[^953]
Having been disciples and followers of the preacher and confessor of the consubstantial Trinity,[^954] and being persecuted with him for its sake, O blessed ones, ye preferred the death by the sword before the tongue-babbling of the heretics.[^955] Therefore, being adorned with martyric crowns from God, and having received boldness, pray that those who honor your memory be delivered from afflictions!

*Both now, Theotokion or Cross-Theotokion, Tone 1, Podoben: "All-praised martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*[^956]
When the ewe-lamb, the all-spotless Lady, beheld her Lamb upon the Cross, having neither form nor comeliness,[^957] weeping she said: "Woe is me! Whither hath Thy beauty set, O sweetest One? Where is Thy majesty? Where is the grace that shines from Thy countenance, O my most beloved Son?"

*At the Aposticha of the Oktoechos.*
*Glory, Tone 3:*[^958]
Spiritual luminaries of the Church and champions of the Trinity, Marcian and Martyrius: having cast down heresies with the shield of faith, they enlightened the world with the light of Orthodoxy. Obeying Paul, the equal of the apostles, the shepherd and true teacher of dogmas, they cast down the division of Arius and Nestorius, turning away the confusion of Sabellius and Severus.[^959] Theologically proclaiming the Unity in Trinity, and God incarnate of the Virgin, one Christ in two natures, the all-praised ones piously preached unto all to hymn Him: therefore, having received crowns of victory from heaven, the godly-speaking ones ask great mercy for us!

*Both now, Theotokion or Cross-Theotokion:*
A sword pierced through thy heart, O most pure one, when thou didst behold thy Son upon the Cross,[^960] and thou didst cry aloud: Leave me not childless, O my Son and my God, Who didst preserve me a virgin after giving birth!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*[^961]
Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received incorruptible crowns from Thee, our God: for having Thy strength, they cast down the tyrants and shattered the powerless insolence of demons: by their intercessions, save our souls!

*At Matins, both canons of the Oktoechos. And the canon of the saints on 4. Its acrostic: Unto the two martyrs I offer songs. Joseph. Tone 4.*[^962]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin, drown, I pray Thee, the tripartite nature of my soul in the depth of dispassion, as of old Thou didst the mighty captains;[^963] that as on a timbrel, in the mortification of the body, I may chant unto Thee a song of victory![^964]

Ever illuminated abundantly by the light of the Holy Trinity, ye are unerring luminaries, O holy ones: therefore, celebrating your radiant feast today in faith, we brightly rejoice!

Desiring, O sufferers, the Word Who shone forth from the Virgin to come down upon earth for our sake, confessing Him to be consubstantial with the Father, ye endured a most violent death with a most steadfast resolve!

Ye were shown forth as godly-speaking followers and disciples of the wise Paul,[^965] and preached the Son to be of equal honor with the Father: therefore, being slain with swords, by the shedding of your blood ye purified the earth, O passion-bearers!

*Theotokion:*
Having conceived the unoriginate Son of God, thou didst give birth unto Him incarnate, O pure one, Who became man through His ineffable mercy: therefore, piously worshipping Him, we glorify thee, the cause of the deification of all!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Not in wisdom, and power, and riches do we boast, but in Thee, O Christ, the Father's hypostatic Wisdom: for there is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind![^966]

*Troparia:*
Enlightened by the divine Spirit with Orthodoxy, ye utterly drove away the darkness of Arius,[^967] having become witnesses of the Almighty!

Theologizing that the Word is equal unto the Father, O holy ones, ye resolved to die, O Marcian and Martyrius, and have passed over unto unending life!

Enrolled into the sacred army, ye were numbered with the angels, and were united most radiantly unto the choirs of martyrs: therefore we praise your honorable memory!

*Theotokion:*
Adorned with golden raiment as a queen,[^968] thou standest now at the right hand of the King and God, O Sovereign Lady, praying for thy servants!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of divine faith" (Бж҃е́ственныѧ вѣ́ры):*[^969]
Confessing the divine commandment even unto blood, which ye received for His sake, O glorious martyrs, having exposed the deceit of the evil-believing and preserved the Orthodox faith: pray unceasingly unto Christ God, that He grant us great mercy!

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
He was not separated from the divine nature when He became flesh in thy womb: but God became man and remained what He was, Who after giving birth preserved thee a virgin, as before childbirth, all-spotless, the only Lord: beseech Him fervently to grant us great mercy!

*Cross-Theotokion:*

The unblemished ewe-lamb, the incorrupt Virgin Mother of the Word, beholding Him Who sprouted forth from her without pangs suspended upon the Cross, lamenting maternally as was meet, cried aloud: "Woe is me, my Child! How sufferest Thou willingly, desiring to deliver man from shameful passions?"[^970]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Sitting in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, Jesus the most divine came upon a light cloud, with an incorruptible hand, and saved those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ![^971]

*Troparia:*
Steadfast with noble resolve and the perfect wisdom of Orthodoxy, O martyrs of the Lord, ye finished the martyric contest and drove away heretical darkness!

He who did not preach the Word to be consubstantial with the Father, becoming senseless with rage unto tormenting you, was strangled by the sinews of your sacred contests,[^972] and was drowned in streams of blood by the grace of God!

Having soldiered for the Lord through faith, O holy ones, and suffered, ye were numbered with the heavenly hosts: therefore ye are called blessed, becoming our strengthening and enlightenment!

Let Marcian the valiant and Martyrius be praised with joyful hymns of praise: breasts of the Church, pouring forth the milk of Orthodoxy and nourishing the pious!

*Theotokion:*
The sacred prophet of old called thee the divine mountain overshadowed utterly with virtues, O all-pure Virgin,[^973] from whom the saving Word appeared for the renewal and enlightenment of our souls!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
The ungodly shall not see Thy glory, O Christ; but we, waking early at dawn, hymn Thee, the Only-begotten, the Radiance of the Father's glory of the Godhead, O Lover of mankind![^974]

*Troparia:*
Ye were shown forth as followers of the all-wise dogmas of Paul, the divine sacred minister: imitating whose ways, O martyrs, ye patiently suffered and were lawfully crowned![^975]

As stars ye shine brightly upon the height of the honorable Church, driving away the deepest darkness of the Arian heresy, O most valiant martyrs!

Having withdrawn from the beauties of this life, O holy ones, ye drew near unto Christ: and by a little blood, O glorious passion-bearers, ye inherited the unshakable kingdom!

*Theotokion:*
Truly hast thou borne a Son upon earth, consubstantial with the Father and the divine [Spirit] and divine Spirit, O Birthgiver of God: Him do thou unceasingly beseech to have mercy on our souls!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I came into the depths of the sea, and a storm of many sins drowned me; but as God, raise up my life from the abyss, O Most-merciful One![^976]

*Troparia:*
Following the voice of the all-wise shepherd Paul,[^977] ye kept the Orthodox faith; and as lambs ye were slain by the blood-drinking swords, rejoicing!

Precious in the sight of the Lord was your death shown forth, O all-glorious and wise warriors:[^978] therefore with yearly honors, O holy ones, ye are called blessed by all!

Having you, O valiant passion-bearers, all-blessed Marcian and Martyrius, as a fountain of wonders, the Church of Christ rejoices unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*
With mouth and tongue we confess thee truly to be the Mother of our God, O Virgin: fervently beseech Him, O pure one, that our souls may be saved!

*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*[^979]
Having fought well from infancy, O Marcian, together with the wise Martyrius, ye cast down the apostate Arius, preserving unharmed the Orthodox faith, following Paul the wise teacher: therefore with him ye found life, as most excellent champions of the Trinity!

*Ikos:*[^980]
As ministers of piety of God the Lover of mankind, O blessed pair: make haste unto me quickly, and deliver me from grievous things, granting me a word of wisdom to praise your suffering; having suffered for the faith, O holy ones, with unhesitating disposition, ye received heavenly crowns. With the choirs of sufferers, and apostles, and teachers, and venerable hierarchs do ye rejoice together, as preachers of the divine Word, and most excellent champions of the Trinity!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Abrahamic Children of old in Babylon trampled down the furnace flame, crying aloud with hymns: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^981]

*Troparia:*
Following well the holy shepherd,[^982] adorned with Orthodoxy, ye were slain by the hands of the evil-believing, O all-glorious warriors!

Preserving faithfully the divine law unbroken, O divine martyrs, ye suffered lawfully, being slain together by the hands of the lawless!

After your holy burial, unto those who draw near with faith unto your sepulcher, ye bury diseases ye bury diseases, O holy ones, by your holy intercession unto the Giver of life!

Drawing forth divine grace mystically from holy fountains, ye give drink unto the faithful with healings, taking away all abounding of diseases!

*Theotokion:*
Delivered by thy childbearing from eternal condemnation, O Mother of God, we cry unto thee: Rejoice, O ever-blessed and full of grace!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
O all-powerful Deliverer of all, having descended into the midst of the flame, Thou didst refresh with dew those who lived piously, and didst teach them to chant: All ye works, bless the Lord, and praise Him![^983]

*Troparia:*
As two lambs ye were slain, preaching the Lamb of God to be consubstantial with the Father, and being offered unto the highest banquet, ye were united unto the assembly of the sufferers!

Strengthened by the power of the Spirit, ye uprooted the fierceness of delusion; and having suffered valiantly, O wise martyrs, ye heal those grievously enfeebled by infirmities!

Having become physicians of the passions of our souls, O holy martyrs: dissolve grievous diseases and assaults of the adversary by your divine prayers!

Let us hymn the unbegotten Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, one uncreated essence and one Godhead, crying out in harmony: All ye works of the Lord, bless the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst bring us who were slain to life, having given birth unto the Life of all, O Birthgiver of God and Sovereign Lady, the glory of martyrs and of the righteous, and a fervent propitiation for transgressions!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Eve through the disease of disobedience brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, through the bud of thy pregnancy didst blossom forth blessing unto the world: therefore we all magnify thee![^984]

*Troparia:*
Fortified by divine strength, O passion-bearers, ye feared not death, but zealously submitting your necks unto the swords, ye valiantly fulfilled the divine contest: therefore are ye worthily called blessed!

As two radiant luminaries ye enlighten the fullness of the universe with the divine splendors of your sufferings, O wise Marcian and Martyrius, driving away the darkness of the gloomy heresy of Arius!

Having overcome the blind-minded Macedonius,[^985] ye worthily received crowns: and standing before the Master's throne, ask for us complete remission of transgressions, O worthy of praise!

Your divine memory hath shone forth brighter than the sun, enlightening the hearts of all hearts of all who celebrate it, O holy and blessed ones: in which beseech the Lord to deliver us from the darkness of transgressions!

*Theotokion:*
Being the pure dwelling of the Light, enlighten the pupils of my soul, blackened by negligence and ever held fast by the deepest darkness of ignorance, O all-immaculate one, that in faith I may call thee blessed!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "In the spirit" (Дꙋ́хомъ):*[^986]
Having been followers of all-blessed Paul, ye finished the course; with him ye obtained the Kingdom of Christ and immortal glory, together with all the saints, and ye stand together before the honorable Trinity, glorifying It worthily!

*Theotokion:*
The ranks of the bodiless praise thy childbearing: for thou alone didst fill those upon earth with joy; therefore we the faithful glorify thee, the all-immaculate one, praising thee with hymns: for thou hast shone forth Light unto those in darkness, as the morning shedding the light of day!

*At the Liturgy: Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* God is marvelous in His saints, the God of Israel.[^987]
*Epistle to the Ephesians, pericopa 224 from the half.*[^988]
*Alleluia, Tone 4:* The righteous cried.[^989]
*Gospel of Matthew, pericopa 34 from the half.*[^990]
*Communion Hymn:* Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous.[^991]

*October 26. The Holy and Glorious Great-Martyr Demetrius the Myrrh-Streamer, and Commemoration of the Great and Fearful Earthquake:*

*If the superior wishes, he celebrates a vigil.*[^992]

*At Small Vespers, Stichera on 4, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^993]
O God Who shakest the foundations of the whole earth, Who alone with currents coverest the bed of the sea: as Creator of all, deliver us from the earthquake of Thy righteous wrath by Thy mercies, O Lover of mankind, we ever beseech Thee! *(3)*

Thessalonica dances with joy at thy memory, and calls together all the surrounding cities, O all-blessed Demetrius, to celebrate thy light-bearing solemnity with whom we also having assembled, praise thy contests with hymns!

Come, ye faithful, having gathered together, let us praise Christ's warrior and good luminary, Demetrius, who trampled down all the violence of the enemy, crying aloud unto him with faith: Pray unto Christ for us, O martyr!

*Glory, Tone 6:*[^994]
Today the whole universe is enlightened with the rays of the passion-bearer, and the Church of God, adorned with flowers, cries out unto thee, O Demetrius: O servant of Christ and most fervent protector, cease not to pray for thy servants!

*Both now:* O Birthgiver of God, thou art the true vine...[^995]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O house of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^996]
We have thee as the giver of many gifts, O servant of the Lord: for thou fulfillest the requests which we ever make!

*Verse:* The righteous shall flourish like the palm-tree, and like a cedar in Lebanon shall he be multiplied.[^997]

Sweet hymns, praises of thy sufferings, recompenses of thanksgivings: receive this offering, O blessed Demetrius!

*Verse:* Those that are planted in the house of the Lord shall flourish in the courts of our God.[^998]

Beauty of sufferers, goodliness of martyrs, guardian of the Church: receive now praise from those who glorify thee with love!

*Glory, both now, Theotokion, the same tone:*
A divinely-fitting work: a Virgin after childbearing; the Born One is God the Word, having human nature, and appearing material!

*Troparion, Tone 3:*[^999]
A great champion in perils did the universe find thee, O passion-bearer, overcoming the heathen: as therefore thou didst cast down the arrogance of Lyaeus, and didst make Nestor bold for the contest,[^1000] so, O holy Demetrius, beseech Christ God to grant us great mercy!

*Glory, both now, of the Earthquake, Tone 8:*[^1001]
O Thou Who lookest upon the earth, and makest it to tremble: deliver us from the fearful menace of the earthquake, O Christ our God; and send down upon us Thy rich mercies, through the intercessions of the Birthgiver of God, and save us!

*At Great Vespers we sing: Blessed is the man, the 1st antiphon.*[^1002]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 8, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^1003]
Thou shinest unto thy flock ever, O glorious martyr Demetrius, as a radiant star: for thou ever protectest this [city] from the ruin of enemies, and deliverest it from affliction and every storm; therefore it joyfully offers hymns, celebrating thy yearly memory, O blessed one, and chanting in faith and love unto the Lord Who glorified thee! *(Twice)*

O martyr, passion-bearer of Christ, with wonders thou shinest upon the world like the sun, O holy Demetrius: therefore on thy memory do we all delight in thy wonders, O blessed one, celebrating with faith exceedingly; having boldness therefore, pray unto the Lord to save thy servants from corruption and afflictions!

Pierced with spears in the ribs, for the sake of the Savior Whose ribs were pierced upon the Cross:[^1004] for with love thou didst emulate His wounding, O passion-bearer Demetrius, showing forth unto all the invincible nature of thy testimony; therefore the persecutor also, having been wounded, fell down, receiving the boldness of thy confession, O sufferer of Christ!

*Other Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^1005]
O most marvelous wonder! Joy in heaven and on earth hath shone forth today on the memory of the martyr Demetrius: he is crowned with praises by the angels, and receives hymns from men; O how he suffered! How well he contended! By whom the deceitful enemy fell down, Christ having conquered him! *(Twice)*

O most marvelous wonder! With the splendors of wonders, brighter than the sun, Demetrius ever enlightens the ends of the world, illuminated by the unwaning Light, and adorning himself in the never-setting Light; by whose radiances the barbarian clouds were driven away, diseases were expelled, and demons were overcome!

O most marvelous wonder! Pierced for the sake of Christ, the thrice-blessed Demetrius ever showed himself unto enemies as a two-edged weapon, cutting down the haughtiness of enemies, and casting down demonic fierce rages; unto whom let us cry aloud: O holy Demetrius, protect us who faithfully honor thee in love ever!

*Glory, Tone 6. By Byzantios:*[^1006]
Today the universal solemnity of the passion-bearer calls us together. Come therefore, ye lovers of the feast, let us brightly celebrate his memory, saying: Rejoice, thou who by faith didst rend asunder the garment of impiety, and by the fortitude of the Spirit didst clothe thyself! Rejoice, thou who didst abolish the wiles of the stranger by the power given unto thee from the one God! Rejoice, thou who with pierced limbs didst spiritually portray for us the blessed Passion of Christ! Beseech Him, O sufferer adornment of sufferers, O Demetrius, that we may be delivered from visible and invisible enemies, and that our souls may be saved!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Who would not call thee blessed, O all-holy Virgin?[^1007]

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day.*[^1008]

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 63):*[^1009]
Turn back from heaven, O Lord, and behold from the house of Thy sanctuary and Thy glory: where is Thy zeal and Thy strength? Where is the multitude of Thy mercy and Thy compassions, that Thou didst bear with us, O Lord? For Thou art our Father: though Abraham knew us not, and Israel recognized us not, yet Thou, O Lord, art our Father; deliver us: for Thy name is upon us from the beginning. Why hast Thou caused us to err from Thy way, O Lord, and hardened our hearts, that we should not fear Thee? Turn back for the sake of Thy servants, for the sake of the tribes of Thine inheritance, that we may inherit Thy holy mountain a little: our adversaries have trampled down Thy sanctuary. We have become as from the beginning, when Thou didst not rule over us, neither was Thy name called upon us. *(Chapter 64)* If Thou shouldest open heaven, trembling shall take hold of the mountains before Thee, and they shall melt, as wax melts before the fire; and the fire shall burn Thine adversaries, and Thy name shall be made manifest unto Thine opponents: from Thy countenance the nations shall be troubled! When Thou shalt do glorious things, trembling shall take hold of the mountains before Thee. From the beginning of the age we have not heard, neither have our eyes seen a God besides Thee, and Thy works are true; and Thou wilt show mercy unto those who wait for Thee. For mercy shall meet those that work righteousness, and they shall remember Thy ways. And now, O Lord, Thou art our Father, and we are clay: Thou art our Creator, and we all are the work of Thy hands. Be not wroth with us exceedingly, O Lord, and remember not our sins in time of wrath: and now look down, O Lord, for we all are Thy people!

*Reading from the Prophecy of Jeremiah (Chapter 2):*[^1010]
Thus says the Lord: I remembered the mercy of thy youth, and the love of thy betrothal: that thou followedst the Holy One of Israel, says the Lord, the Holy One of Israel: Israel is holy unto the Lord, the firstfruits of His increase; all that devour him shall offend, evil shall come upon them, says the Lord. Hear the word of the Lord, O house of Jacob, and all the families of the house of Israel. Thus says the Lord: What iniquity have your fathers found in Me, that they are gone far from Me, and walked and walked after vanities, and became vain? Neither said they: Where is the Lord that brought us up out of the land of Egypt, that led us in the wilderness, through a desert and trackless land, through a dry and unfruitful land, and through the shadow of death, through a land that no man passed through, neither dwelt any son of man there? And I brought you unto Carmel, that ye might eat the fruits thereof and the goods thereof; and ye entered, and defiled My land, and made Mine heritage an abomination. The priests said not: Where is the Lord? and they that handle the law knew Me not: the pastors also transgressed against Me, and the prophets prophesied by Baal, and walked after useless things. Therefore I will yet plead with you, says the Lord, and with your children's children will I plead. Pass over unto the islands of the Chittim, and see; and send unto Kedar, and understand exceedingly, and see if there be such things: whether the nations change their gods, and yet they are no gods; but My people have changed His glory for that from which they shall not profit. The heaven was astonished at this, and shuddered exceedingly, says the Lord!

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Chapter 4):*[^1011]
Though the righteous happen to die, he shall be in rest. For honorable age is not that which stands in length of time, nor is it measured by number of years. But wisdom is the gray hair unto men, and an unspotted life is old age. He pleased God, and was beloved of Him: so that living among sinners he was translated. He was caught away, lest wickedness should alter his understanding, or deceit beguile his soul. For the bewitching of wickedness obscures things that are good; and the wandering of desire perverts the simple mind. Being made perfect in a short time, he fulfilled long years: for his soul pleased the Lord: therefore hasted He to take him away from the midst of wickedness. This the people saw, and understood it not, neither laid they this up in mind, That His grace and mercy is in His saints, and that He hath visitation unto His elect!

*At the Litiya, the Sticheron of the Temple; and the Stichera of the Saint, Idiomela, Tone 4. By George Sikeliotes:*[^1012]
Rejoice in the Lord, O city of Thessalonica! Be glad and leap for joy, having clothed thyself brightly with faith, having in thy bosom as a treasure the all-glorious sufferer and witness of the truth, Demetrius: delight thyself, beholding the healings of wonders; and see him casting down the insolence of the aliens, and thankfully cry unto the Savior: Lord, glory to Thee!

*By Anatolius:*[^1013]
With the beauty of songs let us brighten the present day, and proclaim the contests of the martyr: for great Demetrius offers himself unto us for thanksgiving; for having manfully destroyed the demonic assaults, he came zealously unto the arena, and having gloriously carried off the prizes of victory, he prays unto the Savior, that our souls may be saved!

*Tone 2. By Germanus:*[^1014]
In the supermundane habitations Christ God placed thy spirit blamelessly, O wise martyr Demetrius: for thou wast a champion of the Trinity, and in the arena thou didst manfully suffer as a firm adamant; and being pierced in thy ribs, O all-honorable one, imitating Him Who was pierced upon the Tree for the salvation of the whole world, thou didst receive the working of wonders, abundantly bestowing healings unto men; therefore today we celebrate thy falling asleep worthily, glorifying the Lord Who glorified thee!

*Tone 5, Podoben: "Let us sound the trumpet" (Вострꙋ́бимъ):*[^1015]
Let us run together in faith and love, let us shout festal songs, and let us all clap our hands at the present solemnity of our champion; let the heavenly hosts rejoice, and let them marvel at him who was pierced for the sake of the King and Lord Who was pierced with a spear, and who boldly cast down the arrogance of Lyaeus; and let the earth and sea duly praise the radiant luminary with most glorious sign: the young let them praise the bridler of youth, the staff of old age, the protector in perils, the enricher of the poor, the builder in piety, the ally of soldiers, and the fellow-voyager in the abyss: with all, O city of Thessalonica, pray unto thy firm champion, saying thus: O all-glorious Demetrius, make haste and deliver us from every distress, and save thy servants by thy prayers!

*Both now, Theotokion, the same tone. The composition of Patriarch Germanus:*[^1016]
Let us sound the trumpet of hymns: for bending down from on high, the Queen of all, the Virgin Mother, crowns with purples those who hymn her; let kings and princes gather together, and let queens clap their hands in hymns unto the Queen that bore the King Who in His love for mankind was pleased to deliver those formerly held by death; ye pastors and teachers, coming together let us praise the all-pure Mother of the Good Shepherd: the golden lampstand, the light-bearing cloud more spacious than the heavens; the ensouled ark, the fiery throne of the Master, the golden jar receiving manna, the closed door of the Word, the refuge of all Christians: praising her with divinely-speaking hymns, let us say thus: O palace of the Word, make us humble ones worthy of the heavenly kingdom: for nothing is impossible unto thy mediation!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Idiomela of the Earthquake, Tone 2. By Symeon of the Admirable Mountain:*[^1017]
When the earth is troubled by the fear of Thy wrath, the hills and mountains tremble, O Lord: but looking upon us with the eye of compassion, be not wroth with us in Thine anger, but have mercy upon the works of Thy hands: deliver us from the fearful menace of the earthquake, as God and Lover of mankind!

*Verse, Tone 6:* He looks upon the earth, and makes it to tremble.[^1018]

Fearful art Thou, O Lord, and who shall endure Thy righteous wrath? Or who shall entreat Thee? Or who shall appease Thee, O Good One, for a people that hath sinned and despaired? The heavenly ranks, angels, principalities, and authorities, thrones, dominions, cherubim and seraphim cry unto Thee for us: Holy, holy, holy art Thou, O Lord! Despise not the works of Thy hands, O Good One, for the bowels of Thy mercy: save the city that is in distress!

*Verse:* Thou hast shaken the earth, and hast troubled it.[^1019]

The Ninevites, by reason of transgressions, heard of destruction by the menace of an earthquake; but in the intervening sign of the whale, through the rising of Jonah, repentance entreats; but as Thou didst receive the cry of Thy people, having compassion upon them with infants and cattle, so also spare and have mercy on us who are chastened, for the sake of the third-day Resurrection!

*Glory, Tone 8. By Anatolius:*[^1020]
Thy most divine and spotless soul, O ever-memorable Demetrius, hath indeed the heavenly Jerusalem for its dwelling, whose walls were painted by the all-pure hands of the invisible God; and this most magnificent temple upon earth hath also thine all-honorable and much-suffering body, an unstealable treasure of wonders, and a cure for diseases, whereunto resorting we draw forth healings: preserve, O all-praised one, the city that magnifies Thee from adverse assaults, having boldness toward Christ Who glorified Thee!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
O unwedded Virgin...[^1021]

*At the blessing of the loaves: Troparion of Demetrius, twice; and Birthgiver of God Virgin, once.*[^1022]

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Earthquake, twice; Glory, of Demetrius; Both now, of the Earthquake.*[^1023]

*After the first Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*[^1024]
Celebrating the feast of the passion-bearer Demetrius today with pious wisdom, as he unceasingly prays unto Christ, to grant unto all peace and mercy! *(Twice)*

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
A fervent protection for those in perils, and our helper, and reconciliation unto God, through whom we were delivered from corruption: we the faithful call the Birthgiver of God blessed!

*After the second Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^1025]
Thy memory, O glorious martyr Demetrius, hath brightened today the Church of Christ, and hath gathered all together to praise thee worthily with hymns, O godly-wise one, as truly a warrior and destroyer of enemies: therefore by thy supplications deliver us from temptations! *(Twice)*

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
We hymn thee, O Bride of God, Mother of Christ God, glorifying thine incomprehensible childbearing, by which we were delivered from the delusion of the devil and from all kinds of perils, O Sovereign Lady Birthgiver of God, and in faith we cry aloud: Have mercy on thy flock, O only all-praised one!

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^1026]
Thou shinest forth the radiances of wonders like rays, and enlightenest all the ends of the world like a most radiant star, and sweetly pourest forth fragrance like a second Paradise, and gladdenest souls, and stayest diseases; therefore duly do we hymn thee, O all-blessed one, and with love we honor thy venerable memory, O passion-bearer Demetrius: beseech Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who honor thy holy memory with love! *(Twice)*

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
As the Virgin and only one among women, who without seed didst bear God in the flesh, all generations of men call thee blessed: for the fire of the Godhead dwelt in thee, and as an infant thou sucklest the Creator and Lord; therefore both the angelic and the human race worthily glorify thine all-holy childbearing, and in harmony we cry aloud unto thee: Beseech Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who bow down in faith before thine all-holy childbearing!

*Gradual Hymns, 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*[^1027]
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* God is marvelous in His saints, the God of Israel.[^1028]
*Verse:* In the congregations bless ye God, the Lord from the springs of Israel.[^1029]

...unto Him they sang a worthy song: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all forever![^1030]

Beyond speech, O Maiden, holding in thine arms, and bowing down before and kissing in motherly love the one Lord incarnate, clothed in human likeness, thou didst say: "O sweetest Child, how do I hold Thee thus, Who holdest all creation in Thy hand, and Who deliverest it from the hand of servitude?"

Prepare yourselves, O divine angels, to hymn Him Who is born upon earth! Guided by the star, bring gifts, O Magi! Make haste, O shepherds, to behold Him sitting as an infant in motherly embrace, crying aloud: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all forever!

O unespoused cloud of Light, how dost thou wrap in swaddling-bands Him Who with unutterable gesture clothes the heavens with clouds? How dost thou recline in a manger of dumb beasts the Master Who in His boundless mercy delivers men from irrationality, before Whom all creation bows down in fear, hymning Him unto all ages?

*Another Heirmos:*[^1031]
Praise the Lord as God, all ye works, Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew youths, and changed the flame into dew, and exalt Him above all forever![^1032]

*Troparia:*
All creation prepares firstfruits for Thee, O Christ, Who art about to be born in a cave of the pure Virgin; and it sings a forefestal praise, glorifying Thee, the only Benefactor!

Following Christ Who was born upon earth, O passion-bearers, having faith as a guide even as a radiant star, ye beheld Him, and brought unto Him divine gifts through your suffering!

Strengthened by the power of Christ, O all-wise passion-bearers, ye vanquished the tyranny of the enemy, and showed him to be powerless, hymning Christ unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
He Who is in the bosom of the Father, having been born, rested in a cave upon thy motherly palms, O Birthgiver of God; and unto all who faithfully hymn thee, He opens the kingdom in the heavens!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Marvel not now, O Mother, seeing as an infant Him Whom the Father begat from the womb before the morning star: for I have come manifestly to raise up and glorify together fallen human nature, which magnifies thee in faith and love![^1033]

Rejoice, O all-immaculate receptacle of ineffable joy! For, lo, thou comest to give birth in a cave ineffably unto the Master, Who desires truly to renew all creation that of old had rotted away through transgression: hymning Him in faith, we magnify thee!

All ye souls of the righteous, and ye subterranean ones together, rejoice! For, lo, the redemption of all hath appeared, being born in the city of Bethlehem; and the star proclaims Him unto the Magi who seek Him piously: who having also seen Him in the cave, were filled with awe!

We hymn thee as another heaven, O Virgin, who art about to cause the morning Sun of righteousness to shine forth unto us from thy most pure womb, enlightening those who are in the darkness of death and corruption: therefore as is due, with praises do we magnify thee!

*Another Heirmos:*[^1034]
With one mind, O ye faithful, let us magnify with hymns God the Word, Who through ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam who had fallen grievously into corruption through eating, and Who was ineffably incarnate for our sake of the holy Virgin![^1035]

*Troparia:*
For the sake of all, the divine coming, which the army of sufferers preceded, proclaims a banquet of gladness unto all creation, and receives all the faithful, setting forth its valiance even as food!

Beholding faith as a star, O passion-bearers of the Lord, and holding it firmly, unto the unwaning Sun, for its sake ye were guided without stumbling unto Christ, O wise ones; and ye offered your blood unto Him as gifts!

Having harvested the blossoms of the knowledge of Christ, O passion-bearers, from the withered pastures, ye were filled with His fragrance having suffered; and ye yourselves brought most sweet-smelling myrrh unto Him through your martyrdom!

*Theotokion:*
Grace hath been poured forth, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, among all generations of earthborn, and the richness of thy wonders overflows abundantly in the radiant joy of thy childbearing, and enriches those who glorify thee in faith and love!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "The Disciples Themselves" (Са́мъ ᲂу҆чени́ки):*[^1036]
With Theodulus we crown Zoticus, Agathopus, and Saturninus, Pompeius, together with Eunician, glorious Basilides, and divine Euporus, and glorious Gelasius, and Evarestus, joyfully with songs: that by their prayers we may be delivered from transgressions, and receive crowns from Christ the Savior![^1037]

*Glory, both now, of the Forefeast, Podoben: "Give Ear, Ye Women" (Жены̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите):*[^1038]
The hidden mystery, unutterable unto angels, by the good pleasure of the Father and the cooperation of the Spirit is about to be fulfilled: make haste! For the beginningless Word, having taken a beginning as man, is born of the Virgin for the salvation of the world!

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 6, Podoben: "Go Before, Ye Angelic Hosts" (А҆́гглскїѧ предыди́те):*[^1039]
The mystery foreordained by the Father before the ages, and foreannounced by the prophets in the latter times, hath appeared: and God became man, having taken flesh of the Virgin; the Uncreated One is voluntarily fashioned; He Who is, comes into being; and Christ, the King of Israel, comes!

Wrapped in swaddling-bands, O King, Thou Who wast wrapped in swaddling-bands loosest the cords of my transgressions; and having honored me with immortal and incorruptible glory, Thou hast adopted me wholly unto the Father, fashioning and rebuilding me. Therefore I cry unto Thee: Christ, the King of Israel, comes!

The Light that came forth from Light, and shone forth from the Virgin unto those upon earth: the Magi, stargazers, having seen Him by a star, left the Persian darkness and all the delusion of stars, and in joy sang unto God Who was born: Blessed is our God Who is come: glory to Thee!

Herod the malevolent, having been mocked by the wise men, madly mows down the young blade of infants, and thought to stretch forth his foully-murderous hand against Thee; but Thou camest to drive away the deepest darkness from the Egyptians, with whom we sing: Blessed is our God Who is come: glory to Thee!

*Glory, the same tone and podoben (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Let us sing a new song, O new Israel: sing a heavenly hymn, O eye! Rejoice and exult with joy, and dance! Bring brightly forefestal things: God hath appeared from the south in the flesh. He also desires to be washed as a man in the streams of the Jordan!

*Both now, the same podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Thou didst appear upon earth, and didst dwell with men, and by the decree of Caesar didst enroll Thyself with the servants: Thou didst create without being altered; Thou didst remain unchanged, being wholly God, though Thou wast incarnate. Glory to Thy dispensation, honor, praise, magnificence, both now and unto the ages. Amen!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^1040]
Breathe sweet fragrance, O Zion, the holy manger: for in thee the Master is about to spread forth the rays of His Godhead!

*Verse:* God shall come from Theman, and the Holy One from a dark and shadowy mountain.[^1041]

*Verse:* God shall come from Theman, and the Holy One from a dark and shadowy mountain.
Shelter instead of gold, and love even as myrrh: like frankincense let us offer deeds unto the Creator, Who comes unto His own!

*Verse:* O Lord, I have heard Thy report, and was afraid: O Lord, I considered Thy works, and was astonished.[^1042]
Christ draws near, the star foreannounces: the heavenly multitude of the army of intelligent powers approaches!

*Glory, Tone 3. By the Studite:*[^1043]
O valiant martyrs of the truth, neither the compulsion of the torturers, nor deceitful flattery, nor the severing of limbs, nor the menaces of death were able to separate you from the divine love; therefore having boldness toward Christ, the God Who is over all: ask from Christ to receive, through your prayers, this recompense of painful sufferings, and great mercy!

*Both now, of the Forefeast, Tone 8. By Anatolius:*[^1044]
In Bethlehem is born the Creator of all, and Eden is opened, the pre-eternal King: the flaming sword turns its back; the middle wall of the enclosure is destroyed; the heavenly powers are joined with the earthly; angels with men compose a mighty celebration; the pure ones offer singing unto the Pure One. We behold the Virgin as the cherubic throne of glory, who contained God Who is in nowise containable, and bears Him Whom the seraphim glorify in fear: that He may grant great mercy unto the world!

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Forefeast, Ode 3; and of the Martyrs, Ode 6.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* Unto the saints that are in His earth, marvelous hath the Lord made all His desires in them.[^1045]
*Verse:* I foresaw the Lord always before me, for He is at my right hand, that I may not be shaken.[^1046]
*Epistle to the Ephesians, pericopa 233.*[^1047]
*Alleluia: The righteous cried.*[^1048]
*Gospel of Luke, pericopa 106.*[^1049]
*Communion Hymn:* Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous: praise is seemly for the upright.[^1050]

*In the same month, on the 24th day:*[^1051]
*Forefeast of the Nativity of Christ; and Commemoration of the Holy Venerable-Martyr Eugenia:*[^1052]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Forefeast, Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ):*[^1053]
Thou bearest the form of Adam, being all-perfect in the image of God; and Thou desirest to be held by hands, Who holdest all things by Thy hand and by Thy power! The pure and all-immaculate one addressed Thee, saying: "How shall I wrap Thee in swaddling-bands as an infant? How shall I nourish Thee with breasts, Who nourishest all things? How shall I marvel at Thy poverty which is beyond understanding? How shall I call Thee my Son, being now Thy handmaid? I hymn and bless Thee, Who bestowest great mercy upon the world!"

Beholding the pre-eternal God incarnate from her as an infant, the all-immaculate one, holding Him in her hands, and kissing Him often, and filled with joy, addressed Him: "O God most high, invisible King: how do I behold Thee, and cannot comprehend the mystery of Thy boundless poverty? For the smallest cave, and that a foreign one, contains Thee inside, Who wast born: and having not broken the seal of virginity, having preserved the womb as before childbearing, and granting great mercy!"

Having slave-like uttered these things in awe, the pure one heard the Magi standing together before the cave, and said unto them: "Whom seek ye? For I see that ye have come from a foreign land, having the Persian appearance and wisdom: ye have made a strange departure and journey; and unto Him Who became a stranger from on high, and strangely dwelt within me as He knows, with haste have ye come to bow down, Who bestows great mercy upon the world!"

*Other Stichera of the Saint, Tone 8, Podoben: "O Most Marvelous Wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^1054]
Having left behind the beauty of the world, O all-praised Eugenia, by the beauties of nobility from on high hast thou illuminated the nobility of the soul: having preserved the unclouded grace of the image by thy life, O unconquerable martyr, the godlike rampart of virginity, and the most noble blossom of nature!

By form and by life and by grace, and by patience of soul having adorned thyself, thou didst bring the hosts of sufferers and the assembly of virgins unto Him Who shone forth from the Virgin: and having opened unto all an entrance into the testimony of years, O all-wise Eugenia; with them do thou even now beseech Him to save thy flock!

The Builder of righteousness, having beautifully adorned thee as a pure virgin and as a glorious martyr with double crowns, gave unto thee a beauteous bridal chamber enlightened with manifestations of light: where thou dwellest, O bride of Christ, enriched now with eternal blessedness!

*Glory, both now, of the Forefeast, Tone 2:*[^1055]
Behold, the time of our salvation draws near: prepare thyself, O cave, the Virgin draws near to give birth! O Bethlehem, land of Judah, exult and rejoice, for from thee hath shone forth our Lord! Hear, ye mountains and hills, and the surrounding regions of Judea: for Christ comes, that as Lover of mankind He may save man whom He fashioned!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Forefeast, Tone 4, Podoben: "All-Praised Martyrs" (Всхва́льнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*[^1056]
Bringing gifts unto Thee, O Son, the eastern kings came, having recognized Thee as King about to be born: frankincense, and myrrh, and gold; and lo, they stand before the doors! Command these to behold Thee as an infant, held in my arms, Who art older than ancient Adam!

*Verse:* God shall come from Theman, and the Holy One from a dark and shadowy mountain.[^1057]
Come, enter with haste! the Virgin of old said unto the Magi: and behold the Invisible One, Who hath appeared visibly and become an infant! And they with zeal entered and bowed down, and brought gifts, having fulfilled the divine prophecy!

*Verse:* O Lord, I have heard Thy report, and was afraid: O Lord, I considered Thy works, and was astonished.[^1058]
I carry Thee as an infant in arms, Who bearest all things; and I am amazed, said the unespoused one: How shall I nourish Thee with milk, Who nourishest all? O my Son and my Creator, I glorify Thy boundless condescension unto men, whereby Thou shalt save a perishing world!

*Glory, both now, of the Forefeast, Tone 6:*[^1059]
Triumph, O Zion; rejoice, O Jerusalem, city of Christ God: receive the Creator Who is contained in a cave and manger! Open unto me the gates, and having entered into them I shall see Him as an infant wrapped in swaddling-bands, Who holds creation in the palm of His hand: Whom angels hymn with unceasing voice, the Lord and Giver of life, Who saves our race!

*Troparion of the Forefeast, Tone 4:*[^1060]
Once Mary, being with child of a seedless conception, was enrolled with aged Joseph in Bethlehem, as being of the seed of David: and the time of childbearing arrived, and there was no place for lodging; but as a delightful palace, the cave was shown unto the Queen! Christ is born, that He may raise up the image that of old was fallen!

*At Compline, the Canon, whose acrostic is: "Today I sing of the Great Sabbath."*[^1061]
*The heirmoi are sung twice, and the troparia on 6. Tone 6.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Of old Herod seeks to kill Him Who was hidden in a manger, Who covered the persecuting tyrant beneath the wave of the sea: but with the Magi let us sing: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously magnified![^1062]

*Troparia:*
O Lord my God, I will sing unto Thee a birth-song and a forefestal hymn: Who by Thy divine birth grantest rebirth unto me, and leadest me back unto my former nobility!

Understanding Thee on high upon the throne, and below in the manger, O my Savior, the transcendent and the earthly wondered at Thy dominion; for Thou wast marvelous beyond understanding, two-fold in nature, God-man!

That Thou mightest fill all things with Thy glory, having bowed the heavens, Thou camest down upon earth; for as rain upon the fleece didst Thou descend, of whom now Thou comest two-fold to be born, both God and man!

*Then both choirs sing the heirmos together.*
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
All creation, seeing Thee born in a cave, Who hungest all the earth upon the waters without restraint, was shaken with trembling, crying: There is none holy save Thee, O Lord![^1063]

*Troparia:*
Manifesting the images of Thine ineffable incarnation, O Compassionate One, Thou didst multiply visions, and didst inspire prophecies, which having now come Thou hast fulfilled, being born in the flesh of the pure Maiden in the city of David!

The earth spread forth its shoulders, and receives the Creator Who receives praise from angels, from the heavens a star, from shepherds thanksgiving, from the Magi gifts, and from all the world knowledge!
The prognostications of Balaam the soothsayer are now fulfilled: for a star hath shone forth out of Jacob, and guided the kings, the Magi bringing gifts from Persia, unto the Sun of glory!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Foreseeing Thy coming from the Virgin, Habakkuk cried out in awe: Incarnate from the south, Thou art come, O Deliverer, to call back banished Adam![^1064]

*Troparia:*
The Lord now draws near and comes, the expectation of nations and the salvation of the world: prepare the cave, O city of Bethlehem, and running together with the Magi, ye shepherds, make haste!

By the strength of the Godhead Thou wast mingled with men in unconfused union: in the likeness of flesh, O Savior, Thou renewest Adam, and savest him by Thine assumption of flesh!

The Word having become flesh is clothed, and dwells among us by ineffable providence: come, ye faithful, let us behold His glory as of the Only-begotten of God the Father!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Isaiah, having seen the unwaning Light of Thy divine manifestation, O Christ, which mercifully came to pass unto us, rising early out of the night cried aloud: Behold, the Virgin shall conceive in her womb, and shall bear the incarnate Word, and all the earthborn shall rejoice![^1065]

*Troparia:*
Thou makest new things for the earthborn, having become of dust, O Creator; and the manger, the swaddling-bands, and the cave are images of Thy humility: and the betrothed husband of Thy Mother, Thy supposed father according to the flesh, now fulfills the counsel of the Father Who begat Thee!

By myrrh the kings of the nations signify death, by gold royal dominion, and by frankincense the pre-eminence of the Godhead, bringing firstfruits as gifts unto Thee, born in the cave of Bethlehem of an unespoused Mother!

Having come forth from the unwedded one, O Word co-eternal with the Father, Thou didst dwell in the flesh in a cave, counting the manger as a throne: and by Thy fearful dispensation Thou fillest the Magi and the shepherds with wonder, and the angels with awe: glory to Thy dominion!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Jonah was held in the belly of the sea monster, but was not retained; for bearing the image of Thee Who wast born and appeared in the flesh, he came forth from the beast as from a bridal chamber: for having now been born in the flesh, Thou didst choose to enter the grave and death, and to rise again on the third day![^1066]

*Troparia:*
Now hath the middle wall of ancient enmity perished and been destroyed by Thy coming in the flesh, O Christ, and the flaming sword turns its back unto all; and I faithfully partake of the life-bearing tree of Eden, being straightway shown forth as a cultivator of immortal gardens!

Hades reigns with sin from Adam until Thee: but their inescapable tyranny perishes; for Thou art born in the flesh, O Deliverer, of the seed of David, to be seated upon the throne of his kingdom, and reigning unto the ages!

A slayer of children, but not a slayer of Christ, was fierce Herod shown to be: for though he bitterly mowed down the infants as grass, yet he was not able to seize and slay the Ear of life; for being the Giver of life, He was concealed as God by divine power from the persecutor!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Ineffable wonder! He Who delivered the venerable youths from the flame in the furnace reclines as an infant in a poor manger, for the salvation of us who sing: Blessed art Thou, O God our Deliverer![^1067]

*Troparia:*
The deceitful enemy was wounded, seeing God lying as an infant in a humble manger; and he is slain by the mighty hand of God, for the salvation of us who sing: Blessed art Thou, O God our Deliverer!

Blessed is the manger: for having received the Creator as an infant within itself, it was shown forth as the cherubic throne, for the salvation of us who sing: Blessed art Thou, O God our Deliverer!

According to human law Thou receivest placement in swaddling-bands, having appeared as an infant: whereby Thou loosest the bonds of our transgressions, granting freedom unto those who cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God our Deliverer!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Be astonished in fear, O heaven, and let the foundations of the earth be shaken! For, lo, He Who bears all things in the palm of His hand is wrapped in swaddling-bands, and is received as a stranger in a small manger: Whom ye children bless, ye priests praise, and ye people exalt above all forever![^1068]

*Troparia:*
Bound Adam was loosed, and freedom was bestowed upon all the faithful, when Thou, O Savior, wast wrapped in swaddling-bands, and laid in a small cave and in a manger of dumb beasts; therefore rejoicing we bring a forefestal hymn in faith at Thy Nativity!

The Persian delusion hath ceased: for the eastern kings, stargazers, bring gifts unto Christ the King of all, Who is born: gold, and myrrh with frankincense:

...Whom ye children bless, ye priests praise, and ye people exalt above all forever!

O novel wonders! O goodness! O ineffable patience! For, lo, He Who lives in the highest is accounted as an infant: and God voluntarily flees from Herod: Whom ye children bless, ye priests praise, and ye people exalt above all forever!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Marvel not now, O Mother, seeing as an infant Him Whom the Father begat from the womb before the morning star: for I have come manifestly to raise up and glorify together fallen human nature, which magnifies thee in faith and love![^1069]

*Troparia:*
In thy marvelous childbearing, having escaped the birth-pangs supernaturally, I was called blessed, O beginningless Son; but now seeing Thee fleeing from Herod, I am troubled in soul by the sword of sorrow, crying aloud: Save those who honor Thee!

Unto the land of Egypt I go indeed, but the hand-made idols of Egypt, O Mother, I will cast down with an earthquake, and all the enemies seeking My life I will send into hades: as the only Sovereign One, I will raise up and save those who honor thee!

Let creation rejoice! For the Creator is fashioned; and He Who was before, is now known newly as God: let the Magi meet Him with gifts, let the shepherds clap hands in wonder faithfully, and let men rejoice together with angels!

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Forefeast, 3 times.*
*After the 1st Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 6, Podoben: "The Angelic Hosts" (А҆́гглскїѧ си́лы):*[^1070]
The prophetic sayings are now fulfilled: for our God is born tomorrow of the Virgin Mary, beyond speech; and He remains as He was before childbearing. The Magi gather, carrying gifts; the shepherds pipe: let us also make haste, crying: O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin, Lord, glory to Thee!
*Glory, both now, the same.*

*After the 2nd Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*[^1071]
Silencing the songs of the shepherds' pipes, the angelic host cried aloud, saying unto them: "Cease piping, ye overseers of the flocks; cry aloud, singing: For Christ the Lord is born, Who as God was well pleased to save the human race!"
*Glory, both now, the same.*

*Canon of the Forefeast with the heirmos on 8, and of the Saint on 4.*
*The Canon of the Forefeast, whose acrostic is*[^1072] to show mercy upon us.[^1073]

*Another Canon. Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^1074]
Stretching forth his hands, Daniel stopped the gaping mouths of lions in the den; and having girded themselves with virtue, the youths, lovers of piety, quenched the power of fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*
Thou didst darken all the enemy's wrath, O glorious one, and his struggles, being clothed with the invincible power of Christ; and having become a victor, O Martyr Demetrius, thou didst cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Having displayed the steadfast courage of the martyrs, thou didst inherit the good calling of the martyrs, O martyr. Being washed in that bath which is not defiled by secondary sinful assaults, pierced by the spear, thou didst cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

The multitude of thy wonders, beyond mind, the gifts which Christ bestowed upon Thee, manifest unto all, O glorious one; and the grace of healings which surpasses speech plainly proclaims thy grace unto us who cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
We hymn the one Virgin Mother and pure Theotokos in all generations: for she is the mediatress of our salvation, as having given birth unto the Word, the Deliverer of the world, unto Whom we cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Another Canon. Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^1075]
The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried aloud unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, praise; ye people, exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
That God might make thee manifest unto those in the world, O Demetrius, they preserved the inextinguishable lamp of the soul, ever anointed with the oil of deeds; and as the doors of the bridal chamber were opened unto thee, He shows thee forth as a fountain of divine myrrh, flowing forth, granting this grace as a reward for purity.

Not of blood, as the honorable Gospel says,[^1076] nor of the will of the flesh, O Demetrius, but of God wast thou born, honoring God incarnate; and having zealously shed thy blood for His sake, thou didst receive marvelous grace from above, and thou pourest forth divine myrrh, perfuming souls with sweet fragrance.

Thou wast shown forth as an ensouled ladder, firmly established upon the earth by the steps of the flesh, but reaching unto the heavens; for thou wast enriched by God who is established in thee, O martyr. And thou wast shown forth as a pillar, anointed with the oil of the ascetical labors of martyrdom; and now, as a fountain of divine myrrh, thou streamest forth for those who approach in faith.

David, singing prophetically, prefigured thee, O Demetrius, as the river of God full of water; for lo, inexhaustible is the outpouring of thy myrrh! Therefore the multitude of rivers, overflowing, lift up their voices and clap their hands in praise of God, Who through all these things hath glorified thee.

Thou art the one Thrice-Holy, all-powerful Lord God, Whom the suffering Demetrius, theologizing, was shut up in prison; and having slain the spiritual serpent along with the physical scorpion, and having cast down the pride of Lyaeus through the divine Nestor,[^1077] he is pierced by the spear, and now streams myrrh from the tomb.

*Theotokion:*
For Adam, the Lord; for Eve, thou, O all-pure one; Gabriel for the deceitful serpent; the Cross for the tree; the garden of the tomb instead of Eden; "Rejoice!" instead of sorrow; the spear instead of the fiery sword; and the purple robe instead of fig leaves. Thee therefore do we hymn, O Maiden, as the cause of all things!

*Canon of the Earthquake. Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^1078]
It is impossible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the incarnate Word was made manifest unto men: Whom magnifying, together with the heavenly hosts, we call thee blessed.

*Troparia:*
We all have sinned, and the earth, though it hath not sinned, is terribly tormented; for the Lover of mankind, chastening us, shakes all of it. Let us regain our senses, and take care for our own salvation!

Being the Lord of hours and years, Thou didst desire in the twinkling of an eye to wipe out Thy servants, but Thou didst hold back, O Master, out of Thine abundant tender mercy. We who are without defense give thanks unto Thee, O only Lover of mankind!

From earthquake, the sword, bitter captivity, and pagan invasion, O Christ, from ruin, famine, pestilence, and every other affliction, O compassionate One, deliver Thy city and every land that faithfully hymns Thee!

The earth cries out without tongue, groaning: "Why do all ye men defile me with many evils? For the Master, sparing you, wounds me alone. Regain your senses, and appease God by repentance!"

*Theotokion:*
