Mystery having received in thy childbearing, O Virgin, without suffering corruption, Thou hast delivered us all from corruption and from the most grievous earthquake and sorrow, calming the Master's indignation by thy maternal intercession, O woman full of divine joy!

*Another Canon. Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^1079]
A cornerstone was cut out from thee, an uncut mountain, O Virgin, even Christ, Who joined together the separated natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Theotokos!

*Troparia:*
Tear asunder the bonds of transgressions of those who sing of thee, O passion-bearer; halt the assaults of passions, destroy the tumult of heresies, and quiet the storm of misfortunes by thy prayers!

Direct thy most sacred flock unto the right paths of the heavenly Kingdom, O crown-bearing Martyr Demetrius, unto saving pasture, unto the eternal place!

Pray thou unto the Life-Giving Lord and King of hosts, O Martyr Demetrius, that those who hymn thee may obtain crowns of glory and the attainment of the heavenly Kingdom!

*Theotokion:*
As root, fountain, and cause of incorruption, O Virgin, all we faithful honor thee with praises, placing our hope in thee; for thou hast poured forth hypostatic immortality unto us.

*Another Canon. Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Heaven was astonished at this, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the hierarchies of angels and men magnify thee, O Theotokos!

*Troparia:*
Having received thy spiritual alabaster vessel, filled with the divine myrrh of virtues, Christ bowed His head unto thee, O martyr, as having found rest in the splendor of the purity of thy life; therefore thou wast called blessed, and wast filled with myrrh-streaming grace for the ends of the world.

Lo, a fountain of ever-flowing myrrh, O sufferer, a radiant foreshadowing of the ineffable favors awaiting thee! Standing before God with martyr's boldness, ask victory over enemies for our King,[^1080] and an unending Kingdom!

By the sign of the victory-bearing Cross, thou didst conquer the cruelty of Lyaeus, and didst establish Nestor as victor and fellow-sufferer; and now subdue every barbarian nation in servitude unto our King, collaborating, fighting alongside, strengthening magnifying the ends of the earth, O Demetrius!

Deliver those who flee with fervent love of heart unto thy venerable shrine, O martyr of Christ, which pours forth myrrh in rivers, from soul-corrupting deeds and from the flood of the stench of passions, by thy prayers; and show them forth unto the Lord as most sweet-smelling fragrances!

We celebrate a day of spiritual joy, O Demetrius, beholding the ever-flowing fountain of thy myrrh; do thou dispel the darkness of passions, as one filled with divine light. Grant us to walk becomingly as in the day, and to behold the unapproachable light of God!

*Theotokion:*
Thou art the all-pure golden dove, O Virgin, who as an olive branch bore the Word of God, Who anointed His flesh with the oil of Divinity. Having received Him into his heart as an ark, the suffering martyr streams myrrh from the tomb and anoints the faces of the faithful.

*Exapostilarion, Tone 3, Podoben: "O Unchangeable Light" (Свѣ́те неизмѣ́нный):*[^1081]
Thou didst arm Nestor with the Cross, and through him didst cast down the pride of Lyaeus, O Demetrius, in the arena; as Christ, thou dost open thy side unto the spear, whereby do thou preserve thy flock. *(Twice)*

*Glory, both now:*
O Lord Who lookest upon the earth from heaven and makest it all to tremble: deliver us from the earthquake, by the prayers of her whom Thou hast granted unto Christians as an intercessor!

*At the Praises, Stichera of the Martyr, on 6, Tone 4, Podoben: "As One Noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^1082]
Being pierced, thou didst wound the adversary with thy spears, and didst make the murderous one a corpse; from whose wickedness deliver us, O Demetrius, subduing the strong tormenting passions by thy sacred prayers, melting our hearts and souls with divine fear, O holy one!

Knowing thee to be a river filled with spiritual waters, O holy one, with thy great mercy bedew our souls which are wasted away by transgressions of passions, and wash those who are in defilement, and deliver them from abundant misfortunes and eternal torment!

As a most radiant star thou wast shown forth upon earth, ever illumining the world under the sun with thy rays, O most glorious Demetrius, driving away the mist of darkness from our souls by thy prayers; and heal the sicknesses of those who celebrate thy honorable memory out of love, O glory and praise of martyrs!

*Other Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "Rejoice, O Habitation of the Ascetics" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ по́стническихъ):*[^1083]
Come unto us, O martyr of Christ, who have need of thy merciful visitation; and deliver those who are afflicted by the threats of tyrants and from the fierce fury of heretics, by whom we are driven as captives and naked, passing frequently from place to place, and wandering in caves and mountains. Have compassion therefore, O all-praised one, and give us relief; calm the storm, and quench the indignation against us, praying unto God Who grants great mercy unto the world!

Thou hast been given unto us as a firm rampart, not fearing the assaults of the enemy, bringing barbarian invasions to naught and calming diseases: an indestructible step, an unshakable foundation, a guardian of the city, an inhabitant, and a champion wast thou for thy city, O Demetrius. And now, O all-blessed one, save it by thy prayers as it is in grievous distress and wretchedly besieged, praying unto Christ Who grants great mercy unto the world!

Having embraced every virtue, the choir of passion-bearers was shown forth, whence it worthily inherited the enjoyment of the incorruptible and blessed life; wherein being adorned, O Demetrius worthy of wonder, honored by the imitation of Christ, and boasting in the equality of the spear, pray earnestly to deliver us who honor thee from passions and grievous distresses, warmly praying unto Him Who grants great mercy unto the world!

*Glory, Tone 4, Idiomelon, of Andrew of Jerusalem:*[^1084]
Having inherited through spears the grace of the Savior's ribs pierced by the spear, whence the Savior pours forth for us the waters of life and incorruption: let us honor Demetrius, most wise in teachings, and crowned among the martyrs; who finished the course of suffering with blood, who shone forth with wonders throughout the whole universe, the emulator of the Master, the merciful lover of the poor, the protector of the Thessalonians in many and frequent grievous misfortunes. And celebrating his annual memory, we glorify Christ God, Who through him works healings for all!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Deliver us from our necessities, O Mother of Christ God, who didst give birth unto the Creator of all: that we all may cry unto thee: Rejoice, the only intercession for our souls!

*Great Doxology. And Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes from the* canon of the Earthquake, Ode 3, on 4; and of the Saint, Ode 6, on 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 6:
Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance![^1085]
*Verse:* Unto Thee, O Lord, will I call; O my God, be not silent unto me.
*And of the Saint, Tone 7:*
The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord, and shall hope in Him.[^1086]
*Epistle for the Earthquake, to the Hebrews, section 331, from the midpoint:*[^1087]
Brethren: For whom the Lord loves He chastens.
*And of the Saint, to Timothy, section 292.*[^1088]
*Alleluia, Tone 4:*
O God, Thou hast cast us off and hast destroyed us.[^1089]
*Verse:* Thou hast shaken the earth and hast troubled it.
*And of the Saint:*
The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree, and shall be multiplied like a cedar in Lebanon.[^1090]
*Gospel for the Earthquake, Matthew, section 27.*[^1091]
*And of the Saint, John, section 52.*[^1092]
*Communion Verse:*
Praise the Lord from the heavens, praise Him in the highest![^1093]
*Another:* In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be; he shall not be afraid of evil tidings.[^1094]

*It is Fitting to Know:*[^1095]
*If the feast of the Great-Martyr Demetrius of Thessalonica and the Earthquake falls on a Sunday:*
*On Saturday evening, at Small Vespers, the resurrection stichera, and of the Theotokos according to custom.*
*At Great Vespers:*
*After the customary kathisma, at "Lord, I Have Cried," 3 resurrection stichera, and 3 of the Earthquake, written at Great Vespers at the aposticha; and 4 of Demetrius. Glory, of Demetrius; both now, the 1st Theotokion of the Tone. Prokeimenon of the day. 3 readings.*
*At the Litiya, the sticheron of the temple, and the idiomela stichera of the Saint. Glory, of the Saint; both now, Theotokion. At the Aposticha, resurrection stichera. Glory, of Demetrius; both now, Theotokion in the Tone of the Saint. At the blessing of the loaves, Troparion: "O Birthgiver of God and Virgin," twice; and of Demetrius, once.*
*(If there be no Vigil, after the "Our Father," the resurrection troparion. Note: Glory, of Demetrius; both now, of the Earthquake. At Compline, after "It is Truly Meet" and after the Trisagion, Kontakion of Demetrius; Glory, both now, of the Earthquake.)*
*At Matins: At "God is the Lord," resurrection troparion twice; Glory, of Demetrius; both now, of the Earthquake. After the kathismata, resurrection sessional hymns and their theotokia. Then the Polyeleos; and the troparia "The Angelic Council." Hypakoe of the Tone; and both sessional hymns of Demetrius once each, without theotokia; Glory, the sessional hymn after the Polyeleos; both now, its theotokion* its theotokion. The Gradual hymns of the Tone. Prokeimenon of the Tone. The Matins Resurrection Gospel. Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ. Psalm 50.[^1096] Resurrection sticheron. Canon: The Resurrection canon with its heirmos on 4; and of the Earthquake on 4; and of Demetrius on 6. Katavasia: "I shall open my mouth."[^1097] After Ode 3, Kontakion of Demetrius, and the Ikos, and Sessional Hymn. After Ode 6, Resurrection Kontakion and Ikos. Resurrection Exapostilarion; Glory, of the Saint; both now, of the Earthquake. At the Praises, 4 resurrection stichera; and 4 of Demetrius with the Doxastikon, with his verses. Glory, Gospel sticheron; both now: "Most blessed art thou." Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, the resurrection troparion only. Litanies; and Dismissal; and the First Hour. At the First Hour, the resurrection troparion; Glory, of the Earthquake; both now, the theotokion of the Hours. After the "Our Father," Kontakion of the Earthquake. Thus also at the remaining Hours, reciting the troparia and kontakia alternating; only the resurrection troparion is invariable.
At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Tone on 4; and of the Earthquake, Ode 3, on 4; and of Demetrius, Ode 6, on 4. After the Entrance, the resurrection troparion, and of the Earthquake, and of the temple of the Theotokos (if there be one), and of Demetrius. Then the resurrection Kontakion, and of the Earthquake; Glory, of Demetrius; both now, of the Theotokos. But if it be not a temple of the Theotokos, both now, of the Earthquake. Prokeimenon of the Earthquake, and of the Saint. Epistle of the Earthquake, and of Demetrius. Alleluia, Tone 4: "O God, Thou hast cast us off"; Verse: "Thou hast shaken the earth"; and of the Saint: "The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree." Gospel of the Earthquake, and of Demetrius. Communion Verse: "Praise the Lord from the heavens"; and of the Saint: "In everlasting remembrance." The daily Epistle and Gospel are read under the one heading.

*October 27. The Holy Martyr Nestor:*[^1098]
*At "Lord, I Have Cried," Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O Most Marvelous Wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^1099]
O marvelous sufferer Nestor, * thou didst gird thyself * with the armor of Christ; * bodily grappling with Lyaeus in the arena, * thou didst destroy him; * and with invisible arrows * thou didst strike down and slay the invisible Beliar! * Therefore the Giver of great gifts * hath crowned thy head with the crown of victory!

Truly strengthened by divine words, * O wise Nestor, * through Demetrius the martyr, * thou didst manfully slay the proud tyrant; * and having confessed the immortal God, * Who died and destroyed hades, * thou didst endure an unjust death, O glorious one; * therefore rejoicing, * thou didst inherit unending life!

Thou wast a divine youth, * shining with beauty of body, O wise one, * adorned with divine grace, * and fortified with power. * Therefore having destroyed the power of Lyaeus * by the power of the Almighty, * thou didst suffer rejoicing, * and wast numbered with the choir of martyrs; * together with them, rejoicing, remember us!

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
We pray thee, O all-pure one, * our intercessor: * do not despise thy servants to the end, * who are troubled in sorrows, that they should perish; * but hasten to deliver us * from the present wrath and sorrow, * O pure and most holy one, full of divine joy; * for thou art our rampart * and unconquerable help!

*Stavrotheotokion:*[^1100]
Beholding Thee nailed * upon the Cross, O Jesus, * and willingly accepting the Passion, * the Virgin and Thy Mother, O Master, * cried aloud: "Woe is me, * O sweet Child! * How dost Thou endure unjust wounds, * O Physician, Who hast healed the infirmity of mankind, * and hast delivered all from corruption by Thy tender mercy?"

*Troparion, Tone 4:*[^1101]
Thy martyr Nestor, O Lord, in his suffering received an incorruptible crown from Thee, our God; for possessing Thy strength, he cast down the tyrants and shattered the powerless audacity of demons. By his prayers, save our souls!

*At Matins, Canon, on 4, Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^1102]
He Who is mighty in battle cast Pharaoh's chariots and his host into the sea; let us sing a new song, for He hath been glorified!

*Troparia:*
Having gladly made a fiery chariot by thy blood, O wise one, thou didst ascend with joy unto the heavenly height, where Jesus is, the eternal King.

Having clothed thyself with the spiritual armor of faith, O Nestor, and holding the Cross as a spear in thy hands, thou didst powerfully cut down the ranks of the enemy.

Strengthened by the warmth of Christ's love, Thou didst leap into the arena, and didst shatter all the wiles of Lyaeus, and didst establish a victorious victory.

*Theotokion:*
Conceived Thou didst conceive in thy womb Him Who is co-beginningless with the Father, and without knowing man, beyond the laws of nature, thou didst bear Him in the flesh, remaining truly a pure Virgin; therefore we all honor thee!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^1103]
O Thou Who establishest thunder and createst spirit: establish me, O Lord, that I may truly hymn Thee and do Thy will; for there is none holy as Thou, our God!

*Troparia:*
Strengthened by the might of the power of Him Who shattered the bars and gates of hades, thou didst enter the arena with haste, and didst powerfully shatter the wiles of the enemy.

The ancient author of delusion was stretched out beneath thy feet; for youthfully didst thou stretch forth thyself unto the slaying of the sword, O Nestor, praise of martyrs; therefore do we praise thee!

Having beforehand destroyed the pride of the lawless, thou didst offer a sacrifice unto God, O martyr, yielding thy blood as a holy recompense unto Him; therefore hath He rewarded thee with rich gifts.

*Theotokion:*
The deformity brought upon the race of Adam by eating of the tree, Jesus, carried in thy womb, O Theotokos, having supernaturally restored, hath bestowed upon us our primal beauty.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Raised" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*[^1104]
Not fearing the tyranny of the enemy, nor terrified by wounds of the flesh, thou didst run fearlessly unto the contest, O wise one, bearing the invincible weapon of the Cross; therefore having suddenly slain the deceitful one, thou wast put to death, O Nestor, and wast vouchsafed life, standing before Christ.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
Being a lover of sin, I pray thee who didst bear the sinless God Who takes away the sins of the world, O all-pure one: have mercy upon my much-sinning soul, and cleanse my many sins; for thou art the cleansing of sinners, the salvation of the faithful, and their intercession!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
She who in the end bore Thee in the flesh, Who wast begotten of the beginningless Father, seeing Thee hanging upon the Cross, O Christ, cried aloud: "Woe is me, O most beloved Jesus! How art Thou, Who art glorified as God by the angels, now willingly crucified by lawless men, O Son? I hymn Thy longsuffering!"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^1105]
I have heard Thy report, O God, and I was afraid; I understood Thy works, and was filled with dread, O Lord, that the earth is full of Thy praise!

*Troparia:*
Demetrius' bright-beaming teachings illumining thee, thou didst escape the darkness of Hellenic vanity, and didst shine forth with the light of martyrdom.

With the sweetness of thy good faith, O martyr, thou didst trouble the salty sea of unbelief; and with the depth of thy blood thou didst drown the hosts of the ungodly.

Thou wast shown forth as a tree raised up unto life, nourishing the fullness of the faithful with the good fruit of thy ascetical struggles, O Nestor of Christ, praise of martyrs!

*Theotokion:*
Be thou a cleansing place of sins for me, and give me a helping hand in my distress, O all-pure Sovereign Lady, firm intercessor of Christians!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^1106]
Send down a radiant morning upon us, as Thou art good, and guide us in Thy fear, O Master!

*Troparia:*
Having watered thy mind with the words of divine knowledge, thou didst bring forth a ripe ear of martyrdom, O glorious Nestor!

By the healing of thy words, O Demetrius, martyr of Christ, Nestor put an end to the great lawlessness of godlessness.

Grappling with the visible enemy, thou didst invisibly acquire the help of God, O glorious Nestor!

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O thou who didst bear our God without seed! Rejoice, O all-pure Mother of God! Rejoice, O Bride unwedded!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^1107]
A storm of thoughts having overtaken me, dragged me down into the abyss of immeasurable sins; but do Thou, O good Helmsman, anticipate me, guide me as the Prophet, and save me!

*Troparia:*
Having marked thy heart with grace, thou didst valiantly pierce the heart of Lyaeus with the spear of thy courage, O sufferer, and rejoicing art put to death for the sake of Jesus, the Life of all.

As a bridegroom from a bridal chamber didst thou go forth from thy flesh, O wise one, clearly adorned with garments woven from the sacred blood of thy martyrdom, and thou didst stand before the Master with Christ.

Having lifted up thy feet upon a rock, Christ, the Rock of life, established them in piety,[^1108] and directed thy steps unto Himself, O Nestor, and put thine enemies to shame!

*Theotokion:*
Rend asunder the bonds of my transgressions, O Virgin Mary, who didst bear God Who takes away the sin of the world; for in thee have I placed my hope of salvation!

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben (Подо́бенъ):*

*Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the Highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*
Having contended valiantly, thou hast now inherited immortal glory, for thou wast an excellent warrior of the Master through the prayers of the Martyr Demetrius; with him therefore, O wise Nestor, cease not to pray for us all!

*Ikos:*
Thou didst walk in the way of the Lord from infancy, O thrice-blessed one, and didst piously desire His testimony; and having zealously taken the Cross upon thy shoulders, O God-wise Nestor, thou didst destroy the cruel deceit of idol-fury, crushing and breaking the snares and traps of Lyaeus. Therefore with faith we flee unto thy protection, crying out: O most rich one, pray to Christ God for us all!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^1109]
He Who spoke on the mountain with Moses, and revealed an image of virginity in the burning bush: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
Thou Who hast enlightened the hearts of the faithful with the dawn of the passion-bearer's ascetical struggles: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

Having quenched the flame of impiety with the dew of thy blood, O divine Martyr Nestor, thou dost sing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

Having offered a living sacrifice as a sweet-smelling fragrance unto the Lord, O Martyr Nestor, thou didst cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown forth as more holy than the Cherubim, having borne the all-holy Word of God, O all-holy Sovereign Lady, Who sanctifies us.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^1110]
Earth, and all that is upon it, the sea, and all springs, heaven of heavens, light, and darkness, frost, and heat, ye sons of men, ye priests: Bless the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto ages!

*Troparia:*
The earth received thy precious blood shed for Christ, and the heavens thy soul adorned as a bride; and the choirs of martyrs and the spirits of all the righteous met thee with joy, O martyr!

O thy God-beloved soul, which accounted temporal death as nothing, but chose to live in the Lord! O divine courage, stronger than the fury of the enemies! For thou didst rebuke those who do not confess Christ.

Having fought the good fight, thou hast truly finished the course, O wise martyr, and hast kept thy faith; henceforth there is laid up for thee a crown of righteousness, which Christ shall grant unto thee unto ages.[^1111]
=== END LEAF p281 === *Theotokion:*
I shall not fear the snares of the enemies, having thee as my helper; I shall not fear evil things, having fled unto thee, O Birthgiver of God; for in thee do I boast, and through thee, O pure one, shall I pass over the wall of sin; save me by thy prayers!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^1112]
For He Who is mighty hath magnified me, and holy is His Name, and His mercy is on them that fear Him from generation to generation.

*Troparia:*
Thou wast sacredly clothed with a purple robe reddened by thy blood, and holding the Cross in thy right hand as a scepter, thou reignest with Christ, O blessed Nestor!

Thou wast shown forth as a ripe cluster of the spiritual vine, pressed in the winepresses of torment; thou didst pour forth the wine of compunction for us who ever praise thee.

Dancing with the angels before the face of Jesus and being filled with His glory, ever remember those who faithfully keep thy honorable memory, O Nestor!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast truly shown forth as a radiant receptacle of Him Who dispersed the mist of ancient ignorance, a temple of sanctification, a golden lampstand, being wider than the heavens, O pure one!

*Exapostilarion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Give Ear, Ye Women" (Жєны̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите):*[^1113]
By the power of the Cross and by the prayers of Demetrius the glorious martyr, thou didst cast down the boasting of the godless and the pride of the enemy, having first conquered the passions, O Nestor; and thou wast shown forth as an excellent martyr of Christ, O wise sufferer!

*Theotokion:*
As an unconquerable weapon, an insurmountable rampart, a pillar of all strength, a haven of salvation, truly a divine foundation, and a bridge leading from earth to the heavenly realms, do we sing of thee, O Birthgiver of God, who through thee are saved!

*At the Liturgy: Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*[^1114]
To the saints that are in His earth, the Lord hath made wonderful all His desires in them.
*Verse:* I beheld the Lord ever before me, for He is at my right hand.
*Epistle to the Ephesians, pericope 233 [Eph. 6:10–17].*[^1115]
*Alleluia, Tone 4:*[^1116]
The righteous cried, and the Lord heard them.
*Verse:* Many are the afflictions of the righteous.
*Gospel according to Luke, pericope 106 [Lk. 21:12–19].*[^1117]
*Communion Verse:*
In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be.[^1118]
=== END LEAF p282 === *In the same month, on the 28th day.*[^1119]
*The Holy Martyrs Terentius and Neonilla;*
*And our venerable Father Stephen the Sabaite, composer of canons;*
*And the Holy Martyr Paraskeva, named Friday.*
*Her service is written after this service.*

*Stichera of the Martyrs, Tone 4, Podoben: "As One Noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^1120]
Having valiantly partaken of manifold torments, ye cast down the enemy's haughtiness, confessing before torturers, O blessed ones, Him Who appeared for our sake in the grossness of flesh, and willingly endured the most pure passions, and poured forth upon the world enlightenment and dispassion.

Together with Terentius, the glorious Neonilla shone forth, and the marvelous Nitas and Sarbilus, and Photus and Hierax with them; having lawfully suffered, and having quenched the fire of torments with the divine dew of the Spirit, they became pillars of piety, and acceptable sacrifices, and most pure offerings.

As a most radiant sun joined together with the moon Neonilla, O father Terentius, thou didst bring forth a choir of seven stars, crimsoned with martyr's blood, and shedding forth a bright radiance, who have taken up their abode in the eveningless rest, where the flocks of all sufferers brightly dance!

*Other Stichera of the Venerable Father, Tone 8, Podoben: "O Most Marvelous Wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^1121]
O God-bearing father Stephen, having most radiantly strengthened thy mind with divine understanding, thou didst govern anger with courage, desire with chastity, and all the power of the soul with righteousness; most philosophically didst thou fashion an exquisite chariot of virtues, and mounting it with joy, thou didst ascend unto the heights, O glorious one!

O God-bearing father Stephen, thou didst enlighten thy mind with theology, O venerable one, aiming thy anger like a spear against blasphemous heretics, O father; desiring heavenly nourishment, of which thou wast accounted worthy to partake, O marvelous one, as thou standest before the throne of the King of all and Almighty.
=== END LEAF p283 === O God-bearing father Stephen, having ever enriched thy mind through temperance, thou didst cause it to run unto the Primal Cause, and thou didst cast aside the turmoil of the world; and having shaken off earthly tumults through purity of mind, thou didst mingle thyself, O all-wise one, with the truly utmost Desire, O God-wise, venerable one!

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
*Podoben the same (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Vessel of virginity, and pure, fair dwelling-place of God, I entreat thee, the all-honored one: Comfort my soul, which is afflicted by the serpent's venom and slain by transgressions; that I may glorify thee, who hast magnified all things for the faithful, O full of God's grace!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
*Podoben the same (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
What is this visible sight which is beheld by mine eyes, O Master? Holding all creation, Thou art lifted up upon the Tree, and Thou diest, Who givest life unto all!—the Birthgiver of God said weeping, when she beheld Him Who ineffably shone forth from her, God and Man, lifted up upon the Cross.

*Troparion, Tone 4:*[^1122]
Thy martyrs, O Lord, through their sufferings have received incorruptible crowns from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, they cast down the torturers, and crushed the powerless insolence of demons: by their prayers save our souls!

*Glory, Troparion of Stephen, Tone 8:*[^1123]
Guide of Orthodoxy, teacher of piety and purity, light of the universe, divinely-inspired ornament of hierarchs, O all-wise Stephen, thou hast enlightened all by thy teachings, O spiritual lyre; pray to Christ God that our souls may be saved!

*Both now, Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*
*At Matins: One canon of the Octoechos, and two of the saints. Canon of the Martyrs, Tone 4:*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^1124]
I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I shall pour forth a word unto the Queen Mother; and I shall be seen brightly keeping festival, and rejoicing I shall sing of her wonders.

*Troparia:*
Burdened by the depth of sin, O Lover of mankind, I gaze with the eyes of my heart unto the only abyss of Thy tenderheartedness; pilot and save me by the prayers of the passion-bearers!

The unwaning Sun showed you forth as stars upon the firmament of His Church, illuminating it with the radiance of your ascetical struggles, O all-praised martyrs, seven in number...
=== END LEAF p284 === illuminating it. Champions of the faith, the establishment of the Church, were Terentius, and Nitas, Sarbilus, Theodulus, Hierax, Neonilla, and marvelous Vilis, Eunice and Photus.

*Theotokion:*
Mediatress of joy for us art thou, having received the fullness of all the Godhead, thou wast shown forth, O all-immaculate one; therefore with the Angel we all harmoniously cry unto thee: Rejoice, O full of God's grace!

*Another Canon of the Venerable Father, Tone 8:*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^1125]
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, for He alone has gloriously been glorified.

*Troparia:*
Disperse this dark mist of my despondency with divine light, O father Stephen, granting divine grace to sing of thy memory!

The one character of virtues was known to thee; for thou didst utterly reject worldly malice, being dedicated unto God from infancy, O Stephen.

Having through temperance brought thy whole self unto Christ Thy Savior as a bright, acceptable sacrifice, thou didst become a partaker of immortal life, O glorious one!

The spiritual lightning set thee as an all-radiant pillar, having received the worthiness of the mystical ministry, illuminating all the faithful.

*Theotokion:*
All-pure Birthgiver of God, joy of all, who didst contain God in thy womb, O undefiled one: Pray that they who sing of thee may be delivered from enemies!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^1126]
We boast not in wisdom, nor in power, nor in riches, but in Thee, the hypostatic Wisdom of the Father, O Christ; for there is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*
As a sun joined with the most radiant moon, O Neonilla, glorious Terentius brought forth a choir of seven stars, the martyrs of Christ!

The crushing of thy limbs, O sufferer Terentius, ground the bones of deceit to dust as powder, and mediated ineffable glory for thee.

Feathered with golden wings, O Eunice, thou didst escape the wiles of lawless hunters, and didst enter within the spiritual bridal chamber!

*Theotokion:*
The Wisdom of God, having found thee a most pure habitation, became incarnate of thy pure blood, and showed thee forth incorrupt after childbirth.

*Another [Canon of the Venerable]. Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^1127]
Plant Thy fear in the hearts of Thy servants, O Lord, and be Thou our establishment, who in truth...
=== END LEAF p285 === call upon Thee in truth!

*Troparia:*
Having fled from those who in life defile human souls, O Stephen, the wilderness cleansed thee from childhood of carnal defilements, by divine providence.

The ascetics who led a heavenly life received thee and established thee, O blessed one, in the fear of the Lord unto divine wisdom.

Obeying the teachings of the fathers, thou didst lay down the fear of God as the foundation of wisdom for thine unblemished life, O father!

*Theotokion:*
Seedlessly didst thou conceive the Savior and Lord, O Bride of God, Who delivers from corruption us who truly hymn thee in song.

*Kontakion of Stephen, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Lifted Up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йся):*[^1128]
Thou didst plant a paradise of virtues and water it with the streams of thy tears, having obtained the Tree of life, O all-glorious one; by thy prayers save thy flock from corruption, and deliver from tribulations those who fervently honor thee, O Stephen; for we all have acquired thee with faith and love as a most great protector, O wise one!

*Sessional Hymn of the Martyrs, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^1129]
Like stars bright as the sun, the sacred children visibly shine forth together with Terentius, and enlighten creation; radiantly shining through their manly ascetical struggles, they disperse the night of polytheism. Therefore with gladness we call them blessed in faith as true pleasing servants of God, and cry aloud: O all-praised passion-bearers, entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who lovingly honor your holy memory!

*Glory, of the Venerable, Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word Co-Unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное сло́во):*[^1130]
Having adorned thy life through temperance and put passions to death, thou didst trample down the assaults of the enemy, O father Stephen; and having passed over unto God, thou wast shown forth as a wonderworker, dancing with the angels in everlasting glory; with them pray unceasingly that our souls may be saved!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Fervent and unconquerable protector, certain and unashamed hope, rampart, protection, and haven of those who flee unto thee, O ever-virgin pure one: Pray with the Angels unto Thy Son and God, to give peace to the world, and salvation, and great mercy!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee on the Cross, O Christ, hanging willingly between thieves, Thy Mother, being torn within with maternal compassion, said: O sinless Son, how art Thou unrighteously nailed to the Cross like an evildoer...
=== END LEAF p286 === nailed as an evildoer, desiring to make alive mankind, for Thou art tenderhearted!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^1131]
Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of righteousness, the Church stood in her order, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
Piloted by the all-powerful right hand, dryshod did they pass over the deep of torments, until they attained unto the calm haven of the heavenly Kingdom, O martyrs!

The proud torturer exalted by the dominion of impiety ye cast down with the weapons of faith, O all-praised martyrs, singing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

A choir of seven brothers, passion-bearers with their two parents, an immaculate sacrifice, were brought unto Thee, O Master Christ, Who for their sake wast wonderfully sacrificed like a lamb!

*Theotokion:*
He Who by His will created all things is willingly fashioned beyond word from thee, saving those who with faith cry out, O unwedded one: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Another [Canon of the Venerable]. Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^1132]
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Divinity.

*Troparia:*
By the divine law of the Spirit thou didst subdue all carnal wisdom, and by the Word thou didst drive away irrational impulses of passions, O wise Stephen.

Thou didst arm the waves of anger against the enemy, O Stephen, and didst extend thy desire unto God, the only Lover!

Thou didst behold the radiant light of dispassion, O venerable Stephen, having stripped off the dark mist of passions, being guided by grace.

Considering the things to come in thy mind, thou didst abhor fleeting irrationality; therefore by thy labors thou hast inherited all things that are ever-existing.

*Theotokion:*
The God Whom thou didst bear, O pure Virgin, entreat Him to grant unto thy servants remission of sins and deliverance from adversities!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^1133]
Thou, O Lord, art come as a light into the world: a holy light, turning back from dark ignorance those who sing of Thee in faith.

*Troparia:*
Having acquired Thee, O Lord, as their strength, the martyrs valiantly despised torn flesh, wounds, fire, and death!

O sacred children! O sacrifices acceptable unto God! O company that destroyed the multifaceted snares of the warring enemy!
=== END LEAF p287 === With immortal victories thou didst crown thy head, having conquered the serpent that conquered Eve, O virtuous Eunice!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst bear for us the timeless Light, the radiance of the Father, Who in the riches of goodness came to be subject to time, O all-immaculate one!

*Another [Canon of the Venerable]. Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^1134]
Enlighten us with Thy commandments, O Lord, and by Thy high arm grant Thy peace unto us, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*
Thou wast shown forth from heaven as a lamp unto those who hymn thee, driving away the gloom of evil deeds and depicting the radiance of divine action.

Thou didst acquire watchful prayer, all-night standing, fervent uplifting of hands, and fountains of tears, O father Stephen!

Thou wast truly shown forth as a radiant beacon in the sky of the wilderness, O Stephen, enlightening those held in the darkness of evils!

*Theotokion:*
Pray unceasingly unto Thy Son and our God, O unwedded, pure Mary, to send down mercy upon us, the faithful!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^1135]
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood which flowed through mercy from Thy side!

*Troparia:*
The torturer commanded unmercifully to stretch and bind the martyrs to wheels with manifold kinds of tortures; but he was put to shame, beholding them unconquered!

Having been given as food to wild beasts, ye were shown forth gentler than sheep through your unceasing prayers, and received no harm whatever from them, O holy ones!

Let Sarbilus, Neonilla, Photus, and Theodulus, Vilis, Hierax, and Eunice, and divine Nitas together with Terentius, be praised as noble martyrs of piety!

*Theotokion:*
The bush prefigured the great mystery of thy childbirth which surpasses the mind: surrounded all around by fire, yet in no way consumed, O all-hymned Virgin!

*Another [Canon of the Venerable]. Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^1136]
Thou didst settle Jonah alone in the sea monster, O Lord: save me also who am bound by the nets of the enemy, as Thou didst save him from corruption!

*Troparia:*
Thou wast seen to offer food unto a deer with thy hands, O Stephen; for having cast off the passions of the soul, thou didst reign over wild animals as Adam did formerly in Eden!
=== END LEAF p288 === Preparing a habitation for Christ in the chamber of thy soul, O Stephen, having restrained thy belly and thy tongue, thou didst put carnal pleasures utterly to death.

Thou wast crowned with the victory-bearing wreaths of fasting, O Stephen; for thou didst conquer the hosts of demons, and cast down the assaults of thoughts and passions, O blessed one!

*Theotokion:*
Through thy prayers, O pure one, cause the Lord Who was incarnate in thy womb beyond understanding to be merciful unto thy servants, that we may be delivered from terrible torment!

*Kontakion of the Martyrs, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Lifted Up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йся):*[^1137]
The honorable memory of the martyrs has arrived today, making all things glad, of all-wise Terentius and his spouse; let us therefore fervently assemble, that we may receive healing: for these, having received grace from the Holy Spirit, heal the infirmities and diseases of our souls!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^1138]
In the Persian furnace, Abraham's children, burned by love of piety rather than by flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
Cast into the furnace of torments, O martyrs, ye found dew from heaven through endurance, thankfully crying aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

Having the disposition of the soul rooted upon the rock of the immutable faith of Christ, through their prayer the martyrs cast down the earthquake of the godless ones' ferocity.

Being suspended and severely scraped upon your sides, and burned with most cruel vinegar and fire, O blessed ones, ye burned up all deceitful matter, O glorious ones!

*Theotokion:*
He Who arrays the heaven with clouds, O all-pure one, showed thee forth as a heaven on earth; for from thee He clothed Himself in the whole man, and deifies me through His boundless mercy!

*Another [Canon of the Venerable]. Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^1139]
Thou Who didst bedew the children in the furnace, and preserved the Virgin after childbirth who bore Thee: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
Having found thee a chosen vessel, O Stephen, the grace of the incorruptible Trinity truly rested in thee as it desired, and enriched thee with gifts, and showed thee forth as a physician of soul-afflictions.

Initiate of the most radiant Godhead, beholding and partaking of the ineffable glory of God, grant enlightenment unto us...
=== END LEAF p289 === who celebrate thy memory with faith, O pleasing servant of Christ, all-blessed Stephen!

Like Moses, having traced with thy staff water in an arid land for thy thirsty disciple, O Stephen, thou didst cause it to gush forth, O venerable one, singing unto Christ the Almighty: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^1140]

*Theotokion:*
It was fitting for Him Who was seedlessly incarnate to come forth from thee, O pure Mary, shown forth pure and undefiled brighter than the sun; for in thy purple-clad embraces thou didst bear God the Almighty!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^1141]
Stretching out his hands, Daniel shut the lions' mouths in the den; and having girded themselves with virtue, the children, lovers of piety, quenched the power of fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*
Daring against the fire, enlightened by the light of the divine Spirit, the divine martyrs showed the boiling of caldrons to be ineffective, having received divine cooling from heaven: let us, the faithful, praise them worthily!

A multitude of spikes appeared, visibly cutting you upon the place of contest, O all-praised martyrs, and taking away pains; together with them ye cry aloud the song of the children: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Having a watchful eye toward God in the heart, O Sarbilus, thou didst endure the piercing of thine eyes, rebuking the torturer who was held in the mist of godlessness; therefore we all call thee blessed as a son of day!

*Theotokion:*
By the good pleasure of the Father thou didst receive the Son into thy womb when the divine Spirit overshadowed thee, O all-pure one; and having given birth, thou didst remain most pure as before; pray unceasingly unto Him, O Virgin, that we may be delivered from misfortunes and affliction!

*Another [Canon of the Venerable]. Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^1142]
When musical instruments sounded together and countless peoples worshiped the image in Dura, the three children refused to obey, and sang praises and glorified the Lord unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
Thou didst obey the Lord, O blessed one, by the conduct of an immaculate life; and strengthened thereby, thou dost subdue and crush the enemies under thy feet, having valiantly fought against them with labors as with weapons!

Having a uniform life, it showed forth the word of truth, simple and upright manners, an unmalicious disposition, and a God-loving mind; therefore...
=== END LEAF p290 === therefore singing unto thee, we highly exalt Christ unto the ages.

With all thy soul, O father, thou didst love God, and didst turn away from applause; and traversing the impassable deserts, thou didst delight in thy sole Lover, for Whose sake thou didst count all things as refuse.[^1143]

*Theotokion:*
Knowing thee with certainty to be the Virgin and Mother of God beyond expression, we sing unto thee in all piety, chanting with faith: Bless and highly exalt the Lord unto the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^1144]
A cornerstone was cut out from thee, an uncut mountain, O Virgin, even Christ, Who hath joined together the separated natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Theotokos!

*Troparia:*
Ye were shown forth as a paradise having the tree of life in its midst, and a fountain pouring forth drops of healings, and a river truly filled with the divine waters of the Spirit, O holy ones!

O pillars of the Church of Christ, and most tranquil harbors of those tempest-tossed in the deep of cruel afflictions, ye appeared, O martyrs, nourishers of the world, and fellow-dwellers with the angels!

Ye dwell in the heavenly mansions with joy, gazing together upon the unsearchable beauties and delighting in true deification, O crown-bearing great-martyrs!

*Theotokion:*
O birthgiver of God, pray unto God Whom thou didst bear for us who honor thee, who art wider than the heavens, and holier than the Cherubim, and more honorable together than all creation!

*Another [Canon of the Venerable]. Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^1145]
Let us, the people, gloriously honor the pure Theotokos, who received the fire of the Godhead in her womb without being consumed, and let us magnify her with hymns!

*Troparia:*
Thou didst walk the narrow path, than which truly nothing is more constrained in the world; therefore thou hast entered into Eden, the spacious resting-place.

Meek didst thou show thyself, O Stephen, for in the heavens thou wast crowned with a crown of glory, O blessed one, worthily by Him Who crowned thee.

Thy life blossomed forth marvelously, fragrant as a rose in a garden and as a lampstand in the air, luminously illumining those who honor thee, O all-blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
We all, the faithful, magnify in hymns thee, the most pure Theotokos, who ineffably didst bear in thy womb God and the Savior!
=== END LEAF p291 === *In the same day [October 28], the Holy Martyr Paraskeve:*[^1146]

*At Vespers, the customary Kathisma. At "Lord, I Have Cried", Stichera to 6; and at Matins, the Canon to the Venerable to 6.*[^1147]

*If the superior decide to chant the Polyeleos, then we chant: "Blessed Is the Man", the first antiphon. At "Lord, I Have Cried", Stichera to 8. Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Given a Sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^1148]

Rome offers thee as a garden with fragrant flowers, O much-suffering Paraskeve, making fragrant the minds of the faithful with virtuous scents, and driving away the foul stench of the passions by grace, O glorious one, beauty of martyrs, praise of virgins, and gulf of wonders!

As costly glory unto all is given by Christ our God, having suffered in Rome and having been in it, O glorious martyr: driving away the devilish wickednesses from men by thy protection; therefore we all call thee blessed, and praise thy holy passions today, O much-suffering Paraskeve!

Preserved by the power of Christ, O all-glorious Paraskeve, thou didst disregard bodily suffering; thou didst commit thyself unto contests with a manly mind, casting off womanly weakness, having been strengthened by hope; and thou didst receive illumination together with the firstborn, O all-praised one!

*Other Stichera, Tone 6:*
Assembling, ye faithful, praising thee with love, we call blessed thy struggles and passions, O martyr Paraskeve; pray unto Christ thy Bridegroom, that we may be delivered from misfortunes and assaults by thy prayers!

Labor-lovingly hast thou drunk the true cup of thy blood, desiring to run after Christ thy Bridegroom, O all-praised martyr Paraskeve, singing a sweet-sounding song as a swallow unto Christ God, the Creator of all, concerning those who celebrate thy memory!

Thy parents, honoring the passions of Christ, named thee after the renowned day; and having been reared, thou didst dare unto the passions, and having endured wounds, rejoicing in prison as in a bright bridal chamber, thou didst cry aloud: Never shall I be separated from Thee, O Lord;
=== END LEAF p292 === but send me Thy help and save me, for the sake of Thy great mercy!

*Glory, Tone 2:*
At the right hand of the Savior, as David sings, the queen stood, and thy soul was as if adorned with golden garments; and thou didst present thyself as a sacrifice unto thy Lord, as fragrant incense thou didst offer the blood of thy flesh, O all-praised martyr Paraskeve: pray unto Christ God our Master for us, that He may save our souls!

*Now and ever, Theotokion, or Cross-Theotokion: "Seeing Thee Crucified" (see fol. 87).[^1149]*

*If thou sing the Polyeleos, sing the Resurrection Theotokion: "Who Would Not Call Thee Blessed?" Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. And the Readings:*

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 43):*[^1150]
Thus says the Lord: All the nations are gathered together, and the princes shall be gathered out of them. Who will declare these things among them? Or who will declare to you the things from the beginning? Let them bring their witnesses, and let them be justified, and let them give ear, and let them declare the truth. Be ye My witnesses, and I also am a witness, says the Lord God, and the servant whom I have chosen, that ye may know, and believe Me, and understand that I am He: before Me there was no other God, neither shall there be after Me. I am God, and beside Me there is no Savior. I have declared, and have saved; I have rebuked, and there was no strange god among you. Ye are My witnesses, and I am the Lord God, even from the beginning I am; and there is none that can deliver out of My hands: I will work, and who shall turn it back? Thus says the Lord God, Who redeems you, the Holy One of Israel.

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Chapter 3):*[^1151]
The souls of the righteous are in the hand of God, and no torment shall touch them. In the eyes of the foolish they seemed to die, and their departure was taken for misery, and their going from us to be utter destruction; but they are in peace. For though they be punished in the sight of men, yet is their hope full of immortality. And having been a little chastened, they shall be greatly rewarded; for God tested them, and found them worthy for Himself. As gold in the furnace has He tried them, and received them as a burnt offering sacrifice. And in the time of their visitation they shall shine, and run to and fro like sparks among the stubble. They shall judge the nations, and have dominion over the peoples, and their Lord shall reign...
=== END LEAF p293 === and their Lord shall reign over them unto the ages. They that put their trust in Him shall understand the truth, and such as be faithful in love shall abide with Him; for grace and mercy is to His venerable ones, and visitation to His elect.

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Chapter 5):*[^1152]
The righteous live unto the ages, their reward also is in the Lord, and the care of them is with the Most High. Therefore shall they receive a kingdom of glory, and a diadem of beauty from the hand of the Lord; for with His right hand shall He cover them, and with His arm shall He protect them. He shall take His zeal for complete armor, and make the creature His weapon for the revenge of His enemies. He shall put on righteousness as a breastplate, and unfeigned judgment instead of a helmet. He shall take holiness for an invincible shield; His severe wrath shall He sharpen for a sword, and the world shall fight with Him against the unwise. Then shall the right-aiming thunderbolts go abroad, and from the clouds, as from a well-drawn bow, shall they fly to the mark. And hailstones full of wrath shall be cast as out of a stone bow, and the water of the sea shall rage against them, and the floods shall cruelly drown them. Yea, a mighty wind shall stand up against them, and like a storm shall blow them away: thus iniquity shall lay waste the whole earth, and ill dealing shall overthrow the thrones of the mighty. Hear therefore, O ye kings, and understand; learn, ye that be judges of the ends of the earth. Give ear, ye that rule the people, and glory in the multitude of nations: for power is given you of the Lord, and sovereignty from the Most High.

*At the Litiya, Stichera, Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, [O Life-Bearing Cross]" (Ра́дꙋйся):*[^1153]
Rejoice, O ewe-lamb of the Lord, having successfully likened thyself unto the honorable and blessed passion of the Immortal One upon the Cross, Who destroyed the passions of the world; O fountain pouring forth wonders as waters, watering the withered hearts of the infirm by the bestowal of healings, O most rich passion-bearer! Thou didst destroy the weak arrogance of the idols; having inherited the habitation of the heavenly bridal chamber, pray unto Christ to grant our souls peace and great mercy!

Where the dwelling of those who rejoice is, where the unwaning light is, in the house of the living God, in the midst of the marvelous tabernacle, where the choir of those who celebrate purely with gladness is, having a bright, slumberless lamp: therefore delighting in the transcendent Godhead, and shining richly from thence with splendors, pray unto Christ to grant our souls peace and great mercy!
=== END LEAF p294 === Beholding Paraskeve suffering in the flesh for the sake of the Creator, and being strengthened by His power, the heavenly hosts said: Behold, even virgins upon earth have glorified the Lord! And men, seized with trembling, were amazed, and marveling, glorified with hymns, saying: O what a wonder! A virgin has vanquished the enemy, and prays unto the merciful Master for the salvation of the world, that He may grant peace and great mercy!

Rejoicing, thou didst stand before the governor, O valiant martyr Paraskeve, clearly preaching the faith. Thou didst manfully endure many floggings; having put the torturer to shame, thou wast betrothed unto Christ. Standing before Him, pray for those who honor thee, and who celebrate thy memory with faith!

Rejoice, O all-valiant ewe-lamb, who wast offered as a whole-burnt sacrifice unto the Lamb and Shepherd, being consumed with fire, having been kindled as with fire of love for Christ thy Bridegroom! Having quenched the flame of the priestly service of the idols, and preserved the slumberless lamp of the soul, and having martyrlike vanquished the dark hosts of the demons by the splendors of wonders, thou didst enter with Christ into the heavenly bridal chamber: pray for those who celebrate thy memory, that He may grant us peace and great mercy!

*Glory, Tone 6:*
The deceitful enemy was put to shame, being vanquished by a virgin, even as he made the foremother bold unto sin. For Christ God, the Word of the Father, born from a virgin without change and ineffably as He alone knows, destroyed the curse of Adam and Eve, having worthily crowned the martyr Paraskeve; and for her sake He grants cleansing unto the world, and great mercy.

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "As One Valiant among Martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^1154]
Having stained for thyself the garment of salvation with purple dye from thy blood, O all-praised one, and having been made white by the Spirit, thou wast betrothed unto the Lord, the immortal King, Who preserves thee undefiled and incorrupt unto ages of ages in the heavenly chambers, as a virgin exceedingly adorned and incorrupt!

*Stichos: Unto the saints that are in His earth, the Lord hath been marvelous in them; He hath wrought all His desires in them.[^1155]*

Having approached wounds, and wrestled with fire, and endured bodily scourgings and the boiling of caldrons, thou didst valiantly bear them, and wast unconquered in mind, and didst not sacrifice unto graven images; but having bowed...
=== END LEAF p295 === thy neck unto God, through severance by the sword, bearing a crown thou didst ascend unto the heavens!

*Stichos: Patiently I waited for the Lord, and He gave ear unto me, and heard my supplication.[^1156]*

Stained with drops of blood, thou didst shine forth manifestly as the sun, O all-praised one, and by grace didst drive away the darkness of godlessness, O martyr Paraskeve; and thou didst enlighten the faithful who honor thy valiant struggles, and thy bright, light-bearing, and all-saving memory!

*Glory, Tone 8:*
Thus says Paraskeve unto the torturer: O hostile and ungodly governor, why dost thou rage against the Christians? Neither have I sought a kingdom from thee, nor have I destroyed thy city; but with foolish words thou thinkest to persuade me. I regard thee not, nor thy senseless words: for I spare not my flesh for the sake of my Christ, because He loves me, and grants me a kingdom; unto Him shall I go, and His bride shall I be. And He shall save me from the hands of mine enemies, and shall grant me eternal life!

*Now and ever, Theotokion, or Cross-Theotokion, Tone 8, Podoben: "O Most Marvelous Wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^1157]
I cannot endure, O Child, seeing Thee asleep upon the Tree, Who grantest wakefulness unto all, that unto those who of old fell asleep with the sleep of perdition through the fruit of transgression, Thou mightest give divine and saving wakefulness, cried aloud the weeping Virgin, whom we magnify!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*[^1158]
The all-wise and all-praised martyr of Christ, Paraskeve, having received manly strength and cast off womanly weakness, vanquished the devil and put the torturer to shame, crying aloud and saying: Come, cut my body with the sword and burn it with fire, for rejoicing I go unto Christ my Bridegroom! Through her prayers, O Christ God, save our souls!

*Now and ever, Theotokion, or Cross-Theotokion.*
*At Matins, at "God Is the Lord", the Troparion of the Saint twice; Glory, Now and ever, Theotokion or Cross-Theotokion.*
*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily Anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^1159]
Having loved the Bridegroom, the Creator and God, thou didst leave the corruptible bridegroom, O honorable martyr Paraskeve; and rejoicing, thou didst endure torments, tearings, and fiery torture. Therefore also bearing a crown, thou hast ascended unto the divine bridal chamber, entreating remission of sins for all. *(Twice)*
=== END LEAF p296 === *Glory, Now and ever, Theotokion:*
O all-immaculate Virgin who didst bear the transcendent God, together with the passion-bearer unceasingly pray unto Him to grant us remission of transgressions and amendment of life before the end, who sing unto thee with faith and love as is due, O only all-hymned one!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Lifted Up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йся):*[^1160]
Having loved Christ Thy Bridegroom, and having brightly adorned Thy lamp, thou didst blossom forth in virtues, O glorious one; therefore thou hast ascended into the heavenly bridal chamber, and having received the crown of suffering, deliver us from misfortunes, O Paraskeve, who celebrate Thy memory! *(Twice)*

*Glory, Now and ever, Theotokion:*
Under thy protection, O Theotokos, a divine protection, do I, humble one, flee; falling down I pray: Have mercy, O most pure one, for my sins have exceeded my head, and I fear the torment, O Mistress, and tremble; make supplication, O pure one, unto thy Son, to deliver me from them!

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "[Having Received] the Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^1161]
Having been divinely betrothed unto the Lord, thou didst offer blood and slaying unto Him as a gift, O virgin passion-bearer Paraskeve, and thou hast worthily attained the divine bridal chamber, continually filled with ineffable illumination. Therefore celebrating thy holy and honorable memory spiritually, we glorify the Savior, and faithfully cry aloud unto thee: Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love! *(Twice)*

*Glory, Now and ever, Theotokion:*
As the Virgin and only one among women, who without seed didst bear God in the flesh, all generations of men call thee blessed; for the fire of the Godhead dwelt in thee, and as an infant thou dost suckle the Creator and Lord. Therefore we, the race of angels and of men, worthily glorify thy most holy nativity, and in unison cry aloud unto thee: Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who worship thy most holy nativity with faith!

*Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of the 4th Tone. Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
*God is marvelous in His saints, the God of Israel. Stichos: In the congregations bless ye God, the Lord from the springs of Israel.[^1162]*
*Let every breath praise the Lord. Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 62.[^1163]*
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 4:*
Numbered with the ranks on high, O glorious one, having left the world...
=== END LEAF p297 === and having desired Christ, O all-honorable Paraskeve, entreat peace and great mercy for us!

*Canon of the Theotokos with the Heirmos to 6: Tone 8, Podoben: "Having Traversed the Water" (Во́дꙋ проше́дъ); and of the Saint, two Canons to 8. Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^1164]
I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I shall pour forth a word unto the Queen and Mother, and I shall appear radiant keeping festival, and rejoicing I shall sing her wonders!

*Troparia:*
Illumined from on high with divine light, drive away the darkness of my foolishness, and through thy prayers, O martyr Paraskeve, grant me grace to sing the multitude of thy wonders!

Brighter than the sun, truly thou didst shine forth as the morning, having become the establishment of the Church of Christ; with the rays of thy struggles, O martyr Paraskeve, thou hast illumined the ends of the world with thy wonders!

Having loved Christ the immortal Bridegroom, so that by faith thou wast shown forth a partaker of His passions, thou hast received the eternal kingdom and an incorruptible crown, O glorious martyr Paraskeve!

*Theotokion:*
O all-immaculate Virgin who didst bear Christ, and didst carry Him in thy womb, truly named: dissolve the darkness of my soul, and enlighten me, that I may sing unto thee!

*Another Canon, Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^1165]
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, for He alone has been gloriously glorified!

*Troparia:*
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who has glorified the memory of the martyr, and brings earthly choirs to praise today!

Thou didst despise glory, taking no heed of wealth, O honorable martyr Paraskeve; thou didst surrender thyself unto suffering!

The Master desired thy beauty, O martyr, and led thee into the heavenly bridal chamber!

*Theotokion:*
The race of Adam, having found paradise through thee beyond understanding, O Theotokos, worthily praises thee![^1166]

*Katavasia: "I Shall Open My Mouth".*
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^1167]
The bow of the mighty is weakened, and the weak have girded themselves with strength; therefore my heart is established in the Lord!

*Troparia:*
Beaten unmercifully with rods, the ever-memorable martyr Paraskeve rejoiced when shins and bones were crushed together, crying aloud: Nothing shall separate me from Thy love, O Christ;
=== END LEAF p298 === O Christ!

The mouth of the ungodly speaking iniquity against the righteous, and the boastful tongue hast thou stopped, O martyr Paraskeve, crying aloud and preaching the Savior!

By the sprinkling of thy blood thou didst quench the fire of godlessness, O much-suffering martyr Paraskeve, and didst enlighten the minds of the faithful!

*Theotokion:*
The voices of Isaiah are piously fulfilled, for a virgin hath borne in the flesh Christ the Giver of life, the Savior of our souls!

*Another Heirmos [Tone 8]:*[^1168]
Thou art the confirmation of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of those in darkness, and my spirit sings of Thee!

*Troparia:*
Thou art my confirmation, O Lord, cried the martyr, Thou art also my protection, and unto Thee have I fled, O King of peace!

Thou didst appear at the judgment seat, having a manly soul, and thou didst vanquish the enemy who was cowardly, O all-praised one!

Heal the pupils of my soul, O all-praised martyr, through thy prayers, and direct my mind!

*Theotokion:*
Grant us help through thy prayers, O most pure one, repelling the counsels of cruel afflictions!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "[Having Received] the Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^1169]
Having dried up the streams of ungodliness with the torrents of thy blood, O all-praised Paraskeve, martyr of Christ, by the rain of grace thou dost water the spiritual furrows, wherein thou causest the ear of faith to spring forth. Moreover, O glorious one, even after death thou wast shown forth pouring forth life because of thy suffering, O all-praised passion-bearer! Pray unto Christ God to grant great mercy unto our souls!

*Glory:*
*Another Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily Anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^1170]
Having adorned the purity of the soul with fasting, by the rays of suffering thou dost enlighten the faithful, O much-suffering martyr Paraskeve. Therefore celebrating thy holy and light-bearing day with faith, we piously cry aloud: As thou hast boldness before the Lord, pray that we may be saved!

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
O pure one, by thy divine childbirth thou hast renewed the mortal nature of earthborn men that was decayed in passions, and hast raised all from death unto the life of incorruption. Therefore as is due we all call thee blessed, O Virgin exceedingly glorified, even as thou didst foretell![^1171]

*Cross-Theotokion:*
=== END LEAF p299 === When Thy most pure Mother beheld Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Word of God, weeping maternally she spoke: What is this new and strange wonder, O my Son? How dost Thou, the Life of all, taste death, wishing to give life unto the dead, as Thou art compassionate?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^1172]
Sitting in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, Jesus, the transcendent God, came upon a light cloud, and with an incorruptible hand saved those who cry aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!

*Troparia:*
Wounded by love of Christ, by thy strength thou didst put to death the hostile armies, and dost pour forth ever-living grace; because of thy passions thou hast received retribution, O Paraskeve, beauty of the martyrs of Christ!

Having surpassed all things through love of Christ thy Bridegroom, and having hated carnal love, thou didst firmly endure painful sufferings, O martyr Paraskeve, crying aloud: Separate me not from Thy desire, O Christ, bring to an end the struggle of martyrdom!

Having cast away all the light of the world, thou didst cleave unto God alone, O martyr Paraskeve; and having endured the pains of suffering, O manly bride of God...

*Theotokion:*
The Lord came down as dew upon a fleece into thy womb, even as the prophet spoke of old, O all-immaculate one, Whom thou didst bear in two natures; unto Whom we cry aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^1173]

*Another Heirmos [Tone 8]:*[^1174]
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation, I understood Thy works, and I glorified Thy Godhead!

*Troparia:*
Having heard the evangelical commandments, O martyr Paraskeve, thou didst glorify His Godhead, O Lord!

Thou didst not offer sacrifice at all unto the abominable demons, but gavest thyself as a living sacrifice unto the Lord!

Standing firmly at the judgment seat, the martyr cried aloud: With Christ my Bridegroom am I crucified, and I shall reign with Him!

*Theotokion:*
Grant cleansing of our transgressions, for Thou art sinless, and give peace unto Thy people, as Thou art good, through the prayers of her who gave Thee birth!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^1175]
Thou, my Lord, hast come as a light into the world, a holy light, turning from dark ignorance those who praise Thee with faith!

*Troparia:*
The unwaning light dwelt in thy soul, that by virtue...
=== END LEAF p300 === thou shinest, illuminating those who unceasingly glorify thee.

A torrent of ungodly atheism hast thou dried up, O martyr Paraskeve, by streams of blood unjustly shed, and thou didst extinguish the fire of delusion.

Having a clear mind, thou wast vouchsafed the prophetic gift, and what was to happen thou didst openly foretell unto all, O much-suffering Paraskeve.

*Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, heal the wickedness of my soul, who didst ineffably bear God the Word; therefore all generations call thee blessed.

*Another [Canon]. Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^1176]
Rising early at dawn we cry aloud unto Thee, O Lord: Save us! For Thou art our God; besides Thee we know no other.

*Troparia:*
Rising early at dawn, the martyr Paraskeve cried aloud: Nothing shall separate me from the love of Christ!

Deeming the manly martyr would grow weak, the crafty one showed himself a laughingstock.

Grant me illumination, O all-praised one, and illumine my much-troubled mind by thy prayers!

*Theotokion:*
Cast down the unceasing tempest of passions, O thou who didst bear Christ, the Helmsman and Lord!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^1177]
I will sacrifice unto Thee with a voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, having been cleansed from the demonic blood by the blood which, for mercy's sake, flowed from Thy side!

*Troparia:*
Having shone forth from Rome like a radiant sun, O holy martyr, driving away demonic darkness from men by thy supplications, O much-suffering Paraskeve!

Those who come and touch the shrine of thy relics receive healings; those with flow of blood and the lepers are cleansed, and the demonic legions flee at the invocation of thy holy name!

Thou didst rise early at dawn unto Christ, the unwaning Sun, and with His splendors thou, O divinely wise Paraskeve, didst illuminate thy soul and heart, and rejoicing hast passed over unto the Trinity's Light!

*Theotokion:*
Heal the passions of my soul, O all-immaculate one, who didst bear the Fountain of dispassion, and drive away the torment unceasingly troubling my heart!

*Another Heirmos [Tone 8]:*[^1178]
Grant me a robe of light, Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a robe, O most merciful Christ our God!

*Troparia:*
A robe for me from heaven coming, O Lord, cried Paraskeve...
=== END LEAF p301 === Who clothest the heavens with clouds, O most merciful One!

*Troparia:*
Even as the Lord gave ear unto Daniel out of the pit, so also unto thee praying in prison, He grants healing!

Thou didst cast down the persecutor's pride, and remaining unharmed thyself, thou didst dare unto torment!

*Theotokion:*
The Word of God showed Thee unto earthly beings as a heavenly ladder, for through Thee He came down unto us!

*Kontakion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва дне́сь):*[^1179]
Having offered a holy and blameless martyrdom, as a most precious dowry, unto the immortal Bridegroom Christ, thou didst gladden the angelic choir, and didst overcome demonic wiles; therefore with faith we honor thee honorably, O much-suffering martyr Paraskeve!

*Ikos:*[^1180]
Who can declare the mighty acts of the Lord, or who can recount thy sufferings and virtues, O martyr? Or what tongue can worthily sing of them? Therefore I also, unworthy, attempt to sing hymns, and to praise and glorify the Good Author of life, Who gave thee strength in the stadium not to fear the torturers, confessing the true God. Therefore with faith and love we honor thee, O much-suffering martyr Paraskeve!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^1181]
Thou Who didst save Thy Abrahamic children in the fire and didst slay the Chaldeans, whom justice justly ensnared: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord, the God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
O dove having golden wings and protected by the weapon of the Cross, having driven away the Hagarenes who drew near unto thy city by night, saving the faithful from slaughter, O martyr Paraskeve, who singest: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned God of our fathers!

Having endured manifold afflictions for Christ's sake, O martyr Paraskeve, thou didst find with Him painless rest, and rejoicing dost cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned God of our fathers!

How invincible was thy understanding, O venerable Paraskeve, for thou didst not fear the torments from the chorus of the rulers of the torturers! Struggling with them thou didst pass away as a minister of the mysteries of divine glory, singing: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Blessed is the fruit of thy blessed womb, Whom the heavenly hosts bless...
=== END LEAF p302 === and the assemblies of men praise, as He hath delivered us from the ancient curse, O blessed one, who sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Another Heirmos [Tone 8]:*[^1182]
The Hebrew children having entered Babylon, of old trampled underfoot the flame of the furnace through faith in the Trinity, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
Thou didst appear beyond understanding in wonders, O divine martyr, in the youth of virginity having conquered the persecutor, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Thou didst stop the god-abhorred mouths of the babblers; standing in the midst of the tribunal, thou didst confess God, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Forgetting womanly weakness, having been manfully strengthened with endurance, suffering thou didst cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Incarnate from a virginal womb, Thou didst appear for our salvation; therefore knowing Thy Mother to be the Theotokos, gratefully we cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^1183]
The pious children in the furnace the childbearing of the Theotokos saved; then indeed prefigured, but now in deed fulfilled, it stirs up the whole world to chant unto Thee: Praise the Lord, ye works, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
Illumined with the divine splendors of the spiritual Sun, O all-praised martyr Paraskeve, thou didst pass through the night of atheism, chanting with harmony of soul: Praise the Lord, ye works, and highly exalt Him unto the ages!

As a lamb, as an undefiled heifer, as a divine dove, as a voluntary slaughter thou wast led, O martyr Paraskeve, unto thy Creator, and as a blameless sacrifice thou didst offer thyself, with one accord chanting: Praise the Lord, ye works, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

A radiant robe for thyself from blood by martyrdom hast thou put on, O noble martyr Paraskeve, having truly stripped off the corrupt man with sins, joyfully chanting: Praise the Lord, ye works, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
After thee women ran, drawn by the sweet-smelling myrrh, knowing thee, O all-pure bride of God, alone unwedded, to have brought forth from thy womb the only-begotten Son, Christ God; and with thee they truly reign, hymning Him unto the ages!

*Another Heirmos [Tone 8]:*[^1184]
=== END LEAF p303 === The King of heaven, Whom the angelic hosts hymn, praise ye and highly exalt unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
The King, Christ God, Whom the virgin-martyr Paraskeve confessed, singing: Praise ye and highly exalt Him unto the ages!

For love of the Divine Trinity thou didst extinguish the fire of atheism, crying aloud: Highly exalt Christ unto the ages!

By thy struggles toward God Thou didst cast down the prince of darkness, chanting: Highly exalt Christ unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*
Quench the sparks of the adversaries' assault against us, O Virgin, that we may hymn the Lord and highly exalt Him unto the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^1185]
Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy in spirit, being enlightened, and let the nature of the bodiless minds celebrate, honoring the sacred festival of the Mother of God, and let it cry aloud: Rejoice, O all-blessed Theotokos, pure Ever-Virgin!

*Troparia:*
Thou hast taken up thy dwelling in the heavenly bridal chamber, O all-venerable virgin Paraskeve; and spiritually delighting in the unseen beauties, having received ineffable brilliance, enlighten those who sing and magnify thee!

A crown didst thou receive upon thy head, O valiant-minded Paraskeve, for having finished the course and kept the faith unshaken, the ranks of the righteous joyfully received thee; therefore we unceasingly call thee blessed.

Thou pourest forth drops of healings unto those in need as from a fountain, O all-rich martyr Paraskeve, stopping the flows of diseases and driving away the burning heat of passions, watering the hearts of the pious unto the fruitful bearing of divine deeds.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown forth as the receptacle of Wisdom surpassing the highest mind, O all-immaculate Maiden, an animate throne and door; therefore loving thee as Queen, the virgins were brought in after thee, O Maiden of God!

*Another [Canon]. Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^1186]
Truly we confess thee to be the Theotokos, saved through thee, O pure Virgin, magnifying thee with the bodiless choirs.

*Troparia:*
Truly according to thy name wast thou called, O Paraskeve,[^1187] being conformed to the passions of Christ, dancing with the heavenly hosts.

Thou hast attained the end of martyrdom through thy many sufferings and pains, glorifying Christ on high with the angels, O all-venerable one!

Having boldness before the Savior and Master, pray for those who sing of...
=== END LEAF p304 === thy memory, O glorious martyr Paraskeve!

*Theotokion:*
With hymns we sing unto Thee, O full of God's grace, and unceasingly offer unto Thee: Rejoice! For thou hast poured forth gladness unto us all.

*Exapostilarion. Podoben: "Give Ear, Ye Women" (Жены̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите):*[^1188]
As a most beautiful rose from a thorn-tree didst thou blossom forth from a root, O virgin Paraskeve; by the sprinkling of sacred martyrdom, O martyr, thou didst dye thyself with blood; and now, save from afflictions us who lovingly celebrate thy divine memory!

*Glory, Both now. Theotokion:*
Standing around thee, O divinely wise virgins, O Mary, Bearer of God, as around a Queen by birth, they were brought unto thy Son as brides, O all-pure one, as unto the King of all; Whom do thou implore for us, O Virgin Maiden Mother!

*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As One Valiant among Martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^1189]
Virginity incorrupt, martyrdom all-marvelous, hast thou offered unto Christ, O all-venerable virgin Paraskeve; thou didst cast down the arrogance of godless delusion by the power of the Cross and by manly understanding; therefore the whole Church of Christ celebrates thy holy memory.

At thy suffering, O wise virgin, and resistance and unconquerability, the angelic choirs, O blessed Paraskeve, marveled: how by a manly encounter thou didst shatter the invisible enemy, and richly received the crown of victory from the hand of the Giver of life.

Burnt with fire, by fiery wisdom thou didst extinguish natural flame, and keeping the soul's lamp unharmed, thou didst enter with Christ thy Bridegroom into the heavenly bridal chamber; having put the passions to death with marvelous sparks, like a sufferer thou didst conquer the demonic legions.

*Other Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joy of the Heavenly Ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^1190]
Into the heavenly courts hast thou entered, O martyr Paraskeve, by the blood of thy sufferings; in the vision of the angelic choirs thou hast been numbered equally, O all-praised one!

Thou didst put the persecutor to shame, taking no heed of torments, and thou didst not fear the roaring of the beasts, having Christ as Thy helper, O all-praised one!

Rejoicing didst thou run, O all-praised Paraskeve, hastening to receive the end of martyrdom, O all-praised virgin; and having been betrothed unto Christ there, remember us who ever honor thee!
=== END LEAF p305 === *Glory, Tone 6:*
With hymns let the martyr of Christ be honored, for all praise is fitting for her; for having woven her crown with praises, being unyielding and firm, she cast down the great malice of the torturers. Therefore, suffering in pains she cried aloud: Be Thou my help, O Lord, and forsake me not!

*Both now, Theotokion, or Cross-Theotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having Laid Aside All Earthly Hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*[^1191]
When of old the undefiled ewe-lamb and spotless Lady beheld her Lamb upon the tree of the Cross, weeping maternally she cried out and, marveling, shouted aloud: O sweetest Child, what is this new and glorious sight? How hath the ungrateful synagogue delivered Thee over unto the judgment seat of Pilate, and condemns unto death the Life of all? Yet I hymn Thine ineffable condescension, O Word!

*At the Aposticha, Glory, Tone 4:*
