Holding the Cross as a sovereign weapon in thy hands, O martyr Paraskeve: faith as a shield, hope as a breastplate, and love as a bow: having manfully conquered the torment of the persecutors, thou didst abolish the wiles of the demons by divine power. And being beheaded in the head, thou dancest before Christ; cease not praying for our souls!

*Both now, Theotokion, or Cross-Theotokion, Tone 4, Podoben: "As One Valiant among Martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^1192]
When the ewe-lamb and Thy Mother beheld Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Lord, she was astonished, and cried out: What is this sight, O beloved Son? Have the ungrateful and lawless people rendered these things unto Thee, who enjoyed Thy many wonders? But glory to Thine ineffable condescension, O Master!

*Great Doxology. And the Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes from the first Canon, Ode 3; and of the second Canon, Ode 6.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: God is marvelous in His saints, the God of Israel.*[^1193] *Verse: In the churches bless ye God, the Lord from the fountains of Israel.*[^1194] *Epistle to the Corinthians, composite reading [1 Cor. 181].*[^1195] *Alleluia, With patience I waited for the Lord, and He gave ear unto me.*[^1196] *Gospel of Luke, composite reading [Luke 33].*[^1197] *Communion Verse: In everlasting memory:*[^1198]
=== END LEAF p306 === *In the same month, on the 29th day. The Holy Venerable-Martyr Anastasia the Roman; and our Venerable Father Abramius the Recluse:*[^1199]

*At "Lord, I Have Cried", Stichera to the Martyr, Tone 8, Podoben: "What Shall We Call You, O Holy Ones" (Что̀ ва́съ нарече́мъ):*[^1200]
O martyr and all-praised passion-bearer, having cast down carnal passions through temperance, by suffering thou didst put to death the serpent enemy; having astonished the angels by thy pains, having gladdened mortals by thy sufferings, adornment of monastics, vessel of virginity: pray that our souls may be saved and enlightened!

What shall we call thee, O glorious one? A bride of Christ having shone forth with the beauties of virginity; a chosen daughter of the heavenly Jerusalem; fellow-dweller and companion of the angels; one who delights in the spiritual bridal chamber, O much-suffering Anastasia, adornment of monastics: pray that our souls may be saved and enlightened!

Thou didst endure the uprooting of thy teeth, the cutting off of thy hands, and feet, and breasts, severed limb by limb by lawless torturers, gazing upon the beauty of the Bridegroom, and enduring pains beyond nature, O much-suffering Anastasia, incorrupt bride of Christ: pray that our souls may be saved and enlightened!

*Other Stichera to the Venerable, in the same Tone, Podoben: "O Most Marvelous Wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^1201]
O most marvelous wonder! O divinely wise father Abramius, having become like unto Abraham in spirit, thou wast an emigrant from thy fatherland, O blessed one; having cast away the desires of the flesh, and having joyfully shut thy body in a small cell, thou didst wing thy soul unto heaven, where thou hast visibly acquired thy dwelling.

O venerable father Abramius, having acquired a soul like Abraham according to thy name, thou didst endure temptations, strengthened by divine faith and united unto God by love, thou didst inherit the land of promise, adorned with the splendors of virtues; therefore rejoicing, we celebrate thy memory!

O venerable father Abramius, her who was cruelly swallowed up by the deceptive serpent and slipped into the pit of destruction, by divine providence...[^1202]
=== END LEAF p307 === thou didst draw out and present unto God Who saved her; at whose repentance all were astonished, piously glorifying the all-good Lord!

*Glory, Both now. Theotokion:*
Rejoice, bearer of the Divine Light, all-radiant candlestick, most holy bride of God! A light hast thou shone forth unto us from thy most pure womb, enlightening the ends of the world, and sanctifying by thy goodness; rejoice, beginning of our salvation, O pure one; rejoice, awesome hearing and speech of those who hope in thee!

*Cross-Theotokion:*
What is this visible sight which is seen by mine eyes, O Master? Thou Who holdest all creation, lifted up upon the Tree, and dost Thou die, Who givest life unto all? The Theotokos weeping spoke when she beheld Him Who ineffably shone forth from her, God and Man, being lifted up upon the Cross!

*Troparion of the Saint, Tone 4:*[^1203]
Thy lamb, O Jesus, Anastasia, cries aloud with a great voice: Thee, my Bridegroom, do I love, and seeking Thee I endure suffering, and am crucified with Thee, and am buried with Thee in Thy baptism, and I suffer for Thy sake that I may reign in Thee, and I die for Thee that I may live with Thee; but as a blameless sacrifice accept me who have sacrificed myself with love unto Thee! By her prayers, as Thou art merciful, save Thou our souls!

*Glory, to the Venerable, Tone 8:*[^1204]
In thee, O father, was that which is according to the image saved with certainty; for having taken up the cross, thou didst follow Christ, and by thy deeds didst teach how to overlook the flesh, for it passes away, but to care for the soul, an immortal thing; therefore thy spirit rejoices together with the angels, O venerable Abramius!

*Canon of the Venerable [Martyr]. Whose Acrostic is: Thy manly pains, O martyr, will I praise. A Composition of Joseph. Tone 8.*[^1205]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^1206]
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, for He alone has been gloriously glorified!

*Troparia:*
Unto those who honor this thy light-bearing memory, O divinely wise one, grant me illumination, driving away the darkness of my soul!

All from swaddling clothes didst thou commit thyself unto thy Creator, and with the fire of temperance thou didst consume bodily passions!

Unto the height of martyrdom didst thou ascend, O martyr, not having spared the flesh, and of spiritual bridal chambers as a virgin wast thou vouchsafed!

*Theotokion:*
=== END LEAF p308 === *Theotokion:*
Thee as a ladder reaching unto heaven, whereon God was established, having made mortals heavenly, do we honor, O Maiden!

*Another Canon of the Venerable. Whose Acrostic is: Thy all-radiant life do I honor, O blessed one. Joseph. In the same Tone [8].*[^1207]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^1208]
Having traversed the water as on dry land, and having escaped the Egyptian evil, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!

*Troparia:*
Shining with divine splendors, deliver by thy prayers from the darkening of passions those who celebrate thy light-bearing feast, O all-blessed Abramius!

Divine desire ignited thy soul, O blessed one, quenched the burnings of the flesh, and made thee to live immaterially upon the earth.

Having put to death the members which are upon earth by fasting and all kinds of hardship, O wise father, thou wast vouchsafed a better life in the heavens, O God-bearing Abramius!

*Theotokion:*
From thy sacred blood, God the Word of the Father was incarnate beyond nature, O Virgin Mary; Whom do thou implore to put to death the uprisings of my flesh!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^1209]
Thou art the confirmation of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the Light of the darkened, and my spirit sings of Thee!

*Troparia [to Anastasia]:*
Thou didst stand before the judgment seat of the torturer, O glorious one, proclaiming Christ the Creator of all, the Master, and God the Word!

The beauty of outward appearance, being far removed from visible semblance, O glorious one, showed thee forth most fair unto those who beheld thee.

Christ granted thee an inexhaustible wealth of healings, O maiden, Whose voluntary poverty thou didst love.

*Theotokion:*
Consume the furnace of my sins by the fire of thy prayers, O Mother of the Light, bearing unto me the divine dew of forgiveness!

*Another Heirmos [to Abramius]:*[^1210]
O Lord, Creator of the celestial vault and Builder of the Church: do Thou confirm me in Thy love, O Summit of desires, Confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*
Having in every way desired the summit of desires, thou wast outside the world and the flesh, O God-bearer; therefore thou didst receive otherworldly glory and eternal delight, O father!

Thou wast shown forth as a living temple of the all-effecting Spirit, O father, having shut thy body in a cell...
=== END LEAF p309 === O venerable one; therefore thou didst shine forth with the rays of virtues, divinely deified, O blessed Abramius!

*Troparia:*
By the sowing of labors, O venerable one, thou didst raise up a ripe hundredfold ear of virtues, wherewith they are honorably nourished who celebrate thy venerable falling asleep.

*Theotokion:*
He Who is the Cause of all dwelt in thee, wishing to inhabit thee for His excessive mercy, and to save human nature which had slipped beforehand through transgression.

*After Ode 3, Kontakion of Anastasia, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва дне́сь):*[^1211]
Cleansed with the waters of virginity, O venerable one, crowned with the blood of martyrdom, O Anastasia, thou grantest unto those in afflictions healing and salvation of diseases, who draw near from the heart; for Christ grants Thee strength, pouring forth ever-flowing grace.

*Sessional Hymn [to Anastasia], Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom and the Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^1212]
With the dew of temperance thou didst youthfully extinguish the flame of passions, and with the fire of thy blood thou didst burn up all delusion; and unto the Word the Bridegroom as a gift thou didst bring venerable virginity and valiant suffering; therefore into the bridal chamber of glory He led thee who hadst valiantly struggled and trampled the serpent, O much-suffering Anastasia! Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who lovingly honor thy holy memory!

*Glory, to the Venerable, Tone 1, Podoben: "While the Soldiers Were Guarding Thy Tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*[^1213]
Becoming like unto Abraham of old, O father, thou didst remove thyself far from thy kinsmen according to the flesh, obeying the word of our God, O Abramius; therefore having fasted, thou didst show thy soul to be more radiant than the rays of the sun, O divinely wise and all-blessed one!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Guide us into the way of repentance, who have ever turned aside into the trackless paths of evils and angered the all-good Lord, O unwedded, blessed Mary, refuge of despairing men, dwelling-place of God!

*Cross-Theotokion:*
When the undefiled ewe-lamb beheld her Lamb and Shepherd suspended dead upon the Tree, weeping she spoke, maternally crying aloud: How shall I endure Thy descent which is beyond words, O my Son, and Thy voluntary sufferings, O all-good God?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^1214]
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation, I understood...
=== END LEAF p310 === [I understood] Thy works, and I glorified Thy Divinity.

*Troparia:*
The lawless ones condemned thee who hadst borne the most light yoke of Christ from thy youth, O martyr, to bear iron bonds.

The drops of thy blood quenched the coals of polytheism, and the rays of thy miracles burned up the matter of the passions.

Having stripped thy body, the most crafty one did not strip away grace, but rather procured for thee an unrent robe woven from above.

*Theotokion:*
After giving birth thou didst remain an incorrupt Virgin, even as before birthgiving; for thou didst bear a young Infant, Who is known before the ages.

*Another [Canon to Abramius], the same Heirmos:*
Having washed away the defilements of the soul with the labors of thy tears, O father, thou wast a divine and honorable dwelling of the Spirit, O all-blessed one.

*Troparia:*
All-night vigil, unceasing prayer, detachment, unfeigned love, and perfect faith didst thou keep.

To heal passions and drive away spirits didst thou receive grace, O venerable Abramius, as a most true pleaser of God.

*Theotokion:*
Holden by despondency and now submerged in sin, by thy supplication deliver me, O Mother of God, Ever-Virgin Mother.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^1215]
Rising early at dawn we cry aloud unto Thee, O Lord: Save us; for Thou art our God, besides Thee we know no other.

*Troparia:*
Thou wast shown forth unconsumed by material fire, O martyr Anastasia; for the inward fire of divine love bedewed thee.

Having adorned thyself with blows upon thy face, O martyr Anastasia, thou didst strike down the loathsome folly of the enemies.

Suspended upon the tree, O divinely wise Anastasia, by love for thy Bridegroom thou didst prefigure the divine Passion.

*Theotokion:*
We sing of thee, O all-hymned Sovereign Lady Theotokos; for in the flesh thou didst bear God Who is praised above all, O most pure one.

*Another Heirmos [to Abramius]:*[^1216]
Therefore hast Thou cast me off from Thy face, O Never-setting Light, and an alien darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my ways unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee.

*Troparia:*
As a fruitful branch, O father Abramius, thou didst produce clusters of divine virtues, pouring forth the wine of compunction, driving away the drunkenness of passions from souls, and gladdening the hearts of the faithful.
=== END LEAF p311 === *Troparia:*
Diverse temptations did the all-crafty serpent raise up against thee, O father; but him thou didst put to death with the weapon of the Cross, O God-bearer, and by righteous judgment didst receive from God the crown of victory, O divinely wise Abramius.

Having become a holy dwelling of the divine Spirit through faith, O father, thou didst build a sacred church, and having converted the unholy gathering of unholy delusion through instructions, thou didst present them all unto God.

*Theotokion:*
Enlightened by the divine Spirit, O most pure one, the holy prophets proclaimed thee with sacred voices as Virgin Mother and Maiden; from whom God the Word became incarnate beyond cause and word, for the sake of His immeasurable mercy.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^1217]
Grant me a robe of light, O Thou Who deckest Thyself with light as with a garment, O greatly merciful Christ our God!

*Troparia:*
Crushed with wounds, thy body manifested the uprightness of thy conscience toward Christ our God, O passion-bearer.

Hanging on high, enduring wounds, O martyr, and keeping the nobility of thy soul uninjured, O praiseworthy one.

Thou didst endure the extraction of teeth and the cutting off of hands and feet, O much-suffering martyr, looking steadfastly toward Christ thy Bridegroom.

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth to God, the Lover of mankind, O good-loving Sovereign Lady: pray unto Him that we may be delivered from the fire of Gehenna.

*Another Heirmos [to Abramius]:*[^1218]
I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I proclaim my sorrows, for my soul is filled with evils, and my life has drawn near unto Hades, and like Jonah I pray: From corruption, O God, raise me up!

*Troparia:*
Those who were in the darkness of delusion, having found thee, O God-bearer, as a lamp guiding without deceit unto the ways of life, cast off the darkness of ignorance, and were shown forth radiant through divine faith, O Abramius.

Vigilantly entreating God, Thou dost send forth the enlightenment of salvation unto those holden in the night of idolatrous madness, O divinely wise Abramius, and by grace Thou didst show them forth as sons of the light and of the day.

Having completed thy life in stillness, O all-blessed one, through godly hope thou didst cross the billows of this life, and didst reach the tranquil haven of the heavenly Kingdom and divine radiance, O Abramius.

*Theotokion:*
Weighed down by the slumber of despondency, raise me up through thy mediation...
=== END LEAF p312 === O Mother of God, and let me not fall asleep in the death of sin, O Virgin; for I have named thee the protectress and guide of my life.

*Kontakion of Abramius, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва дне́сь):*[^1219]
In the flesh thou wast shown forth upon earth as an angel, and through fasting thou wast like a planted tree, well nourished by the water of temperance, and washing away defilement with the streams of thy tears; therefore thou wast shown forth, O Abramius, as a divine dwelling of the Spirit.

*Ikos:*[^1220]
Despising corruptible things, thou didst receive incorruptible beauties; thou didst hate carnal pleasures, O wise one, having loved purity from youth. Therefore fleeing the worldly bridal chamber, turning away from a noble bride and parents, and desiring only God as a lover, truly loving Him from thy whole soul and heart, O father: therefore thou wast shown forth as a divine vessel of virginity and purity, O Abramius, of the Spirit.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^1221]
The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trampled the flame and turned the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!

*Troparia:*
Where the sound of those keeping festival is, there dost thou dance, O passion-bearer, singing with the virgins unto God the Creator: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!

Beholding thy limbs crushed and enduring the tearing off of thy nails, thou didst offer thyself as a sacrifice unto God, singing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!

Thou wast shown forth as a divinely wise vine, thy hands and feet being cut off like branches, and spiritually pouring forth wine for us, gladdening hearts and putting away the drunkenness of passions.

*Theotokion:*
Behold, the Undefiled One clothed Himself in the whole human nature except for sin, and came forth from thy womb bearing flesh, O pure one: entreat Him to save those who honor thee with faith.

*Another [Canon to Abramius], the same Heirmos:*
That thou mightest obtain the Kingdom on high and ineffable glory, O wise one, as one sensible and prudent thou didst wisely despise glory that is trampled underfoot and corruptible.

*Troparia:*
When the crafty wolf deceitfully tore thy ewe-lamb, then didst thou shatter his jaws, O wise one; and her, as an excellent shepherd, didst thou lead back unto life...[^1222]
=== END LEAF p313 === thou didst seek, and didst find, and taking her upon thy shoulders as a shepherd, O all-blessed one, thou didst lead her into the fold of repentance.

*Theotokion:*
Thou alone didst bear One of the Trinity in two essences, yet seen in one hypostasis, O Virgin, unto Whom we sing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^1223]
The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the pious; but having seen them saved by a higher power, he cried aloud unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
Youthfully hast thou struggled, O all-praised passion-bearer, and raised trophies of victory against delusion; for thou didst endure the deprivation of hands and feet, and the uprooting of thy breasts and teeth, O martyr, singing in joy: Ye people, supremely exalt Christ unto ages!

Radiant as the sun with the brightness of virginity and the splendors of martyrdom didst thou shine forth, and didst enlighten the world with the beam-like radiance of thy endurance, O passion-bearer, crying aloud: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Christ unto ages!

Defiled with passions is my soul, O bride of God, and blackened by the assaults of the serpent; by thy prayers cleanse it from evils, and with thy radiant illumination enlighten me, O martyr Anastasia, to cry aloud: Ye priests, sing; ye people, supremely exalt Christ unto ages!

*Theotokion:*
Desiring thee, the pure and undefiled Virgin, she kept both body and soul undefiled, having turned to ashes the burning of passions through endurance, and suffered the trial of many torments; and now in the heavenly bridal chambers she rejoices with thee unto ages.

*Another Heirmos [to Abramius]:*[^1224]
Having become conquerors of the tyrant and of the flame by Thy grace, cleaving earnestly unto Thy commandments, the children cried aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*
Into the pit of destruction did the malevolent serpent cast thy pure dove; but her didst thou draw forth, O father, in all-wise manner; bless, hymn the works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto ages![^1225]

Walking in this life, O father Abramius, and after thy falling asleep, thou wast shown forth as a physician of the sick through the divine grace of the Spirit; bless, hymn the works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto ages!
=== END LEAF p314 === *Troparia:*
Having flourished in guilelessness, O blessed one, and in righteousness, temperance, and faith, thou wast numbered with the angels, O venerable one; bless, hymn the works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto ages!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown forth as the all-holy dwelling of the living stream, drinking wherefrom we who were dead inherit life, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^1226]
Heaven was astonished thereat, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God was manifest unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the hierarchies of angels and men magnify thee as the Theotokos.

*Troparia:*
Suspended upon the tree, thou didst prefigure the blessed Passion of God the Word, and didst endure the cutting off of thy hands and feet, the uprooting of teeth, the cutting out of the tongue and breasts, O pure Anastasia, glory of monastics and adornment of martyrs!

As a bride adorned with beauty, betrothed unto the Bridegroom by beneficent sufferings, thou hast taken up thy dwelling within the radiant bridal chambers as one chosen, bearing the lamp of virginity, and now thou reignest, shining with Him Who lives unto ages, O Anastasia!

Thy sufferings distill sweetness, taking away the bitterness of sin; and thy shrine pours forth rivers of healings, drowning all severe passions and diseases, unto the glory of the Savior, O honorable Anastasia, for those who fittingly glorify thee.

The women struggled valiantly, strengthened by Thy Cross, O all-powerful Christ, and courageously crushed the multifaceted head of the enemy, O Word of God; and having obtained a joyful dwelling, they received marvelous deification, singing praise unto Thee.

*Theotokion:*
When Thy martyr recognized Him Who took flesh from woman, O Lord, adorned with virginal rays and dyed with the blood of martyrdom, she was gloriously led unto Thee, her God, following after Thy Mother, as unto the King of creation.

*Another Heirmos [to Abramius]:*[^1227]
Truly we confess thee to be the Theotokos, who through thee are saved, O pure Virgin, magnifying thee with the bodiless choirs.

*Troparia:*
Thou wast counted worthy to behold, O God-bearing father, through hope the things desired of old, which eye hath not seen, and ear hath not heard.[^1228]

Beautiful wast thou shown forth, shining with the divine rays of virtues, O Abramius, and rejoicing dost thou stand before the Master...
=== END LEAF p315 === in beauty.

*Troparia:*
Thou hast joined the choir of all the venerable, and having received deification with them, O venerable one, pray that we all may be saved!

The day of thy venerable falling asleep brings gladness joyfully, O sacred Abramius, as the Church honors thy life.

*Theotokion:*
O good-loving Virgin, bless my soul that is afflicted by sin, and make it a partaker of heavenly good things!

*At the Liturgy, Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*[^1229]
Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.
*Verse:* What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?
*Epistle to the Galatians, Section 213:*[^1230]
*Alleluia, Tone 6:* Blessed is the man that fears the Lord.[^1231]
*Verse:* His seed shall be mighty upon the earth.
*Gospel according to Matthew, Section 43:*[^1232]
*Communion Hymn:* In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be.[^1233]

*In the same month, on the 30th day. The Holy Martyrs Zenobius and his sister Zenobia:*[^1234]

*At Lord, I Have Cried, Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As One Valiant among Martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^1235]
Having dyed thy robe with the blood of martyrdom, O glorious Zenobius, thou didst accomplish the most sacred grace, wherewith thou didst enter as a wise high priest into the Holy of Holies; a blameless sacrifice, an all-pure and perfect offering, thou didst offer thyself unto Him Who was sacrificed for thy sake, O most sacred one!

While thy body was being scraped, the most luminous beauty of thy soul was shown forth even fairer, O hieromartyr Zenobius, divinely wise and richly endowed, adornment of hierarchs, boast of sufferers, ever-flowing fountain of miracles, expeller of unclean spirits, divine champion of Christ!

Thy sister of one mind with thee, O all-wise Zenobius, she who was of the same blood, chose to suffer with thee; for courageously did she endure the boiling of cauldrons, the threatening of fire, and a violent death; therefore she obtained with thee crowns of victory and the Kingdom on high, O sacred initiate Zenobius!
=== END LEAF p316 === *Glory, Tone 6, by John the Monk:*[^1236]
Let us clap our hands in song today, O lovers of the martyrs, in the memory of the all-pious sufferers, Zenobius and Zenobia; for these were champions of the Trinity, and in the arena they courageously strangled the invisible enemy with their precious blood, and gloriously received the crown of victory. Therefore let us cry aloud unto them: O holy pair, blessed and enlightened couple of the Lord, pray unto the Savior for our souls!

*Both now, Theotokion or Cross-Theotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "On the Third Day" (Трїдне́венъ):*[^1237]
Beholding our Life hanging upon the Tree, the all-undefiled Theotokos maternally lamented, crying aloud: O my Son and my God, save those who praise Thee with love!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*[^1238]
Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received incorruptible crowns from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, they cast down the tyrants and shattered the powerless audacity of demons: by their supplications save our souls!

*Glory, both now, Theotokion or Cross-Theotokion.*
*At Matins, both Canons from the Octoechos, and of the Saints to 4.*

*Canon of the Saints, Tone 8, by Joseph. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^1239]
Let us send up a song, O ye peoples, unto our marvelous God, Who freed Israel from bondage, singing a song of victory and crying aloud: Let us sing unto Thee, the only Master!

*Troparia:*
Standing before the throne of Christ as an acceptable priest and most excellent martyr, O thrice-blessed Zenobius, deliver from worldly temptations those who lovingly honor thy radiant memory.

Enlightened, O wise one, by the dawn of the three-sunned Radiance, thou didst dispel the gloom of Hellenic evil belief; and having enlightened all with the light of thy words, thou hast now passed over unto the never-setting radiance.

Shown forth as a spiritual coal kindled by the fire of the divine Spirit, thou didst set the hearts of all on fire unto divine desire, O sacred initiate, and burned up all delusion.

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O all-holy temple, fleece bedewed of God, sealed fountain, stream of immortality! Preserve thy flock, O Sovereign Lady, unconquered by all adversaries!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^1240]
Plant Thy fear, O Lord, in the hearts of Thy servants, and be Thou our confirmation, who truly...
=== END LEAF p317 === call upon Thee.

*Troparia:*
Anointed with the chrism of the spiritual anointing, O Zenobius, thou didst minister as an angel, adorning thyself at the end with martyr crowns.

Having received an increase of souls through divine cultivation, O venerable one, thou didst show them forth as fruitful; therefore thou wast counted worthy of the joy of the Lord.

Thy sister of the same blood was shown forth as of one mind with thee, O most sacred Zenobia; for she chose to suffer with thee, and to enjoy eternal glory with thee.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst give birth to One of the Trinity, Who clothed Himself in humanity, O most pure Virgin Mother: entreat Him to save those who sing of thee.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "While the Soldiers Were Guarding Thy Tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*[^1241]
Anointed with the precious chrism, thou wast shown forth, O all-blessed one, as a priest of the Most High God, strengthened by Whom, O hierarch Zenobius, thou becamest a martyr and a fellow citizen of the angels; therefore celebrating thy all-holy memory today, we sing of thee.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*
O unwedded, pure Virgin Theotokos, only mediatress and protection of the faithful: deliver from perils, afflictions, and all grievous distresses those who place their hope in thee, O Maiden, and save our souls through thy divine prayers!

*Cross-Theotokion:*
Beholding Thee, O Christ, stretched out dead upon the Cross, the all-undefiled Mother cried aloud: O my Son, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, what is this Thine ineffable dispensation, whereby Thou hast saved the creation of Thy most pure hands, O Merciful One?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^1242]
Thou hast mounted upon horses, Thine apostles, O Lord, and taken their bridles into Thy hands, and Thy driving became salvation unto those who faithfully sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
Exalted wast thou shown forth through honorable sufferings, O most sacred one, and received crowns of victory, O wise one, and obtained eternal rejoicing; therefore we honor thee, O father hierarch Zenobius.

Imitating Him Who was willingly stretched upon the Tree, thou didst ascend, and wast not dismayed, O Zenobius; and having put off the grossness of corruption and mortality, O glorious one, O father, boast of priests!

Thy firm soul cast down the demonic fortifications; and the pagan temples were shattered, destroyed through thy most steadfast faith, O glorious Zenobius, boast of hieromartyrs!

*Theotokion:*
By thy marvelous birthgiving...
=== END LEAF p318 === thou hast united them unto Him, O all-hymned one; therefore with loud voice we all glorify thee and cry unto thee with faith: Rejoice, restoration of the earthborn!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^1243]
Therefore hast Thou cast me off from Thy face, O Never-setting Light, and an alien darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my ways unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee.

*Troparia:*
The female nature was strengthened by the Spirit, and trampled him who in Paradise tripped up the foremother of old, and was counted worthy of divine glory in the heavens, shining with virginity and sufferings.

Thy tongue was a rain-bearing cloud, showering the rain of piety, watering the hearts of the faithful, and sacredly guiding unto the fruitful production of virtuous deeds, O Zenobius.

With the rays of virginity thou didst drive away the darkness of voluptuousness, and with the light of sufferings thou didst destroy the night of godlessness, O martyr Zenobia, fair bride of Jesus, dwelling of the divine Spirit!

*Theotokion:*
I am ever weighed down by the heavy burden of sin, O most pure one, and cry unto thee: Lighten this through thy divine mediation; for thou art the most glorified mediatress of sinners, having given birth to the Deliverer and Savior.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^1244]
I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I proclaim my sorrows, for my soul is filled with evils, and my life has drawn near unto Hades, and like Jonah I pray: From corruption, O God, raise me up!

*Troparia:*
The prayer of thy divine soul was accepted like incense, O father; for into the fragrance of the incense of sufferings didst thou run, O all-blessed Zenobius, unto Him Who shone forth from a woman and enlightened the whole creation.

With the blood of suffering, O blessed one, thou didst sanctify the earth, and thy spirit, having ascended unto the heavens, divinely adorned the Church of the firstborn, O Zenobius, luminous boast of hierarchs and martyrs!

Thou didst hem in impiety, O blessed one, with the breadth of true piety, and didst show unto the straying people the paths leading thereto, and saved those who were grievously drowning in the waters of delusion.

*Theotokion:*
Adorn my soul, O Virgin, which is holden by the deformity of passions, and through thoughts of true repentance confirm my wretched heart, and save me who place my hope without doubting in thee, O Virgin!
=== END LEAF p319 === *Kontakion of the Martyrs, Tone 8, Podoben: "As the First-fruits of Nature" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки):*[^1245]
The witnesses of truth and preachers of piety, the pair worthy of honor, let us fittingly honor in divinely inspired hymns, Zenobius together with the wise Zenobia, who lived and passed away together, and through martyrdom received an incorruptible crown.

*Ikos:*[^1246]
The good and great Zenobius let us praise in odes and songs, together with the good and undefiled Zenobia, his companion in suffering; for they cast down the cruelties of the enemy, and having exposed the godlessness of idols, they made clear the faith of Christ; therefore they have now joyfully received the incorruptible crown.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^1247]
The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trampled the flame and turned the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!

*Troparia:*
Having quenched the fire of godlessness with streams of blood, O ever-memorable one, with miracles thou dost ever bedew us with rain, O Zenobius, who bless the Master: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!

Having entered into the divine darkness, thou didst behold the Invisible One as much as was possible, Who enlightens thy soul and mind, O martyr, as thou singest with pious mind: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!

The Master gives thee honor who didst suffer steadfastly, and cast down the wiles of the enemy, and singest with loud voice: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown forth as a place of sanctification, O Virgin, from which God appeared, enlightening us who sing with faith: Blessed, O most pure one, is the Fruit of thy womb!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^1248]
The God-proclaiming children in the furnace, trampling the flame together with the fire, sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*
Thou didst not fear the sword, O hierarch; thou wast not frightened by perils; thou wast not dismayed by death, which showed thee to be a partaker of immortal glory.

Thou wast shown forth as a celebrant of the mysteries, and having cleansed thyself, thou becamest an incense-offering of sweet fragrance, and worthy of the banquet on high, O Zenobius.

Shining with wonderworking, O hierarch, thou didst radiantly shine forth with martyr rays, and didst dispel the darkness of delusion unto the end.

*Theotokion:*
Having conceived the uncultivated Cluster, Which pours forth the wine of forgiveness, O Virgin Mother: change me, I pray thee, from the drunkenness of passions!
=== END LEAF p320 === *Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^1249]
Every ear was dismayed at the ineffable condescension of God: how the Most High of His own will came down even unto the flesh, from a virginal womb, having become man. Therefore, O faithful, we magnify the all-pure Theotokos.

*Troparia:*
Thou didst stand before the tribunals of the torturers, O glorious one, proclaiming with radiant voice the incarnation of the Almighty, and His sufferings, the Cross, and death, and resurrection, whereby He saved us as the Lover of mankind.

O marvel: how in the flesh he conquered the bodiless foes! How by weakness he cast down the manifold schemer! How an earthly man was stirred up to be joined with heavenly ministers! Honoring him, we piously magnify him.

O honorable martyrs, ye delivered your bodies unto diverse wounds in diverse ways, looking unto eternal blessedness; for bound one to another by brotherly love, ye were enlightened by martyr beauties: therefore ye are called blessed.

Today your memory, shining with the radiance of the Spirit, hath shone forth upon all, causing the grace of miracles to flash forth, and pouring forth rivers of healings in abundance; celebrating it, we call you blessed, O martyrs of Christ.

*Theotokion:*
O door of light, enlighten the eyes of my heart, I pray thee, driving far away the thick darkness of sin from my wretched soul: that I may magnify thee, that I may glorify thee, that with love I may sing to thee, the all-praised one.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Же́ны оу҆слы́шите):*[^1250]
O marvel, how they who were made of dust were shown forth to be golden in nature! For neither fire, nor the sword, nor the teeth of wild beasts, nor torments, nor wounds harmed them; but having cast down the bodiless one in the flesh, they pray unto the Lord on our behalf.

*Theotokion:*
Awesome is thy protection, O Mary, adorned by God, and awesome is thy glory throughout all the earth, O Theotokos; for in thee do we now boast, having thee also as mediatress before thy Son and thy Creator: and by thy unceasing prayer we all are saved.
=== END LEAF p321 === *In the same month, on the 31st day. The Holy Apostles Stachys, Amplias, and those with them; and the Holy Martyr Epimachus:*[^1251]

*At Lord, I Have Cried, Stichera of the Apostles, Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мч҃ницы):*[^1252]
Throughout all the earth went forth your [saving] proclamation,[^1253] O [apostles] of the Lord, with the radiance of grace gloriously enlightening the hearts [of all], [and] destroying the darkness of delusion: therefore pray ye [unto Christ], to grant unto our souls peace and [great] mercy.

All-blessed Stachys, the [preacher] and apostle of Christ, and marvelous [Apelles], and Aristobulus, [Urban and] Amplias, and divine [Narcissus], having proclaimed the All-Holy Trinity, enlightened [all] the nations and delivered them from delusion: whom in faith let us all [call blessed].

O most radiant luminaries of Christ, most pure vessels, having contained by faith all the radiance of the Spirit, pillars of the Church, most glorious heavens declaring the glory of God: entreat Him to grant unto our souls peace and great mercy.

*Other Stichera, of the Martyr, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^1254]
By the inspiration of the Holy Spirit, O Epimachus, Thou wast shown forth as a dwelling, divinely glorious; and having received from Him the working of healings, Thou abundantly dispensest health to the sick, who continually cry aloud: O Jesus, Giver of life, Thou art the rejoicing, and the glory, and the boast of Thy martyrs!

With boldness thou didst proclaim the truth, O glorious one, as a most lawful soldier of Christ, not having feared the beast-like rage of the judge who commanded worship unto gods that are not; whom thou didst put to shame by thy God-inspired words, O passion-bearer, and didst proclaim unto all Him Who is the Master, our God.

How beautiful did thy feet become, adorned with martyrdom, to run an irrevocable course! And having indeed finished thy course, thou didst keep the faith, and didst receive the crown from the hand of the Master: entreat Him to deliver from corruption and perils those who celebrate thy most honorable memory with faith.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion, the same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
I have been cast down to the earth, I have been slain by sins, by passions of soul and body;
=== END LEAF p322 === therefore I am covered with the darkness of despair: but raise me up now, guiding me unto incorruptible life, unto the homeland on high, where the voice of those who keep festival is pure, O all-holy Bride of God!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When the ewe-lamb beheld Thee, the Lamb and Shepherd, upon the Tree, she lamented and maternally cried aloud unto Thee: O greatly-desired Son, how art Thou suspended upon the tree of the Cross, O longsuffering One? How were Thy hands and feet pierced with nails by the lawless, O Word, and Thou hast poured forth Thy blood, O Master?

*Troparion of the Holy Apostles, Tone 3:*[^1255]
O holy apostles, entreat the merciful God, that He grant forgiveness of transgressions unto our souls.

*Glory, Troparion of the Martyr, Tone 4:*[^1256]
Thy martyr, O Lord, Epimachus, in his suffering received an incorruptible crown from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, he cast down the torturers, and crushed the powerless boldness of demons: by his supplications save our souls!

*Both now, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion.*
*At Matins, one Canon of the Octoechos, and two of the Saints.*
*Canon of the Apostles, bearing the acrostic: "Faithfully will I praise the servants of God. Joseph." Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^1257]
Thy victorious right hand hath been glorified in strength in a manner befitting God: for that right hand, O Immortal One, as all-powerful, shattered the adversaries, making anew for the Israelites a path through the deep.

*Troparia:*
Deliver from the gloom of passions, and make partakers of eternal joy, those who with faith honor your memory, O sacred and eloquent ones, and who celebrate joyfully.

Following the steps of Christ Who was incarnate because of mercy, O most wealthy ones, and serving His God-ruling commands, ye were shown forth as guides unto piety for all who were led astray.

Having assembled in faith, let us duly call blessed Stachys, and wise Amplias, and Urban, and Apelles, Narcissus, and Aristobulus, who were disciples of Christ.

*Theotokion:*
Let us call blessed her who is blessed among women: the spiritual mountain of God, the holy ark and table, the all-golden jar, the pure palace of Jesus.

*Another Canon, of the Martyr, Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^1258]
I will sing unto Thee, O Lord my God, for Thou hast led forth the people from the bondage...
=== END LEAF p323 === the bondage of Egypt, and didst cover the chariots of Pharaoh and his power.

*Troparia:*
I will sing unto Thee, O Lord my God, for Thou hast shown forth unto us the memory of the martyr, illuminating all things, as the only One Who art abundantly merciful.

I will sing unto Thee, O Lord my God, for Thou hast shown forth Thy martyr as invincible, a champion in torments against godlessness.

I will sing unto Thee, O Lord my God, for with divine gifts Thou hast adorned Thy sufferer Epimachus, and crowned him with honor.

*Theotokion:*
I will sing unto Thee, O Lord my God, for without seed Thou wast born from the womb of the Virgin, for whose sake Thou also savest all, as the only One Who art abundantly merciful.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^1259]
O Thou Who alone knowest the weakness of human nature, and mercifully tookest form therein: gird me with power from on high, that I may cry aloud unto Thee, O Holy One: The living temple of Thine ineffable glory, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*
As a fruitful ear of grain wast thou shown forth, O wise one, by the word of knowledge feeding those languishing in the famine of faith, O God-blessed Stachys, and by the power of the Spirit making them partakers of superior food through grace.

Having taken in a pious manner the net of the tongue, thou didst catch [them] from the deep of deceit, O apostle; and thou broughtest divine food to Him Who came to save the straying, O Narcissus: the King and Benefactor of creation.

Thou didst run like a star hastening from the East, and reachedst the cities of Britain, preaching the Gospel of Christ, and through grace enlightening those who faithfully receive thy word, O blessed Aristobulus.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown forth as the radiant bridal chamber of Christ, and the highest throne, O Birthgiver of God, all-immaculate one; for in thee alone having rested, He took away our labors, and prepared future relief for those who are worthy.

*Another Heirmos:*[^1260]
The bow of the mighty hath grown weak, and the infirm have girded themselves with power: therefore my heart hath been established in the Lord.

*Troparia:*
Boasting in the all-lauded Cross, the invincible crown-bearer of Christ, the martyr Epimachus, darkened the fury of the ungodly.

Having despised temporary glory, and having stripped himself for the contests, the God-wise and great martyr of Christ, Epimachus, rejoiced.
=== END LEAF p324 === *Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O Mother Ever-Virgin, who in thy womb didst contain God the Word, and didst give birth unto Him incarnate, as God and man together.

*Sessional Hymn of the Apostles, Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се):*[^1261]
Your honorable proclamation went forth throughout all the earth, enlightening the souls of unbelieving men, O glorious apostles, through your sacred intercessions. Therefore today we joyfully celebrate your pious and radiant memory, faithfully magnifying you.

*Glory, Sessional Hymn of the Martyr, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃е́ственныя вѣ́ры):*[^1262]
Thou wast an animate pillar of truth, a witness in suffering, having conquered the invisible foes by visible torturers, O most excellent one, receiving a crown from Christ. Therefore we cry aloud unto Thee: Cease not praying, O glorious one, that great mercy be granted unto us!

*Both now, Theotokion:*
Not departing from the divine nature though He became flesh in thy womb, but having become man God remained, Who after birth preserved thee as a virgin mother, as thou wast before childbirth, all-immaculate, the only Lord: earnestly entreat Him, that great mercy be granted unto us!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The undefiled ewe-lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, seeing Him Who sprang from her without pain suspended upon the Cross, maternally lamenting as was meet, cried aloud: Woe is me, O my Child! How sufferest Thou willingly, wishing to deliver man from shameful passions?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^1263]
Habakkuk, with farsighted eyes, perceived thee as a mountain overshadowed by the grace of God, from which the Holy One should come forth, for our salvation and renewal.

*Troparia:*
Having shone forth upon earth, the Sun of righteousness sent forth light as a ray unto the initiates of the mysteries: Stachys, Narcissus, Urban, and Amplias, Aristobulus, and Apelles, who enlighten those sitting in darkness.

Being strengthened by the power of the divine Spirit, thou didst show forth those living in darkness to be sons of light, O Apostle Amplias, performing most glorious deeds of miracles, and abolishing the ranks of demons.

Filled with divine waters, thou didst flow as a saving stream, O Urban, driving away bitter and undrinkable waters, and by grace watering the fulness of those who live piously, O most blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown forth as higher than the angels, O Sovereign Lady, for unto Him Who is awesome unto them...
=== END LEAF p325 === thou wast counted worthy to contain in thy womb ineffably, and to carry in thine arms as an infant Him Who is more ancient than days and years.

*Another Heirmos:*[^1264]
Proclaiming Thy coming upon earth, O Christ God, the prophet cried with rejoicing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
Having disregarded the threatening of the ungodly, the glorious Epimachus arrayed himself for the contests, boldly crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord! (Twice)

Having spat upon earthly things, by death the glorious Epimachus inherited the kingdom, faithfully crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Triadikon:*
The fair-victorious Epimachus proclaimed on the field of contest unto all who knew Him not, the Lord Who is glorified on high with the Father and the Spirit.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^1265]
Thou Who hast enlightened with the brightness of Thy coming, O Christ, and illuminated with Thy Cross the ends of the world: enlighten the hearts of those who praise Thee in an orthodox manner with the light of Thy divine knowledge.

*Troparia:*
The God-chosen sheep of the Good Shepherd were visibly sent forth by Him into the midst of wolves: taming these and leading them by faith unto the spiritual sheepfold.

The great Andrew appointed thee as bishop in Byzantium, O most sacred Stachys, who faithfully steerest those sailing there, and guidest them to quiet havens by divine grace.

As a precious cornerstone did the honorable church of Heraclea possess thee, O blessed Apelles, strengthening her by the word of truth: therefore we duly honor thee.

*Theotokion:*
Beyond understanding thou didst conceive the Word, and gavest birth unto Him, O all-praised one, Whom the Father begat from the womb before the ages, O pure one: therefore as His Mother we duly call thee blessed.

*Another Heirmos:*[^1266]
Send down upon us Thy enlightenment, O Lord, and loose us from the gloom of transgressions, O Good One, granting Thy peace.

*Troparia:*
Shielded with the weapon of the grace of the Cross, the martyr cried aloud to the ungodly: Neither sword nor fire shall cut me off from the love of Christ!

Standing before the tribunal of the lawless, the glorious Epimachus cried aloud with holy boldness: Let the ungodly gods who made not earth and heaven perish!
=== END LEAF p326 === The glorious Epimachus, God-inspired by God, proclaimed unto those who knew not the Trinity: the Father, the Word, and the Holy Spirit in one nature.

*Theotokion:*
Who is able to recount the most glorious manner of thy childbirth beyond speech, O pure one? For beyond speech thou gavest birth unto God incarnate in nature.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^1267]
The lowest depth hath surrounded us, there is none to deliver, we have been accounted as sheep for slaughter: save Thy people, O our God, for Thou art the strength and the setting-right of the infirm!

*Troparia:*
Having stirred up the sea of the nations by your divine ascents, ye saved those who of old were drowning in the burning sea, and brought them saved unto the Redeemer, O God-blessed ones.

Thou didst preach God as the Guide of mercy, and freedst the souls of the pious from deceit, O divine Narcissus, having become a great leader of the Athenians, and a planter of fair gardens, O God-blessed one.

The divine Urban, pouring forth the life-flowing word and the grace of healings, drew the people unto the enlightenment of divine understanding, as an imitator of Him Who was incarnate and saved the world.

*Theotokion:*
We sing of thee, through whom we were justified who were formerly condemned, and were joined unto the immaterial ministers, and were counted worthy of Paradise, O blessed and favored of God among women!

*Another Heirmos:*[^1268]
The prophet Jonah, praying in the whale, prefigured the three-day burial, crying aloud: Deliver me from corruption, O Jesus, King of powers!

*Troparia:*
Epimachus left the things in the world and the world, and manfully gave over his body unto torments for the Master, and became an heir of Christ.

Epimachus abolished the deceit of idols, and was counted worthy of true glory, having received the grace of healings, from which we all the faithful draw.

Having partaken of the sufferings of Christ, Epimachus, by the power of Christ God Who was crucified in the flesh, received from Him crowns of glory as a faithful one.

*Theotokion:*
Pray for thy servants, O Virgin Theotokos, unto our God Who was incarnate from thee: for thee alone do we know as our protectress.

*Kontakion of the Apostles, Tone 8, Podoben: "As the first-fruits of nature" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки):*[^1269]
=== END LEAF p327 === As sacred treasures of the All-Holy Spirit and radiant beams of the Sun of glory, let us duly praise the wise apostles: Apelles, Urban, and Aristobulus, Amplias, Narcissus, and Stachys, whom the grace of our God gathered together.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^1270]
We the faithful perceive thee, O Theotokos, as a spiritual furnace: for as the Exalted One saved the three children, so He renewed the world wholly in thy womb, the praised and most glorious God of the fathers.

*Troparia:*
By the endurance of afflictions ye were delivered from the enemy that tempts you; and by showers of the life-giving word, O glorious apostles, ye quenched all the flame of deceit, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers, Who art praised and exalted above all!

Most radiantly and excellently didst thou advise, and perfectly didst thou instruct thy disciples, O blessed Aristobulus, having hypostatic Wisdom instructing thee to declare the praised and most glorious God of the fathers.

The sacred assembly of the faithful with most sacred voices calls thee blessed, O Apelles; for thou hadst a blessed end, and an angelic life, singing unto the praised and most glorious God of the fathers of all:

*Theotokion:*
Deliver us from perils and afflictions, and diverse sorrows, and the assault of heathen nations, O all-praised Theotokos: that we may glorify thee, and cry aloud unto thy Son: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers, Who art praised!

*Another Heirmos:*[^1271]
Once in Babylon the children of Abraham trampled the furnace flame, crying aloud with hymns: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
Manfully trampling deceit together with fire, the fair-victorious Epimachus cried aloud unto the Crucified One: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

The invincible Epimachus feared not the flame, nor the wild beasts, nor the torments, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Proclaiming unto those who knew not the Father, the Word, and the All-Holy Spirit, Epimachus cried aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
By the unburned bush and the dew-yielding furnace wast thou prefigured, O pure Mother: for ineffably thou gavest birth unto the incarnate Christ God, O blessed one.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
=== END LEAF p328 === *Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^1272]
In the furnace the children of Israel, as in a crucible, shone more purely than gold with the beauty of piety, saying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
Like lightning were ye shown forth in the world, O beholders of God, consuming deceit, and showing those who sing to be sons of day, as they cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

By the sweet salt of thy teachings, O Amplias, thou didst cleanse the rottenness of deceit: therefore honoring thee, we sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

Let the glorious Stachys and Amplias be sung, and Apelles and Narcissus, and Urban, and the all-wise Aristobulus faithfully crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Knowing thee as a wall, and refuge, and ladder leading mankind to the height, O all-immaculate, pure one, we cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Another Heirmos:*[^1273]
Unto Him Who in the form of an angel appeared in the fiery furnace unto the hymn-singers, Christ God: sing, ye children; bless, ye priests; exalt Him above all, ye people, unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
Unto Him Who showed forth the crown-bearer Epimachus as victor over deceit and lawless men: sing, ye children; bless, ye priests; exalt Him above all, ye people, unto all ages!

Unto Him Who granted unto us the passion-bearer Epimachus as a physician of grievous passions and infirmities: sing, ye children; bless, ye priests; exalt Him above all, ye people, unto all ages!

Unto Christ Who magnified the memory of Epimachus upon earth, and thereby healed diverse infirmities: sing, ye priests, and exalt Him above all unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*
Unto the Virgin who ineffably and without seed conceived and gave birth unto Christ our God: sing, ye children; bless, ye priests; exalt her above all, ye people, unto the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^1274]
The unburned bush showed forth a type of thy pure childbirth, and now we pray thee to quench the furnace of temptations that rages against us, that we may unceasingly magnify thee, O Theotokos!
=== END LEAF p329 === *Troparia:*
That Thou mightest enlighten with the light of knowledge the ends of the earth which were filled with the deceit of the adversary and full of the gloom of godlessness, O Compassionate One, Thou hast sent forth Thy saving rays unto them.

Thou wast shown forth according to thy name, O glorious one, as a saving ear of grain, bearing wheat for those in need, which grants the confirmation of divine knowledge and takes away spiritual famine, as with faith and love we sing to thee, O sacred preacher.

Having become the mouth of God, thou didst show the tongues of rhetors to be speechless, and out of the throat of the enemy, O Aristobulus, thou didst snatch souls, and broughtest them saved unto the Lord, perfected by knowledge.

All your lives indeed became God-like, O divine apostles: vouchsafe that those who honor you purely upon earth, and who sincerely call you blessed, may become partakers of eternal brightness.

*Theotokion:*
Thou bearest Him Who bears all things, and with milk feedest Him Who grants food unto all, a great and awesome mystery beyond the mind, O ark of honorable sanctification, Virgin Theotokos: therefore with faith we call thee blessed.

*Another Heirmos:*[^1275]
Thee who didst contain the uncontainable Word in thy womb, wider than the heavens, and more glorious than the cherubim, O pure Theotokos, with songs we magnify!

*Troparia:*
Because thou didst love completely even to die for Christ Who died for our sake, therefore wast thou crowned on high by Him, O martyr: Whom with the Father and the Spirit we magnify with songs.

Because thou didst faithfully confess the Savior God upon earth before the ungodly torturers: now on high by Him before the angels, O witness Epimachus, and by the Father and the Spirit thou hast been crowned with glory.

*Theotokion:*
Thee the light-bearing cloud, and pure and glorious Virgin and Mother of God, who appeared mystically unto Moses on Mount Sinai as the unburned bush, in hymns do we magnify.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Же́ны оу҆слы́шите):*[^1276]
Let the divine Aristobulus, Stachys the most rich, and Apelles the God-wise, and Narcissus, and Amplias, and Urban the all-wise, now be honored by us as apostles of Christ, by whom the world was delivered from manifold deceit.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown forth higher than the cherubim, O all-praised one...
=== END LEAF p330 === *Month of October*
for Him Who sits upon their shoulders, O most pure Maiden, thou didst bear in thine arms, and didst nurse Him with milk as an infant; earnestly entreat Him on behalf of us who sing to thee as the true Mother of God.[^1277]

*(Woodcut tailpiece: A woven basket overflowing with flowers, concluding the Month of October)*[^1278]

### Notes

[^5]: Woodcut illustration heading the month of October, portraying the Protection (Pokrov) of the Theotokos above the clergy and faithful in the church of Blachernae.
[^6]: In the Ruthenian Typikon, the Feast of the Protection of the Theotokos is a Great Feast of the Mother of God with an All-Night Vigil. In this Pochaiv edition, the standard daily service to the Apostle Ananias and Saint Romanus the Melodist is printed first, followed immediately by the full festal service for the Protection.
[^7]: Referring to the vision on the road to Damascus (Acts 9:1–19), where Saul was struck blind by the light from heaven until Ananias laid hands on him and baptized him.
[^8]: *Вѵзанті́ево* (Byzantios) refers to the Byzantine hymnographer (often identified with the Emperor or an early Constantinopolitan poet).
[^9]: Saint John of Damascus (c. 676–749), monk of the Lavra of Saint Sabbas, hymnographer and Father of the Church, author of the primary canon to the Apostle Ananias.
[^10]: Alluding to the Damascus road event and Saul's subsequent baptism by Ananias (Acts 9:10–18), who was initially apprehensive of Saul's reputation as a persecutor.
[^11]: According to the traditional hagiography of Saint Romanus the Melodist, the Mother of God appeared to him in a vision on Christmas Eve, giving him a scroll to eat, after which he miraculously received the gift of composing hymns and sang his famous Kontakion *Today the Virgin gives birth to Him Who is transcendent in essence*.
[^12]: An allusion to the conversion of Saul on the road to Damascus (Acts 9:1–19), where Saul, initially breathing threats and murder like a ravening wolf against the flock of Christ, was tamed into a meek lamb and received baptism from Ananias.
[^13]: Saint Ananias served as the first bishop of Damascus, where he tended his flock, converted many, and ultimately received the crown of martyrdom by stoning under the governor Lucian.
[^14]: Acts 9:18: "And immediately there fell from his eyes as it had been scales: and he received sight forthwith, and arose, and was baptized."
[^15]: Psalm 83:6 (LXX): "Blessed is the man whose help is in Thee; in his heart he has disposed to ascend."
[^16]: Saint Ananias is invoked as the apostolic hierarch, protector, and heavenly patron of Damascus, whose prayers preserve the Christian flock in orthodox piety.
[^17]: Liturgical petition for the reigning sovereign: in the 1761 Pochaiv Menaion, printed under the Polish-Lithuanian Commonwealth during the reign of King Augustus III Wettin (reigned 1734–1763), prayers were offered for the King of Poland (*король*).
[^18]: The Feast of the Protection (*Pokrov*) of the Most Holy Theotokos commemorates the miraculous appearance of the Mother of God in the Church of Blachernae in Constantinople in the tenth century to Saint Andrew the Fool-for-Christ and his disciple Epiphanius, where she was seen praying for the world and spreading her veil (*omophorion*) over the faithful. In the Ruthenian Greek Catholic Church, this service is celebrated with the highest liturgical solemnity.
[^19]: A reference to the historic defense of Constantinople and Christian realms through the intercession of the Virgin and the relic of her veil preserved at Blachernae.
[^20]: Psalm 44:11 (LXX) [Masoretic Psalm 45:10].
[^21]: Psalm 44:13 (LXX) [Masoretic Psalm 45:12].
[^22]: Isaiah 2:2: "For in the last days the mountain of the Lord shall be glorious, and the house of God shall be on the top of the mountains, and it shall be exalted above the hills; and all nations shall come to it."
[^23]: Saint Andrew the Fool-for-Christ of Constantinople (tenth century), who witnessed the Virgin Mary praying at the Blachernae church and spreading her omophorion over the assembly.
[^24]: Psalm 131:8 (LXX) [Masoretic Psalm 132:8].
[^25]: Psalm 44:11 (LXX) [Masoretic Psalm 45:10].
[^26]: Psalm 44:10 (LXX) [Masoretic Psalm 45:9].
[^27]: Psalm 44:13 (LXX) [Masoretic Psalm 45:12].
[^28]: The Greek/Slavonic *ilektron* (ἤλεκτρον / *илектръ*) denotes amber or an alloy of gold and silver renowned in classical and biblical literature (cf. Ezekiel 1:4, 1:27, 8:2) for its brilliant, shimmering radiance.
[^29]: In the 1761 Pochaiv Menaion, the petition for kings and rulers reflects the liturgical use of the Polish-Lithuanian Commonwealth under King Augustus III Wettin.
[^30]: Slavonic *кораля̀* (the King of Poland, Augustus III), typical of Ruthenian Greek Catholic editions printed under the Crown of Poland.
[^31]: An allusion to Gideon's miraculous victory over the Midianites (Judges 7–8) after the sign of the dew upon the fleece.
[^32]: 1 Samuel 17 (1 Kingdoms 17, LXX).
[^33]: Song of Songs 3:7–10, typologically interpreted as the Virgin's womb and the bed of the heavenly Solomon.
[^34]: Daniel 2:34–35, 45, typologically interpreted as the Virgin from whom the cornerstone, Christ, was cut without hands.
[^35]: Ezekiel 1:26–28.
[^36]: Habakkuk 3:3 (LXX).
[^37]: Slavonic *въ россі́йстѣй землѝ* (in the Rus' / Ruthenian land), referring to the lands of historical Kyivan Rus' within the Polish-Lithuanian Commonwealth where the feast of the Pokrov has historically been celebrated with immense devotion.
[^38]: Another explicit liturgical intercession for King Augustus III Wettin of Poland.
[^39]: Saint Andrew the Fool-for-Christ witnessed the apparition of the Mother of God during the all-night vigil at the Church of the Theotokos of Blachernae in Constantinople.
[^40]: Hebrews 9:1–7, the traditional Festal Epistle for feasts of the Theotokos.
[^41]: Luke 10:38–42, 11:27–28, the Gospel reading appointed for feasts of the Mother of God.
[^42]: This detailed Typikon rubric (*Указ*) regulates the conciliar ranking of the Feast of the Protection when combined with the daily commemorations of Saint Ananias and Saint Romanus the Melodist on a weekday, following the Ruthenian liturgical synthesis codified in the Pochaiv editions.
[^43]: The Typikon chapter detailing the confluence of the Feast of the Protection with the Sunday Resurrectional service, regulating the distribution of Resurrectional, Marian, and Apostolic stichera, heirmoi, and troparia.
[^44]: Decorative woodcut headpiece introducing the services of October 2.
[^45]: Saint Cyprian the Hieromartyr and Saint Justina the Virgin-Martyr of Damascus (martyred c. 304 under Diocletian). Saint Andrew of Constantinople, the Fool-for-Christ, who witnessed the Blachernae vision of the Protection, is also commemorated on this day.
[^46]: The classic Troparion of a Hieromartyr (*И нра́вомъ прича́стникъ* / *Καὶ τρόπων μέτοχος*), celebrating the ascetic/practical virtues (*praxis* / *дѣѧ́нїе*) as the ladder of ascent to mystical divine contemplation (*theoria* / *видѣ́нїе*).
[^47]: Before his conversion, Saint Cyprian was a renowned pagan philosopher and sorcerer in Antioch (Pisidia) who, according to his Life, served demonic altars before converting through the steadfast virginity of Saint Justina.
[^48]: An explicit Ruthenian/Byzantine dogmatic formulation of the hypostatic union, expressing the Christological mystery of the Incarnation (*secundum hypostasim* / *по ѵ҆поста́си*) from the Virgin Mary.
[^49]: An allusion to the pious Christian woman Rufina, who according to the ancient Passion of Saints Cyprian and Justina, took their relics after their martyrdom under Diocletian at Nicomedia, brought them to Rome, and buried them at her estate on the Claudian Way.
[^50]: The Epistle reading appointed for a Hieromartyr is typically 1 Timothy 1:12–17 (composite pericope 280, printed here with the numerical scribal variant *с҃н* [250]).
[^51]: Decorative Rococo woodcut tailpiece concluding the service to Hieromartyr Cyprian and Martyr Justina.
[^52]: The rubric prescribing that Saint Andrew the Fool-for-Christ's service be sung at Compline (*Повече́рїе*) when a major feast or hieromartyr's service occupies Vespers and Matins on October 2.
[^53]: Philippians 3:8: "I count all things but refuse [dung / *оу҆меты̀*], that I may win Christ."
[^54]: 1 Corinthians 2:9; Isaiah 64:4.
[^55]: Referring to Saint Andrew's ascension unto the third heaven, modeled on Saint Paul's rapture in 2 Corinthians 12:2–4, where Andrew kissed the Cross of Christ and was given radiant robes of light.
[^56]: 1 Corinthians 4:10: "We are fools for Christ's sake, but ye are wise in Christ."
[^57]: In the Life of Saint Andrew the Fool-for-Christ, the demon appeared to him as a giant, fearsome black Ethiopian warrior whom Andrew wrestled and cast down by the power of Christ.
[^58]: When Andrew first feigned madness, his master chained him inside the Church of Saint Anastasia the Deliverer from Potions, where he was comforted by Saint John the Theologian.
[^59]: Depicting the extreme ascetic trials in Saint Andrew's Life, where he spent freezing nights lying naked in the refuse heaps of Constantinople and was bruised by passersby and passing carriages.
[^60]: 1 Corinthians 1:18.
[^61]: Matthew 5:3.
[^62]: Epiphanius was Saint Andrew's disciple who accompanied him at the Blachernae vision of the Protection; Andrew prophesied that Epiphanius would become Patriarch of Constantinople.
[^63]: Philippians 3:8: "I count all things but refuse [dung / *оу҆ме́ты*], that I may win Christ."
[^64]: In the Life of Saint Andrew the Fool-for-Christ, Andrew witnessed the burial of a notorious usurer in Constantinople whose body was given an elaborate public funeral, while legions of black demons dragged his soul into the abyss.
[^65]: Saint Andrew encountered a monk renowned for asceticism who was secretly enslaved by greed; Andrew saw a monstrous serpent coiled around his neck, rebuked him, and brought him to repentance and redistribution of his wealth to the poor.
[^66]: During one of Saint Andrew's ecstasies, he saw heaven open and the souls of departed righteous ascetics ascending joyfully in the form of white swallows.
[^67]: Matthew 16:25; Mark 8:35; Luke 9:24.
[^68]: A concise Ruthenian liturgical formulation of the Incarnation (*communicatio idiomatum*), where the Second Person of the Trinity (the Uncreated Essence) assumed created human nature from the Virgin.
[^69]: Rococo woodcut tailpiece concluding the service to Saint Andrew the Fool-for-Christ.
[^70]: An elaborate Baroque woodcut headpiece displaying the All-Seeing Eye of Divine Providence (*Око Провидіння*) within a radiant triangle, surrounded by clouds and cherubic heads, marking the feast of Saint Dionysius the Areopagite.
[^71]: Acts 9:15, where Christ refers to Saint Paul as a "chosen vessel" (*сосу́дъ избра́нный*). Saint Dionysius, converted by Saint Paul's sermon on the Areopagus (Acts 17:34), is here depicted as caught by Paul's spiritual fishing hook (*у́дица*), while the printed layout spans across columns.
[^72]: An allusion to Saint Cyprian's conversion in Antioch; after repenting of sorcery through the intercession of the virgin Justina, he publicly burned his magical grimoires (*волше́бныѧ кни́ги*) before the bishop and faithful (Acts 19:19).
[^73]: The standard Hieromartyr's dismissal Troparion (*Пра́вило вѣ́ры* / *Бла́гости научи́вся*), celebrating holy sobriety, pure conscience, and apostolic stewardship.
[^74]: Acrostic of the Matins Canon: *Оу҆́мъ хвалю̀ му́дрыхъ уче́нїй. Ѳеофа́нъ* ("I praise the mind of wise teachings. By Theophanes").
[^75]: In the Dionysian corpus (*De Caelesti Hierarchia* and *De Ecclesiastica Hierarchia*), Dionysius systematically structures the nine angelic choirs and their earthly ecclesiastical reflections.
[^76]: Referring to Saint Dionysius' famous theological treatise *On the Divine Names* (*Περὶ θείων ὀνομάτων* / *О боже́ственныхъ и҆мена́хъ*).
[^77]: Habakkuk 3:2; the fourth Heirmos of Byzantine canons typologically interprets Habakkuk's vision of God coming from Mount Paran as the Incarnation.
[^78]: The apophatic theology of Saint Dionysius on the representation of formless angelic intelligences (*безобра́зныхъ си́лъ*) through sensible material symbols and sacred writing (*священнописа́нїе*).
[^79]: Matthew 13:45–46; Saint Dionysius is compared to the merchant seeking goodly pearls who found the one Pearl of great price, Christ.
[^80]: Tone 8 Kontakion composed by Saint Romanos the Melodist or Byzantine hymnographers, referencing Saint Paul's ascension unto the third heaven (2 Corinthians 12:2).
[^81]: The 12 salutations (*Chairetismoi*) of the Ikos, commemorating Saint Dionysius as a hearer of Saint Paul, converter of Athens, destroyer of pagan temples, and witness to Christ's Passion (referencing the eclipse at the Crucifixion in Dionysius' Epistle to Polycarp).
[^82]: Daniel 3:19–25; the 7-times heated Babylonian furnace quenched by the angel of God.
[^83]: According to ecclesiastical tradition recorded in the Dionysian writings (*On the Divine Names*, III.2), Saint Dionysius was miraculously transported by cloud to Jerusalem with the Apostles to behold the Dormition and burial of the Most Holy Theotokos.
[^84]: Depicting the double crown of hierarch and martyr received by Saint Dionysius, who according to his Passion was beheaded on the hill of Montmartre in Paris.
[^85]: Psalm 149:5.
[^86]: Pericope 40 from the half comprises Acts 17:16–34, recording Saint Paul's speech on Mars Hill in Athens and the conversion of Dionysius the Areopagite and a woman named Damaris.
[^87]: Decorative Rococo woodcut tailpiece concluding the service to Hieromartyr Dionysius the Areopagite.
[^88]: Saint Hierotheus was the first bishop of Athens, mentor and friend of Saint Dionysius the Areopagite, also present with the Apostles at the Dormition of the Theotokos.
[^89]: Saint Dionysius the Areopagite in *On the Divine Names* (III.2) records that both he and Saint Hierotheus were gathered together with the Holy Apostles in Jerusalem at the Dormition of the Most Holy Theotokos.
[^90]: In the same Dionysian account (*De Divinis Nominibus*, III.2), Dionysius describes how Hierotheus, transported in ecstasy (*ἔκστασις* / *и҆з̾ступле́нїе*), composed and chanted sublime hymns of praise at the burial of the Mother of God, moving all the Apostles and hierarchs to tears.
[^91]: The standard dismissal Troparion of a Hieromartyr (*Бла́гости научи́вся*), identical to that sung for Saint Dionysius the Areopagite on October 3.
[^92]: The acrostic in the Theophanes canon reads in Slavonic: *Свѣтоно́сную твою̀ всеблаже́нне па́мять почита́емъ* ("We honor thy light-bearing memory, O all-blessed one").
[^93]: An etymological wordplay on the Greek name *Hierotheus* (*Ἱερόθεος*), from *ἱερός* ("sacred/holy") and *θεός* ("God"), signifying "one made holy unto God" or "sacred to God" (*свяще́нное возложе́нїе*).
[^94]: Before his conversion by Saint Paul, Saint Hierotheus was one of the leading members of the Athenian Areopagus, deeply versed in classical Greek philosophy and rhetoric.
[^95]: Habakkuk 3:2. The fourth Heirmos of the Byzantine canon tradition typically takes its thematic imagery from the Prayer of Habakkuk.
[^96]: Alluding to Saint Dionysius the Areopagite who regarded the writings of his mentor Saint Hierotheus (especially the lost *Elements of Theology*) as secondary sacred oracles second only to the Holy Scriptures.
[^97]: Kontakion Tone 4 modeled on the celebrated podoben *Вознесы́йся на Крⷭ҇тъ во́лею* composed by Saint Romanos the Melodist.
[^98]: Daniel 3:19–25.
[^99]: An ornamental Rococo woodcut tailpiece concluding the service to Hieromartyr Hierotheus, Bishop of Athens.
[^100]: An ornate Baroque woodcut headpiece displaying floral foliage and scrollwork, marking the feast of the Holy Martyr Charitina.
[^101]: Saint Charitina of Amisus (Pontus) suffered martyrdom during the persecution under Emperor Diocletian and Governor Dometian (c. 304). Orphaned in childhood, she was raised by a pious Christian named Claudius.
[^102]: According to her Passion, after Charitina gave up her spirit under torture, her body was thrown into the sea in a sack; after three days the waves washed it ashore, where her foster-father Claudius retrieved and buried it.
[^103]: The standard dismissal Troparion of a Holy Woman Martyr (*А҆́гница твоя̀, І҆ису́се*), confessing union with Christ through suffering and baptism into His death.
[^104]: Acrostic of the canon in Tone 4 composed by Saint Joseph the Hymnographer: *Харїти́ны бла́гость пою̀ любо́вїю. І҆ѡ́сифъ* ("With love I sing the grace of Charitina. Joseph").
[^105]: Exodus 14:21–29; Exodus 17:8–13.
[^106]: The Marian typological symbols in the Tone 4 Sessional Hymn: Gideon's fleece (Judges 6:36–40), the Burning Bush (Exodus 3:2), and Aaron's rod that budded (Numbers 17:8).
[^107]: Habakkuk 3:11 LXX: "The sun and moon stood still in their order."
[^108]: Matthew 25:1–13 (the Parable of the Ten Virgins).
[^109]: According to her Passion, the torturers sheared off Saint Charitina's hair and poured burning coals upon her head, yet she was preserved unconsumed by divine grace.
[^110]: Driven into her flesh by the executioners during her interrogation.
[^111]: Jonah 2:10; John 19:34.
[^112]: Alluding to the Prophet Jonah delivered from the sea monster (Jonah 2), prefiguring the miraculous preservation of Saint Charitina when she was cast into the sea with a heavy stone tied around her neck, whereupon the stone broke away and she walked upon the waves.
[^113]: The Kontakion in Tone 2 portrays the martyr clothed in an imperial purple robe (*червлени́ца* / *πορφύρα*) dyed in her own blood, standing before the King of kings.
[^114]: Daniel 3; Prayer of the Three Holy Children 1:31.
[^115]: Saint Charitina was tied to a revolving wheel over burning coals, but an angel loosed her and extinguished the flames.
[^116]: Colossians 1:24: "Who now rejoice in my sufferings for you, and fill up that which is behind of the afflictions of Christ in my flesh for His body's sake, which is the church."
[^117]: Daniel 6:16–22.
[^118]: According to the Passion of Saint Charitina, her foster-father Claudius recovered her body after it was cast into the sea and washed ashore, burying her with honor.
[^119]: Daniel 2:34, 45. The "cornerstone cut out of the mountain without hands" is a classical messianic and Marian type in Byzantine hymnography.
[^120]: An etymological wordplay on the name *Charitina* (*Харїти́на*), derived from the Greek *χάρις* ("grace", "favor", "joy").
[^121]: An ornate Rococo woodcut headpiece depicting Christ the Teacher / Pantokrator framed by scrolling acanthus foliage, marking the feast of the Holy Apostle Thomas on October 6.
[^122]: John 20:26–28.
[^123]: The patristic paradox of the "faithful unbelief" (*вѣ́рное невѣ́рствїе* / *πιστὴ ἀπιστία*) of Saint Thomas, by which divine providence confirmed the truth of the bodily Resurrection of Christ for all generations.
[^124]: Alluding to the Chalcedonian and Sixth Ecumenical Council dogma: touching the wounds of Christ's physical flesh and blood revealed both His human and divine natures and energies (*сугу́бо дѣ́йство*, *существа̑ сугу̑ба*).
[^125]: Composed by Saint Theodore the Studite (759–826), abbot of the Studion Monastery in Constantinople.
[^126]: According to ancient apostolic tradition, Saint Thomas preached the Gospel to the Parthians, Medes, Persians, and ultimately journeyed to India, where he founded the Christian church in Malabar (Saint Thomas Christians) and was crowned with martyrdom by being pierced with lances.
[^127]: Jude 1:23: "And others save with fear, pulling them out of the fire; hating even the garment spotted by the flesh." The Church Slavonic marginal note records the variant: *ѡ҆скверне́нную же ри́зу*.
[^128]: Acts 2:1–4 (the descent of the Holy Spirit at Pentecost as cloven tongues of fire).
[^129]: John 20:22: "He breathed on them, and says unto them, Receive ye the Holy Ghost."
[^130]: Psalm 18:5 LXX [Psalm 19:4 Masoretic].
[^131]: Psalm 18:2 LXX [Psalm 19:1 Masoretic].
[^132]: John 20:27–28.
[^133]: Attributed to Patriarch Anatolius of Constantinople (reigned 449–458), noted hymnographer of the *Anatolika* stichera.
[^134]: According to the Passion of Saint Thomas, while preaching in Calamina (India), he was condemned by King Misdaeus and martyred by five soldiers who pierced him with lances on a mountain, conforming him to the spear-pierced side of Christ.
[^135]: According to the Passion of Saint Thomas, he was condemned by the pagan king Misdaeus in India and thrust through with five lances by soldiers.
[^136]: Psalm 18:5 LXX [Psalm 19:4 Masoretic].
[^137]: Psalm 18:2 LXX [Psalm 19:1 Masoretic].
[^138]: John 20:19–31 (the Matins Gospel of the appearance of the risen Christ to the disciples and the confession of Saint Thomas).
[^139]: Saint Theophanes the Branded (*Graptos*, c. 778–845), Metropolitan of Nicaea, renowned Byzantine hymnographer and confessor of icon veneration. The acrostic in Greek reads: *Θαυμασίων πληρωτὴν Θωμᾶν ἀγάζομαι. Θεοφάνους* ("I marvel at Thomas who is full of wonders. By Theophanes").
[^140]: The classical Patristic tripartition of the soul (*τὸ τριμερὲς τῆς ψυχῆς* / *трїча́стное ду́ши*), formulated in Evagrius Ponticus, Saint John of Damascus, and Byzantine ascetical theology: the rational/intellective (*τὸ λογιστικόν*), the incensive/irascible (*τὸ θυμικόν*), and the appetitive/desiring (*τὸ ἐπιθυμητικόν*).
