[^376]: 3 Kingdoms 18:38 [1 Kings 18:38]: Elijah's sacrifice consumed by fire from heaven despite being drenched with water.

[^378]: Acts 8:7: "For unclean spirits, crying with loud voice, came out of many that were possessed with them: and many taken with palsies, and that were lame, were healed."
[^379]: Acts 8:5–6: "Then Philip went down to the city of Samaria, and preached Christ unto them. And the people with one accord gave heed unto those things which Philip spake, hearing and seeing the miracles which he did."
[^380]: Daniel 3:28; Song of the Three Children 1:35.
[^381]: An explicit condemnation of the iconoclast emperor Theophilus (reigned 829–842), who fiercely persecuted the venerators of the holy icons of Christ.
[^382]: 1 Timothy 3:15: "The pillar and ground of the truth."
[^383]: Cf. LXX Psalm 67:16–17 [MT Ps. 68:15–16]; Daniel 2:34, 45.
[^384]: Cf. 1 Timothy 6:16; John 1:18.
[^385]: See note 373 above regarding St. Philip's ministry and dormition in Tralles of Lydia.
[^386]: Cf. Ephesians 2:17.
[^387]: Allusion to the Akathist Hymn and the Salutations to the Theotokos.
[^388]: Heirmos of Ode 9, Tone 2, celebrating the paradox of the virginal conception renewing the order of nature.
[^389]: St. Theophanes Graptos stood trial before the Iconoclast emperors Leo V the Armenian and Theophilus, fearlessly rebuking their heresy.
[^390]: LXX Psalm 41:5 [MT Ps. 42:4]: "The voice of rejoicing and thanksgiving, the sound of one that keeps festival."
[^391]: LXX Psalm 18:5 [MT Ps. 19:4]; Romans 10:18.
[^392]: Galatians 6:17: "For I bear in my body the marks of the Lord Jesus."
[^393]: Cf. Matthew 22:11–13 regarding the defiled wedding garment; and the baptismal garment of incorruption.
[^394]: LXX Psalm 18:5 [MT Ps. 19:4]; Romans 10:18.
[^395]: LXX Psalm 18:2 [MT Ps. 19:1].
[^396]: The Seventh Ecumenical Council (Second Council of Nicaea, 787) is traditionally reckoned as comprising 365 or 367 Holy Fathers, convened under the Empress Irene and Patriarch Tarasius.
[^397]: Constantine V "Copronymus" (reigned 741–775), the Byzantine emperor who aggressively promoted Iconoclasm and convened the Council of Hieria in 754.
[^398]: Referring to the Iconoclast Council of Hieria (754), anathematized as a robber council by Nicaea II.
[^399]: The standard liturgical rule for the commemoration of the Holy Fathers of the Seventh Ecumenical Council: it is always kept on the Sunday falling between October 11 and 17 inclusive.
[^400]: Patriarch Germanus I of Constantinople (patriarch 715–730) or Germanus the New (Germanus II, patriarch 1223–1240), an ardent defender of icon veneration and the Ecumenical Councils.
[^401]: Cf. Exodus 20:8–11; Hebrews 8:5; 10:1.
[^402]: Genesis 2:2–3.
[^403]: The *Horos* (Definitio fidei) of the Seventh Ecumenical Council, establishing the Orthodox doctrine on the veneration (*proskynesis*) of holy icons.
[^404]: A patristic symbolic synthesis connecting the four physical elements (earth, air, fire, water) and the Trinity to the completion of the Seven (3 + 4) Ecumenical Councils.
[^405]: 4 Kingdoms 4:34–35 [2 Kings 4:34–35]: Elisha resurrecting the Shunammite's son, who sneezed seven times.
[^406]: The Seven Ecumenical Councils together overthrew Arius and all subsequent heresiarchs who assailed the divinity, humanity, or icons of the Incarnate Word.
[^407]: The famous vision of St. Peter of Alexandria, who saw Christ as a youth in a torn tunic and asked: "Lord, who hath torn Thy garment?" To which Christ replied: "Arius the senseless one, who hath separated My people from Me."
[^408]: The First Ecumenical Council of Nicaea (325), whose Creed confessed Christ as consubstantial (*homoousios*) with the Father.
[^409]: Genesis 14:14. The 318 servants of Abraham are traditionally interpreted as a prophetic type of the 318 Holy Fathers of the First Ecumenical Council of Nicaea (325).
[^410]: Genesis 14:15–20. Melchizedek is the royal and priestly type of Christ (Hebrews 7:1–17).
[^411]: Deuteronomy 1:8–17. Appointed for the Fathers because of the institution of righteous judges and rulers in the assembly of God.
[^412]: Deuteronomy 10:18–21.
[^413]: The holy fathers anathematized the chief heresiarchs: Arius (Arianism), Macedonius (Pneumatomachianism), Nestorius (Nestorianism), Eutyches and Dioscorus (Monophysitism), Sabellius (Modalism), and Severus of Antioch ("the Acephalite" or headless one).
[^414]: The Nicene Symbol of Faith (*Symbolum Nicaenum*), promulgated by the 318 Fathers in 325 and confirmed by the subsequent Ecumenical Councils.
[^415]: The Troparion of the Holy Fathers of the Ecumenical Councils (Tone 8), used for the Fathers of the First Six Councils and the Seventh Council.
[^416]: Exodus 14:16–28; 15:1–21.
[^417]: Isaiah 11:2 (the seven gifts of the Holy Spirit); Acts 2:3–4 (tongues of fire at Pentecost).
[^418]: Genesis 2:2. The Seventh Ecumenical Council is celebrated as bringing the complete fullness and cessation of major Christological heresies.
[^419]: Both the First Ecumenical Council (325) against Arius and the Seventh Ecumenical Council (787) against the Iconoclasts were convened in the same city of Nicaea in Bithynia.
[^420]: St. John of Damascus' defense of iconography: "I do not worship matter, I worship the Creator of matter, Who became matter for my sake and deigned to inhabit matter, Who through matter effected my salvation."
[^421]: LXX Psalm 135:5–6 [MT Ps. 136:5–6]; 1 Kingdoms 2:2 [1 Samuel 2:2].
[^422]: Matthew 16:18: "And the gates of hell shall not prevail against it."
[^423]: Isaiah 12:3: "Therefore with joy shall ye draw water out of the wells of salvation."
[^424]: The Empress Irene (regent 780–790, empress regnant 797–802) and her young son Emperor Constantine VI convoked the Seventh Ecumenical Council at Nicaea in 787.
[^425]: An anathema against the unrepentant iconoclasts who refuse to venerate the icon of the Theotokos or confess the Incarnation.
[^426]: Sessional Hymn (Kathisma) of the Holy Fathers of the Seventh Ecumenical Council (Tone 4).
[^427]: Habakkuk 3:2; LXX Psalm 17:2 [MT Ps. 18:1].
[^428]: The Iconoclasts claimed to honor the symbol of the Cross while tearing down and desecrating the icons of Christ, the Theotokos, and the saints.
[^429]: Joshua 6:4–20.
[^430]: 1 Maccabees 7:43; 2 Maccabees 15:28–35 (the defeat and death of Nicanor the blasphemer who threatened the temple).
[^431]: LXX Psalm 42:2; 87:15 [MT Ps. 43:2; 88:14]; Isaiah 26:9.
[^432]: The anathemas pronounced by the Seventh Ecumenical Council in 787 against all who refuse to venerate the holy icons of Christ, the Theotokos, and the saints.
[^433]: The Seventh Ecumenical Council (Nicaea II, 787) affirmed the veneration of icons and condemned iconoclasm.
[^434]: Theological doctrine of the circumscription (*περιγραφή*) of the Word: uncircumscribed in His divine nature, but circumscribed and depictable in His assumed humanity.
[^435]: Cf. Joel 3:18 and Amos 9:13: "The mountains shall drop sweetness, and the hills shall flow with milk."
[^436]: Allusion to Psalm 44:11–14 [LXX] regarding the King's daughter, applied typologically to the Theotokos and the Church.
[^437]: The Kontakion of the Seventh Council follows the melody of the Kontakion of the Three Holy Children (*Χειρόγραφον εἰκόνα* / *Рꙋкопи́саннагѡ ѡ҆́браза*).
[^438]: The Ikos expounds the Seventh Council's decree: honor paid to the icon passes to the prototype, teaching faith through visual memory.
[^439]: St. Basil the Great, *On the Holy Spirit*, 18.45: "For the honor paid to the image passes to the prototype" (*ἡ γὰρ τῆς εἰκόνος τιμὴ ἐπὶ τὸ πρωτότυπον διαβαίνει*).
[^440]: Slavonic: *я́коже речѐ вели́кїй васї́лїй* ("even as the Great Basil said"), referencing the classical Patristic definition of icon veneration cited at Nicaea II.
[^441]: Cf. Psalm 44:3 [LXX]: "Fairer in beauty art Thou than the sons of men; grace is poured upon Thy lips."
[^442]: Co-worshipped (*спокланя́ема* / *συμπροσκυνουμένη*): in accordance with Orthodox iconographic theology, the Mother of God holding the divine Infant is venerated together with Him in relative honor (*proskynesis*).
[^443]: Daniel 3:19–23; 3:57 [LXX] (The Song of the Three Holy Children).
[^444]: The sevenfold tearing down of heretical pride corresponds to the seven Ecumenical Councils of the Church.
[^445]: Cf. Psalm 63:8 [LXX]: "The shafts of infants have become their wounds."
[^446]: Triadological confession: the consubstantial Trinity is the sole unoriginate source and cause of all creation and goodness.
[^447]: Heirmos of Ode 9 of the Nativity and Theotokos canons, celebrating the womb of the Virgin as wider than the heavens (*platytera ton ouranon*).
[^448]: Cf. 1 Corinthians 13:12: "For now we see through a glass, darkly; but then face to face."
[^449]: The Pochaiv Menaion includes a petition for the King (Augustus III, King of Poland and Grand Duke of Lithuania, r. 1734–1763), reflecting the sovereign prayer of the Polish-Lithuanian Commonwealth under whose rule the Basilian printing house operated.
[^450]: Eschatological petition commemorating the unified legacy of all Seven Ecumenical Councils.
[^451]: The Nicene-Constantinopolitan Creed (Symbol of Faith) formulated at Nicaea I (325) and Constantinople I (381), reaffirmed by the subsequent councils including Nicaea II (787).
[^452]: The Nicene Creed dogmatizes the co-eternity and consubstantiality (*homoousion*) of the Son with the Father against Arius and subsequent trinitarian heresies.
[^453]: Daniel 3:27 [LXX]: "For Thou art righteous in all that Thou hast done for us."
[^454]: Hebrews 13:7–16: standard Epistle reading for the Holy Fathers ("Remember your leaders, those who spoke to you the word of God...").
[^455]: John 17:1–13: the High Priestly Prayer of Christ, standard Gospel pericope for the Sunday of the Holy Fathers.
[^456]: St. Cosmas the Melodist (c. 675–760), foster brother of St. John Damascene, monk of Mar Saba, hymnographer of the Great Feasts, and Bishop of Maiuma near Gaza.
[^457]: Ss. Tarachos, Probus, and Andronikos suffered martyrdom at Tarsus in Cilicia under Emperor Diocletian (c. 304).
[^458]: Hymn attributed to Anatolios, Byzantine hymnographer of the Studite tradition.
[^459]: Attributed to St. Andrew of Jerusalem (St. Andrew of Crete, c. 650–740), author of the Great Canon.
[^460]: Liturgical antithesis of the Crucifixion: He Who hung the earth upon the waters is Himself suspended upon the wood of the Cross.
[^461]: Common troparion of holy hierarchs (Tone 8: *Правосла́вїя наста́вниче*).
[^462]: St. Joseph the Hymnographer (c. 816–886), author of the Canon to the Holy Martyrs Tarachos, Probus, and Andronikos.
[^463]: Etymological wordplay on the name *Cosmas* (*κόσμος*, order/adornment): "for an adornment (*украшение*) of hierarchs wast thou shown forth."
[^464]: Psalm 57:7 [LXX]: "God hath crushed their teeth in their mouth; the Lord hath broken the molars of the lions."
[^465]: Tone 8 Podoben *Взбра́нной воево́дѣ* (*Τῇ ὑπερμάχῳ*), the opening prooimion of the Akathist to the Mother of God.
[^466]: Tone 8 Sessional Hymn model *Премꙋ́дрости и҆ Сло́во* (*Τὴν Σοφίαν καὶ Λόγον*).
[^467]: Classical Byzantine Stavrotheotokion portraying the maternal sorrow of the Virgin at the foot of the Cross, contrasted with the redemption of the world.
[^468]: Habakkuk 3:2–19 [LXX] (Biblical Canticle 4).
[^469]: Slavonic: *копрони́мовыми и҆ блѧди́выми* ("the blasphemous and wanton prattlings of Copronymus"), directly referencing Byzantine Emperor Constantine V Copronymus (r. 741–775), leader of the violent iconoclast persecution, whose theological doctrines St. Cosmas refuted through his festal hymnography.
[^470]: St. Cosmas' explicit Chalcedonian confession: Christ the Word incarnate in two distinct natures united in one divine hypostasis (*во двꙋ̀ є҆стества́хъ, є҆ди́нѣй же ѵ҆поста́си*).
[^471]: Heirmos of Ode 5 based on Isaiah 26:9 and Psalm 87 [LXX].
[^472]: Allusion to John 12:46: "I am come a light into the world, that whosoever believes on me should not abide in darkness."
[^473]: Comprehensive catalogue of St. Cosmas' major liturgical masterworks: the canons for Theophany (*Глꙋбины̀ ѿкры́лъ є҆́сть дно̀*), the Meeting of the Lord (Hypapante: *Сꙋ́шꙋ глꙋбороди́тельнꙋю зе́млѧ*), Lazarus Saturday, Palm Sunday, and the Exaltation of the Holy Cross.
[^474]: Tone 2 Kontakion of the Holy Martyrs Tarachos, Probus, and Andronikos, following the melodic pattern *Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀* (*Τὰ ἄνω ζητῶν*).
[^475]: Daniel 3:49–52 [LXX] (The Song of the Three Holy Children in the Furnace).
[^476]: Daniel 3:52–56 [LXX] (Song of the Three Holy Children: "Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy holy glory").
[^477]: Psalm 44:2 [LXX]: "My heart hath poured forth a good word... my tongue is the pen of a quickly writing scribe."
[^478]: Daniel 3:19, 57 [LXX].
[^479]: Refers to St. Cosmas' great Heirmological canon for Great and Holy Thursday (*Сѣ́ченое сѣче́тсѧ*), especially the hymns commemorating the Mystical Supper and condemning the betrayal of Judas Iscariot.
[^480]: Catalogue of St. Cosmas' famous Holy Week and Festal Canons: Holy Friday (*Къ тебѣ̀ ᲂу҆́тренюю*), Holy Saturday (*Волно́ю морско́ю*), and the Transfiguration of the Lord on Mount Tabor (*Ли́цы і҆сра́ильтестїи*).
[^481]: Isaiah 11:1: "There shall come forth a rod out of the root of Jesse, and a blossom shall come up from his root."
[^482]: Daniel 2:34, 45: typological prophecy of the unhewn stone (*λίθος ἄνευ χειρῶν*) cut from the mountain, prefiguring the virginal birth of Christ without human seed.
[^483]: Liturgical pericopes for the commemoration of the Holy Martyrs Tarachos, Probus, and Andronikos: Prokeimenon (Psalm 15:3, 8 [LXX]); Epistle Ephesians 6:10–17; Alleluia (Psalm 33:18 [LXX]); Gospel Luke 12:8–12; Communion Verse (Psalm 32:1 [LXX]).
[^484]: Holy Martyrs Carpus (Bishop of Thyatira or Gordos in Lydia), Papylas (deacon), Agathodoros (servant), and Agathonike (sister of Papylas), martyred under Emperor Decius (c. 250) or Marcus Aurelius.
[^485]: Acts 7:54–60: St. Papylas, holding the diaconal rank, emulates the Protomartyr Archdeacon Stephen in bearing witness through stoning.
[^486]: Vision of the opened heavens and Christ upon the throne witnessed by St. Carpus, famously recounted in the corpus of Pseudo-Dionysius the Areopagite (*Epistle VIII, To Demophilus*, PG 3:1097–1100).
[^487]: Acrostic of the canon by St. Joseph the Hymnographer: *Ка́рпа и҆ папѵ́ла пѣ́сньми вѣнча́ю. І҆ѡ́сифово* ("I crown Carpus and Papylas with hymns. Joseph's").
[^488]: Exodus 15:6 [LXX] (The First Canticle of Moses: The Song of the Sea).
[^489]: St. Agathodoros, faithful Christian servant of Ss. Carpus and Papylas, tortured to death with ox-hide scourges prior to their execution.
[^490]: Tone 4 Sessional Hymn model *Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ* (*Τοῦ λίθου σφραγισθέντος*).
[^491]: Habakkuk 3:3 [LXX] (Biblical Canticle 4).
[^492]: Romans 8:35: "Who shall separate us from the love of Christ? Shall tribulation, or distress, or persecution, or famine, or nakedness, or peril, or sword?"
[^493]: St. Agathonike, sister of St. Papylas, who voluntarily ran to the place of martyrdom upon seeing the glory prepared for the martyrs, exclaiming: "This banquet is prepared for me also; I must partake of this glorious supper!"
[^494]: Tone 4 Kontakion of the Holy Martyrs Carpus and Papylas, following the melody *Вознесы́йсѧ на крⷭ҇тъ* (*Ὁ ὑψωθεὶς ἐν τῷ Σταυρῷ*).
[^495]: Daniel 3:49–50; the womb of the Mother of God typologically identified as the mystical/spiritual furnace (*νοητὴ κάμινος*) which contained the divine fire without being consumed.
[^496]: Daniel 3:57 [LXX] (The Song of the Three Holy Children in the Babylonian furnace: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and supremely exalt Him unto all ages").
[^497]: Exodus 3:2 [LXX]: The Unburnt Bush (*ἡ βάτος ἡ ἀκατάφλεκτος*) on Mount Horeb as a pre-eminent Marian type of the Virginal conception and birth of Christ without corruption.
[^498]: Patristic Christological confession of Christ as "double yet simple" (*сꙋ́гꙋба про́ста ви́димаго* / *διπλοῦν τῇ φύσει, ἁπλοῦν τῇ ὑποστάσει*): two distinct natures (divine and human) united in the single hypostasis of the Word.
[^499]: Commemoration of the Holy Martyrs Nazarius, Gervasius, Protasius, and Celsus of Milan, martyred under Nero (c. 68), whose incorrupt relics were miraculously uncovered by St. Ambrose of Milan in 395; and our Venerable Mother Parasceve the Younger of Epibatæ (11th century).
[^500]: Stavrotheotokion contrasting Christ's voluntary death and "sleep" upon the Cross with the spiritual lethargy and death brought into the world by Adam's transgression in Paradise.
[^501]: Canon composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer (*Ἰωσήφ*), bearing in the original Greek the alphabetic acrostic: *Πιστῶς γεραίρω τοὺς στεφηφόρους νέους* ("Faithfully do I honor the crown-bearing youths"), rendered literally into Church Slavonic as *Вѣ́рнѡ воспою̀ мꙋ́ченники вѣнцено́сныѧ*.
[^502]: Heirmos of Ode 1: the standard Byzantine festal Heirmos of the Theotokos (*Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου* / *Ѡ҆тве́рзꙋ ᲂу҆ста̀ моѧ̑*).
[^503]: Heirmos of Ode 3: *Τοὺς σοὺς ὑμνολόγους Θεοτόκε* (*Твоѧ̑ пѣсносло́вцы бцⷣе*).
[^504]: Exodus 25–26: The Mosaic tabernacle (*ἡ σκηνή τοῦ μαρτυρίου*) as an Old Testament typological prefiguration of the Mother of God holding the divine Presence.
[^505]: Heirmos of Ode 4: based on Habakkuk 3:2–3 and Isaiah 19:1 (*Ὁ καθήμενος ἐν δόξῃ* / *Сѣдѧ́й въ сла́вѣ*).
[^506]: Heirmos of Ode 5: *Ἐξέστη τὰ σύμπαντα* (*О҆у҆жасо́шася всѧ́ческаѧ*).
[^507]: Genesis 3:24 and John 19:34: The flaming sword guarding the gates of Eden turns aside before the faithful who are redeemed by the blood of Christ.
[^508]: Heirmos of Ode 6: *Τὴν θείαν ταύτην καὶ πάντιμον* (*Бж҃е́ственное сїѐ и҆ всечⷭ҇тно́е*).
[^509]: Psalm 76:20 [LXX]: "Thy way is in the sea, and Thy paths in many waters, and Thy footsteps shall not be known."
[^510]: Daniel 3:28 and Romans 1:25: *Οὐκ ἐλάτρευσαν τῇ κτίσει* (*Не послꙋжи́ша тва́ри*).
[^511]: Heirmos of Ode 8: based on Daniel 3:49–57 (*Παῖδας εὐαγεῖς ἐν τῇ καμίνῳ* / *О҆́троки бл҃гочести̑выѧ въ пещѝ*).
[^512]: Historical translation of relics: St. Nazarius' relics were initially discovered and venerated in Milan by St. Ambrose, and portions were subsequently translated to Rome and Constantinople (at the Church of the Holy Apostles under Emperor Arcadius).
[^513]: Heirmos of Ode 9: *Εὔα μὲν τῷ τῆς παρακοῆς νοσήματι* (*Є҆́ѵа ᲂу҆́бѡ недꙋ́гомъ преслꙋша́нїа*).
[^514]: Luke 1:28: The Angelic Salutation of the Archangel Gabriel (*Χαῖρε, κεχαριτωμένη* / *Ра́дꙋйся, ѡ҆брадо́ваннаѧ*).
[^515]: Patristic Marian confession of the Perpetual Virginity of the Mother of God: *ante partum, in partu, post partum* (Virgin before birth, in birth, and after birth).
[^516]: St. Parasceve the Younger (Petka) of Epibatæ (Thrace), renowned 11th-century ascetic whose relics were venerated in Veliko Tarnovo, Belgrade, and later Iași (Moldavia), widely celebrated across the Balkans and Ukraine.
[^517]: Rubric permitting the elevation of the service of St. Parasceve from a simple six-sticheron commemoration (*на ѕ҃*) to a full festal Polyeleos service with Old Testament readings and Great Doxology according to the discretion of the superior (*настоѧ́тель*) or the patronal feast of the temple.
[^518]: Kallikrateia (on the Sea of Marmara near Epibatæ), where St. Parasceve spent her final years in solitary asceticism at the Church of the Holy Apostles until her blessed dormition.
[^519]: Miracle of St. Parasceve multiplying grain during famine for the relief of the poor and monastic communities.
[^520]: Stavrotheotokion based on Job 26:7 ("He hangs the earth upon nothing") applied to Christ upon the Cross.
[^521]: Wisdom of Solomon 5:15–16 [LXX], the standard festal paremia for the commemoration of monastics and venerable saints (*Чте́нїе преподо́бническое*).
[^522]: Wisdom of Solomon 5:17–6:3 (LXX). This concludes the first festal reading for the Venerable Parasceve commenced on fol. 74r.
[^523]: Wisdom of Solomon 3:1–9 (LXX). Second festal reading at Great Vespers.
[^524]: Wisdom of Solomon 4:7–15 (LXX). Third festal reading at Great Vespers, continued on fol. 75r.
[^525]: Model melody: Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on high" (*Зва́нный свы́ше*).
[^526]: Doxasticon at the Aposticha, Tone 2.
[^527]: Stavrotheotokion, Tone 1, Podoben: "When from the tree" (*Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва*).
[^528]: Troparion of our Venerable Mother Parasceve of the Balkans, Tone 4.
[^529]: Sessional Hymn after the first Kathisma, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (*Ско́рѡ предварѝ*).
[^530]: Magnification (*velichaniye*) for a Venerable Woman.
[^531]: Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos, Tone 8.
[^532]: Sticheron after Psalm 50, Tone 8.
[^533]: Canon 1, Ode 1, Heirmos, Tone 6: "Having traversed the deep" (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ по сꙋ́хꙋ пѣшеше́ствовавъ*).
[^534]: Canon 2, Ode 1, Heirmos, Tone 8: "Having traversed the water" (*Во́дꙋ проше́дъ я҆́кѡ сꙋ́шꙋ*).
[^535]: Canon 1, Ode 3, Heirmos, Tone 6: "There is none holy" (*Нѣ́сть ст҃ъ я҆́коже ты̀*).
[^536]: Canon 2, Ode 3, Heirmos, Tone 8: "Thou art the establishment" (*Ты̀ є҆сѝ ᲂу҆твержде́нїе*).
[^537]: Sessional Hymn after Ode 3, Tone 4.
[^538]: Stavrotheotokion after Ode 3.
[^539]: Canon 1, Ode 4, Heirmos, Tone 6: "Christ is my power" (*Хрⷭ҇то́съ моѧ̀ си́ла*).
[^540]: Canon 2, Ode 4, Heirmos, Tone 8: "I heard, O Lord" (*Оу҆слы́шахъ гдⷭ҇и смотре́нїа*).
[^541]: Canon 1, Ode 5, Heirmos, Tone 6: "With Thy divine light" (*Бж҃їимъ свѣ́томъ твои́мъ*).
[^542]: Canon 2, Ode 5, Heirmos, Tone 8: "Rising early at dawn" (*Оу҆́тренююще вопїе́мъ тѝ*).
[^543]: Canon 1, Ode 6, Heirmos, Tone 6: "Beholding the sea of life" (*Жите́йское мо́ре*).
[^544]: The hymnographer explicitly references the Iconodule defense of holy icons against the iconoclasts, reflecting the 8th-century and 9th-century context often interwoven into Byzantine hymnody.
[^545]: Canon 2, Ode 6, Heirmos, Tone 8: "I will pour out my prayer" (*Мл҃твꙋ пролїю̀ ко гдⷭ҇ꙋ*).
[^546]: Reference to the translation of the relics of St. Parasceve westward unto Epivatai, Trnovo, and the Balkan lands.
[^547]: Festal Kontakion of our Venerable Mother Parasceve, Tone 6.
[^548]: Festal Ikos of our Venerable Mother Parasceve, continued on fol. 78v.
[^549]: Canon 1, Ode 7, Heirmos, Tone 6: "The Angel made the furnace" (*Росода́тельнꙋ ᲂу҆́бѡ пе́щь*).
[^550]: Canon 2, Ode 7, Heirmos, Tone 8: "The Hebrew Children" (*Ѿтроцы є҆вре́йстїи*).
[^551]: Commemoration of the invention and translation of the incorrupt relics of St. Parasceve.
[^552]: Canon 1, Ode 8, Heirmos, Tone 6: "Out of the flame" (*И҆з̾ пла́мене преподо́бнымъ*).
[^553]: Canon 2, Ode 8, Heirmos, Tone 8: "The Heavenly King" (*Цр҃ѧ нбⷭ҇наго*).
[^554]: Miraculous healing of a long-paralyzed man recorded in the Slavic vita and Synaxarion of St. Parasceve.
[^555]: Canon 1, Ode 9, Heirmos, Tone 6: "It is impossible for men to see God" (*Бг҃а чл҃вѣ́кѡмъ невозмо́жно ви́дѣти*).
[^556]: The Pochaiv printing explicitly preserves the royal intercession for the monarch (*коро́лю на́шемꋋ на сопроти́вныѧ да́ждь побѣ́ды*), reflecting the 18th-century Ruthenian Greek Catholic ecclesiastical context under the Polish Crown (King Augustus III of Poland).
[^557]: Canon 2, Ode 9, Heirmos, Tone 8: "Truly we confess thee" (*Вои́стиннꙋ бцⷣꙋ тѧ̀ и҆сповѣ́дꙋемъ*).
[^558]: Stavrotheotokion/Theotokion of compunction following the Exapostilarion, invoking deliverance at the Dread Judgment.
[^559]: Doxasticon of our Venerable Mother Parasceve at the Praises, Tone 8.
[^560]: Theotokion formula concluding the Praises: *Владычице, прїими молитву рабъ твоихъ* (per Oktoechos).
[^561]: Stavrotheotokion at the Aposticha, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (*Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва*).
[^562]: Epistle reading for the Venerable Nun Parasceve: Galatians 3:23–29 (Pericope 208).
[^563]: Alleluia verses: Psalm 39:2 [LXX 39:1].
[^564]: Liturgical readings for the Divine Liturgy of October 14: Gospel according to Matthew 25:1–13 (Pericope 104); Communion Hymn: Psalm 32:1 [LXX 32:1].
[^565]: Feast of the Venerable Father Euthymius the New (the Younger of Mount Athos/Thessalonica, d. 898) and the Holy Venerable-Martyr Lucian, Presbyter of Antioch (d. 312).
[^566]: St. Lucian's body was cast into the sea with a stone around his neck, and after thirty days dolphins brought his holy relics ashore for honorable burial by Christians.
[^567]: Doxasticon of St. Euthymius at "Lord, I Have Cried," Tone 6.
[^568]: Stavrotheotokion at "Lord, I Have Cried," Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (*Тридне́венъ*).
[^569]: Doxasticon of St. Euthymius at the Aposticha, Tone 5.
[^570]: Stavrotheotokion at the Aposticha, Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice" (*Ра́дꙋйсѧ*).
[^571]: Common Troparion of a Venerable Monk, Tone 8: "In thee, O father, was preserved" (*Въ тебѣ̀ ѻ҆́ч҃е и҆звѣ́стнѡ сп҃се́ся*).
[^572]: Common Troparion of a Holy Martyr, Tone 4: "Thy martyr, O Lord" (*Мч҃еникъ тво́й гдⷭ҇и*).
[^573]: Canon 1, Ode 1, Heirmos, Tone 2: "Come, ye peoples, let us sing" (*Гради́те лю́дїе , пои́мъ пѣ́снь*).
[^574]: Canon 2, Ode 1, Heirmos, Tone 4: "I will sing unto Thee, O Lord" (*Вспою̀ тебѣ̀ гдⷭ҇и бж҃е мо́й*).
[^575]: Canon 1, Ode 3, Heirmos, Tone 2: "Establishing me upon the rock of faith" (*На ка́мени мѧ̀ вѣ́ры ᲂу҆тверди́въ*).
[^576]: Canon 2, Ode 3, Heirmos, Tone 4: "The bow of the mighty has grown weak" (*Лꙋ́къ си́лныхъ и҆знемо́же*).
[^577]: Kontakion of the Holy Venerable-Martyr Lucian, Presbyter of Great Antioch, Tone 2, Podoben: "In prayers" (*Въ мл҃твахъ*).
[^578]: Sessional Hymn of the Venerable Father Euthymius, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (*Премꙋ́дрости*).
[^579]: Sessional Theotokion of compunction, Tone 8.
[^580]: Sessional Stavrotheotokion, Tone 8.
[^581]: Canon 1, Ode 4, Heirmos, Tone 2: "I sing unto Thee, for by report" (*Пою́ тѧ , слꙋ́хомъ бо гдⷭ҇и*).
[^582]: The hymnographer refers to St. Euthymius leaving his wife and secular marriage to embrace the solitary monastic life on Mount Olympus and later Mount Athos.
[^583]: Canon 2, Ode 4, Heirmos, Tone 4: "He Who sits in glory" (*Сѣдѧ́й во сла́вѣ*).
[^584]: Canon 1, Ode 5, Heirmos, Tone 2: "O Christ my Savior, the Enlightenment" (*Просвѣще́нїе во тмѣ̀ лежа́щихъ*).
[^585]: Canon 2, Ode 5, Heirmos, Tone 4: "The ungodly shall not see Thy glory" (*Нечести́вїи не ᲂу҆́зрѧтъ сла́вы твоеѧ̀*).
[^586]: Canon 1, Ode 6, Heirmos, Tone 2: "From the whale Jonah cried aloud" (*Ко гдⷭ҇ꙋ ѿ ки́та і҆ѡ́на возопѝ*).
[^587]: The hymnographer plays upon the etymology of the Greek name Euthymius (*Εὐθύμιος*, from *εὖ* "good/well" and *θυμός* "spirit/mind", here glossed as *бл҃годѣ́йствїѧ тезоимени́те*).
[^588]: The Prophet Habakkuk's vision of the "overshadowed mountain" (Habakkuk 3:3) as a prophetic archetype of the Incarnation of Christ from the Virgin Theotokos.
[^589]: Canon 2, Ode 6, Heirmos, Tone 4: "I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise" (*Пожрꙋ́ ти со гла́сомъ хвале́нїѧ*).
[^590]: Allusion to the preservation and retrieval of St. Lucian's incorrupt body from the sea after thirty days.
[^591]: Festal Kontakion of our Venerable Father Euthymius the New, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (*Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀*).
[^592]: Festal Ikos of our Venerable Father Euthymius the New.
[^593]: Canon 1, Ode 7, Heirmos, Tone 2: "The unburned bush upon the mountain" (*Кꙋпина̀ въ горѣ̀ ѻ҆гненеѡпа́лнаѧ*).
[^594]: Continuation of Canon 1, Ode 7, Heirmos, Tone 2: "The unburned bush upon the mountain" (*Кꙋпина̀ въ горѣ̀ ѻ҆гненеѡпа́лнаѧ*), concluded from physical leaf p170.
[^595]: An allusion to Moses on Mount Sinai gazing upon the "back parts of God" (LXX Exodus 33:23).
[^596]: Canon 2, Ode 7, Heirmos, Tone 4: "He Who saved Thy Abrahamic children in the fire" (*Спасы́й во ѻ҆гнѝ а҆враа́мскїѧ твоѧ̑ ѻ҆́троки*).
[^597]: Slavonic *недобмѣ́нну* (a graphic corruption or dialectal form for *необмѣ́нну* / *недоумѣ́нну*, collating Greek *ἀναλλοίωτον* "immutable/unchanging").
[^598]: Slavonic *пѣ́снопо́лче* (vocative of *пѣснопо́лецъ*, translating Greek *μελῳδέ* "melodist / chanter / singer of sacred hymns").
[^599]: Canon 1, Ode 8, Heirmos, Tone 2: "Unto God Who descended into the fiery furnace" (*Въ пе́щь ѻ҆́гненнꙋю ко ѻ҆трокѡ́мъ є҆вре́йскимъ снизше́дшаго*).
[^600]: Allusion to the translation of the Patriarch Enoch without seeing death (Genesis 5:24; Hebrews 11:5).
[^601]: Canon 2, Ode 8, Heirmos, Tone 4: "The Offspring of the Birthgiver of God saved the pious youths in the furnace" (*О҆́троки бл҃гочести̑выѧ въ пещѝ*).
[^602]: Further allusion to the recovery of the incorrupt relics of the hieromartyr Lucian from the Black Sea.
[^603]: Canon 1, Ode 9, Heirmos, Tone 2: "God the Word of God, Who by ineffable wisdom" (*Бг҃а бг҃а сло́ва , неизрече́нною мꙋ́дростїю*).
[^604]: Canon 2, Ode 9, Heirmos, Tone 4: "Eve indeed through the disease of disobedience" (*Є҆́ѵа ᲂу҆́бѡ недꙋ́гомъ преслꙋша́нїа*).
[^605]: Exapostilarion of our Venerable Father Euthymius the New, Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (*Же́ны ᲂу҆слы́шите*).
[^606]: Doxasticon at the Aposticha for our Venerable Father Euthymius the New, Tone 2.
[^607]: Stavrotheotokion at the Aposticha, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (*Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва*).
[^608]: Feast of the Holy Martyr Longinus the Centurion, who stood at the foot of the Cross of Christ (October 16).
[^609]: An allusion to the confession of faith uttered by the Roman centurion at the Crucifixion (LXX Matthew 27:54; Mark 15:39).
[^610]: Allusion to the traditional hagiographical account of the recovery of St. Longinus' severed head in Jerusalem by a blind widow, whose sight was miraculously restored upon touching it.
[^611]: Festal Doxasticon at "Lord, I have cried" for the Holy Martyr Longinus the Centurion, composed by St. John of Damascus (*John the Monk*), Tone 6.
[^612]: Conclusion of the Stavrotheotokion from physical leaf p175.
[^613]: Doxasticon at the Aposticha for the Holy Martyr Longinus, Tone 6, by St. John of Damascus (*John the Monk*), alluding to the words of the Good Thief (Luke 23:42).
[^614]: Stavrotheotokion at the Aposticha, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (*Трїдне́венъ*).
[^615]: General Troparion of a Martyr, Tone 4.
[^616]: Canon of the Holy Martyr Longinus, composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer, Tone 4, bearing the acrostic: *Ло́ггїновꙋ вели́кꙋю воспою̀ сла́вꙋ . І҆ѡ́сифъ* ("I will hymn the great glory of Longinus. Joseph").
[^617]: Canon of the Holy Martyr Longinus, Ode 1, Heirmos, Tone 4: "The Israel of old, traversing the deep of the Red Sea" (*Мо́рѧ чермнꙋ́ю пꙋчи́нꙋ*).
[^618]: Canon of the Holy Martyr Longinus, Ode 3, Heirmos, Tone 4: "Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ" (*Весели́тсѧ ѡ҆ тебѣ̀ це́рковь твоѧ̀ хрⷭ҇тѐ*).
[^619]: Conclusion of Ode 3 Troparion from physical leaf p176.
[^620]: Festal Sessional Hymn for the Holy Martyr Longinus, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (*Премꙋ́дрости*).
[^621]: Stavrotheotokion following the Sessional Hymn, Tone 8.
[^622]: Canon of the Holy Martyr Longinus, Ode 4, Heirmos, Tone 4: "Beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross" (*Вознесе́на тѧ̀ ви́дѣвши це́рковь на крⷭ҇тѣ̀*).
[^623]: Explicit dogmatic formulation of the Sixth Ecumenical Council (Constantinople III, 680–681) affirming Christ's two natural wills and two natural energies (*сꙋгꙋ́ба хотѣ́ньми и҆ дѣ́йствы* / Greek *διπλοῦν ταῖς θελήσεσι καὶ ταῖς ἐνεργείαις*).
[^624]: Canon of the Holy Martyr Longinus, Ode 5, Heirmos, Tone 4: "Thou, my Lord, camest as a Light into the world" (*Ты́ гдⷭ҇и мо́й свѣ́тъ въ мі́ръ прише́лъ є҆сѝ*).
[^625]: Canon of the Holy Martyr Longinus, Ode 6, Heirmos, Tone 4: "I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise" (*Пожрꙋ́ ти со гла́сомъ хвале́нїа гдⷭ҇и*).
[^626]: Kontakion of the Holy Martyr Longinus the Centurion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (*Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ*).
[^627]: Conclusion of the Kontakion of the Holy Martyr Longinus from physical leaf p178.
[^628]: Ikos of the Holy Martyr Longinus the Centurion.
[^629]: Canon of the Holy Martyr Longinus, Ode 7, Heirmos, Tone 4: "In the Persian furnace the Abrahamic youths" (*Въ пещѝ а҆враа́мстїи ѻ҆́троцы , персі́дстѣй*).
[^630]: Canon of the Holy Martyr Longinus, Ode 8, Heirmos, Tone 4: "Daniel, having stretched forth his hands" (*Рꙋ́цѣ распросте́ръ данїи́лъ*).
[^631]: Allusion to the piercing of the side of Christ on the Cross by the Roman lance (John 19:34).
[^632]: Conclusion of Ode 8 Troparion from physical leaf p179.
[^633]: Canon of the Holy Martyr Longinus, Ode 9, Heirmos, Tone 4: "Christ the Cornerstone, Who united the separated natures" (*Ка́мень нерꙋкосѣ́чный ѿ несѣко́мыѧ горы̀*).
[^634]: Exapostilarion of the Holy Martyr Longinus the Centurion.
[^635]: Theotokion following the Exapostilarion of the Holy Martyr Longinus, continuing onto physical leaf p181.
[^636]: Fol. 89v. Conclusion of the Theotokion of the Exapostilarion for St. Longinus the Centurion (Oct 16).
[^637]: An allusion to the symbolic prophetic marriage commanded of Hosea (Hosea 1:2–3).
[^638]: Cf. Luke 9:62. The sticheron continues onto physical leaf p182 (Fol. 90).
[^639]: Fol. 90. Conclusion of the first sticheron at "Lord, I Have Cried" for the Venerable-Martyr Andrew of Crete.
[^640]: An allusion to Emperor Constantine V Copronymus (reigned 741–775), the iconoclast emperor whom St. Andrew confronted.
[^641]: Ode 1 Heirmos of the Canon of Prophet Hosea (Tone 1, Heirmos: *Помо́гшемꙋ бг҃ꙋ* / *Unto God Who helped*).
[^642]: The troparion continues onto physical leaf p183 (Fol. 90v).
[^643]: Fol. 90v. Conclusion of the second troparion of Ode 1 of the Canon of Prophet Hosea.
[^644]: Allusion to Hosea 11:1: "When Israel was a child, then I loved him, and called my son out of Egypt."
[^645]: Ode 1 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Andrew of Crete (Tone 2, Heirmos: *Во глубинѣ̀ постла̀ и҆ногда̀* / *Of old the overwhelmingly armed power*).
[^646]: Play on the name Andrew (*Ἀνδρέας*), derived from Greek *ἀνδρεία* (manliness, courage).
[^647]: Ode 3 Heirmos of the Canon of Prophet Hosea (Tone 1, Heirmos: *Оу҆твердѝ менѐ хрⷭ҇тѐ* / *Establish me, O Christ*).
[^648]: Allusion to Hosea 1:7: "But I will have mercy upon the house of Judah, and will save them by the Lord their God, and will not save them by bow, nor by sword, nor by battle, by horses, nor by horsemen."
[^649]: The Theotokion continues onto physical leaf p184 (Fol. 91).
[^650]: Fol. 91. Conclusion of the Theotokion of Ode 3 of the Canon of Prophet Hosea.
[^651]: Ode 3 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Andrew of Crete (Tone 2, Heirmos: *На ка́мени мѧ̀ вѣ́ры ᲂу҆тверди́въ* / *Having established me upon the rock of faith*).
[^652]: Kontakion of St. Andrew of Crete (Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" / *Дѣва днесь*).
[^653]: Sessional Hymn of Prophet Hosea (Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" / *Премудрости*).
[^654]: Sessional Hymn of St. Andrew of Crete (Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" / *Вознесыйся*).
[^655]: Fol. 91v. Conclusion of the Theotokion of the Sessional Hymn of St. Andrew of Crete.
[^656]: Ode 4 Heirmos of the Canon of Prophet Hosea (Tone 1, Heirmos: *Разомѣ́хъ всеси́лне* / *I understood, O Almighty One*).
[^657]: Ode 4 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Andrew of Crete (Tone 2, Heirmos: *Прише́лъ є҆сѝ ѿ дв҃ы* / *Thou camest forth from the Virgin*).
[^658]: An allusion to the incorrupt, fragrant relics of St. Andrew of Crete.
[^659]: Ode 5 Heirmos of the Canon of Prophet Hosea (Tone 1, Heirmos: *Ѿ но́щи ᲂу҆́треннюющїе* / *Rising early from the night*).
[^660]: The troparion continues onto physical leaf p186 (Fol. 92).
[^661]: Fol. 92. Conclusion of the first troparion of Ode 5 of the Canon of Prophet Hosea.
[^662]: Allusion to Hosea 13:14: "O death, I will be thy plagues; O grave, I will be thy destruction."
[^663]: Ode 5 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Andrew of Crete (Tone 2, Heirmos: *Просвѣще́нїе во тмѣ̀ лежа́щихъ* / *Enlightenment of those lying in darkness*).
[^664]: Ode 6 Heirmos of the Canon of Prophet Hosea (Tone 1, Heirmos: *Проро́ка спа́слъ є҆сѝ* / *Thou didst save the prophet*).
[^665]: Cf. Hosea 13:14.
[^666]: Ode 6 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Andrew of Crete (Tone 2, Heirmos: *Въ бе́зднѣ грѣхо́внѣй* / *Wallowing in the abyss of sin*). Continues onto physical leaf p187 (Fol. 92v).
[^667]: Fol. 92v. Conclusion of Ode 6 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Andrew of Crete.
[^668]: Kontakion of St. Andrew of Crete (Tone 8, Podoben: "To thee the Champion Leader" / *Взбранной*).
[^669]: Ikos of St. Andrew of Crete, featuring 12 salutations (*Chairetismoi*).
[^670]: Ode 7 Heirmos of the Canon of Prophet Hosea (Tone 1, Heirmos: *А҆враа́мстїи ѻ҆́троцы* / *The Abrahamic youths*).
[^671]: Allusion to Hosea 12:10: "I have also spoken by the prophets, and I have multiplied visions, and used similitudes, by the ministry of the prophets."
[^672]: The troparion continues onto physical leaf p188 (Fol. 93).
[^673]: Fol. 93. Conclusion of the second troparion of Ode 7 of the Canon of Prophet Hosea.
[^674]: Ode 7 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Andrew of Crete (Tone 2, Heirmos: *Бг҃опроти́вное велѣ́нїе* / *The God-opposing decree*).
[^675]: Allusion to the iconoclast Emperor Constantine V Copronymus.
[^676]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the Canon of Prophet Hosea (Tone 1, Heirmos: *Въ пла́мени ѻ҆́гненнѣ* / *In the fiery flame*).
[^677]: Allusion to the prophetic actions and symbolic marriage of Hosea (Hosea 1–3).
[^678]: The Theotokion continues onto physical leaf p189 (Fol. 93v).
[^679]: Fol. 93v. Conclusion of Ode 8 of the Canon of Prophet Hosea.
[^680]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Andrew of Crete (Tone 2, Heirmos: *Пе́щь и҆ногда̀ ѻ҆́гненнаѧ* / *Of old in Babylon the fiery furnace*).
[^681]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the Canon of Prophet Hosea (Tone 1, Heirmos: *Ра́дꙋйсѧ дѣ́тственная похвало̀* / *Rejoice, praise of virginity*).
[^682]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Andrew of Crete (Tone 2, Heirmos: *Безнача́лна роди́телѧ сн҃ъ* / *The Son of the unoriginate Father*).
[^683]: The troparion continues onto physical leaf p190 (Fol. 94).
[^684]: Fol. 94. Conclusion of the second troparion of Ode 9 of the Canon of St. Andrew of Crete.
[^685]: Exapostilarion of St. Andrew of Crete (Podoben: *Со ᲂу҆ченикѝ взы́демъ* / *Let us go up with the disciples*).
[^686]: Theotokion of the Exapostilarion, compiling classic Byzantine-Slavonic Old Testament Marian typological symbols (Mountain, Lampstand, Manna Jar, Table, Jacob's Ladder/Bridge, Aaron's Rod, Candle, Throne, Ezekiel's Gate, Temple, Bridal Chamber).
[^687]: Fol. 94v. Feast of the Holy Apostle and Evangelist Luke (October 18).
[^688]: The sticheron continues onto physical leaf p192 (Fol. 95).
[^689]: Fol. 95. Conclusion of the sixth sticheron at "Lord, I Have Cried" for the Holy Apostle and Evangelist Luke.
[^690]: October 6 is the feast of the Holy Apostle Thomas, where the three Old Testament readings for an Apostle (1 Peter 1:3–9, 1 Peter 2:11–24, 1 Peter 3:8–15) and the Litiya rubrics are given in full.
[^691]: Cf. 2 Corinthians 2:15–16.
[^692]: The sticheron continues onto physical leaf p193 (Fol. 95v).
[^693]: Fol. 95v. Conclusion of the third sticheron at the Aposticha for the Holy Apostle and Evangelist Luke.
[^694]: The Theotokion continues onto physical leaf p194 (Fol. 96).
[^695]: Fol. 96. Conclusion of the Theotokion of the Sessional Hymn after the 2nd Kathisma for St. Luke.
[^696]: Ode 1 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Luke (Tone 8, Heirmos: *Пѣ́снь возсле́мъ лю́дїе* / *Let us send up a hymn, O ye people*).
[^697]: Fol. 96v. Ode 3 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Luke (Tone 8, Heirmos: *Нѣ́сть ст҃ъ ꙗ҆́кѡ гдⷭ҇ь* / *There is none holy as the Lord*).
[^698]: Ode 4 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Luke (Tone 8, Heirmos: *И҆з̾ горы̀ приѡсѣне́нныѧ* / *From the overshadowing mountain*). Cf. Habakkuk 3:3.
[^699]: The troparion continues onto physical leaf p196 (Fol. 97).
[^700]: Fol. 97. Conclusion of the second troparion of Ode 4 of the Canon of St. Luke.
[^701]: Ode 5 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Luke (Tone 8, Heirmos: *Оу҆́тренююще вопїе́мъ тѝ* / *Rising early we cry aloud unto Thee*). Cf. Isaiah 26:9.
[^702]: Ode 6 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Luke (Tone 8, Heirmos: *Ризꙋ мнѣ̀ пода́ждь свѣ́тлꙋ* / *Grant me a robe of light*). Cf. Psalm 103:2 LXX.
[^703]: Kontakion of the Holy Apostle and Evangelist Luke (Tone 2, Podoben: "The firm and God-proclaiming preachers" / *Тве́рдыя*).
[^704]: The Ikos continues onto physical leaf p197 (Fol. 97v).
[^705]: Fol. 97v. Conclusion of the Ikos of the Holy Apostle and Evangelist Luke.
[^706]: Ode 7 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Luke (Tone 8, Heirmos: *Ѻ҆́троцы бл҃гочести́вїи* / *The pious youths in Babylon*). Cf. Daniel 3.
[^707]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Luke (Tone 8, Heirmos: *На горѣ̀ ст҃ѣ́й просла́вльшасѧ* / *Him Who was glorified on the holy mountain*). Cf. Exodus 3:1–6.
[^708]: Fol. 98. Ode 9 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Luke (Tone 8, Heirmos: *Тѧ̀ неискꙋсобра́чнꙋю* / *Thee, the Mother of God the Most High*).
[^709]: Exapostilarion of the Holy Apostle Luke (Tone 2, Podoben: "Let us go up with the disciples" / *Со ученики*). It refers to the ancient tradition that St. Luke painted the first icon of the Theotokos (Hodegetria).
[^710]: The sticheron continues onto physical leaf p199 (Fol. 98v).
[^711]: Fol. 98v. Conclusion of the first sticheron at the Praises for the Holy Apostle and Evangelist Luke.
[^712]: Colossians 4:14 explicitly names Luke: "Luke, the beloved physician, and Demas, greet you."
[^713]: Luke 10:16–21 is the Gospel of the Seventy Apostles, of whom St. Luke was one.
[^714]: Fol. 99. Feast of the Holy Prophet Joel and the Holy Martyr Varus (October 19).
[^715]: Joel 2:28 (LXX 3:1): "And it shall come to pass afterward, that I will pour out My Spirit upon all flesh..." (cited in Acts 2:17).
[^716]: Cf. Joel 3:18 (LXX 4:18): "And it shall come to pass in that day, that the mountains shall drop down sweetness... and a fountain shall come forth out of the house of the Lord."
[^717]: St. Varus (died c. 307), an Egyptian soldier who voluntarily joined seven imprisoned Christian monks in their martyrdom.
[^718]: The sticheron continues onto physical leaf p201 (Fol. 99v).
[^719]: Saint Cleopatra of Mount Tabor in Palestine was a pious widow who recovered the precious relics of Saint Varus after his martyrdom in Egypt (c. 307 AD) and transported them to her native village of Dara in Palestine. When her beloved son John died suddenly upon reaching military age, Saint Varus appeared to her in a vision alongside John, consoling her with the revelation that her son was enrolled in the heavenly army of the King of kings.
[^720]: Troparion of the Holy Martyr Varus (Oct. 19), Tone 4. Common dismissal hymn for holy martyrs.
[^721]: Canon of the Holy Prophet Joel (Oct. 19), Tone 2, bearing the acrostic: "I marvel at the prophecy of the Prophet Joel" by Saint Theophanes the Branded. Ode 1 Heirmos: Heirmologion Tone 2, Ode 1.
[^722]: Slavonic *сѣ́мъ ᲂу҆даре́ннымъ* (bruised/struck by these calamities), referring to the locust swarms and severe divine chastisements prophesied in Joel 1:2–2:11.
[^723]: Canon of the Holy Martyr Varus (Oct. 19), Tone 6, by Saint Joseph the Hymnographer, bearing the acrostic: "I praise the passions of Varus, a great glory." Ode 1 Heirmos: Heirmologion Tone 6, Ode 1 (Ex. 15:1–19).
[^724]: Canon of the Prophet Joel, Ode 3 Heirmos: Heirmologion Tone 2, Ode 3.
[^725]: Canon of the Martyr Varus, Ode 3 Heirmos: Heirmologion Tone 6, Ode 3 (1 Sam. 2:1–2 LXX / 1 Kings 2:1–2).
[^726]: Conclusion of the Ode 3 Heirmos of the second canon (Tone 6).
[^727]: Sessional Hymn (Kathisma) of the Prophet Joel, Tone 2, Podoben: "O passion-bearers of the Lord" (*Страстотерпцы Господни*).
[^728]: Sessional Hymn of the Martyr Varus, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy tomb" (*Гроб твой*).
[^729]: Canon of the Prophet Joel, Ode 4 Heirmos: Heirmologion Tone 2, Ode 4 (Hab. 3:2 LXX).
[^730]: Joel 2:28–29 (Pentecost pericope, Acts 2:16–21).
[^731]: Canon of the Martyr Varus, Ode 4 Heirmos: Heirmologion Tone 6, Ode 4 (Hab. 3:2 LXX).
[^732]: Canon of the Prophet Joel, Ode 5 Heirmos: Heirmologion Tone 2, Ode 5 (cf. Rom. 13:12).
[^733]: Canon of the Martyr Varus, Ode 5 Heirmos: Heirmologion Tone 6, Ode 5 (Is. 26:9 LXX).
[^734]: Conclusion of the Ode 5 Heirmos of the second canon (Tone 6).
[^735]: Canon of the Prophet Joel, Ode 6 Heirmos: Heirmologion Tone 2, Ode 6 (Jonah 2:2–6 LXX).
[^736]: Canon of the Martyr Varus, Ode 6 Heirmos: Heirmologion Tone 6, Ode 6 (Jonah 2:3–7 LXX).
[^737]: Kontakion of the Holy Martyr Varus, Tone 4. Common dismissal hymn for holy martyrs.
[^738]: Canon of the Prophet Joel, Ode 7 Heirmos: Heirmologion Tone 2, Ode 7 (Dan. 3:49–50).
[^739]: Prophetic citation from Joel 3:18 LXX (Joel 4:18 MT): "A fountain shall go forth out of the house of the Lord."
[^740]: Prophetic citation from Joel 3:18 LXX (Joel 4:18 MT): "And shall water the torrent of Shittim (acacias)." Slavonic *пото́къ сито́вный*.
[^741]: Canon of the Martyr Varus, Ode 7 Heirmos: Heirmologion Tone 6, Ode 7 (Dan. 3:49–50).
[^742]: Canon of the Prophet Joel, Ode 8 Heirmos: Heirmologion Tone 2, Ode 8 (Dan. 3:57 LXX).
[^743]: Canon of the Martyr Varus, Ode 8 Heirmos: Heirmologion Tone 6, Ode 8 (1 Kings 18:38 LXX / Elijah's sacrifice at Mount Carmel).
[^744]: Saint Varus answered Saint Cleopatra's prayers for her son John, assuring her that John was accepted into Christ's heavenly host and would remain forever united with Saint Varus in glory.
[^745]: Canon of the Prophet Joel, Ode 9 Heirmos: Heirmologion Tone 2, Ode 9.
[^746]: Canon of the Martyr Varus, Ode 9 Heirmos: Heirmologion Tone 6, Ode 9.
[^747]: Conclusion of the Ode 9 Heirmos of the second canon (Tone 6).
[^748]: Typological reference to Isaiah 19:1: "Behold, the Lord sits on a light cloud, and shall come into Egypt, and the idols of Egypt shall be moved at His presence."
[^749]: Feast of the Holy Great-Martyr Artemius (Oct. 20), prominent military commander (*dux Aegypti*) under Emperor Constantine the Great, martyred under Julian the Apostate in Antioch (362 AD).
[^750]: Stichera at "Lord, I Have Cried" for the Great-Martyr Artemius, Tone 4, Podoben: "As noble among the martyrs" (*Яко добля*).
[^751]: Doxastikon of the Great-Martyr Artemius at "Lord, I Have Cried", Tone 2, attributed to Saint Theodore the Studite (759–826 AD). Artemius denounced Julian the Apostate for executing the Christian confessors Eugenius and Macarius.
[^752]: Cross-Theotokion, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the tree" (*Егда от древа*).
[^753]: Doxastikon of Saint Artemius at the Aposticha, Tone 6.
[^754]: Cross-Theotokion at the Aposticha, Tone 6, Podoben: "On the third day" (*Тридневен*).
[^755]: Troparion of the Holy Great-Martyr Artemius, Tone 4. Common dismissal hymn for holy martyrs.
[^756]: Canon of the Great-Martyr Artemius, Tone 2, by Saint Joseph the Hymnographer, bearing the acrostic: "Grant me thy rich grace, O blessed one. Joseph." Ode 1 Heirmos: Heirmologion Tone 2, Ode 1 (Ex. 15:1–19).
[^757]: Canon of the Great-Martyr Artemius, Ode 3 Heirmos: Heirmologion Tone 2, Ode 3 (Is. 35:1 LXX).
[^758]: The Third Ode of the Canon to Saint Artemius continues with further troparia on physical leaf p211.
[^759]: The sessional hymn is directed to be chanted twice (*Два́жды*). Model heading: Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (*Премꙋ́дрости* / *Τὶν Σοφίαν καὶ Λόγον*).
[^760]: Theotokion following the sessional hymn to St. Artemius. 'Spiritual Sun' translates *со́лнца ᲂу҆́мнагѡ* (*νοητοῦ ἡλίου*).
[^761]: Staurotheotokion (Cross-Theotokion) chanted on Wednesdays and Fridays in place of the regular Theotokion.
[^762]: Heirmos of Ode 4, Tone 2 (*Прише́лъ є҆сѝ ѿ дв҃ы* / *Ἐλήλυθας ἐκ Παρθένου*), based on Habakkuk 3:2–3.
[^763]: Syntactic clause carries over across the physical leaf division from leaf p211 (fol. 104v) to leaf p212 (fol. 105r).
[^764]: Heirmos of Ode 5, Tone 2 (*Хода́тай бг҃ꙋ и҆ человѣ́кѡмъ* / *Μεσίτης Θεοῦ καὶ ἀνθρώπων*), alluding to 1 Timothy 2:5.
[^765]: Heirmos of Ode 6, Tone 2 (*Въ бе́зднѣ грѣхо́внѣй валѧ́ѧсѧ* / *Ἐν ἀβύσσῳ πταισμάτων κυκλούμενος*), based on Jonah 2:2–6.
[^766]: The Kontakion to St. Artemius is chanted to the model of Tone 2, Podoben: "The firm" (*Тве́рдыѧ* / *Τοὺς ἀσφαλεῖς καὶ θεοφθόγγους κήρυκας*); the stanza carries over to leaf p213 (fol. 105v).
[^767]: Ikos to St. Artemius, expanding on his renunciation of worldly imperial honors under the apostate emperor Julian.
[^768]: Heirmos of Ode 7, Tone 2 (*Бг҃опроти́вное велѣ́нїе* / *Ἀντίθεον πρόσταγμα παρανομούντος τυράννου*), celebrating the Three Holy Youths in the Babylonian furnace (Daniel 3).
[^769]: Heirmos of Ode 8, Tone 2 (*Пе́щь и҆ногда̀ ѻ҆́гненнаѧ въ вавѷлѡ́нѣ* / *Κάμινός ποτε πυρὸς ἐν Βαβυλῶνι*), based on Daniel 3:57.
[^770]: Ode 8 concludes on leaf p214 (fol. 106r) with the final troparion and Theotokion.
[^771]: Heirmos of Ode 9, Tone 2 (*Безнача́льна роди́телѧ сн҃ъ* / *Ὁ τοῦ ἀνάρχου Γεννήτορος Υἱός*), based on Luke 1:68–79.
[^772]: Exapostilarion to St. Artemius, chanted to the model of Tone 3, Podoben: "O unchangeable Light" (*Свѣ́те неизмѣ́нный* / *Φῶς ἀναλλοίωτον*).
[^773]: Doxasticon at the Aposticha of Matins; the text concludes across the leaf turn onto leaf p215 (fol. 106v).
[^774]: Cross-Theotokion to the model of Tone 6, Podoben: "On the third day" (*Трїдне́венъ* / *Τριήμερος ἀνέστης*).
[^775]: Prokeimenon of the Liturgy, Tone 7, citing Psalm 63:11, 2 (LXX 63:11, 2 [MT 64:10, 1]).
[^776]: Liturgical epistle lection for St. Artemius: 2 Timothy 2:1–10 (pericopa 292).
[^777]: Alleluia of the Liturgy, Tone 4, citing Psalm 91:13 (LXX 91:13 [MT 92:12]).
[^778]: Liturgical Gospel lection for St. Artemius: John 15:17–16:2 (pericopa 52).
[^779]: Communion Hymn (Communion Hymn) for a martyr: Psalm 111:6b (LXX 111:6b [MT 112:6b]).
[^780]: October 21: Commemoration of Our Venerable Father Hilarion the Great of Palestine (c. 291–371), disciple of St. Anthony the Great and founder of Palestinian monasticism.
[^781]: Sticheron 2 at "Lord, I have cried" carries over to leaf p216 (fol. 107r).
[^782]: Doxasticon at "Lord, I have cried" attributed to Anatolius, emphasizing Hilarion's ascetic discipleship under St. Anthony the Great.
[^783]: Cross-Theotokion chanted to Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the tree" (*Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва* / *Ὅτε ἐκ τοῦ ξύλου σε νεκρόν*).
[^784]: Idiomelon (Самогла́сенъ) at the Aposticha of Vespers, Tone 8.
[^785]: Cross-Theotokion at the Aposticha, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder!" (*Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ* / *Ὢ τοῦ παραδόξου θαύματος*).
[^786]: General Troparion of venerable monastics (*Сле́зъ твои́хъ тече́ньми*), Tone 8; concludes on leaf p217 (fol. 107v).
[^787]: The canon to St. Hilarion bears the alphabetical acrostic: *Свѣ́тлость пою̀ всебл҃же́нне твои́хъ чꙋде́съ Ѳеофа́ново* ("Theophanes [the Branded] sings the splendor of thy wonders, O all-blessed one"). Tone 2.
[^788]: Heirmos of Ode 1, Tone 2 (*Во глꙋбинѣ̀ постла̀ и҆ногда̀* / *Ἐν βυθῷ κατέστρωσε ποτέ*), based on Exodus 15:1–19.
[^789]: Heirmos of Ode 3, Tone 2 (*На ка́мени мѧ̀ вѣ́ры ᲂу҆тверди́въ* / *Ἐν πέτρᾳ με τῆς πίστεως στερεώσας*), based on 1 Kingdoms (1 Samuel) 2:1–2.
[^790]: Troparion carries over to leaf p218 (fol. 108r).
[^791]: Sessional hymn to St. Hilarion, chanted twice (*Два́жды*); Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (*Премꙋ́дрости* / *Τὴν Σοφίαν καὶ Λόγον*).
[^792]: Theotokion following the sessional hymn of St. Hilarion.
[^793]: Cross-Theotokion substituted on Wednesdays and Fridays.
[^794]: Heirmos of Ode 4, Tone 2 (*Пою́ тѧ, слꙋ́хомъ бо гдⷭ҇и* / *Εἰσακήκοα Κύριε τὴν ἀκοήν σου*), based on Habakkuk 3:1–3.
[^795]: Ode 4 troparion carries over to leaf p219 (fol. 108v).
[^796]: Heirmos of Ode 5, Tone 2 (*Просвѣще́нїе во тьмѣ̀ лежа́щихъ* / *Ὁ τοῦ φωτὸς χορηγός*), based on Isaiah 26:9.
[^797]: Heirmos of Ode 6, Tone 2 (*Въ бе́зднѣ грѣхо́внѣй валѧ́ѧсѧ* / *Ἐν ἀβύσσῳ πταισμάτων κυκλούμενος*), based on Jonah 2:2–6.
[^798]: Kontakion of St. Hilarion begins at the foot of leaf p219 (fol. 108v) and continues onto leaf p220 (fol. 109r).
[^799]: Kontakion of St. Hilarion the Great, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (*Дѣ́ва дне́сь* / *Ἡ Παρθένος σήμερον*).
[^800]: Ikos of St. Hilarion the Great, concluding with the refrain: "Rejoice, O father, foundation of all ascetics!"
[^801]: Heirmos of Ode 7, Tone 2 (*Бг҃опроти́вное велѣ́нїе* / *Ἀντίθεον πρόσταγμα παρανομούντος τυράννου*), Daniel 3:19–28.
[^802]: Heirmos of Ode 8, Tone 2 (*Пе́щь и҆ногда̀ ѻ҆́гненнаѧ въ вавѷлѡ́нѣ* / *Κάμινός ποτε πυρὸς ἐν Βαβυλῶνι*), Daniel 3:57.
[^803]: Ode 8 troparion continues onto leaf p221 (fol. 109v) in Cohort 23.
[^804]: Heirmos of Ode 9 of the canon to Venerable Hilarion the Great (Tone 2): "The Son of the unoriginate Father, God and Lord..." (Бенача́льна роди́теля сн҃ъ...).
[^805]: Exapostilarion of St. Hilarion the Great. Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите).
[^806]: Aposticha Glory: Idiomelon (Самогласен) in Tone 2 to our venerable father Hilarion the Great.
[^807]: Cross-Theotokion at the Aposticha in Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва).
[^808]: Prokeimenon at the Liturgy for a venerable monastic: Psalm 115:6 (LXX).
[^809]: Prokeimenon verse: Psalm 115:3 (LXX).
[^810]: Epistle to the Corinthians: 2 Corinthians 4:6–15 (pericopa 188), reading appointed for venerable fathers.
[^811]: Alleluia verse: Psalm 111:1 (LXX).
[^812]: Gospel of Luke: Luke 6:17–23 (pericopa 24), reading appointed for venerable monastics.
[^813]: Communion Hymn (Communion Hymn) for the righteous: Psalm 111:6b (LXX).
[^814]: October 22: Commemoration of our Holy Father Abercius, Bishop of Hierapolis, Equal-to-the-Apostles and Wonderworker (c. 167 AD).
[^815]: October 22: Commemoration of the Holy Seven Youths of Ephesus: Maximilian, Jamblicus, Martinian, John, Dionysius, Exacustodian (Constantine), and Antoninus (3rd c. and 5th c.).
[^816]: Doxastikon (Glory) at "Lord, I have cried" for St. Abercius, composed by St. John of Damascus (John the Monk). Tone 6.
[^817]: Cross-Theotokion at "Lord, I have cried" in Tone 6, Podoben: "Great is the power of Thy Cross" (Ве́лїя крⷭ҇та̀).
[^818]: Troparion (Troparion) of St. Abercius in Tone 4: Common troparion for a Hierarch ("The truth of things revealed thee..." / Пра́вило вѣ́ры...).
[^819]: Troparion (Troparion) of the Holy Seven Youths of Ephesus in Tone 4 ("Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings..." / Мч҃нцы твоѝ гдⷭ҇и...).
[^820]: Acrostic of the canon to St. Abercius: "I sing the great glory of Abercius. Joseph." Tone 2.
[^821]: Heirmos of Ode 1 of the canon to St. Abercius (Tone 2): "Come, ye peoples, let us sing..." (Гряди́те лю́дїе...).
[^822]: Heirmos of Ode 1 of the canon to the Seven Youths of Ephesus (Tone 8): "Of old the wonderworking staff of Moses..." (Колесни́цегони́теля фараѡ́на...).
[^823]: Heirmos of Ode 3 of the canon to St. Abercius (Tone 2): "Establish us in Thee, O Lord..." (Оу҆тверди́ на́съ въ тебѣ̀ гдⷭ҇и...).
[^824]: Heirmos of Ode 3 of the canon to the Seven Youths of Ephesus (Tone 8): "Thou Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens..." (Оу҆твержде́й въ нача́лѣ нб҃са̀...).
[^825]: Kontakion of the Seven Youths of Ephesus, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йся на Крⷭ҇тъ во́лею).
[^826]: Ikos of the Seven Youths of Ephesus.
[^827]: Sessional Hymn of St. Abercius, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости).
[^828]: Glory of the Seven Youths of Ephesus, Tone 4, Podoben: "Go before quickly" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ).
[^829]: Cross-Theotokion following the Sessional Hymns.
[^830]: Heirmos of Ode 4 of the canon to St. Abercius (Tone 2): "I sing to Thee; for by report, O Lord..." (Пою́ тя, слꙋ́хомъ бо гдⷭ҇и...).
[^831]: Heirmos of Ode 4 of the canon to the Seven Youths of Ephesus (Tone 8): "Thou art my strength, O Lord..." (Ты́ моя̀ крѣ́пость гдⷭ҇и...).
[^832]: Heirmos of Ode 5 of the canon to St. Abercius (Tone 2): "Enlightenment of those lying in darkness..." (Просвѣще́нїе во тмѣ̀ лежа́щихъ...).
[^833]: Heirmos of Ode 5 of the canon to the Seven Youths of Ephesus (Tone 8): "Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy face..." (Вскꙋ́ю мя̀ ѿри́нꙋлъ є҆сѝ...).
[^834]: Heirmos of Ode 6 of the canon to St. Abercius (Tone 2): "Wallowing in the abyss of sins..." (Въ бе́зднѣ грѣхо́внѣй валя́яся...).
[^835]: Heirmos of Ode 6 of the canon to the Seven Youths of Ephesus (Tone 8): "Cleanse me, O Savior, for my transgressions are many..." (Ѡ҆чи́сти мя̀ сп҃се...).
[^836]: Kontakion of St. Abercius, Tone 8, Podoben: "As first-fruits of nature" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки).
[^837]: Ikos of St. Abercius.
[^838]: Heirmos of Ode 7 of the canon to St. Abercius (Tone 2): "When the golden image was worshipped on the plain of Dura..." (Ѻ҆́бразꙋ злато́мꙋ на по́лѣ деи́рѣ...).
[^839]: Heirmos of Ode 7 of the canon to the Seven Youths of Ephesus (Tone 8): "The fire was put to shame before God's condescension in Babylon of old..." (Бж҃їя снизхожде́нїя, ѻ҆́гнь ᲂу҆стыдѣ́ся...).
[^840]: Heirmos of Ode 8 of the canon to St. Abercius (Tone 2): "Hymn Him as Lord, ye works, Who descended into the fiery furnace..." (Въ пе́щь ѻ҆́гненнꙋю...).
[^841]: Psalm 91:13–14 (LXX): "The righteous shall flourish like a palm tree; he shall be increased like a cedar in Libanus."
[^842]: Allusion to Matthew 5:14 ("A city that is set on a hill cannot be hid") and Hebrews 11:10, 16 (the heavenly city).
[^843]: Canticle of Canticles (Song of Solomon) 3:7: "Behold Solomon's bed; threescore valiant men of the valiant of Israel are about it."
[^844]: Heirmos of Ode 8 of the canon to the Seven Youths of Ephesus (Tone 8): "In his rage the Chaldean tyrant heated the furnace sevenfold..." (Седмери́цею пе́щь...).
[^845]: Allusion to 4 Baruch (Paraleipomena Jeremiae) 5:1–34, where Abimelech the Ethiopian fell asleep in Agrippa's vineyard under a basket of figs for 66 years until the return from Babylonian captivity, a classic patristic type of the resurrection.
[^846]: Heirmos of Ode 9 of the canon to St. Abercius (Tone 2): "God the Word, Who by ineffable wisdom..." (Бо́га Бо́га Сло́ва, неизрече́нною му́дростїю...).
[^847]: Allusion to Isaiah 52:7 and Romans 10:15: "How beautiful are the feet of them that preach the gospel of peace."
[^848]: Allusion to Zechariah 4:2 and the prophetic type of the golden lampstand bearing the divine light.
[^849]: Heirmos of Ode 9 of the canon to the Seven Youths of Ephesus (Tone 8): "Heaven was stricken with awe at this..." (Оу҆жасе́ся ѡ҆ се́мъ нб҃о...).
[^850]: Commemoration of the miraculous awakening of the Seven Youths of Ephesus after their long sleep in the cave of Mount Celion during the reign of Emperor Theodosius II (c. 408–450 AD).
[^851]: Exapostilarion of St. Abercius. Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите). The hymn recounts the life of St. Abercius, who traveled to Rome to deliver Lucilla, the daughter of Emperor Marcus Aurelius, from demonic possession, and subsequently commanded the demon to transport a massive marble altar pillar to Hierapolis.
[^852]: The unusual Slavonic compound title "безцарю̀" addresses Christ as the Sovereign King Who has no emperor or master over Him (the King of kings and Lord of lords).
[^853]: Prokeimenon at the Liturgy for a hierarch: Psalm 48:4 (LXX).
[^854]: Prokeimenon verse: Psalm 48:2 (LXX).
[^855]: Epistle to the Hebrews: Hebrews 7:26–8:2 (pericopa 318), standard epistle appointed for hierarchs.
[^856]: Alleluia verse: Psalm 36:30 (LXX).
[^857]: Alleluia verse: Psalm 36:31 (LXX).
[^858]: Gospel of John: John 10:9–16 (pericopa 36), the Good Shepherd pericope appointed for hierarchs.
[^859]: Communion Hymn (Communion Hymn) for a righteous hierarch: Psalm 111:6b–7a (LXX).
[^860]: October 23: Commemoration of the Holy Apostle James, the Brother of the Lord (Adelphotheos), first Bishop of Jerusalem and hieromartyr (+ c. 62 AD).
[^861]: According to Eusebius (Eccl. Hist. II.23) citing Hegesippus, St. James was taken to the pinnacle of the temple by the scribes and Pharisees during the Passover to dissuade the people from believing in Jesus; instead, he proclaimed Christ as the Son of God sitting at the right hand of the Great Power, whereupon he was cast down and beaten to death with a fuller's club.
[^862]: Theotokion Dogmatikon in Tone 6: "Who would not call thee blessed, O all-holy Virgin? Who would not hymn thy childbirth without pangs?..." (Кто́ тебѐ не оу҆блажи́тъ...).
[^863]: Prokeimenon / Aposticha verse: Psalm 18:5 (LXX).
[^864]: Allusion to the holy tradition that St. James the Brother of the Lord accompanied the Righteous Joseph, the Mother of God, and the Infant Jesus during the flight into Egypt (Matthew 2:13–15).
[^865]: Aposticha verse: Psalm 18:2 (LXX).
[^866]: Troparion (Troparion) of the Holy Apostle James, Brother of the Lord, Tone 2: "As the Lord's disciple, O righteous one, thou didst receive the Gospel..." (Ꙗ҆́кѡ гдⷭ҇нь ᲂу҆чени́къ воспрїя́лъ є҆сѝ првⷣне є҆ѵⷢлїе...).
[^867]: Theotokion in Tone 2: "All above understanding, all most glorious are thy mysteries..." (Всѧ̑ па́че смы́сла, всѧ̑ пресла̑внаѧ твоѧ̑ бц҃е та̑инства...).
[^868]: Sessional Hymn after the first Kathisma: Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й сп҃се).
[^869]: Sessional Hymn after the second Kathisma: Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезначáльное Сло́во).
[^870]: Heirmos of Ode 1 of the canon to the Holy Apostle James, composed by St. Theophanes the Graptos (Tone 4): "Having traversed of old on foot the Red Sea's deep..." (Мо́ря чермнꙋ́ю пꙋчи́нꙋ...).
[^871]: Allusion to the Epistle of James 1:17: "Every good gift and every perfect gift is from above, and comes down from the Father of lights."
[^872]: Katavasia of the Theotokos (Ode 1, Tone 4): "I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit..." (Ѿве́рзꙋ ᲂу҆ста̀ моѧ̑...).
[^873]: Heirmos of Ode 3 of the canon to the Apostle James (Tone 4): "Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ..." (Весели́тся ѡ҆ тебѣ̀ це́рковь твоя̀ хрⷭ҇тѐ...).
[^874]: Sessional Hymn after Ode 3: Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости).
[^875]: Heirmos of Ode 4 of the canon to the Apostle James (Tone 4): "For the sake of the love of Thine image, O Compassionate One..." (Любвѐ ра́ди ще́дре...).
[^876]: Allusion to Habakkuk 3:8, 15: "Thou didst ride upon Thine horses... Thou didst walk through the sea with Thine horses."
[^877]: Allusion to Daniel 2:34, 45: the stone cut out of the mountain without hands that struck the image and broke it in pieces.
[^878]: Heirmos of Ode 5 of the canon to the Apostle James (Tone 4): "Thou, O my Lord, hast come as a light into the world..." (Ты́ гдⷭ҇и моѝ свѣ́тъ въ мі́ръ прише́лъ є҆сѝ...).
[^879]: Heirmos of Ode 6 of the canon to the Apostle James (Tone 4): "I will sacrifice to Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord..." (Пожрꙋ́ ти со гла́сомъ хвале́нїя гдⷭ҇и...).
[^880]: Allusion to Exodus 28 and Leviticus 8 (the priestly vestments of Aaron) contrasted with the higher spiritual vesture of the New Testament hierarch.
[^881]: Kontakion of the Holy Apostle James, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йся на Крⷭ҇тъ во́лею).
[^882]: Ikos of the Holy Apostle James.
[^883]: Allusion to Psalm 36:15 (LXX): "their bow shall be broken." The mention of Ishmaelites reflects the historical context of Saracen/Arab incursions into the Byzantine Levant.
[^884]: Heirmos of Ode 7 of the canon to the Apostle James (Tone 4): "In the Persian furnace the children of Abraham..." (Въ пе́щи а҆враа́мстїи ѻ҆́троцы персі́дстѣй...).
[^885]: Allusion to Acts 2:1–4: the descent of the Holy Spirit upon the disciples in the Upper Room at Pentecost.
[^886]: Heirmos of Ode 8 of the canon to the Apostle James (Tone 4): "Daniel, stretching out his hands..." (Рꙋ́цѣ распросте́ръ данїи́лъ...).
[^887]: Allusion to Luke 24:50–51: "And He led them out as far as to Bethany, and He lifted up His hands, and blessed them. And it came to pass, while He blessed them, He was parted from them, and carried up into heaven."
[^888]: Heirmos of Ode 9 of the canon to the Apostle James (Tone 4): "A stone cut out without hands from an uncut mountain, O Virgin..." (Ка́мень нерꙋкосѣ́чный...).
[^889]: Allusion to the traditional title of St. James as "the Just" (Oblias / ὁ δίκαιος), attested by Hegesippus in Eusebius, *Historia Ecclesiastica* II.23.
[^890]: Exapostilarion of the Holy Apostle James: Tone 3, Podoben: "In the spirit in the sanctuary" (Дх҃омъ во святи́лищи).
[^891]: Stichera at the Praises: Tone 8, Podoben: "Lord, even if before the tribunal" (Гдⷭ҇и, а҆́ще и҆ на сꙋди́щи).
[^892]: According to Hegesippus in Eusebius, *Historia Ecclesiastica* II.23, the scribes and Pharisees set James upon the pinnacle (wing) of the temple during the Passover so that he might deny Christ before the multitude; but he loudly confessed Jesus as the Son of God.
[^893]: An allusion to the historical martyrdom of St. James: after being cast down from the pinnacle of the temple and stoned, a fuller struck him on the head with the wooden club (wooden beam) used to beat cloth, thereby ending his earthly life.
[^894]: St. James was cast down from the southeastern battlement or pinnacle of the Temple enclosure in Jerusalem.
[^895]: Doxastikon (Glory) at the Praises composed by Byzantios (Byzantine hymnographer), Tone 4.
[^896]: Prokeimenon at the Liturgy for an apostle: Psalm 18:5 (LXX).
[^897]: Prokeimenon verse: Psalm 18:2 (LXX).
[^898]: Epistle to the Galatians: Galatians 1:11–19 (pericopa 200), containing St. Paul's testimony: "Then after three years I went up to Jerusalem to see Peter... But other of the apostles saw I none, save James the Lord's brother."
[^899]: Alleluia verse: Psalm 88:6 (LXX).
[^900]: Gospel of Matthew: Matthew 13:54–58 (pericopa 56), where the people of Nazareth ask: "Is not this the carpenter's son? is not His mother called Mary? and His brethren, James, and Joses, and Simon, and Judas?"
[^901]: October 24: Commemoration of the Holy Great Martyr Arethas (al-Harith ibn Ka'b) and the 4,299 Christian martyrs of the city of Najran in southern Arabia (Himyar), martyred under the Jewish king Dhu Nuwas (c. 523 AD).
[^902]: Stichera at "Lord, I have cried": Tone 1, Podoben: "All-praised martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мч҃ницы).
[^903]: A direct reference to King Dhu Nuwas (Yusuf As'ar Yath'ar), the ruler of the Himyarite kingdom who converted to Judaism and brutally besieged, captured, and slaughtered the Christian population of Najran when they refused to renounce Christ.
[^904]: Doxastikon (Glory) at "Lord, I have cried" composed by Anatolius, Tone 4.
[^905]: An allusion to the traditional account of the martyrdom in Najran, wherein an ominous fiery cloud and divine portents heralded the imminent downfall and overthrow of Dhu Nuwas by King Kaleb (Elesbaan) of Aksum.
[^906]: Cross-Theotokion at "Lord, I have cried": Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́бля).
[^907]: Doxastikon (Glory) at the Aposticha of the Oktoechos composed by Luke (Лꙋки́ново), Tone 8.
[^908]: Continuing the Doxastikon (Glory) of the Aposticha at Vespers for St. Arethas and his fellow-martyrs of Najran (Tone 8, by Luke). The hymn alludes to the siege and slaughter perpetrated by the Jewish ruler Dhu Nuwas against the Christian population.
[^909]: According to the *Martyrium Sancti Arethae*, King Dhu Nuwas had deep trenches and pits dug and filled with combustible materials, burning alive the clergy, monastics, and consecrated virgins of Najran before massacring the citizens led by Arethas.
[^910]: Cross-Theotokion at the Aposticha: Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ).
[^911]: A classical Byzantine Stavrotheotokion portraying the maternal lamentation of the Virgin Theotokos at the foot of the Cross, contemplating the paradox of the immortal Life-giver dying in the flesh.
[^912]: Common Troparion of Many Martyrs, Tone 4: "Entreated, O Lord, by the sufferings..." (Болѣ́зньми ст҃ы́хъ...).
[^913]: The alphabetic acrostic in Greek reads: Μυκτηρικὸν σύνταγμα θεοπρεπῶς μελπωδῶ (Church Slavonic: Мꙋ́ченическїй собо́ръ, бг҃окра́снѣ воспое́мъ пѣ́сньми — "The martyric choir let us hymn godly-fairly with songs").
[^914]: Canon of the Holy Martyr Arethas and his companions, composed by St. Theophanes the Branded (Graptos), Tone 8.
[^915]: Heirmos of Ode 1 of the canon (Tone 8): "Let us send up a hymn, O ye people..." (Пѣ́снь возсле́мъ лю́дїе...).
[^916]: Direct reference to the Himyarite king Dhu Nuwas (Yusuf As'ar), who broke his oaths and covenants of safe-conduct given to the inhabitants of Najran and subsequently massacred them.
[^917]: Heirmos of Ode 3 of the canon (Tone 8): "O Lord, Creator of the vault of heaven..." (Нбⷭ҇нагѡ крꙋ́га верхотво́рче гдⷭ҇и...).
[^918]: The hymnographer contrasts Christ's historic deliverance of the Israelites out of the tyranny of Pharaoh in Egypt with the cruelty of Dhu Nuwas and his partisans who massacred the Christians of Najran.
[^919]: An allusion to Psalm 32:15 (LXX): "He Who fashioned their hearts one by one, He Who understands all their works."
[^920]: Sessional Hymn (Kathisma) after Ode 3 of the canon: Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости).
[^921]: Cross-Theotokion (Stavrotheotokion) following the Sessional Hymn after Ode 3.
[^922]: Heirmos of Ode 4 of the canon (Tone 8): "Thou art my strength, O Lord..." (Ты́ моѧ̀ крѣ́пость гдⷭ҇и...). The heirmos paraphrases Habakkuk 3:2–19 (LXX).
[^923]: Addressed to the holy martyr Arethas as leader and protector of the Christian city of Najran. The Greek text refers to his venerable age (πολιᾷ σεμνυνόμενος / "dignified with gray hairs"), as Arethas was an elder of ninety-five years at the time of his martyrdom.
[^924]: Allusion to Psalm 86:3 (LXX): "Glorious things are spoken of thee, O city of God."
[^925]: Heirmos of Ode 5 of the canon (Tone 8): "Why hast Thou cast me away..." (Вскꙋ́ю мѧ̀ ѿри́нꙋлъ є҆сѝ...).
[^926]: An allusion to the historical martyrdom of St. Arethas and his fellow-citizens of Najran who were martyred by the Himyarite king Dhu Nuwas in 523 AD.
[^927]: Heirmos of Ode 6 of the canon (Tone 8): "Cleanse me, O Savior..." (Ѡ҆чи́сти мѧ̀ сп҃се...).
[^928]: An allusion to the tradition preserved in the *Martyrium Sancti Arethae*, wherein a nursing infant miraculously spoke in defense of Christ and cast itself into the fiery pit to join its martyred mother.
[^929]: Allusion to Psalm 49:2 (LXX): "Out of Zion is the magnificence of His beauty."
[^930]: Kontakion of the Holy Martyr Arethas and his companions: Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ дне́сь).
[^931]: Ikos of the Holy Martyr Arethas, elaborating on his confession of the divine and human natures of Christ against Monophysite and Jewish adversaries.
[^932]: Heirmos of Ode 7 of the canon (Tone 8): "The fire in Babylon was once put to shame..." (Бж҃їѧ снизхожде́нїа...). Paraphrases the Prayer of Azariah and the Song of the Three Holy Children (Daniel 3:26–56, LXX).
[^933]: According to historical accounts of the Najran martyrdom, Christian mothers willingly offered their infants and small children into the fiery trench rather than allow them to be raised in Judaism or apostasy.
[^934]: An allusion to the Parable of the Wise and Foolish Virgins (Matthew 25:1–13).
[^935]: Heirmos of Ode 8 of the canon (Tone 8): "Sevenfold did the Chaldean tyrant..." (Седмери́цею пе́щь...). Paraphrases Daniel 3:19–90 (LXX).
[^936]: The legendary martyrdom of the three-year-old child of the noblewoman Ruhm (or Mahya) who bit the hand of King Dhu Nuwas and leaped into the flames to be burned with his mother.
[^937]: Heirmos of Ode 9 of the canon (Tone 8): "Every ear was terrified..." (Оу҆страши́ся всѧ́къ слꙋ́хъ...).
[^938]: Exapostilarion of the Holy Martyr Arethas: Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите).
[^939]: Before his execution, St. Arethas prophesied that the Jewish tyrant Dhu Nuwas would soon perish and that a Christian king would come to overthrow him and restore the Church in Himyar.
[^940]: St. Elesbaan (Kaleb), King of Aksum (Ethiopia), invaded southern Arabia c. 525 AD at the request of the Roman Emperor Justin I and the Patriarch of Alexandria, defeating and killing Dhu Nuwas.
[^941]: Najran was rebuilt as a major Christian pilgrimage center and ecclesiastical see following the victory of King Elesbaan.
[^942]: Prokeimenon at the Liturgy: Psalm 15:3 (LXX).
[^943]: Prokeimenon verse: Psalm 15:8 (LXX).
[^944]: Epistle to the Hebrews: Hebrews 11:33–40 (pericopa 331), recounting the heroes of faith who "stopped the mouths of lions, quenched the violence of fire, escaped the edge of the sword."
[^945]: Alleluia verse: Psalm 33:18 (LXX).
[^946]: Gospel of Matthew: Matthew 10:16–22 (pericopa 38): "Behold, I send you forth as sheep in the midst of wolves: be ye therefore wise as serpents, and harmless as doves."
[^947]: Communion Hymn (Communion Hymn) for martyrs: Psalm 32:1 (LXX).
[^948]: October 25: Commemoration of the Holy Martyrs and Notaries Marcian and Martyrius of Constantinople (martyred c. 355 AD under the Arian Emperor Constantius II).
[^949]: Stichera at "Lord, I have cried": Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ).
[^950]: An allusion to 2 Timothy 4:7: "I have fought a good fight, I have finished my course, I have kept the faith."
[^951]: Marcian and Martyrius served as secretarial notaries and chanters to Patriarch Paul the Confessor of Constantinople, staunchly defending the Nicene dogma of the consubstantial Trinity (*homoousion*) against the Arians.
[^952]: Reference to St. Paul the Confessor, Patriarch of Constantinople, who was exiled and martyred by the Arians in Cucusus, Armenia, in 350 AD.
[^953]: Doxastikon (Glory) at "Lord, I have cried" composed by Patriarch Germanus I of Constantinople, Tone 4.
[^954]: St. Paul the Confessor, their spiritual father and archbishop.
[^955]: The Arian prefect Hermogenes offered Marcian and Martyrius immense wealth and episcopal honors if they would anathematize Paul and subscribe to Arianism; when they refused, they were beheaded.
[^956]: Cross-Theotokion at "Lord, I have cried": Tone 1, Podoben: "All-praised martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мꙋ́ченицы).
[^957]: Allusion to Isaiah 53:2 (LXX): "He hath no form nor comeliness; and when we shall see Him, there is no beauty that we should desire Him."
[^958]: Doxastikon (Glory) at the Aposticha of the Oktoechos, Tone 3.
[^959]: A comprehensive hymnographic catalog of Trinitarian and Christological heresies: Arius (denial of the Son's co-eternity/consubstantiality), Nestorius (division of Christ into two persons), Sabellius (modalistic conflation of the three Persons), and Severus of Antioch (Monophysite conflation of the two natures).
[^960]: Allusion to the prophecy of Simeon in Luke 2:35: "Yea, a sword shall pierce through thine own soul also."
[^961]: Common Troparion of Martyrs, Tone 4: "Thy martyrs, O Lord..." (Мꙋ́ченницы твоѝ гдⷭ҇и...).
[^962]: Canon of the Holy Martyrs Marcian and Martyrius, composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer, Tone 4. The acrostic in Greek reads: Δοιοῖς ἀθλοφόροις ὕμνους φέρω. Ἰωσήφ (Slavonic: Дво́мъ приношꙋ̀ мꙋ́ченникѡмъ пѣ́нїа. Їѡ́сифъ).
[^963]: In Platonic and Christian ascetic psychology (Evagrius, Maximus the Confessor), the tripartite soul comprises the rational (*logistikon*), the irascible (*thymikon*), and the concupiscible (*epithymetikon*).
[^964]: Heirmos of Ode 1 of the canon (Tone 4): "O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin..." (Трїста́ты крѣ̑пкїѧ...). Paraphrases the Song of Moses in Exodus 15:1–19 (LXX).
[^965]: Patriarch Paul I the Confessor of Constantinople, under whom Marcian and Martyrius were trained as notaries and defenders of the Nicene Symbol of Faith.
[^966]: Heirmos of Ode 3 of the canon (Tone 4): "Not in wisdom, and power, and riches do we boast..." (Не мꙋ́дростїю, и҆ си́лою...). Paraphrases 1 Kings 2:2–10 (1 Samuel 2:2–10, LXX) and Jeremiah 9:23–24.
[^967]: The Arian heresy denied that Jesus Christ was true God of true God, co-eternal and of one essence (*homoousios*) with the Father, asserting that the Son was a created being.
[^968]: An allusion to Psalm 44:10, 14 (LXX): "At Thy right hand stood the queen in a vesture of gold... all the glory of the daughter of the king is from within, with golden borders, clothed in embroidery."
[^969]: Sessional Hymn (Kathisma) after Ode 3 of the canon: Tone 3, Podoben: "Of divine faith" (Бж҃е́ственныѧ вѣ́ры).
[^970]: Stavrotheotokion following the Sessional Hymn after Ode 3, meditating upon the sorrow of the Virgin Mother at the voluntary self-offering of Christ upon the Cross.
[^971]: Heirmos of Ode 4 of the canon (Tone 4): "Sitting in glory upon the throne..." (Сѣдѧ́й въ сла́вѣ...). Paraphrases Isaiah 19:1 and Habakkuk 3:2–19 (LXX).
[^972]: The hymnographer personifies the Arian heresy or refers to its chief champions (such as the Arian Patriarch Macedonius I or the prefect Hermogenes) being spiritually defeated by the unflinching confession of Marcian and Martyrius.
[^973]: Allusion to Habakkuk 3:3 (LXX): "God shall come from Teman, and the Holy One from a mountain overshadowed and densely wooded."
[^974]: Heirmos of Ode 5 of the canon (Tone 4): "The ungodly shall not see Thy glory..." (Нечести́ві́и не ᲂу҆́зрѧтъ...). Paraphrases Isaiah 26:9–11 (LXX) and Hebrews 1:3.
[^975]: St. Paul the Confessor, who was strangled with his own omophorion by the Arians in Cucusus; his disciples Marcian and Martyrius likewise endured martyrdom for the same confession.
[^976]: Heirmos of Ode 6 of the canon (Tone 4): "I came into the depths of the sea..." (Прїидо́хъ во глꙋбины̑ морскі́ѧ...). Paraphrases Jonah 2:3–6 (LXX).
[^977]: St. Paul the Confessor, Patriarch of Constantinople, whose disciples and notaries Marcian and Martyrius were.
[^978]: An allusion to Psalm 115:6 (LXX): "Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His saints."
[^979]: Kontakion of the Holy Martyrs Marcian and Martyrius: Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йсѧ).
[^980]: Ikos of the Holy Martyrs Marcian and Martyrius.
[^981]: Heirmos of Ode 7 of the canon (Tone 4): "The Abrahamic Children of old in Babylon..." (А҆враа́мстїи и҆ногда̀ въ вавѵлѡ́нѣ ѻ҆́троцы...). Paraphrases Daniel 3:26–56 (LXX).
[^982]: St. Paul the Confessor.
[^983]: Heirmos of Ode 8 of the canon (Tone 4): "O all-powerful Deliverer of all..." (И҆зба́вителю всѣ́хъ всеси́льне...). Paraphrases Daniel 3:57–88 (LXX).
[^984]: Heirmos of Ode 9 of the canon (Tone 4): "Eve through the disease of disobedience..." (Е҆́ѵа ᲂу҆́бѡ недꙋ́гомъ преслꙋша́нїѧ...).
[^985]: Macedonius I of Constantinople, the Semi-Arian bishop who opposed the consubstantiality and deity of the Holy Spirit.
[^986]: Exapostilarion of the Holy Martyrs: Tone 3, Podoben: "In the spirit" (Дꙋ́хомъ).
[^987]: Prokeimenon at the Liturgy: Psalm 67:36 (LXX).
[^988]: Epistle to the Ephesians: Ephesians 6:10–17 (pericopa 224 from the half), on the whole armor of God.
[^989]: Alleluia verse: Psalm 33:18 (LXX).
[^990]: Gospel of Matthew: Matthew 10:1–8 (pericopa 34 from the half).
[^991]: Communion Hymn (Communion Hymn): Psalm 32:1 (LXX).
[^992]: October 26: Commemoration of the Holy and Glorious Great-Martyr Demetrius of Thessalonica, the Myrrh-Streamer (martyred c. 306 AD under Emperor Maximian), and the Commemoration of the Great and Fearful Earthquake at Constantinople in 740 AD.
[^993]: Stichera at Small Vespers: Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ).
[^994]: Doxastikon (Glory) at Small Vespers: Tone 6.
[^995]: Theotokion at Small Vespers: "O Birthgiver of God, thou art the true vine..." (Богородице Дѣво, ты еси лоза истинная...).
[^996]: Aposticha at Small Vespers: Tone 2, Podoben: "O house of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ).
[^997]: Verse at the Aposticha: Psalm 91:13 (LXX).
[^998]: Verse at the Aposticha: Psalm 91:14 (LXX).
[^999]: Troparion of the Great-Martyr Demetrius: Tone 3: "A great champion in perils..." (Вели́ка ѡ҆брѣ́те въ бѣда́хъ...).
[^1000]: Demetrius encouraged the young Christian warrior Nestor to enter single combat against the pagan champion gladiator Lyaeus, blessing him: "Thou shalt conquer Lyaeus and suffer for Christ."
[^1001]: Troparion of the Earthquake: Tone 8: "O Thou Who lookest upon the earth..." (Призира́ѧй на зе́млю...).
