[^1002]: At Great Vespers: the first kathisma of the Psalter, "Blessed is the man" (Psalms 1–3).
[^1003]: Stichera at "Lord, I have cried": Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва).
[^1004]: St. Demetrius was executed in his prison cell by soldiers thrusting spears into his right side and ribs, recalling the lance piercing Christ's side on the Cross (John 19:34).
[^1005]: Stichera at "Lord, I have cried": Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ).
[^1006]: Doxastikon (Glory) at "Lord, I have cried" composed by Byzantios: Tone 6.
[^1007]: Dogmatic Theotokion of Tone 6: "Who would not call thee blessed..." (Кто̀ тебѣ̀ не ᲂу҆блажи́тъ...).
[^1008]: Evening Entrance and Prokeimenon of the day of the week.
[^1009]: First Paremia at Great Vespers: Isaiah 63:15–19; 64:1–5, 8–9 (LXX).
[^1010]: Second Paremia at Great Vespers: Jeremiah 2:2–12 (LXX).
[^1011]: Third Paremia at Great Vespers: Wisdom of Solomon 4:7–15.
[^1012]: Stichera at the Litiya, Idiomelon composed by George Sikeliotes (the Sicilian): Tone 4.
[^1013]: Sticheron at the Litiya composed by Anatolius: Tone 4.
[^1014]: Sticheron at the Litiya composed by Patriarch Germanus I of Constantinople: Tone 2.
[^1015]: Doxastikon (Glory) at the Litiya: Tone 5, Podoben: "Let us sound the trumpet" (Вострꙋ́бимъ).
[^1016]: Theotokion at the Litiya composed by Patriarch Germanus I of Constantinople: Tone 5.
[^1017]: Stichera at the Aposticha of the Earthquake: Tone 2, Idiomelon composed by St. Symeon the Stylite of the Admirable Mountain (521–592 AD).
[^1018]: Verse at the Aposticha: Psalm 103:32 (LXX).
[^1019]: Verse at the Aposticha: Psalm 59:4 (LXX).
[^1020]: Doxastikon (Glory) at the Aposticha composed by Anatolius: Tone 8.
[^1021]: Theotokion at the Aposticha: "O unwedded Virgin..." (Безневѣ́стнаѧ дв҃о...).
[^1022]: At the Artoklasia (Blessing of the Loaves): Troparion of St. Demetrius twice, and "Rejoice, O Virgin Birthgiver of God" (Богородице Дѣво) once.
[^1023]: At Matins, at "God is the Lord" (Psalm 117:27 LXX): Troparion of the Earthquake twice; Glory, Troparion of St. Demetrius; Both now, Troparion of the Earthquake.
[^1024]: Sessional Hymn (Kathisma) after the 1st Stichology: Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь).
[^1025]: Sessional Hymn after the 2nd Stichology: Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ).
[^1026]: Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos: Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости).
[^1027]: Anavathmoi (Hymns of Ascent): First Antiphon of Tone 4 ("From my youth many passions fight against me...").
[^1028]: Matins Prokeimenon: Psalm 67:36 (LXX).
[^1029]: Matins Prokeimenon verse: Psalm 67:27 (LXX).
[^1030]: Codicological Note: In this physical exemplar of the 1761 Pochaiv Menaion, Folio 130r (leaf p261) directly succeeds Folio 129r (leaf p260). However, the printed text shifts abruptly from the concluding rubrics of Matins for St. Demetrius (October 26) to the concluding odes of the Canon for December 23 (Forefeast of the Nativity of Christ and the Ten Holy Martyrs of Crete), reflecting a signature disruption or composite binding in this historical volume.
[^1031]: The second canon of Ode 8 for December 23, composed by St. Theophanes Graptos, celebrating the Holy Martyrs of Crete.
[^1032]: Daniel 3:52–56 (Song of the Three Holy Youths in the Babylonian Furnace).
[^1033]: Heirmos of Ode 9 of the Forefeast canon, adapting the classic Heirmologion incipit *Не дивися ныне, о Мати* (*Marvel not now, O Mother*).
[^1034]: The second canon of Ode 9 for December 23.
[^1035]: Ode 9 Heirmos: *Бога Бога Слова...* (*God the Word of God...*).
[^1036]: Tone 2 Exapostilarion prosomoion: *Сами ученицы* (*The Disciples Themselves*).
[^1037]: The Ten Holy Martyrs of Crete, martyred under Decius (c. 250): Theodulus, Saturninus, Euporus, Gelasius, Eunician, Zoticus, Pompeius, Agathopus, Basilides, and Evarestus.
[^1038]: Prosomoion: *Жены услышите* (*Give Ear, Ye Women*).
[^1039]: Tone 6 Prosomoion: *Ангельския силы предыдите* (*Go Before, Ye Angelic Hosts*).
[^1040]: Tone 2 Prosomoion: *Доме Евфрафов* (*O House of Ephratha*).
[^1041]: Habakkuk 3:3 (LXX).
[^1042]: Habakkuk 3:2 (LXX: *Κύριε, εἰσακήκοα τὴν ἀκοήν σου καὶ ἐφοβήθην...*).
[^1043]: Doxastikon attributed to St. Theodore the Studite (759–826).
[^1044]: Forefeast Theotokion attributed to Anatolius.
[^1045]: Psalm 15:3 (LXX: *Τοῖς ἁγίοις τοῖς ἐν τῇ γῇ αὐτοῦ...*).
[^1046]: Psalm 15:8 (LXX: *Προωρώμην τὸν Κύριον ἐνώπιόν μου διαπαντός...*).
[^1047]: Ephesians 6:10–17 (Epistle reading for martyrs).
[^1048]: Psalm 33:18 (LXX: *Ἐκέκραξαν οἱ δίκαιοι...*).
[^1049]: Luke 21:12–19 (Gospel reading for martyrs).
[^1050]: Psalm 32:1 (LXX: *Ἀγαλλιᾶσθε, δίκαιοι, ἐν Κυρίῳ...*).
[^1051]: December 24: Eve of the Nativity of Christ (Paramony).
[^1052]: Holy Venerable-Martyr Eugenia of Rome (c. 262) and those with her: Protus, Hyacinth, and Claudia.
[^1053]: Tone 5 Melodic model: *Радуйся, постнических* (*Rejoice, O Habitation of the Ascetics*).
[^1054]: Tone 8 Melodic model: *О преславнаго чудесе* (*O Most Marvelous Wonder*).
[^1055]: Idiomelon of the Forefeast in Tone 2 (*Се время приближися*).
[^1056]: Tone 4 Melodic model: *Всхвальнии мученицы* (*All-Praised Martyrs*).
[^1057]: Habakkuk 3:3 (LXX).
[^1058]: Habakkuk 3:2 (LXX).
[^1059]: Idiomelon of the Forefeast in Tone 6 (*Сионе торжествуй*).
[^1060]: Troparion of the Forefeast of the Nativity of Christ, Tone 4 (*Написовашеся иногда*).
[^1061]: The Great Compline Canon of the Forefeast of the Nativity (December 24), modeled directly upon the Holy Saturday canon (*Волною морскою*). Acrostic: *Днесь же субботу пою великую* ("Today I sing of the Great Sabbath").
[^1062]: Ode 1 Heirmos: *Волною морскою...* (*Beneath the wave of the sea...*), adapting the Holy Saturday heirmos of St. Cosmas / St. Mark of Otranto.
[^1063]: Ode 3 Heirmos: *Тебе на водах повесившаго...* (*Thee Who hungest all the earth upon the waters...*).
[^1064]: Ode 4 Heirmos: *Девы пришествие Твое провидя Аввакум...* (*Foreseeing Thy coming from the Virgin, Habakkuk...*).
[^1065]: Ode 5 Heirmos: *Богоявления Твоего, Христе...* (*Of Thy divine manifestation, O Christ...*), citing Isaiah 7:14 and 26:9.
[^1066]: Ode 6 Heirmos: *Ят бысть, но не удержан...* (*Jonah was held, but was not retained...*), citing Jonah 2:1–11.
[^1067]: Ode 7 Heirmos: *Неизреченное чудо...* (*Ineffable wonder...*).
[^1068]: Ode 8 Heirmos: *Ужаснися бояся небо...* (*Be astonished in fear, O heaven...*).
[^1069]: Ode 9 Heirmos: *Не дивися ныне, о Мати...* (*Marvel not now, O Mother...*).
[^1070]: Tone 6 Sessional Hymn after the 1st Kathisma (*Пророческая речения ныне исполнишася*).
[^1071]: Tone 8 Sessional Hymn after the 2nd Kathisma (*Свирелей пастырских*).
[^1072]: Matins Canon of the Forefeast of the Nativity of Christ for December 24 (acrostic continued on leaf p271).
[^1073]: Codicological Note: In this physical exemplar of the 1761 Pochaiv Menaion, Folio 134v (leaf p271) returns to the October sequence, directly succeeding Folio 134v of the December quire (leaf p270). The codex resumes here with the concluding troparia of Ode 8 of the canons for the Earthquake and St. Demetrius on October 26, facing Folio 135 (leaf p272).
[^1074]: Daniel 6:16–23 (Daniel in the lions' den) and Daniel 3:49–50 (Song of the Three Holy Youths).
[^1075]: Daniel 3:19–20 (Nebuchadnezzar's furnace heated sevenfold).
[^1076]: John 1:13 ("Which were born, not of blood, nor of the will of the flesh, nor of the will of man, but of God").
[^1077]: St. Nestor the Martyr of Thessalonica (commemorated October 27), disciple of St. Demetrius who slew the giant Lyaeus (Lyaios) in the gladiatorial arena.
[^1078]: The Typikon of the Great Church and Studite tradition assigns this canon on the Earthquake (composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer) alongside the canon to St. Demetrius.
[^1079]: Daniel 2:34–35, 45 (the stone cut out of the mountain without hands).
[^1080]: Polish-Lithuanian royal prayer (*королю на́шемꙋ*, "unto our King"), characteristic of the 1761 Pochaiv recension published under King Augustus III of Poland.
[^1081]: Tone 3 Exapostilarion prosomoion: *Свѣ́те неизмѣ́нный* ("O Unchangeable Light").
[^1082]: Tone 4 Prosomoion: *Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ* ("As One Noble").
[^1083]: Tone 2 Prosomoion: *Ра́дꙋйсѧ по́стническихъ* ("Rejoice, O Habitation of the Ascetics").
[^1084]: Idiomelon attributed to St. Andrew of Jerusalem (St. Andrew of Crete, c. 660–740).
[^1085]: Psalm 27:9 (LXX: *Σῶσον τὸν λαόν σου...*).
[^1086]: Psalm 63:11 (LXX: *Εὐφρανθήσεται δίκαιος ἐν τῷ Κυρίῳ...*).
[^1087]: Hebrews 12:6–10, 26–28 (Epistle reading for the earthquake: "For whom the Lord loves He chastens...").
[^1088]: 2 Timothy 2:1–10 (Epistle reading for the Great-Martyr: "Thou therefore, my son, be strong in the grace that is in Christ Jesus...").
[^1089]: Psalm 59:3–4 (LXX: *Ὁ Θεός, ἀπώσω ἡμᾶς...*).
[^1090]: Psalm 91:13 (LXX: *Δίκαιος ὡς φοῖνιξ ἀνθήσει...*).
[^1091]: Matthew 8:23–27 (Gospel reading for the earthquake: Christ calming the storm at sea).
[^1092]: John 15:17–16:2 (Gospel reading for the Great-Martyr: "These things I command you, that ye love one another...").
[^1093]: Psalm 148:1 (LXX).
[^1094]: Psalm 111:6 (LXX: *Εἰς μνημόσυνον αἰώνιον ἔσται δίκαιος*).
[^1095]: Typikon chapter specifying the liturgical rank and order of service when the feast of St. Demetrius and the Earthquake coincides with a Sunday.
[^1096]: Psalm 50 (LXX: *Ἐλέησόν με, ὁ Θεός...*).
[^1097]: Katavasia of the Theotokos: *Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου* ("I shall open my mouth"), Ode 1 heirmos.
[^1098]: October 27: Commemoration of the Holy Martyr Nestor of Thessalonica (c. 306), who by the blessing and prayers of St. Demetrius entered the stadium and threw the pagan giant champion Lyaeus upon spears, putting an end to the gladiatorial slaughter of Christians.
[^1099]: Tone 8 Melodic model: *Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ* ("O Most Marvelous Wonder").
[^1100]: Stavrotheotokion (Hymn to the Cross and the Theotokos) appointed for Wednesdays and Fridays or general commemoration.
[^1101]: General Troparion of a Martyr, Tone 4 (*Мученик Твой, Господи, Нестор*).
[^1102]: Ode 1 Heirmos: Exodus 15:4, 1 ("Pharaoh's chariots and his host hath He cast into the sea...").
[^1103]: Ode 3 Heirmos: Amos 4:13 (LXX: *Ὁ στερεῶν βροντὴν καὶ κτίζων πνεῦμα...*).
[^1104]: Tone 4 Sessional Hymn prosomoion: *Вознесы́йсѧ на крⷭ҇тъ* ("Thou Who Wast Raised upon the Cross").
[^1105]: Ode 4 Heirmos: Habakkuk 3:2 (LXX: *Κύριε, εἰσακήκοα τὴν ἀκοήν σου καὶ ἐφοβήθην...*).
[^1106]: Ode 5 Heirmos adapting the dawn hymn (*Посли нам свет Твой* / *Утро светлое*).
[^1107]: Ode 6 Heirmos: Jonah 2:1–10.
[^1108]: Psalm 39:3 (LXX: *Καὶ ἔστησεν ἐπὶ πέτραν τοὺς πόδας μου...*).
[^1109]: Exodus 3:1–6 (Moses and the burning bush on Mount Sinai/Horeb as a prophetic type of the Virgin Birth).
[^1110]: Song of the Three Holy Youths, Daniel 3:52–90 (LXX: Prayer of Azariah and Song of the Three Children).
[^1111]: 2 Timothy 4:7–8 ("I have fought a good fight, I have finished my course, I have kept the faith: Henceforth there is laid up for me a crown of righteousness, which the Lord, the righteous judge, shall give me at that day").
[^1112]: Luke 1:49–50 (The Magnificat / Canticle of the Theotokos).
[^1113]: Tone 2 Exapostilarion prosomoion: *Жєны̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите* ("Give Ear, Ye Women").
[^1114]: Psalm 15:3, 8 (LXX: *Τοῖς ἁγίοις τοῖς ἐν τῇ γῇ αὐτοῦ... Προωρώμην τὸν Κύριον ἐνώπιόν μου διαπαντός...*).
[^1115]: Ephesians 6:10–17 (Epistle reading for a martyr: "Finally, my brethren, be strong in the Lord, and in the power of His might. Put on the whole armor of God...").
[^1116]: Psalm 33:18, 20 (LXX: *Ἐκέκραξαν οἱ δίκαιοι, καὶ ὁ Κύριος εἰσήκουσεν αὐτῶν... Πολλαὶ αἱ θλίψεις τῶν δικαίων...*).
[^1117]: Luke 21:12–19 (Gospel reading for a martyr: "Settle it therefore in your hearts, not to meditate before what ye shall answer... In your patience possess ye your souls").
[^1118]: Psalm 111:6 (LXX: *Εἰς μνημόσυνον αἰώνιον ἔσται δίκαιος*).
[^1119]: October 28: Commemoration of the Holy Martyrs Terentius and Neonilla and their seven children (Sarbilus, Photus, Theodulus, Hierax, Nitas, Vilis, and Eunice), martyred under Decius (c. 250); St. Stephen the Sabaite (725–794 or 807), hymnographer and monk of St. Sabbas Lavra; and the Great-Martyr Paraskeva of Iconium, surnamed Friday (Piatnytsia).
[^1120]: Tone 4 Prosomoion: *Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ* ("As One Noble").
[^1121]: Tone 8 Melodic model: *Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ* ("O Most Marvelous Wonder").
[^1122]: General Troparion of Martyrs, Tone 4 (*Мученицы Твои, Господи*).
[^1123]: General Troparion of a Hierarch and Teacher, Tone 8 (*Православия наставниче*), appointed here for St. Stephen the Sabaite.
[^1124]: Katavasia of the Theotokos: *Ἀνοίξω τὸ στόμα μου* ("I shall open my mouth"), Ode 1 heirmos.
[^1125]: Ode 1 Heirmos: Exodus 15:1–19 (Song of Moses at the Red Sea).
[^1126]: Ode 3 Heirmos adapting 1 Samuel (1 Kings LXX) 2:2, 30 and Jeremiah 9:23–24 ("Let not the wise man glory in his wisdom, neither let the mighty man glory in his might, let not the rich man glory in his riches...").
[^1127]: Ode 3 Heirmos: Psalm 110:10 (LXX: *Ἀρχὴ σοφίας φόβος Κυρίου*).
[^1128]: Tone 4 Kontakion prosomoion: *Вознесы́йсѧ на крⷭ҇тъ* ("Thou Who Wast Lifted Up upon the Cross").
[^1129]: Tone 8 Sessional Hymn prosomoion: *Τὴν Σοφίαν καὶ Λόγον* / *Премꙋ́дрости [и҆ Сло́ва]* ("The Wisdom and Word").
[^1130]: Tone 5 Troparion/Sessional prosomoion: *Τὸν συνάναρχον Λόγον* / *Собезнача́льное Сло́во* ("The Word Co-Unoriginate").
[^1131]: Ode 4 Heirmos: Habakkuk 3:11 (LXX: *Ἐπήρθη ὁ ἥλιος, καὶ ἡ σελήνη ἔστη ἐν τῇ τάξει αὐτῆς...*).
[^1132]: Ode 4 Heirmos: Habakkuk 3:2 (LXX: *Κύριε, εἰσακήκοα τὴν ἀκοήν σου καὶ ἐφοβήθην, κατενόησα τὰ ἔργα σου καὶ ἐξέστην...*).
[^1133]: Ode 5 Heirmos: John 12:46 ("I am come a light into the world, that whosoever believes on me should not abide in darkness").
[^1134]: Ode 5 Heirmos: Isaiah 26:9, 12 (LXX: *Ἐκ νυκτὸς ὀρθρίζει τὸ πνεῦμά μου πρὸς σέ, ὁ Θεός, διότι φῶς τὰ προστάγματά σου ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς...*).
[^1135]: Ode 6 Heirmos: Jonah 2:10 (LXX: *Ἐγὼ δὲ μετὰ φωνῆς αἰνέσεως καὶ ἐξομολογήσεως θύσω σοι...*) and John 19:34 (blood and water from Christ's pierced side).
[^1136]: Ode 6 Heirmos: Jonah 2:1–10 (Jonah in the belly of the sea monster).
[^1137]: Tone 4 Kontakion prosomoion: *Вознесы́йсѧ на крⷭ҇тъ* ("Thou Who Wast Lifted Up upon the Cross").
[^1138]: Ode 7 Heirmos: Daniel 3:52–57 (LXX Song of the Three Holy Children: *Εὐλογητὸς εἶ ἐν τῷ ναῷ τῆς ἁγίας δόξης σου...*).
[^1139]: Ode 7 Heirmos: Daniel 3:49–50 (The angel of the Lord making the midst of the furnace as a blowing dewy breeze).
[^1140]: Exodus 17:6 and Numbers 20:11 (Moses striking the rock at Horeb/Meribah to bring forth water for the thirsty people).
[^1141]: Ode 8 Heirmos: Daniel 6:16–23 (Daniel in the lions' den) and Daniel 3:49–50 (Song of the Three Holy Children).
[^1142]: Ode 8 Heirmos: Daniel 3:1–18 (The golden image on the plain of Dura and the three youths refusing to worship it).
[^1143]: Philippians 3:8: "Yea doubtless, and I count all things but loss... and do count them but dung (σκύβαλα / Slavonic ᲂу҆ме́ты), that I may win Christ."
[^1144]: Ode 9 Heirmos: prophetic reference to Daniel 2:34, 45, the stone cut out of the mountain without hands, prefiguring the Virgin Birth of Christ.
[^1145]: Another Heirmos of Ode 9 for the Venerable Stephen: Byzantine heirmos type honoring the Mother of God who contained the uncontainable divine fire within her womb.
[^1146]: October 28 in the Pochaiv Menaion commemorates the Holy Martyr Paraskeve (Iconium / Rome). The name Paraskeve (*Παρασκευή*, *Параске́ва*) signifies "Preparation" or "Friday", referring to the Crucifixion and Passion of Christ.
[^1147]: Rubric governing the rank of the commemoration: optional Polyeleos at the discretion of the superior (*nastoyatel*), in which case the service expands to 8 stichera at Vespers with paremias.
[^1148]: Podoben *Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Given a Sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе)*, derived from the Great Ochtoechos Saturday / festal cycle.
[^1149]: Slavonic rubric cross-reference `днї́стъ п҃з` (fol. 87), referring to the text of the Stavrotheotokion *Seeing Thee Crucified* (*Ви́дѧщи тѧ̀ распина́ема*) printed earlier in the Menaion volume. Masoretic / Menaion coordinate.
[^1150]: LXX Isaiah 43:9–14 (Masoretic 43:9–14). Solemn prophetic paremia prescribed for martyrs and confessors.
[^1151]: Wisdom of Solomon 3:1–9. Canonical wisdom reading for martyr and saint commemorations.
[^1152]: Wisdom of Solomon 5:15–6:3. Standard reading for martyrs and righteous rulers.
[^1153]: Podoben *Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, [O Life-Bearing Cross]" (Ра́дꙋйся)*.
[^1154]: Podoben *Tone 1, Podoben: "As One Valiant among Martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ)*.
[^1155]: LXX Psalm 15:3 (Masoretic 16:3).
[^1156]: LXX Psalm 39:2 (Masoretic 40:1).
[^1157]: Stavrotheotokion Podoben *Tone 8, Podoben: "O Most Marvelous Wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ)*.
[^1158]: General / festal Troparion (Troparion) of the Holy Great-Martyr Paraskeve of Iconium / Rome, Tone 4.
[^1159]: Sessional Hymn after the first Kathisma, *Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily Anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ)*.
[^1160]: Sessional Hymn after the second Kathisma, *Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Lifted Up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йся)*.
[^1161]: Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos, *Tone 8, Podoben: "[Having Received] the Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости)*.
[^1162]: LXX Psalm 67:36, 27 (Masoretic 68:35, 26).
[^1163]: Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 62 (Matthew 15:21–28 / 25:1–13 per Slavic usage for female martyrs).
[^1164]: First Canon of the Saint, Ode 1 Heirmos, Tone 4: the classic Heirmologion incipit *Ѿве́рзꙋ ᲂу҆ста̀ моѧ̑* ("I Shall Open My Mouth"), attributed to St. John Damascene.
[^1165]: Second Canon of the Saint, Ode 1 Heirmos, Tone 8: *Пои́мъ гдⷭ҇ви* ("Let Us Sing unto the Lord"), based on the First Canticle of Moses (Exodus 15:1).
[^1166]: The printed Church Slavonic text contains a typographical dittography: `ра́й ѡ҆брѣ́тъ ѡ҆брѣ́тше бцⷢе` ("having found... having found"), translated cleanly here.
[^1167]: Ode 3 Heirmos, Tone 4: *Лꙋ́къ си́льныхъ и҆знемо́же* ("The Bow of the Mighty Is Weakened"), based on the Third Canticle (1 Kingdoms / 1 Samuel 2:4).
[^1168]: Second Canon, Ode 3 Heirmos, Tone 8: *Ты̀ є҆сѝ ᲂу҆твержде́нїе* ("Thou Art the Confirmation of Those Who Flee unto Thee, O Lord").
[^1169]: Sessional Hymn after Ode 3, *Tone 8, Podoben: "[Having Received] the Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости)*.
[^1170]: Sessional Hymn after Ode 3, *Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily Anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ)*.
[^1171]: Allusion to Luke 1:48: "For, behold, from henceforth all generations shall call me blessed" (Slavonic: `ꙗ҆́коже прорекла̀ є҆сѝ`).
[^1172]: First Canon, Ode 4 Heirmos, Tone 4: *Сѣдѧ́й въ сла́вѣ* ("He Who Sits in Glory upon the Throne of the Godhead"), Habakkuk 3:1.
[^1173]: Prophetic type of the Virgin Birth: the dew upon Gideon's fleece (Judges 6:37–40; LXX Psalm 71:6).
[^1174]: Second Canon, Ode 4 Heirmos, Tone 8: *Оу҆слы́шахъ гдⷭ҇и смотре́нїѧ твоегѡ̀ та́инство* ("I Have Heard, O Lord, the Mystery of Thy Dispensation"), Habakkuk 3:2.
[^1175]: First Canon, Ode 5 Heirmos, Tone 4: *Ты̀ гдⷭ҇и мо́й свѣ́тъ въ мі́ръ прише́лъ є҆сѝ* ("Thou, My Lord, Hast Come as a Light into the World"), John 12:46.
[^1176]: Ode 5, Heirmos of the Second Canon (Tone 8): Оу́тренююще вопїе́мъ тѝ Го́споди спаси́ ны (Rising early at dawn we cry aloud unto Thee, O Lord: Save us), based on Isaiah 26:9.
[^1177]: Ode 6, Heirmos of the First Canon (Tone 4): Пожрꙋ́ ти со гла́сомъ хвале́нїѧ Го́споди (I will sacrifice unto Thee with a voice of praise, O Lord), based on Jonah 2:10.
[^1178]: Ode 6, Heirmos of the Second Canon (Tone 8): Ри́зꙋ мнѣ̀ пода́ждь свѣ́тлꙋ (Grant me a robe of light), echoing Psalm 103:2.
[^1179]: Kontakion of the Holy Martyr Paraskeve, Tone 3. Melodic model: Today the Virgin (Дѣ́ва дне́сь).
[^1180]: Ikos of the Holy Martyr Paraskeve. The incipit echoes Psalm 105:2 (Кто̀ возглаго́летъ си̑лы Госпо́дни).
[^1181]: Ode 7, Heirmos of the First Canon (Tone 4): Спасы́й во ѻ҆гнѝ а҆враа́мскїѧ твоѧ̑ ѻ҆́троки (Thou Who didst save Thy Abrahamic children in the fire), based on Daniel 3.
[^1182]: Ode 7, Heirmos of the Second Canon (Tone 8): І҆ꙋде́и доше́дше ѻ҆́троцы (The Hebrew children having entered Babylon), based on Daniel 3.
[^1183]: Ode 8, Heirmos of the First Canon (Tone 4): Ѻ҆́троки бл҃гочести̑выѧ въ пещѝ (The pious children in the furnace), based on Daniel 3.
[^1184]: Ode 8, Heirmos of the Second Canon (Tone 8): Царѧ́ небе́снаго (The King of heaven), text continues on physical leaf p304.
[^1185]: Ode 9, Heirmos of the First Canon (Tone 4): Всѧ́къ земноро́дный да взыгра́етсѧ (Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy in spirit).
[^1186]: Ode 9, Heirmos of the Second Canon (Tone 8): Вои́стиннꙋ Богоро́дицꙋ тѧ̀ и҆сповѣ́даемъ (Truly we confess thee to be the Theotokos).
[^1187]: The Greek name Paraskeve (Παρασκευή) signifies Preparation, specifically the Day of Preparation (Friday) for the Sabbath, referencing Christ's Crucifixion.
[^1188]: Exapostilarion of the Holy Martyr Paraskeve. Melodic model: Give Ear, Ye Women (Жены̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите).
[^1189]: Stichera at the Praises, Tone 4. Melodic model: As One Valiant among Martyrs (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ).
[^1190]: Other Stichera at the Praises, Tone 4. Melodic model: Joy of the Heavenly Ranks (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ).
[^1191]: Theotokion / Cross-Theotokion at the Praises, Tone 6. Melodic model: Having Laid Aside All Earthly Hope (Всѐ ѿло́жше).
[^1192]: Theotokion / Cross-Theotokion at the Aposticha, Tone 4. Melodic model: As One Valiant among Martyrs (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ).
[^1193]: Prokeimenon of the Liturgy, Tone 4 (Psalm 67:36 LXX).
[^1194]: Prokeimenon verse (Psalm 67:27 LXX).
[^1195]: Liturgical Epistle lection: 1 Corinthians, Section 181 (1 Cor. 12:7–11).
[^1196]: Alleluia of the Liturgy (Psalm 39:2 LXX).
[^1197]: Liturgical Gospel lection: Luke, Section 33 (Luke 7:36–50).
[^1198]: Communion Hymn (Kinonikon) for a righteous woman or martyr (Psalm 111:6b LXX).
[^1199]: Commemoration of the Holy Venerable-Martyr Anastasia the Roman (3rd c.) and the Venerable Father Abramius the Recluse of Mesopotamia and his niece Blessed Mary (4th c.).
[^1200]: Stichera at "Lord, I Have Cried" to the Martyr, Tone 8. Melodic model: What Shall We Call You, O Holy Ones (Что̀ ва́съ нарече́мъ).
[^1201]: Stichera to the Venerable, Tone 8. Melodic model: O Most Marvelous Wonder (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ).
[^1202]: An allusion to St. Abramius's niece Mary, who fell into sin and despair, but whom Abramius reclaimed through prayer and repentance.
[^1203]: Common Troparion for a Woman-Martyr, Tone 4: А́гница твоя̀ І҆исо́усе (Thy lamb, O Jesus).
[^1204]: Common Troparion for a Venerable Monastic Father, Tone 8: Въ тебѣ̀ ѻ́тче и҆звѣ́стнѡ спасе́ся (In thee, O father, was that which is according to the image saved with certainty).
[^1205]: Canon to the Holy Venerable-Martyr Anastasia, Tone 8, composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer (9th c.). Acrostic: Мꙋ́жескїѧ твоѧ̑ мꙋ́ченице восхвалю̀ болѣ́зни (Thy manly pains, O martyr, will I praise).
[^1206]: Ode 1, Heirmos (Tone 8): Пои́мъ Го́сподеви (Let us sing unto the Lord), based on the First Canticle of Moses (Exodus 15:1).
[^1207]: Canon to the Venerable Abramius the Recluse, Tone 8, composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer. Acrostic: Твоѐ чествꙋ́ю всесвѣ́тлое житїѐ блаже́нне. І҆ѡ́сифъ (Thy all-radiant life do I honor, O blessed one. Joseph).
[^1208]: Ode 1, Heirmos of the Canon to Abramius (Tone 8): Во́дꙋ проше́дъ ꙗ҆́кѡ сꙋ́шꙋ (Having traversed the water as on dry land), based on the crossing of the Red Sea.
[^1209]: Ode 3, Heirmos of the Canon to Anastasia (Tone 8): Ты̀ є҆сѝ ᲂу҆твержде́нїе притека́ющихъ къ тебѣ̀ Го́споди (Thou art the confirmation of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord).
[^1210]: Ode 3, Heirmos of the Canon to Abramius (Tone 8): Небе́снагѡ крꙋ́га верхотво́рче Го́споди (O Lord, Creator of the celestial vault).
[^1211]: Kontakion of the Venerable-Martyr Anastasia, Tone 3. Melodic model: Today the Virgin (Дѣ́ва дне́сь).
[^1212]: Sessional Hymn to the Venerable-Martyr Anastasia, Tone 8. Melodic model: Of Wisdom and the Word (Премꙋ́дрости).
[^1213]: Sessional Hymn to the Venerable Abramius, Tone 1. Melodic model: While the Soldiers Were Guarding Thy Tomb (Гро́бъ тво́й).
[^1214]: Ode 4, Heirmos (Tone 8): Оу҆слы́шахъ Го́споди смотре́нїѧ твоегѡ̀ та́инство (I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation), based on Habakkuk 3:2; text continues on physical leaf p311.
[^1215]: Ode 5, Heirmos of the Canon to Anastasia (Tone 8): Оу́тренюще вопїе́мъ тѝ Го́споди спаси́ ны (Rising early at dawn we cry aloud unto Thee, O Lord: Save us), based on Isaiah 26:9.
[^1216]: Ode 5, Heirmos of the Canon to Abramius (Tone 8): Вскꙋ́ю мѧ̀ ѿри́нꙋлъ є҆сѝ ѿ лица̀ твоегѡ̀ свѣ́те незаходи́мый (Therefore hast Thou cast me off from Thy face, O Never-setting Light), echoing Psalm 42:2 / 87:15.
[^1217]: Ode 6, Heirmos of the Canon to Anastasia (Tone 8): Ри́зꙋ мнѣ̀ пода́ждь свѣ́тлꙋ (Grant me a robe of light, O Thou Who deckest Thyself with light as with a garment), echoing Psalm 103:2.
[^1218]: Ode 6, Heirmos of the Canon to Abramius (Tone 8): Моли́твꙋ пролїю̀ ко Го́сподꙋ (I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord), based on Jonah 2 and Psalm 87:4 (LXX).
[^1219]: Kontakion of the Venerable Abramius the Recluse, Tone 3. Melodic model: Today the Virgin (Дѣ́ва дне́сь).
[^1220]: Ikos of the Venerable Abramius the Recluse.
[^1221]: Ode 7, Heirmos of the Canon to Anastasia (Tone 8): Ѻ҆́троцы є҆вре́йстїи въ пещѝ (The Hebrew children in the furnace), based on Daniel 3.
[^1222]: An allusion to St. Abramius's rescue of his niece Blessed Mary from moral and spiritual ruin, reclaiming her to monastic repentance.
[^1223]: Ode 8, Heirmos of the Canon to Anastasia (Tone 8): Седмери́цею пе́щь (The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold), based on Daniel 3.
[^1224]: Ode 8, Heirmos of the Canon to Abramius (Tone 8): Побѣди́тели мꙋчи́телѧ и҆ пла́мене (Having become conquerors of the tyrant and of the flame), based on Daniel 3.
[^1225]: An allusion to St. Abramius's rescue of his niece Blessed Mary from the pit of destruction where the adversary cast her.
[^1226]: Ode 9, Heirmos of the Canon to Anastasia (Tone 8): Оу҆жасе́сѧ ѡ҆ се́мъ не́бо (Heaven was astonished thereat).
[^1227]: Ode 9, Heirmos of the Canon to Abramius (Tone 8): Вои́стиннꙋ Богоро́дицꙋ тѧ̀ и҆сповѣ́даемъ (Truly we confess thee to be the Theotokos).
[^1228]: 1 Corinthians 2:9, citing Isaiah 64:4.
[^1229]: Prokeimenon of the Liturgy, Tone 7 (Psalm 115:6, 3 LXX).
[^1230]: Liturgical Epistle lection: Galatians, Section 213 (Gal. 5:22–6:2).
[^1231]: Alleluia of the Liturgy, Tone 6 (Psalm 111:1–2 LXX).
[^1232]: Liturgical Gospel lection: Matthew, Section 43 (Matt. 11:27–30).
[^1233]: Communion Hymn (Kinonikon) for a venerable monastic (Psalm 111:6b LXX).
[^1234]: Commemoration of the Holy Hieromartyr Zenobius, Bishop of Aegae in Cilicia, and his sister Zenobia (c. 285).
[^1235]: Stichera at "Lord, I Have Cried", Tone 4. Melodic model: As One Valiant among Martyrs (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ).
[^1236]: Doxasticon at "Lord, I Have Cried", Tone 6, by St. John of Damascus (John the Monk).
[^1237]: Theotokion / Cross-Theotokion, Tone 6. Melodic model: On the Third Day (Трїдне́венъ).
[^1238]: Common Troparion of Martyrs, Tone 4: Мꙋ́ченицы твоѝ Го́споди (Thy martyrs, O Lord).
[^1239]: Canon to the Holy Martyrs Zenobius and Zenobia, Tone 8, composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer. Ode 1, Heirmos: Пѣ́снь возсле́мъ лю́дїе (Let us send up a song, O ye peoples), based on Exodus 15:1.
[^1240]: Ode 3, Heirmos (Tone 8): Стра́хъ тво́й Го́споди всадѝ (Plant Thy fear, O Lord, in the hearts of Thy servants); text continues on physical leaf p318.
[^1241]: Sessional Hymn to the Holy Martyrs, Tone 4. Melodic model: While the Soldiers Were Guarding Thy Tomb (Гро́бъ тво́й).
[^1242]: Ode 4, Heirmos (Tone 8): Всѣ́лъ є҆сѝ на ко́ни (Thou hast mounted upon horses, Thine apostles, O Lord), based on Habakkuk 3:8.
[^1243]: Ode 5, Heirmos (Tone 8): Вскꙋ́ю мѧ̀ ѿри́нꙋлъ є҆сѝ ѿ лица̀ твоегѡ̀ свѣ́те незаходи́мый (Therefore hast Thou cast me off from Thy face, O Never-setting Light), echoing Psalm 42:2 / 87:15.
[^1244]: Ode 6, Heirmos (Tone 8): Моли́твꙋ пролїю̀ ко Го́сподꙋ (I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord), based on Jonah 2 and Psalm 87:4 (LXX).
[^1245]: Kontakion of the Holy Martyrs Zenobius and his sister Zenobia, Tone 8. Melodic model: As the First-fruits of Nature (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки).
[^1246]: Ikos of the Holy Martyrs Zenobius and his sister Zenobia.
[^1247]: Ode 7, Heirmos (Tone 8): Ѻ҆́троцы є҆вре́йстїи въ пещѝ (The Hebrew children in the furnace), based on Daniel 3.
[^1248]: Ode 8, Heirmos (Tone 8): Бг҃оглаго́ливїи ѻ҆́троцы въ пещѝ (The God-proclaiming children in the furnace), based on Daniel 3.
[^1249]: Ode 9, Heirmos (Tone 8): Оу҆страши́ся вся́къ слꙋ́хъ (Every ear was dismayed at the ineffable condescension of God), based on Luke 1:48, 52 and Habakkuk 3:2 (LXX).
[^1250]: Exapostilarion of the Holy Martyrs, Tone 1. Melodic model: Give Ear, Ye Women (Же́ны оу҆слы́шите).
[^1251]: October 31. Commemoration of the Holy Apostles of the Seventy: Stachys, Amplias, Urban, Narcissus, Apelles, and Aristobulus (cf. Romans 16:8-10); and the Holy Martyr Epimachus of Pelusium (Alexandria, c. 250).
[^1252]: Stichera of the Holy Apostles at "Lord, I Have Cried", Tone 1. Melodic model: O All-lauded Martyrs (Всехва́льнїи мч҃ницы).
[^1253]: Physical facsimile Fol. 160r (leaf p322) bears an archival paper repair strip along the outer right margin, obscuring the line-end syllables in the first two stichera. Bracketed restorations are supplied from the parallel Ruthenian Menaion witness and surviving ink traces.
[^1254]: Stichera of the Holy Martyr Epimachus, Tone 4. Melodic model: As One Valiant Among Martyrs (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ).
[^1255]: Common Troparion of the Holy Apostles of the Seventy, Tone 3: А҆пⷭ҇ли ст҃і́и моли́те (O holy apostles, entreat the merciful God).
[^1256]: Common Troparion of a Martyr, Tone 4: Мꙋ́ченикъ тво́й гдⷭ҇и є҆піма́хъ (Thy martyr, O Lord, Epimachus).
[^1257]: Canon to the Holy Apostles of the Seventy, Tone 4, bearing the acrostic "Вѣ́рнѡ похвалю̀ бж҃їя слꙋгѝ" (Faithfully will I praise the servants of God), composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer. Ode 1, Heirmos: Твоѧ̀ побѣди́тельнаѧ десни́ца (Thy victorious right hand), based on Exodus 15:6.
[^1258]: Second Canon, to the Holy Martyr Epimachus, Tone 4. Ode 1, Heirmos: Ѡ҆спою̀ тебѣ̀ гдⷭ҇и бж҃е мо́й (I will sing unto Thee, O Lord my God), based on Exodus 15:1; heirmos concludes on physical leaf p324.
[^1259]: Ode 3, Heirmos (Tone 4): Е҆ди́не вѣ́дый человѣ́ческагѡ сꙋщества̀ не́мощь (O Thou Who alone knowest the weakness of human nature), based on 1 Samuel (1 Kings LXX) 2:2.
[^1260]: Ode 3, Heirmos of the Second Canon (Tone 4): Лꙋ́къ си́льныхъ и҆знемо́же (The bow of the mighty hath grown weak), based on 1 Samuel (1 Kings LXX) 2:4.
[^1261]: Sessional Hymn of the Holy Apostles, Tone 1. Melodic model: While the Soldiers Were Guarding Thy Tomb (Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се).
[^1262]: Sessional Hymn of the Holy Martyr Epimachus, Tone 3. Melodic model: Of the Divine Faith (Бж҃е́ственныя вѣ́ры).
[^1263]: Ode 4, Heirmos (Tone 4): Го́рꙋ тя̀ бл҃года́тїю бж҃їею приосѣне́ннꙋю (Habakkuk, with farsighted eyes, perceived thee as a mountain overshadowed by the grace of God), based on Habakkuk 3:3 (LXX).
[^1264]: Ode 4, Heirmos of the Second Canon (Tone 4): Твоегѡ̀ на землѝ ꙗ҆вле́нїя (Proclaiming Thy coming upon earth, O Christ God, the prophet cried with rejoicing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord), based on Habakkuk 3:2.
[^1265]: Ode 5, Heirmos (Tone 4): Просвѣти́вый сїѧ́нїемъ прише́ствїя твоегѡ̀ (Thou Who hast enlightened with the brightness of Thy coming, O Christ), based on Isaiah 26:9.
[^1266]: Ode 5, Heirmos of the Second Canon (Tone 4): Просвѣще́нїе твоѐ гдⷭ҇и, низпослѝ на́мъ (Send down upon us Thy enlightenment, O Lord), based on Isaiah 26:9.
[^1267]: Ode 6, Heirmos (Tone 4): Ѡ҆бы́де на́съ послѣ́дняя бе́здна (The lowest depth hath surrounded us), based on Jonah 2:5-6 and Psalm 43:22 / Romans 8:36.
[^1268]: Ode 6, Heirmos of the Second Canon (Tone 4): Грозопѝ проѡбразова̀ погребе́нїе тридне́вное (The prophet Jonah, praying in the whale, prefigured the three-day burial), based on Jonah 2:1-2.
[^1269]: Kontakion of the Holy Apostles, Tone 8. Melodic model: As the First-fruits of Nature (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки).
[^1270]: Ode 7, Heirmos (Tone 4): Тебѐ оу҆́мнꙋю бцⷣе пе́щь (We the faithful perceive thee, O Theotokos, as a spiritual furnace), based on Daniel 3.
[^1271]: Ode 7, Heirmos of the Second Canon (Tone 4): А҆враа́мстїи и҆ногда̀ въ вавѵлѡ́нѣ ѻ҆́троцы (Once in Babylon the children of Abraham trampled the furnace flame), based on Daniel 3.
[^1272]: Ode 8, Heirmos (Tone 4): Въ пе́щи ѻ҆́троцы і҆и҃левы (In the furnace the children of Israel, as in a crucible), based on Daniel 3.
[^1273]: Ode 8, Heirmos of the Second Canon (Tone 4): Во ѡ҆́бразѣ а҆́гг҃ла ꙗ҆́вльшагося въ пе́щи ѻ҆́гненнѣй (Unto Him Who in the form of an angel appeared in the fiery furnace), based on Daniel 3.
[^1274]: Ode 9, Heirmos (Tone 4): Ѡ҆́бразъ чⷭ҇та́гѡ ржⷭ҇тва̀ твоегѡ̀ (The unburned bush showed forth a type of thy pure childbirth), based on Exodus 3:2.
[^1275]: Ode 9, Heirmos of the Second Canon (Tone 4): Тя̀ невмѣсти́мое сло́во во оу҆тро́бꙋ вмѣсти́вшꙋю (Thee who didst contain the uncontainable Word in thy womb), based on Luke 1:48.
[^1276]: Exapostilarion of the Holy Apostles, Tone 1. Melodic model: Give Ear, Ye Women (Же́ны оу҆слы́шите).
[^1277]: Liturgical conclusion of the final Exapostilarion Theotokion of October 31 (Feast of the Holy Apostles Stachys, Amplias, Urban, Narcissus, Apelles, and Aristobulus of the Seventy, and the Holy Martyr Epimachus of Alexandria), concluding the complete hymnographic cycle of the Month of October.
[^1278]: Typographical tailpiece of the Pochaiv Lavra printing house, displaying a stylized woven basket overflowing with blossoming carnations, tulips, and foliage, traditionally placed at the conclusion of a liturgical volume.
[^1279]: Interwar Galician provenance inscriptions dating to October 1933, recording signatures of parish cantors and readers (Ivan Lazurkevych, Stefan Kolodiy, Dmytro Konova, Afonina).
[^1280]: Canonical visitation endorsement in Ukrainian script by Servant of God Metropolitan Andrey Sheptytsky (1865–1944), Archbishop of Lviv and Metropolitan of Halych for the Ukrainian Greek Catholic Church: "Submitted at the time of canonical visitation. — Lviv, May 24, 1934. + Andrey".
[^1281]: Archival juxtaposition leaf depicting the printed title page of the September Menaion (Volume 1) from the same 1761 Pochaiv press run, exhibiting identical royal, episcopal, and monastic dedications under King Augustus III of Poland-Lithuania, Protoarchimandrite Hypatius Bielinski, OSBM, and Bishop Sylvester Lubieniecki-Rudnicki of Lutsk and Ostroh.
[^1282]: Codicological witness of the complete book block fore-edge, displaying the uniform carmine-red edge tinting applied by the Pochaiv Lavra monastic bindery to seal the text block against dust and moisture.
[^1283]: Archival witness of the binding spine: authentic 18th-century dark brown calfskin over five raised cord bands dividing the spine into six compartments with blind-tooled horizontal fillets, displaying authentic handling patina and minor abrasions at the headcap and tailcap.
[^1284]: Archival paper shelfmark label affixed to the lower compartment of the spine over remnants of an earlier red-bordered label. The inscription combines modern printed and handwritten Cyrillic notations identifying the volume as containing both the September and October Menaia ("Мінея / Почаїв 1761 / вересень / жовтень") alongside the official library call number "Стд. 173/5 (нв. 355)" (Early Printed Books collection, inventory no. 355).
[^1285]: Codicological witness of the book block tail edge, displaying the trimmed red-stained paper leaves and the lower binding turn-ins, concluding the physical photographic documentation of the monument.
